# Environment flags — the audited catalog

> **A new `MEMRA_*` read needs a row here IN THE SAME COMMIT.** `tools/hooks/pre-push` runs
> `tools/check-flags.sh` on every push (+0.55 s) and refuses one that adds an uncovered name. That
> arm landed 2026-08-23 after main went red three times in one day on this exact rule — the census
> was correct, but its only enforcement was a CI job, so every violation was discovered *after*
> landing and the lanes queued behind it each paid a repair commit. The refusal message names the
> missing rows; the emergency escape and its logging are in CLAUDE.md beside the other hook arms.
>
> **There is no grandfather list to add a name to** — do not go looking for one. A 75-name baseline
> existed until 2026-08-23 and was deleted once measurement showed all 75 were documented here
> anyway, i.e. every exemption was dead while still able to absorb a live one: with it in place,
> *deleting* the row for `MEMRA_SPEC` or `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE` kept the gate green. Its absence is now
> asserted, so restoring it is a deliberate act needing an owner ruling and an expiry check, not a
> file. Document the flag instead.
>
> Note for whoever writes the next such banner: a family row is written as the backticked name plus
> a trailing `*`, and this paragraph deliberately does not spell one out. An illustrative prefix in
> prose is a LIVE prefix row to the census — the draft of this note used the tcol family as its
> example and would have covered every future `MEMRA_TCOL_` flag while all three existing ones are
> catalogued individually below. Same trap the v0.98 review caught on the dspark family.

Doctrine (CLAUDE.md): **winners are defaults** — naked commands run the tuned path. A flag exists only when it is (a) runtime parameter, (b) machine-specific config, (c) documented rollback seam, (d) diagnostic, or (e) explicitly-blocked experimental door. When an experiment concludes negative or flat, the flag and dispatch arm are deleted — the `research/tune-data/*.jsonl` row is the record, not dead code. The 2026-07-08 flag audit enforced this repo-wide; removed-flag ledger at bottom.

Provenance dates refer to `research/tune-data/rig5090.jsonl` / `cloud-rtx6000.jsonl` rows from
that date. Some older rows also cite lane handover notes; those were archived out of this repo on
2026-08-17 as work coordination, and the dated JSONL rows are the surviving primary source.

Rig names below are receipt provenance, not the current gate roster. The 5090 carries the
development-iteration battery and single-card regression boards; pre-merge/pre-tag validation
and final tuning were measured on 2x RTX PRO 6000 pairs (hardware shape, not a deployment
statement — deployment facts live outside this repo). Existing measurements keep their
original rig labels.

**Coverage, stated honestly** (re-audited 2026-08-19 on v0.92.0, 8b61604cc0): the code has
546 distinct literal `MEMRA_*` names at read sites — `env::var`/`env::var_os`/`env!`/
`option_env!` plus the local env helpers in the Rust tree (including `build.rs`), plus
`getenv` in the CUDA/C sources and the `tools/memra_cpu_experts.cpp` companion. After this
audit every one of them is catalogued in §1-§7 or named in the removed ledger (a few families
use their row's shorthand: `MEMRA_LANE_MAX_INTERACTIVE`/`_JUDGE`/`_HARVEST`,
`MEMRA_PENALTY_REPEAT`/`_FREQ`/`_PRESENT`/`_LAST_N`). The previous "residue lives at its read
sites" policy is retired because the v0.92.0 release found it had drifted — 124 read-site
names had no entry here. The internal ones are still internal:
per-kernel A/B forcers, dump/trace probes, and gate/bench-bin inputs are marked as such in
§3-§5 and are not operator knobs — setting one in a deployment is a measurement act, not a
configuration. Zero live-catalogued flags were found without read sites (the stale direction
of the drift is clean; past removals are all in the ledger below). Do not read a terse entry
as an invitation — the doctrine at the top still holds: winners are defaults, and a naked run
needs none of the internal seams.

Scope note: the merged dsv4 knobs are catalogued individually in §5 below — the
numeric arms (`MEMRA_DSV4_DECODE_PATH`, `MEMRA_DSV4_DENSE_ARM`) and the serve-door
seams (`MEMRA_DSV4_DRAFTER`, `MEMRA_DSV4_DEVICES`, `MEMRA_DSV4_SPEC_DEPTH`,
`MEMRA_DSV4_VT`, `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_TAU`/`_FLOOR`,
lane/dsv4-flash-revival-20260822). The dsv4 door itself is **experimental engine
support, not a serving-grade path** (docs/MODELS.md has the posture and the numbers;
tuning is issue #32). The remaining `MEMRA_DSV4_*` names are that door's bench/gate
instrument seams (bench drivers, NVTX/profile hooks, gate dumps, sweep inputs),
covered here as a prefix — none of them is a serving knob.
Unmerged lane flags stay deliberately absent — they arrive with
their lanes and get catalogued at merge. **The dspark knobs left that list in v0.98.0**: the
q38 dspark lanes merged, so every `MEMRA_DSPARK_` name is now catalogued individually below
(and the census gate treats a backticked `NAME*` as a documented prefix, so keeping a merged
family in this note would blind the gate to its future undocumented flags — the v0.98 review
caught exactly that on `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH`/`_DEFER_READBACK`). **The spec-cache knobs
left that list in v0.95.0**, the same promise kept earlier:
`MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED`, `MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY`, `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION`,
`MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_REPUBLISH`, `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_LOAD_GUARD` and `MEMRA_SPEC_BOUNDARY_TRACE`
arrived with their three lanes and are catalogued in §7 below. The automated census reads 507
literal runtime names at v0.95.0 (501 at v0.94.0 + these six).

**Correction, v0.94.0**: treat 546 as a FLOOR, not a total. That audit was measured with the
same instrument that missed `MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER` — the flag deciding whether a
model loads at all — which `tools/check-flags.sh` could not see for two independent reasons:
its crate (`crates/memra-tokenizer/src`) was not in the script's hand-written list of three
runtime dirs, and the read is const-indirected
(`std::env::var(ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER_ENV)` carries no `MEMRA_` name on its line). Both
are fixed: the script now discovers every `crates/*/src` and resolves
`const NAME: &str = "MEMRA_…"` bindings that are actually passed to an env call, with
`tools/test_check_flags.sh` gating one regression case per cause in CI. The automated census
went 494 → 501 literal names on that change; the seven it newly sees
(`MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER`, `MEMRA_INVALID_PINNED_FRAC_TEST_CHILD`, `MEMRA_FATBIN`,
`MEMRA_FP8_FOLD`, `MEMRA_MOE_MMAP_ADVICE`, `MEMRA_NV_W4`, `MEMRA_ST_TEST_DIR`) were all
already catalogued here, so the gate stayed green — but it stayed green for the wrong reason
before, and the 546 hand count has not been re-run against the widened instrument.

---

## 1. Runtime parameters

### Generation (run-gen / run-spec / run-eagle)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_PROMPT` | numeric ids `101..612` | prompt TEXT (tokenized with the model's tokenizer) |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_FILE` | — | prompt text from a file (wins over `MEMRA_PROMPT`) |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_TOKENS` | — | Diagnostic-only explicit token-id prompt for `verify_shape_bisect`; accepts ids separated by non-digit characters so a failing serving tape can be replayed without tokenizer/template ambiguity. |
| `MEMRA_NGEN` | bin-specific (64–256) | number of tokens to generate |
| `MEMRA_CHAT` | off | `1` wraps the prompt in the model's chat template (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_PRINT_TEXT` | off | `1` decodes gold tokens between markers (agent-loop harness, run-spec, 2026-07-08) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_ONLY` | off | run-spec: skip prime-inclusive timing, report gen-only |
| `MEMRA_SEED` | 0 | sampler seed (run-gen sampling path) |
| `MEMRA_TEMP` | greedy | temperature; enables the sampling chain |
| `MEMRA_TOP_K` / `MEMRA_TOP_P` / `MEMRA_MIN_P` | off | sampling filters (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_PENALTY_REPEAT` / `_FREQ` / `_PRESENT` / `_LAST_N` | off | repetition penalties (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_STOP` | EOS only | comma-separated stop strings (run-gen) |
| `MEMRA_MODELS_DIR` | `~/.cache/memra/models` | where `hf:owner/repo[:file]` specs download to and are looked up in (`hf/<owner>--<repo>/`; `frspec-owngen` also puts datasets under `hf-datasets/`). The one knob for putting weights on a different filesystem — point it at the fast local NVMe rather than symlinking a cache |

### Speculative decode (run-spec / serve)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_SPEC_K` | serve: request policy; `run-spec`: K=1..8 battery | Explicit non-negative values pin draft depth for every eligible request; `0` pins plain decode. With the flag unset, serving chooses per request: `K=0` for measured losing placement/concurrency cells, `K=2` for a long prompt with at least 1024 actually-resumed tokens (`K=5` when the loaded MTP head is FR-Spec rank-TRIMMED — the ~29 us trimmed draft lm_head re-prices the kpolicy-20260808 verdict; ornith15 cached-long 350.8 vs ~273 tok/s, research/orndecode-20260822), and `K=3` otherwise. The cached-long direction is measured, not assumed: K=2 beats K=3 by 1.49% on q9 and 7.11% on q27, N=3. The full table and raw receipts are in `research/kpolicy-20260808/`. `run_hy3_local_5090.sh` additionally accepts `all` to omit its override and run the mandatory K=1..8 battery in one model load |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HOST_EMBD` | off | `1` keeps the token-embedding table in host memory for the Hy3 spill profile, reducing HBM pressure at the cost of a host-row transfer |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN` | 0.0 | p-min confidence gate — stop the draft chain when head confidence drops below it (0.15–0.3 typical). K-DEPENDENT (2026-08-05, both rigs): at K=5 on 188 SM it's worth +6.1% e2e (0.3 optimum, 0.2/0.4 both worse); at the 5090's K=3 serve optimum it's FLAT-to-negative despite the same +4–15pp acceptance jump — a K=3 chain is too short for gating to pay. Exactness clean both rigs (byte-identical greedy, run-spec 9 PASS lever-on). Receipts: `research/spec-levers-5090-20260805/`, `research/q27-deepdive-p2-20260805/`. **glm5 scope (lane/glm5-loop-port, 2026-08-30)**: the glm5 T-parallel loop honors the same pair — native chain gates on p-of-pick (`prob_of_token_device`, one 4-byte read per armed draft, breaks BEFORE the next full-MoE-layer chain forward is paid); the DFlash2 arm truncates the proposal at the first selector-q slot below p_min (tau-slot form; greedy walk now records its T=1 q via `dflash2_walk_greedy_q`). Truncation moves draft counts only, never the tape (`gpu_confidence_gate_truncates_drafts_never_the_tape`, both arms, decisive p_min=1.1 zero-draft + slot-0-survivor arms). Engagement receipt `[glm5-spec] draft confidence gate armed: PMIN=..`. VALUE IS UNMEASURED ON GLM5 — stays unset until the box-B tau ladder prices it (per-model law: q27 lost 1.9% at 0.3; step37 ships 0.5) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN0` | off | `1` lets p-min gate slot 0 too (zero-draft rounds). Pays below ~75% base acceptance, hurts above ~90% (2026-07-08, 35B +13–23 tok/s). glm5 (lane/glm5-loop-port): honored — a zero-draft glm5 round verifies the anchor row alone (m=1 = a plain decode step, always legal; sampled rounds take the shared full-accept bonus draw) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HPOST` | off | `1` feeds the MTP head POST-output_norm hidden — acceptance lever on the 27B (>100 tok/s crossing, 2026-07-06). Per-model choice |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` | off | **MULTI-HEAD CHAINS FIXED (2026-08-27):** the trim used to `mtp_extra.clear()` — under `MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=3` it silently collapsed the chain to ONE trimmed head recursed at offsets it was never trained for, measured as the K=3 deep-slot collapse (0.734/0.330/0.053 vs 0.731/0.538/0.282 untrimmed; bf16-head and no-W8 arms reproduced it bit-for-bit — the banked single-head-recursion signature, NOT the wrong-rows mechanism this row briefly claimed). NOW: per-head trim — the d2t ranking is token-frequency and head-independent (all remaps keep reading head 0's map; the chain validator requires d2t equality across heads), and each extra head's ROWS gather from its OWN block's `nextn.shared_head_head.weight` (this family ships a different lm_head per block; a block without its own head ends the chain, never substitutes). Receipt line: `[frspec-trim] per-head trim: N extra chain head(s)...`. Even the collapsed 1-head form WON both shapes under the PMIN fence (greedy 93.18 vs 86.62 untrimmed; sampled 81.79 vs 77.44) — the fence truncates what recursion breaks while the 3.6x-smaller head keeps its speed; the per-head form is upside on deep slots, gate pending |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` | off | **HEAD SOURCE FIXED 2026-08-25 (step-3.7-flash):** the trim gathered its rows from the TRUNK's `output.weight`, which is exact only for tied-head families. step-3.7-flash ships a DIFFERENT lm_head per nextn block, and reading the trunk head there is this repo's measured 0/248-acceptance defect across K=1..8 *with* self-consistency PASS — no exactness gate catches it. The gather now prefers `blk.<n_trunk>.nextn.shared_head_head.weight` when the artifact carries it (pinned by `frspec_trim_prefers_the_nextn_blocks_own_head_name`) and falls back to the trunk head only for families that genuinely share it; `d2t_from_target_head` is set false in the own-head case so DFlash2 never borrows an MTP-head-derived trim. **STEP37 LADDER, MEASURED 2026-08-25** (K=1 = step37's banked policy, agentic turn-8 ctx, x2 reps, prime-exclusive receipt): plain 75.97 · 3-head untrimmed 58.15/57.97 · 1-head untrimmed 59.74/59.63 · **1-head trimmed to 32768 60.63/60.67**. Acceptance is BYTE-IDENTICAL at every rung (drafted 229 / accepted 170), so a 3.9x narrower draft vocabulary is acceptance-neutral; it buys +1.5% throughput and, more importantly, residency — the trimmed rungs run at the natural 262144 context where the 3-head chain OOMs (each untrimmed nextn head is BF16 [128896,4096] = 1.06 GB against 0.27 GB trimmed). Spec remains 0.80x plain because verify-wait is 68.5% of the round (GPU-bound) and routed experts do not amortize across verify columns. **FLOAT HEADS SUPPORTED (same lane):** step-3.7-flash keeps `lm_head.weight` and every `nextn.*.shared_head.output.weight` in BF16 [128896, 4096] while its experts are NVFP4, and `from_quant_bytes` panics on BF16 — the gather now lands BF16 rows in `GpuTensor::FloatBf16` and F32 rows in `Float`, quantized rows unchanged. A row gather is dtype-agnostic (rows are independent, nothing is requantized). |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` | off | `<ranks.txt>`, `<frspec.gguf>`, or `hf:owner/repo:file-substring`: self-trimmed draft lm_head — gathers top-frequency rows from the model's own `output.weight` via the d2t ranking. An `hf:` spec resolves into `MEMRA_MODELS_DIR` like a model path, so the q38 DFlash2 default can fetch its rank artifact declaratively. A **ranks `.txt`** does the trim at load time, which is the safetensors path and needs no separate draft file; a `.gguf` carries a pre-trimmed head. Generic rankings transfer across same-vocab heads, specialised ones do not (2026-07-07). Published ranks: Qwen3.8-27B agentic/prose/mixed in [Avifenesh/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-GGUF](https://huggingface.co/Avifenesh/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-GGUF); Gemma family under `research/gemma4-bringup/*-ranks-*.txt` Since lane/dflash2-head-trim (2026-08-25) the DSPARK/DFlash2 selector round consumes the same self-trim BY DEFAULT when it is loaded: the round's draft lm_head matmul + top-k run over the gathered rows and candidates remap through d2t before the selector walk — verify stays full-vocab (exactness unchanged; boot receipt `[dspark] <model>: DFlash2 draft head TRIMMED to N rows`). Measured PRO 6000 x3: gate trim-q4 160.4 vs full-q4 157.9 spec tok/s; served steady chat 142.9 vs 136.3 / agentic 157.2 vs 154.5 (accept 0.64/0.73 — the wall wins the acceptance trade). Markov/chain drafter families keep the full head by construction. glm5_next (2026-08-30): the T-parallel spec loop (`MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC`) consumes the same trim — draft argmax over the gathered rows, d2t remap before verify, verify full-vocab untouched; the trunk-head gather arm is exact BY CONTRACT for this family (the NextN block ships no private lm_head and projects through the trunk head); glm5 ranks mint pending (owner lane), self-trim d2t arm live. **glm5 DFlash2 draft head WIRED as a DEFAULT-OFF DOOR (lane/frspec-dflash2-20260902, re-landed onto main 2026-09-03):** the serving DFlash2 route (`MEMRA_GLM5_DFLASH`, NextN block NOT loaded) used to leave this contract a SILENT NO-OP: the self-trim only landed on a loaded MtpHead, so the boot receipt said `draft head FULL target vocab` and the drafter projected every round through the full 154,880-row head. Same contract, no new flag: with the door ON the ranks file is gathered ONCE at load into an `[n_ranks x d]` slab of the trunk head's own rows (`frspec_gather_trimmed_head`, the one gather program every trim arm shares; `HybridModel::dflash_trim`), the DFlash2 round scores its draft logits over the slab and remaps candidate RANK ids through d2t before the selector walk; the verify walk is full-vocab and untouched, so the target's output distribution and the greedy tape are unchanged BY CONSTRUCTION and the trim moves acceptance only. DEFAULT OFF = unset (full-vocab draft head, the served arm). ARM ON = `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM=<ranks.txt>` on this route. ROLLBACK = unset. MEASURED VERDICT (2x B200 pair, 2026-09-03, vendor sampling, effort low, 512 tok x5 short + 42k x1, fresh boot per arm, best posture + W8 + DSA=2; glm53-flash-nvfp4 + DFlash2 `b33c0347` + the sxc32768 ranks, K=3 and K=5): NEUTRAL short (code / prose walls within 2%, noise: K=3 83.0/81.5 and 83.1/82.7 code, 58.6/59.7 and 58.4/59.7 prose; K=5 88.5/88.5 and 88.6/86.4 code, 56.7/56.4 and 57.2/56.6 prose, noranks/ranks tok/s) and a CONSISTENT LOSS at 42k decode in every pair, -10 to -22% (K=3 42.5/33.1 and 39.4/31.3; K=5 38.5/34.8 and 38.0/32.2). The 'up to 7%' does not exist on this artifact + head. Receipts: darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/floor/raw/finalclean/spec-{noranks,ranks}-{2,3}-*.jsonl` and `spec-k5{,ranks}-{1,2}-*.jsonl` (every ranks arm's boot log carries `draft head RANK-TRIMMED n_ranks=32768 src=1804027e6148414c`). The door stays OFF as the INSTRUMENT for a future workload-keyed mint (a ranks file minted on the traffic it will draft), never as a candidate default; a flip needs its own interleaved pair receipts. BOOT ADMISSION (owner order), on EVERY consumer of this env var (the glm5 DFlash2 slab, the MtpHead self-trim, the MEMRA_MTP_SKIP stub; the `.txt` reader itself is strict): a `.txt` is parsed STRICTLY (every non-blank line an integer id) and validated against the head it indexes (no duplicates, max id < rows, count <= rows, non-empty); a wrong-model file REFUSES the boot by name with its sha16 (`frspec_validate_ranks`), never loads silently and never reaches the gather's assert. Receipts when ON: boot `[frspec-trim] glm5 DFlash2 draft-head slab: N rows of V gathered from main output.weight (...) src=<sha16>` and the ARMED line's `draft head RANK-TRIMMED n_ranks=N src=<sha16>` (replacing `FULL target vocab`); per session `[glm5-spec] draft head RANK-TRIMMED n_ranks=N src=<sha16>`; per request the `[glm5-acc] ... trim_rounds=<n>` counter (== rounds when engaged, 0 otherwise). Preference when BOTH a target-head-trimmed MtpHead and the drafter are loaded: the MtpHead rows, no second slab. The worker's automatic K policy is UNCHANGED (the cached-long K=5 re-price keys on a loaded MtpHead's d2t, `spec_trim_head`; the pair's K=5 vs K=3 read was mixed too, code +6.6% / prose -2.5% / 42k -8%, so K stays 3). Rig gate: `glm5_dflash_session_gpu` gates 8 and 13 (tape byte-identical trimmed vs untrimmed vs plain at K=1..7 with acceptance free to move, every drafted id inside the ranks set with the untrimmed census proving the set binds, slab rows bit-identical to the head rows they were gathered from, the remap-skipped red arm, and the subprocess receipt matrix incl. the three boot refusals). |
| `MEMRA_MTP_HEAD_NVFP4` | off | `1` loads each NextN block's own lm_head as **NVFP4** instead of the BF16 the step-3.7-flash checkpoint ships (`f32_to_nvfp4` emits the internal QK=64 / 36-B block layout the decode dp4a path already consumes, so no kernel changes). RESIDENCY is the point: an untrimmed head is BF16 [128896, 4096] = 1.06 GB, so a 3-head chain spends 3.18 GB and OOMs beside a 262144-token cache — NVFP4 takes the three to 0.89 GB. This is the repo's own draft-regime standard (`tools/make-trimmed-draft.sh`: "block Q4_K_M + head NVFP4 … NVFP4 head measured zero acceptance cost"), done in-engine because that builder is a GGUF pipeline and this family is safetensors. Draft-head precision cannot change served output — verify arbitrates every drafted token — so acceptance is the only quantity at risk, and it is what the trim ladder measures. **MEASURED 2026-08-25, K=1 agentic ctx, prime-exclusive:** acceptance is UNCHANGED by the 4-bit head — 170 accepted in every arm (trimmed or full, 1-head or 3-head). ctx 8192: bf16-trim 60.62 (head 256 MiB) vs nvfp4-trim 60.05 (72 MiB, 3.6x); bf16-3head 58.13 (3x1007 MiB) vs nvfp4-3head 58.57 (3x283 MiB). NVFP4 is the RESIDENCY choice, not the speed one: -0.9% on the trimmed head because the dp4a/LUT unpack is ALU-bound near 0.8 TB/s where q8 mmvq measures 1.71, +0.8% on the chain where smaller heads relieve cache pressure. At the natural 262144 context only the SINGLE trimmed head fits (60.18 tok/s): the 3-head chain still OOMs after the 2.2 GB saving, and so does W8 alongside it. |
| `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM_NVFP4` | off | `1` re-quantizes the FR-Spec trimmed rows BF16 -> NVFP4 (same encoder, same reasoning). A trimmed head goes 0.27 GB -> 0.076 GB. Independent of `MEMRA_MTP_HEAD_NVFP4`, which covers the untrimmed heads. |
| `MEMRA_MTP_HEADS` | all declared | `N` caps the embedded NextN chain at N heads. A measurement knob, added 2026-08-25 with the step-3.7-flash trim work: `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` forces the chain to ONE head, so a trimmed-vs-untrimmed A/B otherwise confounds the trim with the loss of the chain. With this the ladder is 3-head untrimmed -> 1-head untrimmed -> 1-head trimmed and each step is attributable. Prints `[mtp-chain] MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=N: capping the embedded chain from M heads`. Note `MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=0` is silently ignored (the parser filters non-positive values); "load no MTP block at all" is `MEMRA_MTP_SKIP`, below. |
| `MEMRA_MTP_SKIP` | off | `1` skips loading the embedded MTP/NextN block(s) at model load: the block's attention mixer, full FFN (MoE included), and nextn glue (enorm/hnorm/eh_proj, per-block head) are never uploaded, reclaiming their VRAM on deployments whose drafting program is dspark/DFlash2 and whose MTP spec arm is disabled anyway (the q38 serving shape; owner call 2026-08-30, goal: extra session headroom on the serving card). Receipt: `[mtp-skip] MEMRA_MTP_SKIP=1: skipping N embedded MTP/NextN block(s) blk.X..=blk.Y (~M MiB of weights not loaded)`. The DFlash2 trimmed draft head SURVIVES the skip: with `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` set, the loader gathers the trimmed rows from the trunk `output.weight` as usual and parks them in a dedicated stub (`DflashTrimHead`; receipt `[mtp-skip] FR-Spec stub draft head built: N rows of main output.weight ...`), which the dspark round consumes identically, so boot still prints `[dspark] <model>: DFlash2 draft head TRIMMED to N rows` with the same N. `model.mtp` stays `None`, so `mtp_spec_capable` and every MTP forward path are off by construction, never reachable through the stub. REFUSES LOUD instead of no-opping (each arm executed in the lane receipts): skip together with `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT` (contradictory); skip + trim on an artifact that ships its own per-block lm_head (the trim rows would live in the skipped block: the 0/248-acceptance wrong-head class); skip + trim with an empty d2t list; any value other than `0`/`1`; and, with no dspark drafter armed, ANY `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC` state other than the literal `0` (refuse-loud contract 2026-08-31, hermes finding baf261e2bfdae118): an explicit non-zero value is a spec request the server cannot honor (`[mtp-skip] <model>: ... cannot be honored`), and unset/blank leaves serving spec ON by default, so booting would silently serve plain decode at reduced speed with no receipt (the DFlash2 2026-08-25 incident class; this supersedes the original announce-and-boot default). Only `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0` boots, announcing `[mtp-skip] <model>: serves PLAIN decode by explicit choice ...` per affected model. A model with no declared NextN block is untouched (a global env must not kill a co-loaded plain model), and the flag only exists on the hybrid loader (`load_from_source_impl`); the dsv4 research loader keeps its own MTP path. OFF arm (default, unset or `0`): byte-identical boot receipts and outputs, gated. Rollback seam: unset the env and restart; no persisted state. Default OFF by design: the skip trades the embedded MTP spec arm away, a choice only a dspark-armed (or deliberately plain) deployment should make. VRAM delta, boot-receipt parity, and exactness receipts: `research/mtp-skip-q38-20260830/`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_MTP` | off | `1` loads the glm5_next NextN/MTP block as the embedded draft head (MLA + own k-pool indexer + MoE, serial residual — the checkpoint ships no private lm_head, so the draft projects through the trunk `lm_head`). OFF arm = byte-identical pre-lane serving. The three-way decision window then compared drafters on the same placement (`research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/3way-decision-20260830/`): DFlash2 is THE DRAFTER OF RECORD (owner decision — acc/cycle 1.907 vs native 1.452 at K=3 greedy, +31.3%; vendor-default sampled 1.889 vs 1.365, +38.4%; TTFT penalty near-constant vs native's O(prompt); 66/66 byte identity), and the BEST spec arm still loses to plain: DFlash2 K=1 34.98 vs plain 35.41 tok/s = **0.988x**, no K crosses the break-even `tok/cycle > (31.6 + 20.1*K)/28.24` (K=1 needs 1.862, delivers 1.839). | `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_PREFIX` | **OFF (default)** | `=1` (AND `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT=1` — the predicate requires both) arms the glm5 spec x prefix-cache interplay, BOTH sides on one flag (the `MEMRA_DSPARK_PREFIX_RESTORE` precedent; lane/glm5-prefix-latent2, 2026-09-01). WHY IT EXISTS: under spec serving (`MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC=1` + DFlash2, the launch shape) the prefix cache was structurally DEAD — spec sessions own their cache so nothing ever captured, and a prefix HIT failed `glm5_cold` and demoted the request to the plain route. CAPTURE side: DFlash2-source sessions take a prompt-boundary capture at creation (conv/ssm `Cache::snapshot` + per-layer latent boundary tails via `LatentKvLayer::snapshot_tail`; the append-only planes — latent rows, final pool keys, trunk KV — are sliced from the LIVE cache at publish via `snapshot_plane_at`, legal because the glm5 verify rollback never truncates below the prime boundary); the worker's sweep publishes once the drafter KV covers the boundary (`prefix_capture_ready` — glm5 ingests prompt features at round 1, not creation), carrying the DFlash2 drafter's readable tail (sliding-window 2048, the ~85 MB dspark bound) so the restore half can exist at all. RESTORE side: a whole-entry hit with a non-empty suffix >= PRIME_MIN_T on a spec-capable request rebuilds the drafter KV from the entry tail (`DflashKv::from_tail`, at admission) and the first spec tick builds `glm5_spec_session_from_restored`: restored trunk cache + suffix primed through the continuation program with `HcTapSink` origin-anchored taps + anchor drawn from the suffix logits (fresh Philox — a restore is a NEW session) + a republish capture at the DEEPER boundary so multi-turn conversations deepen every turn. Empty-suffix full-cover hits kept the plain boundary-logits resume until memra#74 showed what that cost (the premise was true of PREFILL and said nothing about DECODE: the drafter never engaged and the whole generation ran plain); they are now the `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_FULLCOVER` arm, its own row; sub-floor suffixes keep the plain hit; native-MTP-source restores refuse (the plane cannot be re-warmed from restored KV). Receipts: `[glm5-spec] ... restored=1` at admission, `[glm5-spec] RESTORED session: N prefix + M suffix` at the tick, `[prefix-cache]` insert lines tagged `glm5-boundary`. OFF arm (default): byte-for-byte the pre-lane posture — spec sessions never capture, hits demote to the plain route (which, with `MEMRA_HYPER_SUFFIX_PRIME`, still primes the suffix at prefill speed). DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN (new-flags law): the restored-session program is unmeasured until the box battery banks restored-vs-cold byte identity ON THE CONTINUATION under the spec route plus the owner-law 8-turn cache twin; the launch recipe arms it together with `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT` + `MEMRA_HYPER_SUFFIX_PRIME`. Rollback seam: unset (both sides revert, memory profile included — no tail is stored with no possible consumer). Receipts: `research/glm5-prefix-latent2-20260901/` |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_FULLCOVER` | **OFF (default)** | `=1` (on top of `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_PREFIX=1`, whose restore side this extends) admits the glm5 spec route on a FULL-COVER prefix hit: a hit whose restored prefix already covers the WHOLE prompt, so there is no suffix left to prime. WHY IT EXISTS (memra#74, lane/glm5-fullcover-spec-route 2026-09-02): the parent lane refused an empty suffix on the premise that "the plain boundary-logits resume is faster than any prime". True of PREFILL, silent about DECODE: with the drafter left un-armed the whole generation then ran at plain speed, so a cache hit cost the customer half the decode speed on the repeated-prompt shape. Measured on the live glm5 serving box (memra-0.123.0-6a23ef199612, vendor-default sampled, 67-token prompt, 512 max_tokens, single stream, same minute and vantage): six identical prompts 29.46 s wall median / 30.8 tok/s decode vs fresh-nonce prompts 57.75 / 69.7, and the slot log read `route=plain K=3 ... cold=0 restored=0` on every hit. Repeated byte-identical prompts are exactly what retries, eval harnesses and provider latency probes send; the claim cell and the reverify wave both salt a fresh `cache_salt` per request, which is why no instrument caught it before exposure. MECHANISM: with an empty suffix the restored session's state IS a cold session's state at the same boundary: restored trunk cache at `fed.len()`, drafter ctx KV rebuilt from the entry's published tail at `fed.len()` (`DflashKv::from_tail`, the same rows a cold prime's tap ingest would have produced), NO prime, NO pending tap rows (so round 1's `kv.len == cache.pos` invariant holds without an ingest), and the anchor drawn from the ENTRY's boundary logits by the same rule the cold burst applies to its own first token. This is the shape the MTP restore has served since lane/spec-cache (`spec_restore_refusal` admits a full-cover hit whenever the entry carries its boundary hidden + logits). Also lands, INDEPENDENT of the flag: the `[glm5-spec]` admission receipt now carries `reason=<token>` on every `route=plain` (`full-cover-hit`, `suffix-below-prime-floor`, `no-drafter-tail`, `prefix-restore-off`, `k-shed-to-zero`, `penalized`, and the rest, all from `glm5_route_decline_reason`, pinned equivalent to `glm5_route_admits` over the whole matrix), so an operator can size the cost of a plain route instead of reading `cold=0 restored=0` and guessing. DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN (new-flags law): the arm has NO GPU receipt. FLIP CONDITION, pre-stated: (1) `gpu_full_cover_restore_bytes_match_plain_decode_and_cold_acceptance` (gate 13 of `crates/memra-engine/tests/glm5_dflash_session_gpu.rs`) green on a CUDA box, i.e. full-cover restored tape byte-identical to plain decode AND drafter acceptance identical to a cold session's; (2) the repeated-prompt cell on the glm5 serving box: the same six-identical-prompts probe that produced the numbers above, re-run with the flag armed, showing `route=spec ... restored=1`, the tick receipt `[glm5-spec] RESTORED session: N prefix tokens + 0 suffix from cache ... arm full-cover`, a `[glm5-acc]` engagement line, and decode recovering toward the cold ceiling; (3) the owner-law 8-turn larger-prompt cache twin. OFF arm: byte-for-byte the pre-lane serving posture (full-cover hits serve plain), the one difference being the route line's `reason=` token; the probe emits an extra `[prefix-cache]` line only for the entry anomaly `full-cover-hit-no-boundary-logits`, never for a shape or posture the route line already names. Rollback seam: unset. Read per call, so one gate process drives both arms. Receipts: NONE YET, by construction — the ONLY receipts that may retire this sentence are the three FLIP CONDITION cells above; the defect measurement itself is banked in darklanes `research/glm5-serving-launch-20260901/soak-20260901/receipts/go-live/20260902T065928Z/` (`publicity-cell-rig-x5.json` vs `publicity-cell-rig-x5-nonce.json`). **FLEET-FATAL DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED ON THE ARM (memra#95, lane/glm5-fullcover-panic 2026-09-02), FLAG STILL OFF.** The first measured run of the arm (darklanes `research/glm5-fullcover-measure-20260902/`) panicked the GPU worker on a full-cover RESTORED tick (`walk: candidate 4294967295 outside codebook vocab`, dflash.rs:221 greedy / :804 sampled; 72% of vendor-default hits did not complete), one respawn then the process exits. CAUSE, a CUDA stream-ordering hole: the drafter ctx KV is imported on the CALLER's stream at admission (`DflashKv::from_tail`), while round 1's draft phase reads it through `glm5_head_engine`, which under a live ppN split is the last stage's OWN Engine used OUTSIDE any `rt.enter` scope, i.e. that Engine's own stream. Nothing ordered the two. The full-cover arm is the only restored path with NOTHING between the import and that first read: the suffix arm calls `prime_cache`, whose logits come back through `Engine::dtoh` (a `stream().synchronize()`) and incidentally drain the caller's stream, while the full-cover arm draws its anchor from the entry's host-side boundary logits and never touches the device. The unordered read gave a NaN drafter row, and the NaN row reached the selector as the top-k's documented exhausted-slot sentinel `0xffffffff`. NOTE `rt.fence_stages_behind` is NOT the fix and was this lane's first wrong answer: it orders `StageRt::stream`, the enter-scope stream, not a stage ENGINE's own stream. FIX: the new `pp::PpNRt::order_engine_behind` orders the head engine's own stream behind the caller's, called UNCONDITIONALLY in `glm5_spec_session_from_restored` before the suffix branch, so the ordering belongs to the restore contract instead of to a host sync that belongs to the prime; and both DFlash2 proposal seams call `dflash2_guard_candidates` before the d2t remap, turning a fleet-fatal worker panic into one refused request (the memra#87 `guard_vocab_token` trade). The walk's assert is unchanged and nothing is clamped. SCOPE, stated: the proposal seams are shared with the LIVE dspark/q38 serve arm, so that route's failure mode changes too, from a worker panic to a refused request; that is the only behaviour change outside this default-OFF flag. Gates: `dflash::dflash2_tests::the_fullcover_restore_ordering_seam_is_live_in_comment_stripped_source` (red against BOTH the no-ordering shape and the `fence_stages_behind` shape), `an_unfilled_selector_slot_is_refused_by_name_not_clamped`, `the_walk_assert_survives_the_guard`. The FLIP CONDITION above is UNCHANGED and still unmet: the fix has no GPU receipt, so cell (2) must re-run the memra#74 battery on a rented non-production box before the flag flips anywhere. |

FAIL-CLOSED SURFACE: a sharded layer poisons every plain path (`kda_core`, `mla_attn_cached`, scan replay, cache snapshot/snapshot_into all refuse by name); glm5 spec sessions are co-refused unless `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_TP=1` arms the gated composition (own row); the SERVING WORKER refuses the flag outright at spawn (serving wiring — per-session TP state admission/rollback — is the named box-lane increment). COMPOSITION MATRIX (merge-forward onto the verify-batch + decode-diet head, 2026-08-31; v1 refuses every unproven pair by name at the load preflight, before any TP CUDA state): TP x `MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE` REFUSED (fused mHC pre-chain gated on the unsharded walk only); TP x `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS` REFUSED (the workspace decode walk carries no TP mixer branches); TP x `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ` REFUSED, either arm (the fused six-projection door is gated on full-width projections, never head shards); TP x `MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT` REFUSED (absorb/decompress split gated on the full-head geometry); TP x `MEMRA_GLM5_VERIFY_BATCH` needs no boot refusal: the batched verify walk exists only inside glm5 spec sessions — co-refused while the TP door is armed until `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_TP=1` arms the GATED composition (own row; the spec x TP pair now HAS its composition gate, `glm5-tp-gate` arms S2/Q-S4) — and the mixer choke points additionally refuse a sharded layer on every plain path if ever reached. Every OTHER pair unlocks only with its own composition gate; until then the refusal is the contract. OFF arm = byte-identical pre-lane behavior, zero cost. Rollback seam: unset the flag (load-time only, no persistent state). Gate: `glm5-tp-gate` (fixture, red-armed, one-card same-device dual-context emulation) holds the TWO-REGIME bar: DECODE (t=1) split-vs-plain BYTE identity per layer-class arm and model level (28 t=1 steps, full logits + tape, ALL ARMS PASS 2026-08-31); PRIME (t>=2) is the documented shard-shape near-tie class — batched GEMM widths (cuBLASLt f32/f16, MMQ tiles) select shape-dependent K-reduction splits, so a shard legally differs by ulps from the full tensor for the same logical row (measured green 2.8e-5..4.9e-5 max-rel; calibrated band 2e-4; reds land 6.7e1..2.7e2, six orders louder; tape identical throughout; the MLA-only arm is byte-identical even at prime). TP-4 arms (lane/glm5-composition, quad fixture 4 KDA + 4 MLA heads, 8 experts, same-device FOUR-context emulation, ALL ARMS PASS 2026-09-01): decode BYTE-identity holds at four ranks on every arm incl. diet + peer-pull, transport-vs-transport byte-identical decode AND prime, reds bite at 1.5e2..4.0e3; the quad prime near-tie class measures 4.013e-4 (thinner shards select different K-reduction splits; reproduced bit-for-bit across runs), own calibrated band PRIME_BAND_QUAD=4e-3 (10x-over-worst precedent); envelope refusals gated (TP-3, TP-4 on a 2-head geometry, ranks=2 map on a TP-4 load). Real-artifact classes (BF16-resident matvec, NVFP4 experts) re-gate on the box. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tp2-20260831/` + `composition-20260901/rig-gates/` |
| `MEMRA_EP_MAP` | unset | GENERAL fleet flag for measured expert placement (generalized from `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_MAP`, lane/glm5-extract-general; env seam `ep_map::ep_map_env()` — the alias row below stays honored; BOTH names set to DIFFERENT paths refuses loudly at load, naming both; every downstream refusal names the flag that actually armed the load). The `memra-ep-map-v1` reader is fleet-shared by design — hy3/qwen adopt this same flag + reader; family loaders add their own geometry laws. ABSENT BY DESIGN (default = the even split, byte-unchanged; a measured map ships only with its own box A/B receipts — LAW:coactivation-expert-placement, owner directive 2026-08-31). `=<path>` points the glm5 TP-2 EP shard builders at a MEASURED expert-placement map — the frozen fleet format `memra-ep-map-v1` (JSON: `format`/`ranks`/`entry_rank`/`expert_count`/`layers[].{layer,assignment}` consumed; the mint's self-receipting `stats`/`traces`/`params` ride along uninspected), minted by the SHARED tool `tools/build_expert_placement_map.py` (strategies coactivation/frequency/even; spec `research/ep-placement-map-20260831/REPORT.md`) from `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE` id traces (+ optional `MEMRA_MOE_WEIGHT_TRACE` hotness) — bundles co-active experts per card under VRAM balance, always-active set pinned to the entry rank. FAIL-CLOSED on every axis, at the load preflight BEFORE any TP CUDA state (reader `ep_map.rs`, house JsonObj parser, no serde): unreadable/malformed file, `format` != memra-ep-map-v1, rank count != 2, `entry_rank` != 0 (the glm5 first-hop card is root — router + combine + shared expert — re-mint with `--entry-rank 0`, never silently remapped), expert count != the model's routed bank, a layer row missing or foreign to the EP-armed set, an empty rank — all refuse by name; SET WITHOUT `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` on a glm5-class plan refuses at load (a placement that silently reverts to the even split is the trap; the check is scoped to KDA-carrying plans so co-loaded non-glm5 models never trip it — the MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM lesson). The armed load prints the map's sha256 (`[glm5-tp-preflight] ep-map armed`). CORRECTNESS IS PLACEMENT-INDEPENDENT BY CONSTRUCTION: ownership selects which rank runs the identical per-expert program over identical fanned-out bytes; the combine stays slot-ordered on root — proven by `glm5-tp-gate` arm M (deliberately skewed map, all-but-one expert on rank 0: decode BYTE-identical to plain, prime in band), reds R4 (`corrupt-ep-map` bites) + H1-H5 (fail-closed refusals), and the reader is byte-anchored on the tool's committed example emission (unit test `parses_the_committed_example_map_bytes`). The map changes bytes MOVED, never bytes COMPUTED — it is a PERF artifact priced by the named box A/B (naive vs measured, interleaved), never a correctness input. | `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT` | **host-canonical** (OFF by written decision) | GENERAL TP transport seam (generalized from `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_TRANSPORT`, lane/glm5-extract2 — the alias row below stays honored). ENGINE-GENERIC BY CONTENT: `tp_transport.rs` is `Rank`, a publication event and named point-to-point hop shapes over dense blocks — not one function in it knows a layer type, a mixer, an expert or a model family (`pp.rs`'s `BoundarySlot` is where the shape came from). Rank-widened to TP-N by lane/glm5-composition and merged here: ranks are plain indices (`0` = root, owner-first unchanged), the ladder runs over every ordered rank pair, and at two ranks each arm reproduces the v1 walk HOP FOR HOP so the banked TP-2 numbers stay comparable. The glm5 TP-N shard walk is the ONLY consumer today and keeps its rank geometry, owner-first law and shard maps; an hy3/step TP walk will arm the same transport once it exists — the seam is general, the second consumer is not written. Same silence as the EP doors and worth the same warning: `tp_transport::transport_env()` has exactly ONE caller (the glm5 TP loader), so setting this on a non-glm5 deployment today does nothing and says nothing. RECEIPT TAGS ARE THE CALLER'S (the phase-1 `[glm5-phase]` rule): glm5 passes `glm5-tp-transport`, so every banked receipt line from lane/glm5-tp-transport keeps its exact bytes. TWO-NAME LAW: this is a VALUED flag resolved ONCE at arm time, so both names set to DISAGREEING values REFUSES THE LOAD naming both (`tp_transport::resolve_transport`) — unlike the per-call boolean doors, which fall closed, because arming happens before any session exists and a load that silently picked a transport the operator did not choose is worse than a refused load. Every refusal and every peer-access grant names the flag the OPERATOR set. Gate arms: `glm5-tp-gate` X0/X1/X2/X3/XT/XF drive the alias (the banked path), **XG** arms peer-pull through the general name with the alias unset (peer_pulls must be >0), **XD** sets a disagreeing pair and asserts the load refuses naming both. Original lane text: glm5 TP-2 TRANSPORT ARM (lane/glm5-tp-transport, 2026-09-01) — WHICH BYTES-MOVER the armed `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` seam uses for its cross-rank hops. Values (`tp_transport::parse_transport`, any other spelling REFUSES by name at load): `host-canonical` / `0` / unset = the v1 arm, every hop a `dtoh` -> host `Vec` -> `htod` pair; `peer-pull` / `1` = every hop a consumer-issued device peer copy (`cuMemcpyDtoDAsync` on the READING rank's stream) published by one CUDA event per rank, with ZERO host boundaries. WHY THE ARM EXISTS: the TP program's cross-rank hops are pure movement (the one arithmetic site, the MoE slot-ordered `fmaf` combine, stays on root), so the transport cannot change a bit — and the movement census derived from code puts the v1 arm at **446 draining `Engine::dtoh` calls and 14.63 MiB per decode token**, which at the measured **13-18 ms/token v1 join tax** (`tp2-battery-20260831/RESULTS.md` cell 3) is **29-40 us per host round trip** and leaves bytes contributing 2% of the tax. It is a COUNT tax, and this flag deletes the count. BOTH ARMS, so a reviewer can price the rollback: OFF keeps v1's hop pattern hop-for-hop (not just byte-for-byte — the sequential EP walk deliberately keeps its one-z-`dtoh`-then-per-token-row-`htod` shape so `=0` reproduces the banked 22.65 tok/s engine-twin walk and not a faster cousin); ON does 4 peer reads + 3 local copies + 4 event primitives per KDA layer-call, 5 + 3 + 5 per MLA, 2 per dieted MoE. PULL not push, by design: `RESEARCH.md` §2.2b measures SM-issued peer reads at ~52 GB/s against writes at 2.6 GB/s (110 us push vs 26 us pull) and b12x banks push collectives as a receipted dead end — that scoping is SM-issued and our copies are copy-engine (~54-56 GB/s both ways), so the load-bearing reason is ORDERING: a consumer-issued copy is ordered against the consuming kernel for free, where a producer-issued push needs a second event. ATOMICS-FREE and SENTINEL-FREE, forced: every SM120 PCIe pair reports `NativeAtomicSupported=0` (§2.1) and CAS on peer memory loses barrier tokens LOAD-DEPENDENTLY, and a payload-value sentinel prototype caused a launch-day production stall (§6.13) — so `nvidia-smi topo -p2p a` returning `NS` is EXPECTED here, not a fault. THE DELTA VS THE STEP SEAM, which is why this is a new arm and not a copy of `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P`: `tp.rs`'s pull sites fence the producer with a host `engine.stream().synchronize()` (the "PRODUCER FENCE (2026-08-20 flake fix)" comments at `tp.rs:2636`/`:3543`) — correct, and a full stream drain, i.e. it keeps the cost class this arm exists to delete. This arm takes the `pp.rs` `BoundarySlot` ordering contract instead (`ev.record(tx)` then `rx.wait(&ev)`), which the hy3 PP-4 qualification closed at 50/50 fresh processes and 200/200 runtime probes with zero byte mismatch on this exact RTX PRO 6000 card class. FAIL-CLOSED ARMING: on a real pair, `tp::grant_peer_access` in BOTH directions (both halves — `cuCtxEnablePeerAccess` for legacy allocations AND `cuMemPoolSetAccess` on the owner's default pool, because the former does not map stream-ordered pool allocations and every memra buffer is one), then a byte-integrity PULL ladder through the exact primitive the walk uses, both directions, at 16 KiB / 64 KiB / 1 MiB / 64 MiB, against a poisoned destination, compared on `f32::to_bits`; one differing word refuses the load with zero shards built. Announce `[glm5-tp-transport] armed transport=... performance_claim=false` plus the ladder PASS line, and all four pre-existing seam announces (`[glm5-tp-preflight]`, `-kda`, `-mla`, `-ep`) now print the LIVE transport instead of the hardcoded `transport=host-canonical` they printed before (a hardcoded value would have made this very A/B unreadable from the boot log the battery greps). WHAT THE LADDER DOES NOT PROVE, stated because §2.4 is emphatic: it is a TRANSFER-tier check. It does not prove a KERNEL peer dereference works, and on direct-attach (`NODE`) hosts — our box class — the driver stages SM-issued peer access through SysMem by default (§2.3b) while `topo -p2p r` reads OK and `cudaMemcpy` looks healthy. This arm never dereferences a peer pointer from a kernel, so that default cannot bite it and NO `ForceP2P` modprobe change is required to ship it; the kernel path is measured separately by the lane's `peer-read-probe.cu` (a `simpleP2P`-class byte-validating kernel peer read) which `tools/box-health.sh` runs at every box window, and which gates the FUTURE fused-collective arm. WHY THE DEFAULT IS OFF: on the day this landed the ON arm had ZERO receipts on real peer hardware — the rig is one card (`LAW:rig-exactness-only`), so its gate arms run the code over two contexts on ONE device and prove bit-preservation only, never fabric engagement. Unmeasured behaviour does not default ON. The default flips in the same commit as the box window's interleaved-x5 re-price receipt, never before. ROLLBACK SEAM: `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_TRANSPORT=0` (or unset) restores the v1 walk exactly; the flag is read once at preflight and frozen on the runtime, so no request can straddle arms. GATES: `glm5-tp-gate` ALL ARMS on BOTH transports under the same-device emulation, bars UNCHANGED (decode BYTE identity on the non-MoE classes, the calibrated 2e-4 prime band, the pre-registered EP band, reds orders above), PLUS a transport-vs-transport byte-identity bar decode AND prime — because a transport that moved prime bits inside the calibrated band would hide inside it — PLUS census non-vacuity (OFF: `host_syncs>0 && peer_pulls==0`; ON: `peer_pulls>0 && host_syncs==0 && host_legs==0`). COMPOSITION: orthogonal to `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_DIET` and `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_GROUPED_PRIME` by construction (those cut hop COUNT, this cuts hop COST) and both compose; every `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` refusal (PP>1, step doors, spec sessions, the four decode-diet doors) applies unchanged. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tp-transport-20260901/LANE.md` (the receipt-correction note, the movement census, the payload histogram, the TP-2/TP-4 predicted arithmetic), `gates/` — plus the two probes that lane wrote, now promoted to `tools/box-health.sh` and `tools/peer-read-probe.cu` (lane/glm5-extract2; docs row in docs/TESTING.md). |

Same values, same fail-closed parse, resolved by `tp_transport::resolve_transport`; set alone it is honored and refusals then name THIS flag; set alongside a DISAGREEING `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT` the load refuses naming both. | `MEMRA_EP_DIET` | off | GENERAL EP dispatch-diet door (generalized from `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_DIET`, lane/glm5-extract2 — the alias row below stays honored). The door names a MOVEMENT CLASS, not a family: one bulk peer activation fan-out per layer-call instead of per-token uploads, compact peer staging with one bulk return instead of a per-slot round-trip dribble, one scatter launch instead of the `t*n_used` sequential axpy chain. The glm5 TP-2 walk is the ONLY consumer today; an hy3/step EP walk will be able to arm the same door once it has one to arm — the door is general, the second consumer is not written. NOTE while that is true: the load-time co-refusal that catches an armed-but-inert door is scoped to glm5-class plans, so setting this on a non-glm5 deployment today does nothing and says nothing. `glm5-tp-gate` arm B2G proves the GENERAL name reaches the walk (alias unset, engagement counter non-vacuous) and arm B2X proves a disagreeing pair falls closed (counter 0). Consumer contract: glm5 TP-2 EP DISPATCH DIET (lane/glm5-ep-diet, 2026-08-31): the EP walk keeps its per-slot expert kernels and its exact slot-ordered fmaf combine chain, and restructures ONLY the data movement — the tp2-battery's measured **13-18 ms/token v1 join+dispatch tax** (per-token host fan-out x42 layers, ~4-5 sync peer-slot DtoH->HtoD round-trips/layer, host-blocked interleaved issue; `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tp2-battery-20260831/RESULTS.md` cell 3). ON: (1) ONE bulk peer z fan-out per layer-call ([t, n_embd] in one upload; SKIPPED entirely when the call routed no peer-owned expert — the placement-map multiplier), (2) peer rows compute back-to-back into a compact peer block and return in ONE bulk DtoH+HtoD (zero per-slot round-trips; root issue never blocks on a peer return), (3) the t*n_used sequential `axpy_f32` launches collapse into ONE `moe_pairs_scatter` launch over the compact pair slab with a slot-ordered id table — the kernel's header carries the byte-identity contract vs the zeros+sequential-axpy chain, re-anchored for this consumer in `glm5_ep_diet_doors_gpu` (host-fma bitwise, id-table + weight-placement reds). EXACTNESS: decode stays BYTE-identical to the v1 walk (same kernels over same bytes; copies preserve bits; the one arithmetic site keeps its chain) — `glm5-tp-gate` arms B2 (tp-all + diet: decode byte, prime band, engagement counters non-vacuous), M2 (skewed measured map + diet: placement-agnostic by construction), R2D/R3D (wrong-expert-weights and skip-peer-combine reds bite THROUGH the dieted walk). Transport stays HOST-CANONICAL; the two bulk hops are the NAMED native-P2P swap points for the box arc (`MEMRA_STEP_TP_BULK_P2P` precedent: step peer copies 61,452 -> ~21/layer; box is P2P-OK both directions per the tp2-battery topology receipt — the rig same-device emulation cannot qualify real peer transport). ENABLED without `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` on a glm5-class plan REFUSES at load (`=0` is a pin, never refuses). DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN: no box timing receipt exists (rig is exactness-only) and the door changes the round SYNC STRUCTURE — the class the diet window warned does not transfer from counts to wall by arithmetic; it ships with count receipts (`GLM5_EP_DIET_DISPATCHES`, `GLM5_EP_DIET_BULK_RETURNS`, `GLM5_EP_DIET_PEER_ROUNDTRIPS_AVOIDED`, `GLM5_EP_DIET_FANOUT_UPLOADS_AVOIDED`) and an UNPRICED wall band (reclaim ~7-13 of the 13-18 ms/token, stated as a band); the box window prices it. Announce once: `[glm5-ep-diet] engaged`. Rollback: read PER CALL — `=0`/unset restores the v1 per-slot walk byte-for-byte. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/ep-diet-20260831/LANE.md` Two-name resolution is the phase-2 flag-alias law (`alias_door_from`, `crates/memra-engine/src/lib.rs`): either name `=1` arms the door, `=0` on either name is a deliberate pin, both names set to the SAME value resolve to the general name, and both set to DISAGREEING values fall the door CLOSED to the shipped program with one `[flag-alias]` stderr line naming BOTH flags (a per-call door refuses by not arming — an abort in the GPU worker thread would kill every live session). Read PER CALL, so a gate can drive both arms in one process. Every refusal that cites the flag names the one the OPERATOR set. |
