When unset on a live PP-2 prime split, the geometry targets up to eight microchunks with a 128-token nominal floor: `min(4096, max(128, ceil(T/8)))` for calls with `T >= 256`. An explicit value remains authoritative and always requests fixed-size ranges. The naked auto range schedule is selected by `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK_SCHED`. PURE long-ctx OOM/transient control since the grain-free fix (lane/chunkinv-flip, 2026-08-05): every chunk — including chunk 0 — attends through the quantized KV cache (quantize-then-attend), so the chunk size no longer steers arithmetic and prefill logits are bit-identical across values (chunkinv gate, naked env) — that fix and gate cover the attention precision class; on chunked-GDN archs a boundary off the `gdn_chunk_size()` WY fold grid remains the documented lawful near-tie class, which is why serve-CHOSEN boundaries are grid-aligned (see `MEMRA_PRIME_GRID_ALIGN`) and, since 2026-08-23 (lane/hermes-perf-fixes), the AUTO internal range boundaries are snapped to the same grid at schedule time (`align_prime_ranges_to_gdn`; `MEMRA_PRIME_GRID_ALIGN=0` is the shared rollback seam; an explicit value keeps its operator-authoritative unaligned fixed ranges). On step35 (Step-3.7-Flash) a SECOND door reopened this per-arch and was closed on 2026-08-07 — see `MEMRA_STEP35_SWA_TKV` and `research/step35-chunkfix-20260807`. History of the class edge it used to move: `research/chunk-invariance-20260805/VERDICT.md`. **SCOPE EXTENDED 2026-08-28 (lane/glm53-1m-context) to the mHC residual trunk, and the DEFAULT THERE IS THE SAME 4096 — an explicit decision, with its reasons.** `prime_cache_hyper` (`ResidualTopology::HyperConnections`, i.e. glm5_next / GLM-5.3-Flash) was written "deliberately UNCHUNKED", so its per-call `t` was the ADMISSION LIMIT itself and every transient in the walk was a function of the CONTEXT rather than of a working set. At the model's native 1,048,576 that is **1711.0 GB per call**, of which the DSA indexer's `t * n_pools` score plane is 1099.5 GB (N-SQUARED, per MLA layer) and the rest is not: q_lat 137.4, o_lat 137.4, mHC stream state 68.7, q_b 68.7, attn 68.7, trunk per-site 85.9, q_index 17.2, embedded 17.2, index list 8.6 GB. Zeroing the quadratic term alone still leaves 611 GB, 3.2x past a 2x96 GB box, which is why the fix is the SPLIT and not a tiled score plane. `hyper_prime_ranges` now delegates to this schedule: **1711.0 GB -> 6.7 GB per call at 1M**, and peak prime residency (including the whole-prime hidden stack, which no split can bound) is 23.9 GB. **A PROMPT AT OR UNDER 4096 TAKES THE OLD BODY VERBATIM** (`ranges.len() == 1` short-circuits), so every banked short-prompt receipt for this model is unaffected. **A PROMPT OVER 4096 CHANGES BITS BY DEFAULT** on this trunk, and that is a documented NEAR-TIE CLASS rather than the byte-identity the serial trunk's `chunkinv` gate holds: the mHC `mixes` GEMM in `hyper::pre` is `Engine::linear` (cuBLASLt f32), whose ALGORITHM SELECTION IS m-DEPENDENT. Measured on the rig 5090 under flock, TF32 off (`research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/1m-context-20260828/02`): `Engine::linear` at m=32 vs m=200 moves 9601/12288 output bits, worst 3.815e-6, while m=128 and m=199 vs m=200 are bit-identical; the chunked prime's worst is the SAME 3.815e-6 and its FIRST divergent row is ROW 0 — which no cross-token state can reach, so the split exposes the near-tie rather than creating one. `hyper.rs`'s own header already called that GEMM "a serving trunk, not a byte-parity oracle". Consequence to plan for: any banked long-prompt greedy transcript for this model is not byte-stable across this change, and an argmax near-tie can flip. The bar is therefore a calibrated band anchored on TRUTH, not on the sibling arm: `glm5_chunked_prime_gpu` compares the chunked prime AND a chunked-prime-then-decode against `memra_reference::execute` at 2e-5 relative, with the chunked-vs-monolithic comparison kept only to isolate the split. Proven able to fail: a chunk-order mutation lands at 1.346e0, five orders above the band and in the range of the serial trunk's real 2026-08-05 defect (1.813e0), and the negative control asserts a one-token prompt change escapes the band. Capacity is gated separately and was banked RED first (`glm5_prime_capacity`, receipts 01/03). ROLLBACK SEAM is unchanged and is `0` (monolithic walk, byte-for-byte the pre-change body at any length) — which is also the gate's oracle arm. The dynamic schedule does not engage here: it needs PP-2 auto geometry and this trunk refuses `MEMRA_PP_SHARD` outright. **SCOPE EXTENDED AGAIN 2026-08-29 (lane/glm53-1m-demo): the mHC ppN prime now walks the SAME `hyper_prime_ranges` schedule** — the follow-up the 08-28 note named. The monolithic ppN walk carried the whole prompt as one staged call, which capped the PP4 placement three independent ways on the 4x96 GB box (measured, receipts `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/1m-demo-20260829/`): per-call transients OOM'd from ~32k prompt tokens, and every launch placing `t` in grid.y (kda_conv_silu, kda_gate, per-row rms_norm, the router) hits the CUDA 65,535 grid.y ceiling from t=65,536 (instant `CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE` at a 128,566-token prime). Per chunk the staged walk is bit-identical to the unsplit walk (`glm5_hyper_ppn_gate` arm 2), so over the SAME schedule the chunked ppN prime composes to bit-identity with the chunked single-engine prime; the near-tie class above is the chunking itself, unchanged. A prompt at or under one chunk takes the monolithic ppN body verbatim, and `0` remains the shared rollback seam (monolithic walk, both placements). **LAUNCH WALL CLOSED FOR THE SERIAL WALK TOO, 2026-09-01 (lane/ornith-cold-long-66k-20260901): `0` now means monolithic up to `PRIME_CHUNK_LAUNCH_CAP` = 65,520 tokens, and an explicit value caps there as well (ring-off walks; ring-on keeps its `PRIME_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS` clamp).** The serial walk had the SAME grid.y ceiling the 08-29 ppN note measured: with `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK=0` (the ornith serving config) every cold prompt of 65,536+ tokens died at launch inside layer 0 — `ssm_conv1d_gdn_state_f32` (`ssm_conv1d_gdn_state_pad`), the out_f<128 dp4a matvec family (where the GDN ssm_beta/ssm_alpha projections land), and `router_gemv` all place the call's `t` in grid.y (darklanes research/ornith-move-20260829 F2: cold 64,984 PASS / 65,643 FAIL, 10/10 `CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE`; re-hit on prod by the 2026-09-01 stress campaign at 66,045/79,717/82,440 — client saw an empty 200 with an in-stream engine_error frame, ledger honest 500/unbilled). Every t <= 65,535 still schedules as the IDENTICAL single monolithic range (the `< PRIME_MIN_T` tail fold collapses the split in the 65,521..65,535 band), so `0`'s rollback-seam and oracle-arm semantics are byte-for-byte unchanged everywhere they ever produced output; above the wall the walk now chunks at the cap instead of erroring. The spec MTP fill's ring-off `0` schedule caps at 65,535 the same way (its loop has no tail fold). Unit-gated in `prime_chunk_schedule_tests` (cap arithmetic + range legality across the boundary band); the GPU acceptance gate — a cold 66-83k prime byte-vs-recompute on an ornith-class artifact — is owed before any long-context ornith serving claim |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK_SCHED` | `dynamic` | PP-2 naked-auto microchunk schedule (lane/cx-dynamic-microchunk, 2026-08-08). `dynamic` keeps the fixed policy's chunk count, emits a short fill chunk, then places the remaining boundaries at equal increments of the linear-heavy cumulative work model `W(L)=L^2+8*T*L`, producing a larger middle followed by a shrinking tail. `fixed` is the rollback to the measured equal-token ranges. Scheduler-only on the classes `ppsplit` pins (attention precision); `chunkinv35`/`tickinv35` remain that authority. Those gates never constrained GDN WY block membership — on chunked-GDN archs BOTH auto schedules now emit grid-aligned internal boundaries (`align_prime_ranges_to_gdn`, 2026-08-23), so the off-grid near-tie class documented at `MEMRA_PRIME_GRID_ALIGN` is reachable only through an explicit `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK`. An explicit `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK` overrides this seam and stays fixed |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_F32CHUNK0` | off | `1` restores the LEGACY first-chunk arithmetic (chunk 0 attends this batch's f32 K/V via fa_prefill instead of the quantized cache) — the rollback seam for the grain-free chunk-invariance fix, and the chunkinv gate's canary injection. Chunk-VARIANT by construction; never on in a measured default run |
| `MEMRA_STEP35_SWA_TKV` | off | `1` restores step35's PRE-FIX SWA prefill arm predicate (kernel keyed on the chunk's own `t_kv > win` instead of the request's `seq_end > win`) — the rollback seam for the step35 chunk-invariance fix, and the `chunkinv35` gate's canary injection. Chunk-VARIANT by construction: it makes the FA rows a prefix `[0,P)` with `P = c*floor(win/c)`, so `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK` moves the logits (measured 1.813e0 at T=4883, greedy text diverging at step 6, with a one-token 512-vs-513 verdict flip). step35 only; never on in a measured default run. `research/step35-chunkfix-20260807`, defect receipt `research/step37-p2-20260806/raw/chunkinv-step35-GAP2-CONFIRMED-20260807.txt` |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_CALLLOCAL` | off | `1` restores the FULL pre-fix tick-segmentation arithmetic: the PER-CALL `seq_end` (`cache.pos + t`, ignoring the caller's `queued_after`) plus the raw, unaligned step35 SWA view offset. Both halves are required after the windowed hd128 FA default: the predicate alone selects between bit-identical FA results while the raw offset restores the call-boundary-dependent tile grid. This is the rollback seam for the tick-segmentation fix and the `tickinv35` gate's canary injection; never on in a measured default run. `research/tick-seg-20260807`, `research/v072-fix1-20260808` |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_GRID_ALIGN` | on | `0` restores the LEGACY serve-chosen prime boundaries (plain/spec checkpoint stop at the raw turn-marker index; prefix-cache LCP-split and message-boundary captures at the raw positions) — the rollback seam for the prime-grid boundary alignment (lane/spec-longctx-20260821) and the box-side long-ctx byte gate's broken arm. Default ON rounds every serve-CHOSEN stop/capture boundary DOWN to `gdn_chunk_size()` (`grid_align_boundary`, worker.rs), because cross-call prime splits on the qwen hybrid are bit-identical to the monolithic prime **iff the split sits on the GDN WY fold grid** (measured at 24k ctx: aligned splits rows_diff 0, EXACT; off-grid splits diverge from exactly the split row with greedy flips at p0.0 near-tie margins — the GATES-SMOKE-20260821 B3/B1-fold class, 13/16 turns at agent lengths). Costs at most `gdn_chunk_size()-1` re-primed suffix tokens per resume. Engine law pinned by `primegrid`/`primegridc` (tools/prime-grid-gate.sh); mechanism receipts `research/multiturn-cache-20260821/LONGCTX-EXACTNESS-20260821.md` (darklanes). Off-grid ENGINE-level splits remain the documented lawful near-tie class; never off in a measured default run |
| `MEMRA_STEP35_SWA_FA` | on | `0` rolls step35's SWA prefill back to the f32 windowed floor (`sdpa_naive_w_quantized_view`) instead of the windowed hd128 FA stamp (`fa_prefill_view_ws_w_hd128`, default since lane/pp-prefill 2026-08-07). The floor was the single largest cost in the pp4096 anatomy — 565 ms x 33 SWA layers = 41% of the prime, while the FA family did the harder causal-4096 in 3.3 ms (`research/pp-prefill-20260807`). Different NUMERIC CLASS (bf16-MMA online softmax vs f32 serial), selected on the request's `seq_end` like every step35 prime arm, so it is chunk-size-invariant by the chunkfix construction. The rollback is the A/B seam and the previous numeric config; never mix arms within one measured comparison |
| `MEMRA_STEP35_BATCH` | on | `0` disables the Step35 batched trunk and caps serving chunks at B=1. On PP-N, B=1 is deliberately refused too: the eager B=1 trunk is a different numeric class from `step35_decode_batch_layers`, and allowing the live scheduler to cross that boundary made greedy bytes depend on load history (`research/p0iso-20260810`, fixed by `research/b1fix-20260810`). An unsplit deployment retains its existing eager B=1 route. Default ON = the real batched arm (`step35_decode_batch_layers`: per-layer n_head/partial-rope/per-session SWA views/head gate at m=B; batched over weights, per-session over KV) serves B=1..8 — decode aggregate 34-flat → **81/96/116/129 tok/s at c=1/2/4/8** before the PP-N correctness default (both PRO 6000 pairs, N=3 medians, `research/step35-batch-20260808`; updated B=1 cost in `research/b1fix-20260810`). Also the `b2geo35c` canary seam: the gate's live assertions must fail under it. Rollback seam only; never off in a measured default run |
| `MEMRA_STEP35_PRIME_BATCH` | on | `0` disables step35's dedicated cross-request **fresh-prompt** concat-prime range walk and restores the named refusal/fallback to serialized single primes. Default ON batches the legacy owning-stage weight-streaming trunk at `m=sum(T)` while keeping each request's step35 attention geometry, absolute `seq_end`, KV append, and cache isolated; under PP-N each layer range runs on its owning stage with the same fence/transport/publication discipline as `prime_chunk_ppn`. When `MEMRA_STEP_TP_PREFILL=1`, the concat path refuses before cache mutation because the TP4 B=2 implementation missed its live-server performance bar; the server restores the queues and runs the proven per-session grouped path instead. Interactive serving drains each eligible fresh prompt in full before this call. Carried/dark-lane continuation batches still refuse and fall back because they need per-request queued-after metadata. Gate: `step35-prime-batch-gate.sh` (`pbatch35`/`pbatch35c`) requires serial byte identity through teacher-forced reads of the primed KV plus live PP-split evidence. `research/primebatch-20260808/` |
| `MEMRA_MOE_VRAM_FRAC` | 0.85 | SLRU expert-cache fraction of free VRAM (sweep 2026-07-06: 0.40=25.0 → 0.85=28.5 tok/s). The measured Hy3 5090 launcher sets 0.90 for both soft and hard fractions; lower it on rigs co-running other GPU work |
| `MEMRA_MOE_HARD_VRAM_FRAC` | 0.80 | machine-specific hard ceiling for the SLRU allocation (`0.10..=0.95`). Raise only after a local OOM-gated sweep. Hy3 on the 24GB RTX 5090 validated `0.85` at 21.21GiB peak framebuffer use: 2,006 slots, exact argmax/tokens, and 0.53 -> 1.03 tok/s on the warm repeat together with worker depth 16 |
| `MEMRA_MOE_SLOTS` | auto | force an exact SLRU slot count (spill experiments used 64/512) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT` | on | `0` forces the SLRU path even when experts fit VRAM (fits-VRAM resident = 169.55 vs 28.5 tok/s on the local 35B) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_GB` | free − trunk − headroom | resident-experts budget override, absolute GB (rtx6000 M3 partial-resident tier). Default is RESIDENT-IF-FITS (2026-08-02): exact expert-bank bytes summed from the GGUF header (per-layer x n_layer misprojects UD-quants both ways: Ornith-35B +7%, Qwen3.6-35B −2%) vs free VRAM minus the file's non-expert bytes minus the headroom reserve. Under PP, the expert numerator is partitioned by the actual stage-to-device placement; stages sharing one device are combined, while distinct devices decide from only their owned layer slices. The old 0.80 x free default reserved 20% of the card and spilled the Ornith-35B 19.5GB bank that fits: −33% decode / −54% prefill (research/residency-cap-20260802/) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_HEADROOM_GB` | 2.0 | machine-specific headroom the resident decision reserves beside weights (CUDA ctx + KV + workspace; measured ~1.7GB at board shape on the 24GB 5090, serve c=8 peaked 23220/24463 MiB). Raise it on rigs co-running other GPU work or serving many long-ctx sessions |
| `MEMRA_MOE_PINNED` | pinned when MOE_CACHE on | force pinned host expert slabs |
| `MEMRA_MOE_SIZE_AWARE` | off | `1` partitions SLRU slots by expert projection size so mixed-size Hy3 tiers cannot strand capacity |
| `MEMRA_MOE_LFU` | off | `1` enables frequency-aware GPU SLRU eviction; this is separate from the fixed LRU policy in the optional CPU projection cache |
| `MEMRA_MOE_LFU_DECAY` | off | finite `(0,1]` epoch decay for GPU-cache frequency scores; `1.0` retains counts while still enabling forward-epoch accounting |
| `MEMRA_MOE_LFU_MTP_WEIGHT` | 1.0 | finite `0.25..=64` multiplier applied to GPU-cache frequency updates from MTP/verify epochs |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_DISK` | off | set = enable the NVMe disk tier for MoE experts (M3-class models that exceed host RAM) |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_PINNED_FRAC` | 0.60 | finite `(0,1]` fraction of `MemAvailable` the spill tier may pin. An out-of-range or non-numeric value warns once, uses the safe 0.60 default, and increments `config_fallbacks`; model load continues |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_IO` | `mmap` | expert storage backend: `mmap` (default and fallback), `pread` (blocking positioned-read oracle), `worker` (bounded CPU positioned-read prefetch), or `direct` (the same worker pipeline with O_DIRECT for 4 KiB-aligned expert extents). **Fail-closed (2026-08-13, hermes 8fca…): an unrecognized configured value REFUSES to start** (`configured_mode()` panics; unset still means `mmap`). Only runtime degradation is soft: unaligned direct extents and runtime backend/read failures fall back to mmap and increment the runtime `fallbacks`/error counters. Spill mode selects how expert bytes reach the GPU, so a silent downgrade hid a correctness-critical misconfiguration and let a spill benchmark report mmap numbers under a requested mode. |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_PREAD_DEPTH` | 2 | pinned-buffer count and worker-thread count for `worker` (`1..=64`; also sizes the blocking `pread` pool). An out-of-range or non-integer configured depth REFUSES to start (fail-closed since 2026-08-13); unset means depth 2. Hy3 on the local RTX 5090 measured depth 16 as the knee: it matched depth 32 at 1.18 tok/s after a reverse-order repeat, used 107MB rather than 214MB of pinned RAM, and removed depth-8 ring saturation (a typo'd depth had made a spill benchmark report default-depth numbers — hence the refuse). |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_WORKER_EXPERT_WINDOW` | `(pread_depth-1)/3`, minimum 1 | complete experts kept in the rolling positioned-read window; each expert occupies three projection buffers |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_LIB` | off | path to a trusted build of memra's optional Hy3-only native CPU-expert companion (ABI v2; no external inference runtime). `dlopen` executes constructors before ABI validation. Complete frozen-cache hits stay on CUDA; experts missing any projection run wholly on CPU |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_THREADS` | 8 | OpenMP compute threads for one-token CPU expert work (`1..=256`); the two-pass means for 8 and 12 threads differed by 0.7%, but the winner reversed by about 8% between individual reverse-order observations (100 timed calls each), so the lower-contention value remains the local default |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_IO_THREADS` | compute thread count | positioned-read workers used by the CPU expert backend (`1..=256`) |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_PIPELINE` | off | `1` overlaps companion reads with compute (cached experts compute immediately, missing experts in read-completion order, output byte-identical; the Hy3 launcher opts in). Requires `OMP_WAIT_POLICY=ACTIVE`: with passive waits the ready-batch region entries pay futex+C-state wakes and decode compute inflated 3.0→8.4 s per 32 tokens; with ACTIVE the spinners starve the caller between calls and e2e is flat vs serial (2026-07-22 bisect chain, N=3 4.55 vs N=5 control 4.50). Stays a door until the single-region structural fix removes the wait-policy dependence |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_IO_CPUSET` | inherit | optional cpu list (e.g. `8-15`) pinning the async read pool off the compute cores |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_EXECUTOR_CPUSETS` | inherit | `"0-7;8-15"` pins each CPU-expert executor to its own core group and sizes its OMP team to that group (`MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_EXECUTOR_THREADS="8;8"` overrides). Asymmetric partitioning for hybrid P/E silicon: one team per group, so a slow group never straggles a fast group's barrier. MEASURED +11% at m=3 mixed prompts (5.74/5.73/5.77 vs 5.30/4.94/5.27) and far more reproducible than the P-only baseline. Knees: 8 E-cores in a second group is the optimum (a 14-core E team falls back to baseline) and executors should be FEWER than in-flight calls, or the weakest group becomes the critical path. Requires GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY unset (a global affinity overrides per-thread pinning) and a process taskset covering the groups. Helps only when several expert calls are in flight — single-stream issues one at a time |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_SHM` | off | `1` backs the expert cache with a named tmpfs segment and persists the index across restarts: a clean reopen starts warm (validated 100% hit / zero re-reads in the cross-process smoke). The object is created atomically with mode `0600`; an existing object is adopted only when `fstat` proves it is owned by the effective uid with mode exactly `0600`. Foreign or permissive objects are explicitly REFUSED and the process uses its private cache. Exclusive flock sends a second cooperating process to the same private fallback. Crash → dirty header → cold-but-correct start; cache keys pin device/inode/size/ctime, persisted rows are bounds-checked before pointer construction, and reload verifies a checksum over the whole entry up to 32 KiB or eight evenly spaced 4 KiB windows for larger entries. Costs THP on this rig (`shmem_enabled=never`) unless the sysctl is flipped to `advise` |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_SHM_NAME` | `/memra-expert-cache-v1` | POSIX shm name for the persistent cache. A pre-existing name is usable only when its object is owned by the effective uid with mode exactly `0600`; unsafe adoption is refused rather than repaired |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_PROFILE` | off | path to a freeze-profile sidecar. A warmup run saves the frozen residency set (versioned header binds slot geometry); a later run restages it directly and SKIPS the profiling warmup. Measured pair (2026-07-22): 5285/5285 blocks restaged, startup wall 7:37.6→6:02.0 (−21%), identical 32 tokens, argmax MATCH, decode band unchanged. Mismatched/stale profiles fall back to a normal warmup; the post-freeze argmax gate still validates the assignment either way |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_GB` | 16 | requested normal-RAM cache ceiling for CPU-routed expert projection bytes; the measured Hy3 5090 launcher requests 20 GiB and still applies the live-stack headroom gate below |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_RESERVE_GB` | 4 | live-stack RAM headroom retained by capping `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_GB` to `MemAvailable - reserve` when the CPU companion initializes and prints the effective budget |
| CPU expert cache eviction | LRU | fixed winners-only policy; experimental LFU and layer-aware least-stale arms did not beat the measured LRU path and were removed |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_IO` | `buffered` | CPU expert reads: `buffered` or aligned `direct` positioned I/O |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_MIRROR_MAP` | off | tab-separated inode/alternate-path map for splitting large direct reads across byte-identical expert mirrors on two NVMe devices |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_CACHE` | off | `1` freezes HBM expert residency so CPU/GPU backend assignment cannot drift after warmup; required for exact repeatable Hy3 hybrid serving |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_WARMUP_TOKENS` | off | discarded tokens used to profile/fill HBM before freezing; the Hy3 5090 launcher uses 128 after one same-session, identical-token N=32 pair measured 4.52 tok/s versus 4.46 at 8 tokens (single pair, not a median), and `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_WARMUP_SPEC_K` selects speculative warmup depth in run-spec |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_PROFILE_ADMIT` | off | `1` lets CPU-routed warmup misses vote for HBM admission before the residency set freezes; current-token execution remains on CPU |
| `MEMRA_CPU_AFFINITY` | `0-7` in `run_hy3_local_5090.sh` | CPU list used by the Hy3 launcher for `taskset` and `GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY`; wrapper-only machine topology setting |
| `MEMRA_WORKER_CPUSET` | **off, by design** | GENERAL worker-cpuset flag (renamed from `MEMRA_WORKER_AFFINITY`, lane/glm5-extract2, coordinator ruling 2026-09-01 — it matches the `*_CPUSET` convention, because the VALUE is a cpuset spec and "affinity" named the mechanism rather than the thing being set; the alias row below stays honored). Nothing about it was ever family-specific: it pins the ONE `memra-gpu-worker` thread every family's decode runs on. TWO-NAME LAW: a VALUED flag resolved ONCE on the startup thread, so both names set to DISAGREEING values REFUSES THE BOOT naming both (`affinity::resolve_worker_cpuset_env`) — the same stance as `MEMRA_EP_MAP` and `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT`, and deliberately NOT the per-call doors' fall-closed, because no request exists yet and a server silently pinned to a cpuset the operator did not choose is worse than a boot that says why it stopped. Every parse refusal names the flag the OPERATOR set. Receipt lines are byte-unchanged (`[worker-affinity] off` / `engaged … effective=` / `REFUSED`) so the banked host-audit window runner and `affinity-identity-gate.sh` keep matching; only the OFF line's parenthetical now names both flags, which nothing greps. Original lane text: CPU affinity for the ONE `memra-gpu-worker` thread every family's decode tick runs on (`memra-server/src/affinity.rs`, applied in `worker::run` before `Engine::new` so the CUDA context and the per-step scoped threads inherit the mask). Values: a Linux cpu list (`8-15,104-111`); `ccx`/`on` = the L3 domain the worker is running on; `ccx:N` = the Nth L3 domain in sysfs order; `0`/`off`/unset = no `sched_setaffinity` call at all. Domains are read from `cache/index3/shared_cpu_list`, never assumed from a core count. **Not an affinity of sessions** — `MEMRA_AFFINITY` and `MEMRA_DEBUG_AFFINITY` are the unrelated session-affinity pair; the name collision is real and flagged for an owner ruling (`MEMRA_WORKER_CPUSET` would match the `*_CPUSET` convention). Both arms announce (`[worker-affinity] engaged request=… effective=… cpus=… l3_domains=…` / `[worker-affinity] off`) and the ON line reports the `sched_getaffinity` READBACK, flagging `CLAMPED-BY-OUTER-CPUSET` when a cpuset narrowed it — an arm identified by the absence of a line is indistinguishable from a regressed announce. A malformed or unsatisfiable value FAILS THE BOOT through the worker's load verdict rather than serving unpinned, because a mistyped cpu list that degrades to off gets its numbers filed under the pinned arm. WHY OFF: a mask is machine-specific (the right one on a 12-CCD EPYC 9654 is wrong on box12's Core Ultra 9 285K and wrong again inside a narrow cpuset — the `card-keyed-defaults-need-full-pins` shape), and pinning strictly REDUCES the worker's available CPU. Provenance: measured unpinned and migrating on Box B (45 s procfs sample of a live serving boot, primary worker on 11 distinct CPUs across 3 L3 domains, 5.6 involuntary preemptions/s, 51% of wall in system time, against 184 unpinned tokio runtime threads on the same 192 logical CPUs). Non-numeric: a CPU mask selects no kernel and reorders no reduction; asserted anyway by 24-step byte identity ON vs OFF. Arms and receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/host-audit-20260901/LANE.md` |
| `MEMRA_WORKER_AFFINITY` | **off, by design** | FAMILY-ERA ALIAS of `MEMRA_WORKER_CPUSET` (the name lane/glm5-host-audit shipped, its window runner and identity gate set, and every banked host-audit receipt was minted under; lane/glm5-extract2). Same values, same validation, same announce lines, resolved by `affinity::resolve_worker_cpuset_env`; set alone it is honored and refusals then name THIS flag; set alongside a disagreeing `MEMRA_WORKER_CPUSET` the boot refuses naming both. Never silently dead. |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_BATCHED_PRIME` | off | rollback seam: `1` restores batched prompt replay after Hy3 CPU/GPU expert residency freezes; the local spill path otherwise replays tokenwise to avoid transiently rereading the missing expert bank |
| `MEMRA_ST_PINNED` | off | `1` pins the safetensors expert store — ONLY for fits-in-RAM checkpoints (pinning 26GB evicted the page cache: 30x regression, 2026-07-07). **THE FITS-IN-RAM ARM NOW HAS ITS OWN RECEIPT (2026-08-28, GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4, rented bench box (2x RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server 96 GB, 499 GB RAM):** a 171 GB expert set against `free_pinnable_ram=300378 MiB` pins COMPLETELY (no mmap tail — the partial-coverage shape is exactly the 2026-07-07 failure). Real-prompt steady-state decode, 192 completion tokens, 4 reps, `reasoning_effort` pinned: greedy **8.54 -> 20.35 tok/s (117.1 -> 49.1 ms/token, 2.38x)**, vendor-default sampled 14.29 -> 23.69. `staged_bytes` per token is UNCHANGED across the arms (795.1 vs 797.5 MiB, 176.7 vs 177.2 misses) and greedy output is **BYTE-IDENTICAL** (`fd006d0d50eb59b5` in both, and in a third arm at 6000 slots) — this is transport only. What it buys, solved from two slot-count arms at the same transport: pinned staging runs at 49.5 MiB/ms (~53 GB/s, PCIe5 x16 line rate) against the pageable mmap path's 9.4 MiB/ms (~10 GB/s). GPU utilization 28-35% -> 95%. Cost: host RSS +171 GB, model load 36 s -> 94-202 s. Default stays OFF: the guard that makes it safe (whole expert set fits `free_pinnable_ram`) cannot be evaluated on a safetensors source, because `exact_expert_bytes` is GGUF-only (`hybrid.rs` `match src.gguf()`), so a capacity-keyed default needs a whole-model expert census first. Serving recipes on fits-in-RAM boxes should set it explicitly. |
| `MEMRA_ST_REPACK_DISK` | on | `0` forces in-RAM gather instead of the `.memra-repack` disk cache (safetensors stream-repack loader) |
| `MEMRA_Q4F16` | capacity-keyed auto | dense-gemma (12B/31B geometry) f16 prefill mirrors on the cuBLASLt lane — the sm_120a sibling of the Hopper `MEMRA_PP_F16` class (`memra-engine/src/hybrid.rs`). With the flag unset, mirrors are admitted iff free VRAM covers the admissible f16 mass (every 2D Q8_0/Q4_0/Q6_K/Q4_K/Q5_K tensor `build_q8_f16` admits) + 8 GiB serving headroom (zoo-fusion arc, 2026-08-17: the 31B downQ6K trunk's Q6_K ffn_down otherwise rides the 3.46 ms/call dequant-GEMM prefill wall — 30% of c8 GPU time; mirrors measured c8 agg +37% / ttft −70%). `1` forces (still requires the f16 lane compiled in), `0` kills; 24 GB rigs refuse by construction |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_EXECUTORS` | 1 | CPU-expert executor count without explicit cpusets (`1..=8`; `memra-engine/src/cpu_experts.rs`). Ignored when `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_EXECUTOR_CPUSETS` is set — the group list then determines the count |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_PREFETCH_ANNEX_GB` | 1.5 | prefetch annex byte budget in the native companion (`tools/memra_cpu_experts.cpp`): LRU-held prefetched projection bytes awaiting promotion, clamped `0.125..=16.0` GiB. Companion-side (requires `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_LIB` + `MEMRA_MOE_PREFETCH=1`) |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_PREFETCH_MAX_INFLIGHT` | 32 | max in-flight prefetch reads in the companion (`1..=4096`; out-of-range falls back to 32). Same companion scope as the annex budget |
| `MEMRA_MOE_PREFETCH_TOP` | 1 | how many predicted next experts the `MEMRA_MOE_PREFETCH=1` pipeline prefetches per layer (`1..=8`; `cpu_experts.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_PREFETCH_MIN_LAYER` | 40 | first layer index eligible for the prediction-table prefetch (early layers churn; `cpu_experts.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_F8F4` | off | `1` = W4A8-FP8 prefill MMQ (e4m3 fold + f8f6f4 MMA, 381-TF class). pp +3.9-6.3% ALL models, TTFT -4-5.6%; e2e spec MODEL-SIGNED via the prefill-KV acceptance law (27B ST +7.2%, 9B -3.5/-6.1%) — per-model serve adoption (NV-27B ST config), never a global default (2026-07-10 flip battery). **RE-VERDICT 2026-08-06 (`research/f8f4-flip-20260806/`): still NO global flip.** The route got faster — `7c0df07b`'s block_scale MMA form is 1.2153x (q27) / 1.1485x (q9) and **bit-exact** vs plain f8f4, so it cannot repair a route-level acceptance dip. Reachable on **2 of 12** gate models (the single `QT_NVFP4 && use_w4a8` dispatch arm); everything else is a structural no-op. Red cells: q27 bare-head K=3 (its serve K) −3.6pp deterministic 5/5, and q9 + its **production regime drafter** −9.5pp on a short prompt (OFF==OFF2 control green). All exactness gates PASS both arms (incl. chunk-invariance and serve-smoke/stress c=64) and greedy divergence lands *past* the 20-token goldens — the battery is blind to this class. The acceptance **sign follows (model × drafter × prompt)**, not the model: it inverted between the embedded MTP head and the regime drafter on the same day. Adopting the route on a new artifact requires its own receipt via `research/f8f4-flip-20260806/tools/regime_accept_ab.sh`. Expert-tile twin measured NEGATIVE (-6.4% best variant, not pipe-bound) and was deleted — tag moe-f8f4-negative. **sm_100a:** available through the plain-E4M3 MMA form and exercised on the pinned Qwen3.5-9B B200 battery. It remains model-signed and opt-in; the B200 result does not authorize a global or cross-artifact default. |

For the official Step-3.7 FP8 checkpoint, `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` also selects the
expert numerical program at load time. Unset or `1` keeps the stacked expert
banks native and runs the dynamic-E4M3 W8A8 correctness reference. Literal
`0` materializes the historical Q8 diagnostic representation before serving.
That diagnostic process is never Step FP8 completion or performance evidence.

**B200 `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ` override (2026-09-01):** on `sm_100a`, the tcgen05/TMEM twin stays
literal-`1` explicit even for the native-resident source. It passed the nine-shape
exact/random/tail oracle, K=1..8, sampled serving, and concurrency 2 on pinned
`Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B-FP8`, with 1,200 live pp1483 dispatches and zero shape/scale/NaN refusals. It is
not tuned: 530.9 versus 3,071.1 tok/s for the fallback = **0.173x**, teacher-forced NLL 20.790810
versus 20.621361, and last-logit top-20 overlap 17/20. `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ=1` therefore remains explicit
on B200 and the state is `NativeReference`, not `NativeQualified`. Receipts:
`research/b200-kernel-twins-dry-20260901/receipts/b200-fp8-q38-20260901T1820Z/`.

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_B200_PRIME_V2` | **off** (DEFAULT-ON CANDIDATE for glm5_next on PP-2, pending the owner's flip) | `1` = the B200 mHC PRIME schedule door (lane/b200-prefill-roofline-20260902). Two arms, one door, and NOTHING else changes: decode, spec, batch, every non-hyper trunk, and every kernel are untouched by construction (the only call sites are `hyper_prime_ranges` and `prime_cache_hyper`'s pp branch). **WHY.** `prime_chunk_tokens` applies the `PRIME_PIPE_MICROBATCHES = 8` geometry whenever a PP-2 stage fence exists, so a 4096-token glm5 prime splits into EIGHT calls -- but that geometry belongs to `prime_cache_pp2_pipelined`, the SERIAL trunk's microbatched PP-2 prime, which the mHC walk has never called. glm5 paid the split and collected none of the overlap. MEASURED on the 2x B200 pair 2026-09-02 (int2 8c31be2f4, all doors + W8, `MEMRA_PRIME_PROF=1`, cold primes, prefix cache off): the 4k rung (6,920 tokens) ran as 8 microchunks of 433/1016/981/950/922/896/872/850 and cost **7.3-8.3 us per token per MoE layer**, while the 42k rung's ~4000-token chunks cost **4.1 us** on the same binary and the same boot -- about 2x per token, on a phase that is 5.09 s of that rung's 5.5 s TTFT. The 128k rung's chunks are already large and MoE falls to ~40% there. **ARM 1 (schedule):** `hyper_prime_ranges` takes the natural chunk (`PRIME_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS`, the same clamp the 42k prime already gets) instead of the microbatch geometry. An explicit `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK` stays authoritative and turns arm 1 off for that call -- an operator override is never overridden by a door. **ARM 2 (pipeline):** with two or more chunks the ppN prime runs `prime_cache_hyper_pp2_pipelined` -- stage 0 of chunk k+1 on device 0 while stage 1 of chunk k runs on device 1, on two scoped host threads over disjoint per-stage caches (`PrimeCacheStages`). Two threads are REQUIRED, not a style choice: the grouped MoE prefill's sigmoid host oracle drains its stage stream once per layer, so two CUDA streams on one host thread serialize however the calls are ordered -- the host is the serializer, not the device. Arm 2 DECLINES BY NAME (`[hyper-prime-pipe] DECLINED`, once per process, falling back to the serial chunk walk) on `MEMRA_PRIME_PIPE=0`, on a vision embedding overlay (the splice is a stage-0 intake transform whose gate ran on the serial ppN body), and on an ARMED DFlash2 hc tap sink (the per-stage cache shells carry `hc_taps: None`, so the draft-source rows would go silently missing). Arm 1 is unaffected by an arm-2 decline. **NUMERIC CLASS.** Arm 1 is NOT bit-identical and no chunk-size change on this trunk ever was: `Engine::linear`, the cuBLASLt f32 mixes GEMM in `hyper::pre`, is not m-invariant and the arms diverge at ROW 0 where no cross-token state can reach (`hyper_prime_ranges`' own header documents the near-tie). The bar is the calibrated band `tests/glm5_chunked_prime_gpu.rs` already holds the chunked prime to -- relative maxdiff <= 2e-5, five orders below the 1.813e0 signature of a real chunk-invariance defect -- plus argmax equality. Arm 2 IS bit-identical by construction: same ranges, same per-chunk program, same operand bytes, exact boundary copy; only which host thread issues a stage changes. **GATE.** `glm5-prime-v2-gate` (fixture-driven, one process, both arms): arm 1 band + argmax and FAILS if the door did not change the chunk count; arm 2 bit identity and FAILS unless `HYPER_PRIME_PIPELINED_CHUNKS` advanced by the schedule's chunk count. Red arms `MEMRA_PRIME_V2_GATE_RED=schedule\|pipe` force each non-vacuity assertion to fire. **ENGAGEMENT RECEIPTS** (print-once, and the counters the gate asserts on): `[prime-v2] arm1 engaged t= chunk= chunks= widths=` with `HYPER_PRIME_NATURAL_SCHEDULES`, and `[prime-v2] arm2 pipelined stages=2 chunks= overlap= t= devices= taps=armed\|none` with `HYPER_PRIME_PIPELINED_CHUNKS` -- the arm-2 line prints once per TAP STATE, so a log cannot show only the uninteresting half. They exist because the first box run of this door produced a 22-45% TTFT win with NO greppable line: "the flag was set" is not a receipt, and a door that silently did nothing reads the same way. **ARM 2 UNDER AN ARMED DFlash2 TAP SINK** (the SPEC route, i.e. the shipped one). Arm 2 used to refuse whenever the drafter had armed an hc tap sink, because `PrimeCacheStages` gives each stage a shell with `hc_taps: None` and the sink's single `base` cannot describe two stages on different chunks at once. That refusal WAS the product-route cost: at 256,756 tokens on the pair the SPEC route measured 152.95 s door-on against 153.12 s door-off (arm 2 never engaged) while the PLAIN route got 69.08 s against 128.87 s. The sink is now SHARED across the two stage threads rather than split -- splitting is unaffordable, a prime sink spans the whole prompt -- and sharing is sound by construction: the stages own DISJOINT SLOT COLUMNS (a tapped layer belongs to exactly one stage, the same fact `glm5_tap_drain` relies on) AND DISJOINT ROW WINDOWS (different chunk bases). The mutex makes the shared `&mut` legal and is held for the row memcpy only. Bit-identical, held by the gate's arm 4 with its own red arm. **RECEIPTS.** Roofline, call graph and lever ranking: `research/b200-prefill-roofline-20260902/LANE.md` (+ `roofline.py`). Box A/B on the 2x B200 pair, real artifact, all doors + W8, cold primes, 3 reps at 4k/42k and 1 at 128k (darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`): **boot B baseline 4.08 / 16.73 / 56.30 s; boot C (`MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK=4096`, arm 1 through the operator override) 2.68 / 16.69 / 56.11; boot F (this door, arms 1+2) 2.09 / 9.59 / 30.63 s = 3,307 / 4,364 / 4,181 tok/s.** Against boot C that is -22% at 4k and -43/-45% at depth; against baseline -49 / -43 / -46%. Decode unchanged (35-37 tok/s), no errors. Boot E (`MEMRA_F16G_BDB=1` on top of C) measured 2.70 / 16.70 / 56.15 = ZERO effect. The default stays OFF until the owner flips it with these receipts. **ROLLBACK.** Unset the variable. `MEMRA_PRIME_PIPE=0` keeps arm 1 and drops arm 2. |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_ROW_ILP` | **ON on sm_100a builds** (2026-09-04), off elsewhere | **DEFAULT (arch-keyed, `MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH`): unset = ON for `100a` builds, OFF for every other build; `=1`/`=0` override, read per call.** RECEIPT (darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`, nvab.sh, 2x B200 SXM pair, PP-2, assembled posture with the decode graph door ON, interleaved x3, vendor sampling, greedy tape per boot): base 74.79 / 74.85 / 75.22 -> 74.85; ilp 76.33 / 75.74 / 79.21 -> 76.33 (+1.98%); `MEMRA_B200_MR1_FILL=16` alone 76.37 (+2.03%); both 76.37 / 76.76 / 76.78 -> **76.76 (+2.55%)**; tape `9437b599f6b9d2a9` on all twelve boots. No SM120 receipt, hence the arch key. lane/glm5-nvfp4-row-ilp-20260904: the split-plane NVFP4 trunk matvec (`qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_mr2_rp`, and `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_rp` when the B200 grid-fill arm picks mr1) takes its `_ilp` twin: FOUR groups' quant words and scale words per lane (per row) issued ahead of the table lookups and dp4a chains, then the shipped single-group tail. WHY: 74 launches per plain glm5_next t=1 token on the 2x B200 pair (door-ON census 2026-09-04: 0.9 ms of a ~13.7 ms token; the 4096-row shapes run 2,048 warps on a 148-SM part) and root ncu on the rig at that shape (darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/ncu-rig/kda6.csv`) reads long-scoreboard stalls 68-70%, issue-active 37-39%, DRAM 30% of peak, occupancy 49%: latency-bound with one 16 B load per row in flight per lane. EXACTNESS by construction: per row the accumulation order is the shipped one (`acc[r] += adg * partial`, g ascending, `partial` by the same two-sub-block chain), same bytes read, warp tree and `* yscale` epilogue verbatim. Both twins carry `MEMRA_PDL_ENTRY` and ride the mr2 kernel's PDL launch class (so does the shipped mr1 kernel now, which had no PDL entry). Gate: `b200_matvec_bench` family 4 (mr2_ilp and mr1_ilp bitwise vs the shipped mr2 at the 4096x4096 shape); the box greedy tape holds it at model scale. Read per call; announce once `[nvfp4-row-ilp] engaged`. |
| `MEMRA_B200_MR1_FILL=<n>` | **16** (2026-09-04; was 2) | RECEIPT (nvab.sh, same cell as the row above): 16 alone +2.03% (76.37 vs 74.85), with the ILP twin +2.55%; only reachable under `MEMRA_B200_MATVEC_ARM=1`, itself sm_100a-only, so the default moves nothing on other cards. lane/glm5-nvfp4-row-ilp-20260904: the threshold of the B200 grid-fill arm (under `MEMRA_B200_MATVEC_ARM=1`): the NVFP4 m=1 decode matvec forces mr1 (one warp per row, double the grid, the shipped `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_rp`, byte-identical per row) when the mr2 grid would be fewer than `n` four-warp blocks per SM. `2` was the lane/b200-matvec-occupancy-20260902 threshold, under which the served 4096-row shapes (512 mr2 blocks = 2,048 warps on 148 SMs, 22% of the warp slots) stay mr2; `16` is one full wave of four-warp blocks. Priced on the pair together with `MEMRA_NVFP4_ROW_ILP` (interleaved A/B, receipts in darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`). |
| `MEMRA_B200_MATVEC_ARM` | **off** | `1` = the sm_100a occupancy arms for the plain-decode MoE/matvec family (lane/b200-matvec-occupancy-20260902). Restricted to `sm_100a` BUILDS at runtime (`MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH`, compiled in): setting the var on an `sm_120a` build is a no-op and the naked sm_120a defaults stay byte-identical, per the per-hardware arm selection law. |

**`MEMRA_B200_MATVEC_ARM` (2026-09-02, lane/b200-matvec-occupancy-20260902).** THE MEASUREMENT
that motivated it: nsys on 2x B200 SXM (sm_100a build, GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4 W4A16 mint, resident
expert slab, PP2, plain decode, ~224 generated tokens, both devices summed, ~2,900 launches/token)
found `moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8` at **20.0%** of GPU time (24,696 launches, 54.6us avg — ~9x its
NVFP4 roofline estimate of ~6us for one layer's 8-expert gate+up), `moe_down8_fma_q8` at **10.5%**,
`matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows` at **17.0%** (47,364 launches, 24.1us avg — ~3x its bf16 roofline
estimate of ~8us for a [8192,4096] KDA projection), `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_mr2_rp` at **5.2%**
(30,400 x 11.5us) and `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_fused2_rp` at ~4% (13,440 x 10.0us). B200 is
memory-LATENCY/occupancy bound in kernels tuned for the RTX PRO 6000 (188 SMs, 1.8 TB/s), not
bandwidth bound at 148 SMs / 8 TB/s HBM3e.

Four dispatch arms (five kernel-level changes total — arm 1 is a matched gate/up + down pair),
all bit-identical per output (packing/grid-fill changes which block/warp computes an output,
never the per-output f32 accumulation order — see the `.cu` kernel comments for each):

1. `moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8_w4` / `moe_down8_fma_q8_w4` — the existing `MEMRA_MMVQ_ROWS`
   warp-packing idiom (already shipped for the `_rows`/`_rows_w4` verify pair) applied to the
   plain-decode base kernels: `block=(32,1,1)` (one warp/block, occupancy-capped by the per-SM
   resident-BLOCK limit rather than the resident-warp limit) becomes `block=(32,4,1)`.
2. NVFP4 rp sub-wave grid-fill: when the RPW=2 `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_mr2_rp` grid is under a full
   wave on this device's SM count (`cudaDevAttrMultiProcessorCount` via `Engine::sm_count`), the
   dispatch forces RPW=1 and reuses the ALREADY-SHIPPED `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_rp` kernel — doubling
   the grid for the same output rows. No new kernel; a pure dispatch-policy change
   (`qmatvec_mmvq_into`, lib.rs).
3. `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_fused2_rp_g2` — the same grid-fill idea for the fused gate+up dual
   launch: instantiates the existing `nvfp4_mmvq_fused_seg_rp<1>` template (the shipped kernel
   instantiates `<2>`) behind the same sub-wave-grid check.
4. `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows_pf` — software-pipelined (double-buffered) K-loop loads: the
   next iteration's weight/activation reads issue before the current iteration's 8-fma chain
   runs, hiding DRAM latency without touching the fma accumulation order.

A microbench, `crates/memra-engine/src/bin/b200_matvec_bench.rs`
(`MEMRA_GPU_LOCK=/tmp/memra-gpu.lock cargo run -p memra-engine --release --bin b200_matvec_bench`),
runs shipped-vs-arm for all five kernels directly (bypassing the env door, which is a
process-wide `OnceLock` and cannot flip mid-process) with a bit-for-bit output check. **Receipt
pending the B200 box A/B** — this flag ships default OFF and stays OFF until that A/B lands;
see `research/b200-matvec-occupancy-20260902/LANE.md`. Rollback seam: unset or `=0`.

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_B200_BF16_GEMV_LT` | **off** | `1` = route the bf16 decode row matvec (`matvec_bf16_rows_into`, i.e. `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows`) through `cublasLtMatmul` at m=t instead of the memra kernel. REFERENCE DOOR, not a serving arm. Restricted to `sm_100a` BUILDS (`MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH`, compiled in), so it is a documented no-op on sm_120a. |

**`MEMRA_B200_BF16_GEMV_LT` (2026-09-02, lane/b200-gemv-hbm-20260902).** WHY IT EXISTS: the
census above puts `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows` at 23.6us for 64 MB of bf16 weight reads =
**2.7 TB/s, 34% of B200's 8 TB/s HBM3e wall**, and the fused `qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32` at 93.8us
for ~200 MB = **2.1 TB/s (26%)**. Before writing a faster memra kernel it is worth knowing what
a TUNED VENDOR LIBRARY reaches on exactly those bytes on exactly this part, because that number
bounds what "a well-scheduled GEMV" looks like on sm_100a and tells the next arm whether it is
chasing a scheduling gap or a hardware one. cuBLAS/cuBLASLt are already linked libraries here
(`cu/f16_prefill.cu`, `MEMRA_PP_F16`/`MEMRA_PP_BF16`), so this adds no dependency; it reuses the
same TN plan cache and the same PER-DEVICE handle (a `cublasLtHandle_t` created with another
device current returned `CUBLAS_STATUS_EXECUTION_FAILED` on the 2x B200 pair, 2026-09-02).

NUMERIC CLASS `bf16_gemv_lt`, DEFAULT OFF, and it is NOT a bit-identical twin — two things
change and both are stated rather than measured away: (1) the ACTIVATION is cast f32 -> bf16
before the multiply (the shipped kernel keeps the f32 activation and only widens the bf16
weight), (2) the K summation order is the library's, not the shipped per-thread chain plus
`red[]` tree. It is therefore a MEASUREMENT INSTRUMENT: it may not become a serving default, and
promotion would need its own argmax/serving acceptance, not this row. `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ`'s
bf16 arm declines while this door is on, for the same reason it declines under the W8 mirrors —
its bit-identity bar is against the unrerouted `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows` — which is also what
puts the three KDA bf16 projections through the library GEMV. A cuBLASLt refusal (unaligned
weight, no algo) DECLINES the shape back to the shipped kernel and is announced once per shape;
engagement is announced once per shape too, so an arm that never took the path cannot look like
one that did. Bench: `b200_matvec_bench` prints shipped vs LT-reference us/GB\s per bf16 family.

**BOX VERDICT (2026-09-02, B200 dev 0): the library is not a ceiling.** 32.3 us on BOTH bf16
shapes — 1.03x the shipped kernel at 4096->8192 and 0.95x (a loss) at 8192->4096 — against
`matvec_bf16_v2`'s 23.7 / 24.3 us. cuBLASLt has no answer for an m=1 GEMV of this shape, which
is exactly what this door existed to find out, and it retires the "maybe we are simply badly
scheduled versus a tuned GEMM library" hypothesis for the whole decode matvec class. The door
therefore stays what it was built as: an INSTRUMENT, default OFF, never a serving arm, no
promotion path from this row. See `research/b200-gemv-hbm-20260902/LANE.md` section 8. Rollback
seam: unset or `=0`.

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_Q8_ROW_ILP` | **ON on sm_100a builds** (2026-09-04), off elsewhere | **DEFAULT (arch-keyed, `MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH`): unset = ON for `100a` builds, OFF for every other build; `=1`/`=0` override, read per call.** RECEIPT (darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`, q8ab.sh, 2x B200 SXM pair, PP-2, assembled posture with the decode graph door ON, interleaved x3, vendor sampling, greedy tape per boot): base 73.29 / 73.23 / 72.88 -> 73.23 vs ilp 74.73 / 77.64 / 74.81 -> **74.81 tok/s (+2.16%)**, tape `9437b599f6b9d2a9` on all six boots, `[q8-row-ilp] engaged` on every ON boot. No SM120 receipt, hence the arch key. lane/glm5-q8-row-ilp-20260904: the W8-posture q8_0 row walk takes its `_ilp` twins (`qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq_rp_v2_ilp` at t=1, `qmatvec_kda6_q8f32_rp_v2_ilp` for the fused six-projection group): the SAME per-row program with FOUR blocks' loads per lane (eight int4 + four scales) issued ahead of the dp4a chains, then the shipped two-deep round, then the shipped tail. WHY: the two kernels are 1.8 ms of a plain glm5_next t=1 token on the 2x B200 pair (door-ON census 2026-09-04: kda6 35/token x 36.2 us = 1.27 ms at ~49% of 8 TB/s for ~143 MB of mirror bytes; trunk q8 47/token x 11.5 us) and root ncu on the rig at the served geometry (darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/ncu-rig`) reads the row body at 62-72% occupancy, DRAM 46-49% of peak, long-scoreboard stalls 71-76%, issue-active 7-12%: latency-bound on global loads with two blocks' loads in flight per lane. EXACTNESS by construction: per-lane accumulation order unchanged (`acc += dw * sad[blk] * (float)sumi` in blk order, same statement form), warp tree verbatim, same bytes read; the kda6 kernel and its twin are ONE templated body. Gate: `b200_matvec_bench` families 7 and 9 print the shipped-vs-ilp and fused-vs-ilp bitwise compares (all six outputs for kda6); the box greedy tape holds it at model scale. Read per call; announce once `[q8-row-ilp] engaged`. |
| `MEMRA_B200_GEMV_V2` | **off** | A LEVEL, not a boolean. `1` = the v2 bf16 family AND the W8-posture q8_0 family (`qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq_rp_v2` at t=1, `qmatvec_q8_0_rows_tw_v2` at the t<=8 verify width, and the fused `qmatvec_kda6_q8f32_rp_v2` under `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ`): `matvec_bf16_v2` (+ its split-K pair) and `qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32_v2`. `2` = v2 plus `matvec_bf16_v3` / `qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32_v3`, the same walk with its weight tiles staged through shared memory by `cp.async`; v3 declines per call to v2 when a shape wants split-K or when its 36 KB of dynamic smem would exceed the 48 KB default cap. Any other value is off (a typo must not arm a kernel arm). Every arm is BIT-IDENTICAL per output except the split-K class named below, which the shipped decode shapes never select. Restricted to `sm_100a` BUILDS (`MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH`, compiled in): a no-op on sm_120a. Takes precedence over `MEMRA_B200_BF16_GEMV_LT` on the bf16 rows. **The MoE v2 twins are built and benched but NOT dispatched** — see the box receipt below. |

**`MEMRA_B200_GEMV_V2` (2026-09-02, lane/b200-gemv-hbm-20260902).** THE MEASUREMENT it is
designed against — nsys, 2x B200 SXM (8 TB/s HBM3e, 148 SMs, 228 KB smem/SM), sm_100a,
GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4 W4A16 mint, resident PP2, plain decode t=1, both devices summed, **with
every occupancy door already ON**:

| kernel | us/launch | bytes moved | achieved | % of 8 TB/s |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32` | 93.8 | ~200 MB | 2.1 TB/s | 26% |
| `moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8_w4` | 52.4 | ~50 MB | 1.0 TB/s | 12% |
| `moe_down8_fma_q8_w4` | 28.2 | ~25 MB | 0.9 TB/s | 11% |
| `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows_pf` | 23.6 | 64 MB | 2.7 TB/s | 34% |
| `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_mr2_rp` | 12.2 | — | — | — |
| `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_fused2_rp` | 9.9 | — | — | — |

On the RTX PRO 6000 (1.8 TB/s GDDR7, 188 SMs) the same kernels sit near their DRAM wall; on
B200 they are 3 to 9x off it, and the previous lane's warp-packing/prefetch arms bought ~5%.
So the residual is NOT block-slot occupancy. It is BYTES IN FLIGHT PER SM: Little's law at
8 TB/s against a ~700 ns HBM3e round trip wants ~5.6 MB of reads outstanding across the die
(~38 KB per SM) at all times, and the shipped kernels hold ONE weight load per thread per K
step behind a serially dependent fma chain.

The four arms, all the SAME arithmetic rescheduled:

1. `matvec_bf16_v2` — 8 rows per block accumulated CONCURRENTLY (8 independent accumulators)
   with the f32 activation loaded ONCE per K step and reused across all eight, so a block reads
   `8*in_f*2` B of weight against `in_f*4` B of activation (4:1) where the shipped kernel reads
   `4*in_f*2` against `4*in_f*4` (1:2). Two-stage software pipeline, `__launch_bounds__(256)`,
   `ld.global.nc`. SASS receipt (nvcc 13.1, `-gencode arch=compute_100a,code=sm_100a -O3`):
   the steady-state loop issues **ten back-to-back `LDG.E.128.CONSTANT`** (8 weight rows + 2
   activation `float4` = 160 B/thread) before the first `FFMA` consumes one; 64 registers,
   4 B spill. The eight rows' reductions run in LOCKSTEP, so a block pays ONE barrier chain
   (7 barriers at blockDim=128) instead of four chains (28). BIT-IDENTICAL: for a given row,
   thread `tid` accumulates the shipped kernel's exact subset in the shipped order with the
   same eight `acc +=` fma expressions; the shared-memory tree is replayed step for step and
   its last five steps become `__shfl_down_sync` by 16/8/4/2/1, which pairs the SAME lanes in
   the SAME order (taken only when `mmv_block()` is a power of two, since the shipped `s >>= 1`
   walk lands on 16 only then).
2. `qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32_v2` — the same walk inside the fused six-projection kernel: its three
   BF16 ranges take 8 rows/block instead of `kda6_bf16_rows4`'s four sequential rows; the three
   f32 ranges keep `f32_mmvq_row1` verbatim. Bit-identical per row.
3. `moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8_v2` — 8 warps/block on `threadIdx.y`, and the `g` walk unrolled by
   two so BOTH groups' weight, scale and activation loads issue before either dp4a chain runs
   (603 LDG in the SASS vs 201 for the `_w4` twin). The 36 B NVFP4 block layout leaves a row's
   quant bytes only 4 B aligned, so wider loads are illegal here and DEPTH is the only lever.
   Bit-identical per (o, j): `accg += dot(g); accu += dot(g); accg += dot(g+32); accu += ...`
   is the shipped per-accumulator order, unrolled.
4. `moe_down8_fma_q8_v2` — the shipped kernel walks its 8 experts SEQUENTIALLY inside ONE warp,
   so the launch is `out_f` warps wide (4096 warps for the GLM-5.3 down shape = 0.43 of a
   full-occupancy B200 wave) and each expert's DRAM round trip is serialized behind the last.
   Here ONE BLOCK owns one output row and warp `j` owns expert slot `j`: `out_f * n_used` warps
   wide, eight experts' bytes in flight together. STILL BIT-IDENTICAL — each expert's partial is
   the shipped per-expert chain plus the same `warp_reduce_sum`, and the final
   `chain = __fmaf_rn(w.v[k], part[k], chain)` runs on ONE thread in the SAME ascending slot
   order. Parallelising the experts moved no bits because the slot chain was never the parallel
   part.

NUMERIC CLASSES. Arms 1-4 as dispatched are bit-identical twins and add no class. The ONE
exception is the split-K arm, `matvec_bf16_v2_sk` + `matvec_bf16_v2_sk_combine`: class name
**`bf16_gemv_v2_splitk`**, a row's K sum split into `ksplit` contiguous chunks reduced
independently and combined in a FIXED ASCENDING order (never atomics, never a
scheduling-dependent reduction), so it is deterministic but not bit-identical. `gemv_v2_ksplit`
returns 1 — the bit-identical kernel — whenever the row grid already covers two waves of CTAs
over `Engine::sm_count()`; the GLM-5.3 KDA decode shapes (out_f 4096/8192 at t=1 give 512/1024
CTAs against 2x148 = 296) never reach it, so the class is reachable only for shapes narrower
than the ones this lane exists for. The bench prints the chosen `ksplit` and the bit-identity
verdict per shape so it can never engage silently.

BENCH: `crates/memra-engine/src/bin/b200_matvec_bench.rs`, extended to run shipped vs
LT-reference vs v2 for every family (including a new `qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32` family), N=5
interleaved, printing us, GB/s and bit-identity or max abs diff:
`MEMRA_GPU_LOCK=/tmp/memra-gpu.lock cargo run -p memra-engine --release --bin b200_matvec_bench -- 5 3`.

**BOX RECEIPT (2026-09-02, B200 dev 0, `b200_matvec_bench 5 3`, int2 `18df26ad6`;
`research/glm5-b200-20260902/box/gates/gate-gemv-bench.txt` in the private lane repo).** Median
us over N=5 interleaved, shipped -> `MEMRA_B200_MATVEC_ARM` -> v2:

| family | shipped | arm | v2 | v2 vs shipped | identity |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| `matvec_bf16` kda up 4096->8192 | 33.4 | 28.5 | **23.7** (2.83 TB/s) | **1.409x** | bit-identical |
| `matvec_bf16` kda down 8192->4096 | 30.65 | 24.9 | **24.3** | **1.26x** | bit-identical |
| `qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32` | 100.8 | — | **65.0** (3.18 TB/s) | **1.55x** | y5 bug, now fixed |
| `moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8` | 55.0 | 53.3 | 54.4 | 1.011x | bit-identical |
| `moe_down8_fma_q8` | 37.6 | 36.0 | 43.2 | **0.870x** | bit-identical |
| `bf16_gemv_lt` reference (both bf16 shapes) | — | — | 32.3 | 1.03x / 0.95x | class, see below |

Four things that receipt settled, and each changed the code rather than the prose:

1. **The bf16 rows and the fused kda6 group are the win**, and they are the two families this
   door now dispatches. kda6 at 3.18 TB/s is 40% of the wall against 26% shipped.
2. **`qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32_v2` was NOT bit-identical on that run**: `MISMATCH n=64
   max_abs_diff=2.442e2`, and the serving A/B was correctly aborted. Cause: the first cut
   dropped the `b -= nb` before the LAST of the six ranges, so every `b*R + p` landed past
   `out5`, `f32_mmvq_row1` returned for all of them and y5 was never written — n=64 was exactly
   `out5`, and 2.442e2 exactly `max |y5|`. Range 5 is the only range with no `if (b < nb)` guard
   behind it, so nothing else caught it. Fixed; the bench now prints PER-PROJECTION mismatch
   counts so a location, not a total, comes back next time.
3. **The MoE v2 twins are measured and not dispatched.** gate/up is inside noise of `_w4` (54.4
   vs 53.3) exactly as its static receipt predicted (longest LDG burst moved only 18 -> 19), and
   down REGRESSED 20% (43.2 vs 36.0) despite being bit-identical and 8x wider: eight warps per
   block now contend for the same L2 sectors, and the shipped single warp was already covering
   the latency it was accused of exposing. `moe_fused_epi_launch` therefore keeps the
   shipped/`_w4` pair unconditionally; the v2 kernels stay in the fatbin and in the bench as
   measured arms.
4. **cuBLASLt at m=1 is NOT a ceiling.** 32.3 us on both bf16 shapes, i.e. 1.03x the shipped
   kernel on one and 0.95x on the other, against v2's 23.7/24.3. The library has no answer for
   this shape, which retires the "maybe we are just badly scheduled versus a tuned GEMM library"
   hypothesis. `MEMRA_B200_BF16_GEMV_LT` stays an instrument only. (That run also reported
   `max_abs_diff=inf` for the LT class: the compare was fine, the bench's synthetic bf16
   generator randomised the full exponent, so a 4096-term dot overflowed f32 — invisible while
   both arms run the same program, fatal the moment one sums in a different order. The generator
   now samples the same [-2, 2) range as the activations and `compare` PANICS on a non-finite
   value rather than printing `inf`.)

**LEVEL 2 (v3) IS STILL PENDING ITS RECEIPT.** v2's in-flight budget is register-bound (96
registers on the kda6 kernel -> ~102 KB per SM outstanding), which is what caps it at 40% of the
wall. Of the three levers this lane had named, only staging changes that number: a persistent CTA
leaves in-flight bytes unchanged (it attacks launch count, the graph lane's axis), and a 2-CTA
cluster sharing the activation caps out around 12% of load instructions because the activation is
already only a quarter of v2's traffic. `cp.async` staging moves the bytes out of registers into
shared memory: 2 stages x 8 rows x (blockDim*8) x 2 B = 32 KB in flight per CTA plus a 4 KB
reduction window = 36 KB, so 228/36 = 6 CTAs/SM = **~192 KB per SM, 1.9x v2**, and the register
count drops to 58/64 so registers stop binding. It adds NO barriers: each thread copies and reads
exactly its own 16 B lane in every row, so the pipeline waits are per-thread. Chosen over TMA
because the arithmetic is the same and the mbarrier protocol is not something to get wrong on a
part this lane cannot run; TMA is the follow-up if v3's issue rate becomes the wall.

**ROUND 3 RETARGET: THE DOOR WAS AIMING AT KERNELS THE SERVING POSTURE DOES NOT RUN.** Serving
A/B pair 1 on the pair (2026-09-02, all doors + `MEMRA_GLM5_W8=1` as the base) moved nothing:
49.2 -> 49.3 tok/s code, 48.8 -> 48.7 prose, **and the boot log printed no engagement line at
all**. The cause is the posture, not the kernels: with `MEMRA_GLM5_W8` on (PR #86, +10% plain),
`matvec_bf16_rows_into` reroutes every bf16-resident KDA/MLA projection through
`matvec_bf16_via_q8_mirror(_t)` BEFORE the bf16 arms are reached, so
`matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows` and `qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32` are not on the t=1 decode path at all.
The bf16 v2/v3 arms stay for the non-W8 posture; the door now also covers what W8 actually runs:

| where | kernel the W8 posture runs | v2 twin |
|---|---|---|
| t = 1 decode trunk | `qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq_rp` (via `matvec_bf16_via_q8_mirror` -> `qmatvec_mmvq_into`, `QT_Q8_0` + `rp`); `_rp_g2` only when `out_f/4 < 4*SMs`, i.e. `out_f < 2368` on B200, so not the 4096/8192 KDA rows | `qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq_rp_v2` |
| t = 2..=8 verify walk | `qmatvec_q8_0_rows_tw` (via `matvec_bf16_via_q8_mirror_t` under `MEMRA_Q8T_WONCE`) | `qmatvec_q8_0_rows_tw_v2` |
| KDA stage-1 group | SIX separate launches — the fused door's bf16 arm declines outright under W8 | `qmatvec_kda6_q8f32_rp_v2` |

W8-posture nsys census (int9 `fc4b71ef7`, all doors + W8, ~224 measured tokens, both devices):
`qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq_rp` 455.4 ms over 47,364 launches = **9.6 us**, 8.1% of GPU, ~211
launches/token. At the [4096 -> 8192] q8_0 shape that is 8192 x 128 x 34 B = 35.65 MB per launch
= **3.71 TB/s, 46% of the 8 TB/s wall** — already the most efficient matvec on the die, so the
bytes-per-launch design buys less here than it did on bf16, and the honest lever is LAUNCH
COUNT plus the activation-read overhead. The q8_0 rp mirror is a 32 B quant plane plus a 2 B
scale plane, so unlike NVFP4's 36 B blocks every weight fetch is ALREADY an aligned 16 B
`__ldcs`: there is no load-width lever. What there is: `qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq_rp` reads 36 B of
ACTIVATION (32 B `aq` + a 4 B `ad` scale) for every 34 B of weight, per lane, per
block-iteration, and that activation is identical for all 8192 rows in the launch. The v2 twins
stage it into shared memory once per CTA (`in_f + nblk*4` = 4.6 KB at in_f=4096, one barrier),
pack 8 warps per block instead of 4, and unroll the block walk by two. BIT-IDENTICAL per output:
same warp-per-row mapping, same `blk = lane, lane+32, ...` walk, same eight `dp4a`, same
`acc += dw * ad[blk] * (float)sumi` association, same `warp_reduce_sum`.

`qmatvec_kda6_q8f32_rp_v2` is the launch-count lever and it is a NEW fusion, not a twin: the W8
path had none. `qmatvec_kda6_q8f32_mmvq` addresses interleaved 34 B blocks (a resident
plain-layout Q8_0 tensor) while the W8 mirror is the split-plane rp4 form, and
`MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ`'s bf16 arm declines whenever W8 is on. Per KDA layer the stage-1 group is
3 q8 launches plus 3 f32 projections that each cost a cuBLAS `dot_kernel` + `reduce_1Block_kernel`
PAIR (the census: 248.5 ms over 72,000 + 145.8 ms over 72,000 = 394 ms, ~643 launches/token,
~1.8 ms/token, for the rank-128 low-rank KDA projections the loader keeps as F32) = **9 launches
per layer, plus six redundant `quantize_q8_1` calls on the same `x`**. The fused arm makes that
**1**: over 34 KDA layers, 306 -> 34 launches per token. It engages only with BOTH
`MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ` and `MEMRA_B200_GEMV_V2` set, so it carries its own receipt; without the
v2 door W8 declines to the unfused path exactly as before. Numeric classes are the sibling fused
kernels': the three mirrored ranges are bit-identical to `qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq_rp` per row, and the
three f32 ranges take the same deterministic warp tree the q8 arm of `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ`
already ships and has pinned.

STILL ON cuBLAS after this lane: `f_b` and `g_b`, the `[8192 x 128]` stage-4 halves of the
low-rank gate pairs. They are chained off `f_a`/`g_a`'s outputs so they cannot join the stage-1
launch, and at 2 projections x 2 launches x 34 layers they are ~136 launches/token of the
census's cuBLAS pair. Named, sized and NOT done here — see the lane doc's open items.

ROLLBACK SEAM: unset or `=0` (a strict per-process `OnceLock` read, no persistent state) leaves
every call site byte-identical to pre-lane behaviour. See
`research/b200-gemv-hbm-20260902/LANE.md`.

### Build-time (build.rs / nvcc)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_NVCC` | `nvcc` on PATH | nvcc binary override |
| `MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH` | auto-detected | build target: `120a` (Blackwell consumer), `100a` (B200), `90a` (Hopper), `89` (portable eval). Unset probes `nvidia-smi compute_cap`: 12.x→120a, 10.0→100a, 9.0→90a, 8.9→89; GPU-less/unknown falls back to 120a. B200 auto-detection was enabled only after the 2026-09-01 sealed runtime/model/serving battery; it does not make every model or optional kernel `NativeQualified`. Explicit values always win. `89` builds the secondary correctness-first eval lane: native-FP4 prefill compiled off, tiled GDN on its generic fallback, prompt attention through f32 SDPA |
| `MEMRA_ARCH_CHECK` | on | `=0` skips the Engine::new device-vs-binary compute-capability guard (fatbins are single-arch SASS; the guard fails early with a rebuild hint on mismatch) |
| `MEMRA_CUTLASS` | off | set = compile the CUTLASS sm120 NVFP4 GEMM (`cutlass_smoke`, `MEMRA_FP4_CUTLASS` door) |
| `MEMRA_CUTLASS_ROOT` | flashinfer venv tree | CUTLASS header tree location |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_Q45K` | 64 | k-quant MMQ X-tile (compile-time sweep seam) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_Q4` | 128 | q4_0 MMQ token-tile rebuild (`64\|96`; same axis as the q45k/w4a8 seams — build-time, the tile is a template constant) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_FP8` | v2 default (X=256 wide candidate) | per-block FP8 prefill tile token-width rebuild — sets the WIDE candidate; the launcher picks between it and `FP8_MMQ_X_SMALL` per call by wave fill (`build.rs`; history in the `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ` §2 entry) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_IQEXP` | 128 | expert-segmented IQ MMQ token-tile rebuild (round-45 kernel-rate dig: smaller tiles trade MMA j-reuse for CTAs/SM) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_FOLD_CEILING` | off | **CEILING PROBE, NOT SHIPPABLE**: rebuilds the NVFP4 W4A8 MMQ with the per-k01 f32 scale-fold collapsed to an s32 add — output is NUMERICALLY WRONG by construction (exactness gates WILL fail, by design). Measures the upper bound of fold-removal levers only (`research/prefill-gemm-20260806/`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_PP_MINBLOCKS` | kernel default | build-time sweep of `fa_prefill_f32_pp`'s `__launch_bounds__` minBlocksPerMultiprocessor (tune seam) |
| `MEMRA_IQEXP_K16` | off | Rollback seam for the IQ-experts/IQ4_XS-dense MMQ tiles' MMA **depth** (`cu/mmq_iq_experts.cu`, 2026-08-07, lane/iq-experts-k32). Set `=1` = rebuild with the ORIGINAL two-`m16n8k16.s8` form per 32-value k step; unset (default) = ONE `m16n8k32.s8` — the int8 pipe is K-FREE on sm_120a (both 16.06 cyc/warp-MMA, so k32 = 2x MACs/instr) and the fold arity halves (1 C tile / 1 dA / 1 FMA vs 2/2/2). Legal because all three tile loaders replicate the per-32 scale into both per-16 x_df slots (`s>>1` indexing), so one k32 MMA sums both halves in the exact s32 accumulator under one shared scale. **NOT bit-identical to k16** (the PTX audit's "candidate bit-identical" missed the f32 fold-shape change: k16 rounds `C0*d + C1*d` separately, k32 rounds `(C0+C1)*d` once — prime logits differ ~80% of bytes, maxdiff 1.7e0/6.7e-1 gemma/KAT, argmax MATCH, top-10 sets near-identical); branch-(b) class, and the kernel was already gated vs dp4a on closeness+argmax+spec, never byte-identity. SASS: 1536x IMMA.16832 vs 3072x IMMA.16816 (exact 2:1). Gates on k32: kernel-check ALL GREEN model-backed (KAT rel<=2.7e-4, bar 1e-3), run-gen argmax MATCH (gemma26b + KAT), run-spec K=1..8 8/8 PASS (KAT + owntrim drafter). Perf N=5 interleaved adjacent-alternating pairs, one lock hold: gemma26b pp2311 **+5.15%** (6893.4 -> 7248.5, DISJOINT, 5/5), KAT pp2048 **+2.82%** (3903.6 -> 4013.5, DISJOINT, 5/5). Receipts: `research/iq-k32-20260807/` |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_W4A8` / `MEMRA_MMQ_Y_W4A8` | 128 / 128 | W4A8 MMQ tile sizes. Swept 2026-07-06: defaults optimal on the 5090 (X=32 −28%, Y=64 −2%); kept as autotuner levers for other silicon |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_F8F4` on `sm_100a` | off | The optional route compiles through `MEMRA_F8F4_PLAIN_MMA`, the already-gated bit-identical plain-E4M3 form; `sm_120a` keeps the faster block-scale identity form. On the pinned Qwen3.5-9B B200 battery, the required model manifest was green and its 64-token greedy continuation matched default W4A8. The flag remains opt-in because its acceptance sign is model, drafter, and prompt specific; each serving artifact still needs its own adoption receipt. |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_FP8BLK_PLAIN` | off | Rollback seam for the per-block FP8 prefill tile's MMA **form** (`cu/mmq_fp8_blk.cu`, 2026-08-06, lane/rp-on-st). Set = issue plain `mma.sync.aligned.kind::f8f6f4.m16n8k32`; unset (default) = `kind::mxf8f6f4.block_scale.scale_vec::1X … ue8m0` with the **identity scale `0x7F7F7F7F`**, which computes the bit-identical e4m3×e4m3 product at **16.06 cyc/warp-MMA instead of 32.02** on sm_120a. Worth **+6.54% e2e pp512** on the 27B block-128 checkpoint (N=6 adjacent alternating pairs, DISJOINT, spread <0.09%); bounded below the 1.994x MMA-rate ratio because this tile measures 110–130 TF and was never MMA-bound. Exactness is not inherited: `fp8-mmq-check` reports **byte-for-byte identical** verdicts across the two forms (9 shapes × 2 arms, EXACT arm 0 bit-mismatch, 254/254 e4m3 codes) and live prefill logits over 1040 dispatches differ in **0 of 993280 bytes** (md5 equal, two passes). Same one-line change as `MEMRA_MMQ_F8F4_PLAIN` on the W4A8 tile. Receipts: `research/rp-on-st-20260806/` (`fp8blk-form-ab.*`, `pplogits-*`, `blksc-noreg.*`) |
| `ACCPROBE_F32_PLAIN` | off | **Research instrument only** — rebuilds the F32 arm of `accprobe-bench`'s kernel (`cu/mmq_q8_0_f32acc.cu`) on the plain `kind::f8f6f4` MMA form. Exists solely so the FP8-ST v3 gate's published Q1 numbers stay reproducible: that gate measured its F32 arm at 32.02 cyc/warp-MMA against an s32 control at 16.06 and attributed the whole delta to the **accumulator**, so its "+16.7/+23.1pp weighted → GO" was the MMA form. Equalized (the default now), the sign flips to **−6.7/−8.8pp** and the v3 case is refuted — see `research/fp8v3-gate-20260805/VERDICT.md`'s correction header and `research/rp-on-st-20260806/accprobe-*`. No dispatch seam; this TU is never linked into a serving path |
| `MEMRA_GEMM_K1_LAUNCH` | shipped (128,128,8) | `"BM,BN,NWARP"` kernel1 launch-tile override — MUST match the `-D K1_BM/K1_BN/NWARP` the swept fatbin was built with (tools/sweep) |

`MEMRA_*_FATBIN*` are **internal build plumbing** (build.rs → rustc-env fatbin paths consumed
by `include_bytes!(env!(...))`), never user flags. The exhaustive set (2026-08-19 audit):
`MEMRA_ENGINE_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN` (+ its KV-format variants `MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN_KF8`,
`MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN_VF8`, `MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN_VQ4`, `MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN_KF8VF8`,
`MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN_KF8VQ4`), `MEMRA_GEMM_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_QMATVEC_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_ROUTER_FATBIN`,
`MEMRA_SAMPLE_FATBIN` (spec_sample), `MEMRA_HYBRID_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_KDA_FATBIN` (glm5_next Kimi
Delta Attention kernels, added with the kernel lane 2026-08-28), and `MEMRA_FATBIN` (memra-probe's
own module). The same class covers the four build-identity vars below and
`MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH` (memra-engine build.rs → the
`MEMRA_ARCH_CHECK` device-vs-binary guard's compiled-arch side). `MEMRA_KV_KFMT`/`MEMRA_KV_VFMT`
are the `-D` defines build.rs derives from `MEMRA_KV_K`/`MEMRA_KV_V` per flash fatbin.
`MEMRA_MMVQ_ROWS` is a compile-time `#define` in qmatvec.cu, not an env var.

**Build identity: no flag, by decision** (lane/real-system-fingerprint, 2026-09-01). The
OpenAI-shape `system_fingerprint` is `memra-<crate version>-<content id>`, always real and
never overridable. There is deliberately **no env var that disables, downgrades, or replaces
it**: the only thing such a knob could buy is publishing an unverifiable identity, and the
defect this lane closed was exactly a fingerprint nobody could verify (prod served
`memra-unknown` for a deploy generation because a git lookup failed silently inside the
release container). Default: **always-on real fingerprint, no flag**. Rollback seam: none by
design: the degraded path is not a mode, it is a fallback that WARNS at build time
(`cargo:warning`) and at every boot. Receipts + full scheme: `docs/SERVING.md` →
*The build fingerprint*; consumed by darklanes `tools/check-claim-builds.mjs --live`.

These four are set by memra-server's build.rs and read through `env!`, never from the
environment at runtime:

| var | value | read as |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_BUILD_ID` | 12 lowercase hex: content digest of the workspace's compiled inputs. Never empty, never `unknown` | `SYSTEM_FINGERPRINT` (with the crate version) |
| `MEMRA_BUILD_ID_SRC` | `source-tree` or `degraded` | `memra_server::BUILD_ID_SRC` → the boot WARN |
| `MEMRA_BUILD_ID_NOTE` | why the id is degraded; empty when it is not | `memra_server::BUILD_ID_NOTE` |
| `MEMRA_BUILD_SHA` | the build's git sha, or `unknown`. **Extra provenance only, never the identity**: it does not survive a history rewrite | `memra_server::BUILD_GIT_SHA` |

---

## 3. Rollback seams (default ON — `=0`/set reverts to reference path)

These exist because correctness discipline needs a same-binary oracle. Each is a measured winner; the seam is the documented way back.

| flag | revert semantics | provenance |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_CKPT=0` | dspark round only (armed route): partial-accept restore back to the snapshot+replay oracle instead of the MTP column-stash direct restore | default-on in the dspark route 2026-08-18; column-stash cut restore 22ms/round -> 0.35ms, spec 23->38 tok/s at placeholder acceptance; `MEMRA_DSPARK_CKPT_GATE=1` byte-gates the two arms per partial round |
| `MEMRA_STATE_COPY_BATCH=0` | dspark verify round: per-layer GDN snap/commit state copies back to the eager per-buffer dispatch loop instead of the batched pointer-table kernel | default-on 2026-08-20 (engine-bundle slice 1); batching cut snap 0.63->0.19 ms/rd + commit 0.24->0.13 ms/rd, agentic gate +3.1%; launch-structure only, bytes unchanged (E2E-gated) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_FA_ROWS=0` | qwen35 t-parallel verify: full-attention per-row KV-append + fa-decode loop instead of the z-batched seqs twins (one append + one fa main + one combine per layer) | default-on 2026-08-20 (fa-execupdate slice 4); agentic gate 109.4->114.3 tok/s (-0.9 ms/rd), verify wall 18.51->17.64; straddle rounds fall back per row automatically; bytes unchanged (E2E + bank-matched) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_VG_AUTOFREE=1` | `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH=1` graphs only: instantiate captured verify graphs with `AUTO_FREE_ON_LAUNCH` instead of `USE_NODE_PRIORITY` | PRIORITY default 2026-08-20 (fa-execupdate slice 4): the AUTO_FREE launch-time mem-pool scan cost 25.6 us x 16 segment launches ≈ 0.41 ms/rd; captured alloc nodes are balanced by in-graph frees so AUTO_FREE had nothing to reclaim |
| `MEMRA_MTP_CHAIN_GRAPH=0` | multi-head MTP draft chain (mtp_extra non-empty; step37's 3-head step-modulo prefix-replay) back to the EAGER chain only — no per-head chain-graph capture (`DraftChainGraphs`: per-head `interior` append+carrier graphs + `last` head+tail graphs, replayed in the exact eager launch order with the chain policy host-side, so graph-vs-eager drafts are bit-identical by construction). Both modes revert together (greedy chain and sampled chain). A failed capture already degrades loudly to eager (the draft-graph WARN), and `=0` prints its own one-line disarm note, so neither arm is ever silent. Single-head capture untouched. | default-on 2026-08-30 (lane/step37-draft-graph-serving-20260830; capture was structurally unreachable at MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=3 before — the `mtp_extra.is_empty()` conjunct — and the qualified step37 serving shape never even attempted it). Gates + A/B: `research/step37-draft-graph-serving-20260830/RESULTS.md`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GRAPH_FILTERED=0` | sampled draft-graph capture back to the PURE-TEMP-ONLY regime: truncation-filtered requests (top_k/top_p/min_p — including the step37 vendor default temp 0.5 / top_p 0.9) draft EAGER instead of capturing the filter in-graph (`filter_stats` + `gumbel_perturb_filtered_ctr` nodes; counter and stats read from device, seed/temp/filters baked and carried by `SampledGraphKey`, so a regime change drops the parked graph). Exactness both arms: the draft draws from the SAME filtered distribution the verify's accept test reconstructs — in-graph the stats come from `filter_stats` on the retained q inside the capture, and the accept path recomputes them from that retained q with the same deployment-keyed program, so th/z are bit-identical; penalties never capture (per-round history). Pure-temp capture body is byte-identical either way. | default-on 2026-08-30 (lane/step37-draft-graph-serving-20260830; the pure-temp-only law dated from the graph-s-key exactness lane 2026-08-19, whose bug class — draw and accept seeing different distributions — is exactly what the in-graph filter removes). Seeded graph-vs-eager identity + acceptance-identity receipts: `research/step37-draft-graph-serving-20260830/RESULTS.md`. |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_CALIBRATE=0` | skip the BOOT ADMISSION CALIBRATION probe and keep the static `SPEC_SHRINK_RESERVE` (1536 MiB) transient floor plus a zero per-session draft-state charge until live captures observe one (the pre-lane admission arithmetic).
