Default ON: at boot, before the worker reports ready, one spec-shaped generation (synthetic one-chunk prompt, vendor-shaped filtered sampling, pinned seed; a memory-shape probe, never a perf cell) runs through the real serving path and the driver-kept pool high-water on every device becomes the deployment's measured transient floor (`[admit-cal] boot calibration done:` receipt line), never below the static constant. It also materializes the first-request one-time pools (prime slabs, cuBLAS workspaces, draft/vg graph pools) at boot and records the first per-session draft-state observation. Probe failure is LOUD and non-fatal (static floor serves, pool trimmed back). | default-on 2026-08-30 (lane/step37-vram-admission-20260830: the static floor was measured 4.6x low on a medium-class deployment, 1,611 MB charged vs 7,458 MiB measured first-burst transient, so admission admitted past the card with `admission_vram_defers=0` and c=8 hard-failed from a clean boot; receipts `research/step37-vram-admission-20260830/RESULTS.md`). |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_CAPTURE_GATE=0` | draft-graph capture back to TRY-AND-FAIL: attempt every capture and rely on the OOM error path (diagnostics door; the trim-on-OOM recovery and the no-stranded-partial-state contract stay active either way). Default ON: before any draft-graph capture arm allocates, the device's effective free (driver free + async-pool cached) must cover the capture's expected appetite (the model's measured per-session draft-state high-water, bootstrap estimate before first observation) plus a post-capture safety floor; a short device refuses the capture pre-attempt through the same LOUD once-per-flip `[spec] WARN: ... capture failed (insufficient VRAM headroom ...)` line and the session serves eagerly with headroom intact. When the driver alone is short but cached pool blocks would cover it, the pool is trimmed first (`[spec] pre-capture pool trim:` line): graph instantiate and cuBLAS allocate from the driver, not from our pool. | default-on 2026-08-30 (lane/step37-vram-admission-20260830: a capture attempt on a tight card walked the device to the edge and stranded the eager fallback, the owner's single-session second-prompt OOM: capture WARN then 28 step-OOM engine errors at 5 MiB free; receipts `research/step37-vram-admission-20260830/RESULTS.md`). |

| `MEMRA_MOE_SLAB_POPULATE` | `.memra-repack` expert-slab page-cache population at load: `fits` (**default ON**) reads each slab file sequentially when it leaves ≥20% of MemTotal available; `off` keeps the pure demand-fault tier | **Default-ON decision (2026-08-28, GLM-5.3-Flash bring-up, cloudbox bench box).** Both arms measured. OFF arm: the slab mmap carries `MADV_RANDOM`, so every expert access demand-faults page-by-page — bpftrace measured **4096 B per block read, 181 603 reads, 3027 IOPS ≈ 11.8 MB/s** on the single `memra-gpu-worker` thread (stack `filemap_fault → folio_wait_bit_common`), and **no request ever produced a first token** inside the 90 s `TIMEOUT_MS_MAX` platform ceiling. ON arm: one sequential pass at the volume's full rate; serving then reads experts from page cache. The guard preserves the >RAM sizing case the disk tier exists for (MiniMax-M3 REAP50, 122 GB slab on a 60 GB host) — that slab fails the floor and keeps today's behavior, so the default cannot regress it. Cost is load time, paid once, for bytes the demand-fault path pays anyway but on every request's critical path. Not applied to the GGUF spill tier: those maps are whole shards, not expert slabs (`spill.rs`). Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/`. |

| `MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE=0\|1\|2` | **ARCH-KEYED DEFAULT since 2026-09-04: unset = `2` (the v2 arm) on sm_100a builds, unset = OFF on every other build; `0` is the explicit unfused chain, `1`/`2` opt in anywhere.** The pair has served `=2` in every posture since 2026-09-02 (darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`, door ladder and every A/B after it, tape `9437b599f6b9d2a9`).  glm5_next FUSED mHC SITE PRE-CHAIN (lane/glm5-decode-diet lever 1, 2026-08-31): the three per-site kernels of `hyper::pre_finish` (`memra_dsv4_rowsq_scale` + `memra_dsv4_hc_sinkhorn_m` + `memra_dsv4_hc_collapse`) run as ONE `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused` launch per (site, token) — the launch-diet census's top mHC increment (mhc-sites family: 3.306 ms/token GPU + 362 launches/token on the serving arm; 20 serial Sinkhorn iterations ran as their own 18.3 us launch behind a 15.5 us rowsq launch, 90 sites/token -> 3 launches each become 1). Every body is the unfused kernel's VERBATIM (rowsq's 8-wide f64 reduction at the pinned blockDim=128; Sinkhorn on shared operands; collapse per-element expression), so the claim is BIT identity, not a band. The kernel also carries a BIT-PRESERVING Sinkhorn stationarity exit: iterations are a deterministic map, so a full (row,col) application that leaves every comb bit unchanged proves all later iterations are identity — the exit fires only on bitwise stationarity (measured on the gate fixture: exit at min=6 / mean=10.09 / max=20 of the 20-iteration cap) and needs no flag of its own. Engages at any t (block-per-token, per-token bytes t-invariant) when streams <= 8; other shapes fall through to the unchanged three-kernel chain. Announced once per boot (`[hc-fused-pre] engaged`), counted (`hyper::HC_FUSED_PRE_DISPATCHES`). `=2` (lane/b200-sinkhorn-fusion-20260902 follow-up, same door, second value): identical stage 1 (rowsq) and stage 3 (collapse) to `=1`'s kernel, copied VERBATIM. Stage 2 (Sinkhorn) is restructured for hc<=4 (rows=(2+hc)*hc<=32, GLM-5.3-Flash's own hc=4): every shared-memory index the Sinkhorn stage touches lives inside warp 0's 32 lanes, so the stage runs inside `if (t < 32)` with every `__syncthreads()` in that scope replaced by `__syncwarp()` — a SYNCHRONIZATION-PRIMITIVE SUBSTITUTION ONLY (same operands, same operators, same sequential summation order per lane as `=1`'s body), so `=2` is bit-identical to `=1` and to the unfused chain by construction, not by tolerance. Motivated by a fresh B200 nsys census with `=1` ON: `dsv4_hc_pre_fused_kernel` (the `=1` kernel) measured 29,160 launches x 32.8 us avg = 15.6% of GPU time, ~4.3 ms/token (130 launches/token) in serving — the second-largest kernel, because at t=1 the real per-site math (hc=4, d=4096) is tiny and almost the entire 32.8 us is up to 20 serial `__syncthreads()` pairs synchronizing a 128-thread/4-warp block for work only 4-16 threads (all within warp 0) ever touch. For hc>4 (rows>32) the warp-0-only invariant does not hold; the host wrapper (`memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused_v2`) falls back to calling the `=1` kernel internally rather than an unverified multi-warp scheme, so `=2` is always correct and only sometimes faster than `=1`. Counted separately (`hyper::HC_FUSED_PRE_V2_DISPATCHES`), announced with `arm=2` in the same `[hc-fused-pre] engaged ...` line. | **DEFAULT OFF, deliberately (2026-08-31, lane/glm5-decode-diet).** ON arm (gate RUN GREEN 4/4, rig 5090 TF32-off 2026-08-31, `crates/memra-engine/tests/hc_fused_pre_gpu.rs`): per-site bit identity fused-vs-unfused (`to_bits` on pre/post/comb/y) at t in {1,2,7,15,64} x (hc,d) in {(4,128),(4,4096),(2,640)}; stationarity exit bit-invisible vs the full-20 unfused chain; isolation reds (scale[0]/[1]/[2] flips each caught on exactly pre+y / post / comb); whole-model 24-step decode byte identity ON==OFF on the mini glm5_next hc fixture with the engagement counter 0->100 in the ON arm and flat OFF. hyper_connections_gpu re-run green 6/6 in both arms; the glm5 GPU suites (tparallel 7/7, spec_session 9/9, dflash_session 10/10, mtp_head 5/5) green with the flag ON. OFF arm: the shipped three-kernel chain, untouched, and the rollback seam — the flag is read PER CALL (not latched). NOT default-ON: no throughput receipt (the rig is exactness-only; the ~2.5-3 ms/token expected value is census arithmetic, WINDOW-20260830.md §3.2). Flip condition: interleaved x3 fresh-boot A/B on the serving card class (x5 on anomaly, per the amended law), greedy + vendor-default sampled twin, engagement announce in BOTH arms. Lane doc: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/decode-diet-20260831/LANE.md`.  **`=2` DEFAULT is also OFF** (the door's default is unset/`0` either way; `=2` is a second opt-in value, not a new door) — same rollback seam, read PER CALL. `hc-fused-gate` (`crates/memra-engine/src/bin/hc_fused_gate.rs`) is `=2`'s gate: bit-identity (`to_bits` on pre/post/comb/y, unfused vs `=1` vs `=2`) plus N=5 per-call timing at t in {1,4,8} for the pinned GLM-5.3-Flash shape (hc=4, d=4096, iters=20, eps=1e-6). B200 box receipt, 2x SXM dev 0, `=1` vs unfused, N=5 wall us/call (bit-bad=0 at every tested t): t=1 unfused=112.6 fused=101.0 (`hc_post` alone, unrelated to this fusion, =13.8); t=4 unfused=118.2 fused=117.6; t=8 unfused=140.6 fused=123.0 — matches nsys's per-launch number once host launch+sync overhead is subtracted out, and is the receipt this row's `=1` arm was missing. `=2`'s own box receipt (fused_v2 column, same shapes) is pending the next box window; not yet measured. Flip condition for either value is unchanged: interleaved x3 fresh-boot A/B on the serving card class, greedy + vendor-default sampled twin, engagement announce in BOTH arms. Lane doc: `research/b200-sinkhorn-fusion-20260902/LANE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK=<n>` | **ARCH-KEYED DEFAULT since 2026-09-04: 512 on sm_100a builds (receipt: +10.25% with the register Sinkhorn on the 2x B200 pair, hcpreab 2026-09-03, tape `9437b599f6b9d2a9` unchanged), 128 elsewhere.** Was: **128 (default) = `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused_v2` VERBATIM, bit-identical to the shipped arm.** The CUDA block width of the fused mHC pre-chain when `MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE=2` selected the v2 arm; values 32..1024, powers of two only (anything else is refused BY NAME at read time with one `[hc-pre-block]` line and falls back to 128, because a bad width would otherwise reach the launcher and surface as an opaque 40023). WHY: `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused_v2` launches `<<<s, 128>>>`, one block per ROW. At t=1 decode s is 1, so the entire call is ONE block of 128 threads on a 148-SM B200 and 147 SMs sit idle. nsys on the 2x B200 pair in the current best posture (`MEMRA_GLM5_W8` + `MEMRA_B200_GEMV_V2` + `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ` + `MEMRA_B200_DSA_DECODE=2` + `MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV`, 2026-09-03, 54.22 tok/s under the profiler) makes it the LARGEST kernel in the decode profile: **17.5% of kernel time, 31.1 us average, 23,220 launches** over the profiled window, which is 90.7 per token = exactly the 2 sites (attn, mlp) on each of 45 layers. Those 31 us move about 128 KB (stage 1 reads hc*d = 4x4096 floats, stage 3 reads them again to write d), i.e. **4.1 GB/s on a card with 8 TB/s of HBM3e** — four warps cannot hold enough loads in flight to cover HBM latency, so this is a launch-geometry defect, not an arithmetic one. EXACTNESS, stated rather than assumed: stage 3 (collapse) is bit-identical at ANY width because each output sums the same `hc` terms in the same order and only the owning thread moves; stage 2 (Sinkhorn) is warp-0-only at every width by the same `hc<=4` invariant v2 documents; stage 1 (rowsq) is NOT, because `dsv4_block_sum` reduces over `blockDim.x` and a wider block gives each thread a different subset of the row, changing the double accumulation order. The f32 narrowing of `1/sqrt(tot/w + eps)` is EXPECTED to absorb a last-ulp double difference, but expected is not constructed, so every width other than 128 is the NAMED NUMERIC CLASS `hc_pre_rowsq_blockwide`. **DEFAULT 128 BY DESIGN** (new-flags law): the default is the bit-identical width, and a wider one becomes a default only with an interleaved fresh-boot A/B, a greedy tape, and an argmax gate on the pair. `hc>4` (rows>32) falls back to `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused` exactly as v2 does. Rollback: unset, or `=128`. Read per call. Kernel `dsv4_hc_pre_fused_v3_kernel`, launcher `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused_v3`, reader `hc_pre_block()`. MEASURED, 2x B200 pair, TWO interleaved rounds of fresh boots, 20 reps per arm, vendor sampling, 512-token generations, best posture with `MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV=1`, greedy tape per arm: **128 -> 55.91** (55.88, 55.93) | **256 -> 56.98 (+1.9%)** | **512 -> 57.23 (+2.4%)** | **1024 -> 57.13 (+2.2%)**. Knee at 512. The default width reproduces the pre-door baseline exactly, which is what the bit-identity claim at 128 required. EVERY width produced greedy tape sha16 `9437b599f6b9d2a9`, so `hc_pre_rowsq_blockwide` is real in principle and absorbed in practice by the f32 narrowing — one prompt over two rounds, not a proof, and the argmax gate is still owed before a flip. READ THE SIZE HONESTLY: +2.4% is far less than the kernel's 2.82 ms/token share predicts. Working backwards, the kernel went from 31.1 us to roughly 26 us, so four times the threads bought 15% of it. Stages 1 and 3 scale with width and stage 2 does not, and the artifact's `hc_sinkhorn_iters` is **20**, so the fixed warp-0-only Sinkhorn cost is the likely floor and more threads will not move it. `box/hcnsys.sh` names the kernel under nsys at both widths and replaces that inference with a number. |
| `MEMRA_HC_PRE_SINK_REG=1\|0` | **ARCH-KEYED DEFAULT since 2026-09-04: ON on sm_100a builds (receipt: +7.86% alone at block 128, +10.25% with block 512, hcpreab 2026-09-03, tape `9437b599f6b9d2a9`), OFF elsewhere; `=0` is the rollback seam, read per call.** Was: OFF by default with absence as the seam. Runs the fused hc pre-chain's Sinkhorn stage in REGISTERS with `__shfl_sync` instead of shared memory. Rides the v3 kernel, so it is only meaningful alongside `MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK`. WHY, from two nsys measurements rather than an argument: the SAME kernel at two block widths on 2x B200 (2026-09-03, best posture) reads **128 threads -> 31.194 us** and **1024 threads -> 26.609 us**. Stages 1 and 3 scale with the block; stage 2 does not (warp-0-only at every width by the `hc<=4` invariant). Solving `S + P = 31.194` and `S + P/8 = 26.609` gives `P = 5.24 us` and **`S = 25.95 us`** — the Sinkhorn is **83% of the kernel**, i.e. 90 launches x 25.95 us = **2.34 ms of an 18.44 ms token, 12.7% of the token**, spent normalising an `hc x hc` matrix (16 floats at hc=4) for the artifact's `hc_sinkhorn_iters` = **20** rounds. It is not arithmetic: per round the shared path issues ~`2*hc` DEPENDENT shared loads per lane plus six `__syncwarp` and a shared `atomicOr`, on ONE warp with no other warp resident to cover the latency, so every round trip is fully exposed. **BIT-IDENTICAL BY CONSTRUCTION, and this is the design, not a hope.** `comb` lives one element per lane (lane `l` holds `comb[l]`, `l < hc*hc <= 16`) and every row/column sum is gathered IN THE SAME ORDER the shared loop used: `for k: sum += __shfl_sync(mask, cv, r*hc+k)` against `for k: sum += comb[t*hc+k]`. Same addends, same sequence, same running float. The stationarity test is the same bitwise compare with one `__any_sync` in place of the shared atomic. **So this is NOT a numeric class and carries no argmax gate**, unlike `hc_pre_rowsq_blockwide` in the same door family — a tree reduction would have been fewer instructions and a different association, and is deliberately not used. FAIL-SAFE: the launcher falls back to the shared path when `hc*hc > 32` (the matrix must fit one warp); at the `hc <= 4` the launcher already enforces that always holds, but it is checked rather than assumed. MEASURED, 2x B200 pair, TWO interleaved rounds, 20 reps per arm, vendor sampling, 512-token generations, greedy tape per arm, kernel named per boot by the `[hc-fused-pre]` announce: **base 55.94/55.89 -> 55.91** | **`MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK=512` alone 57.25/57.22 -> 57.23 (+2.36%)** | **512 + this door 61.56/61.59 -> 61.58 (+10.12%)**, the winning arm's rounds agreeing to 0.03 tok/s and its range (60.32-61.79) overlapping no other arm's. Registers on top of width = **+7.6%**, this door's own contribution. **EVERY arm produced greedy tape sha16 `9437b599f6b9d2a9`**, and the cell's own verdict reads "BIT IDENTITY both: HOLDS — the door's construction claim is upheld" — the same-order-by-shuffle construction holding on a real served tape, not a fixture. A THIRD arm (this door alone at width 128) read 55.88 and was VOID: the dispatch reached v3 only on width, so the door was unreachable at the default width and the announce said `kernel=hc_pre_fused_v2`; fixed in the same PR (v3 on EITHER door) and re-run at the end of the box chain. **DEFAULT OFF** still, by the new-flags law's letter: the receipt is one card class and one model; the flip needs the same receipt the MLA split door earned (x3 interleaved, sampled twin, both-arm announce), and this cell is x2. Flip needs an interleaved fresh-boot A/B plus a greedy tape (which must MATCH, since the claim is bit identity — a differing tape here is a DEFECT, not a class). Rollback: unset, or `=0`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_W8` | off | Strict `1` (unset/`0` = bf16): q8_0 mirror of the glm5_next KDA/MLA decode trunk's bf16-resident projections. NEW NUMERIC-CLASS door (int8 dp4a dot with per-32 scales vs the bf16xf32 fma chain `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows` runs — same class as `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8`), but its OWN independent boolean: not step37-family-armed, not gated behind `MEMRA_W8_HYBRID`. Reuses the SAME building block `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8`'s hybrid half already ships — the pointer-keyed `w8_mirrors`/`w8_act` caches, `encode_q8_0_from_bf16` + `build_q8_rp4_raw` at first decode use, `qmatvec_mmvq_into`/`matvec_bf16_via_q8_mirror(_t)` for the matvec — so this door adds no new CUDA kernel. Wired at the SHARED dispatch level (`matvec_bf16_rows_into` for m in 1..=32, `matmul_rows_exact` for the verify-rows m in 2..=32 tier), which is where `matmul()`'s FloatBf16 branch already routes every KDA/MLA projection call (`kda_core_gated`'s six-projection group + wo, `mla_attn_core_pre_wo`'s wq_a/wq_b/wkv_a + wo). Engagement is therefore NOT scoped to KDA/MLA by name — on glm5_next's actual residency census (the `MEMRA_BF16_MMV` acceptance row: 148 resident tensors per boot, 34x kda_q/k/v/out at 33.5M elements, 12x indexer.attn_q_b at 6.3M, plus embed/head members) it engages wherever a bf16-resident weight satisfying `in_f%32==0 && out_f>=64` reaches this path, which on this model is the KDA/MLA trunk plus any other qualifying resident tensor the same census names. `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ`'s bf16 arm (`qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32`) now also declines when this door is on (its bit-identity bar is against the unmirrored `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows`, so it must decline whichever door reroutes that target — same reasoning it already applies to `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8`+`MEMRA_W8_HYBRID`). BYTES/TOKEN ARITHMETIC (measured on the pair 2026-09-02, receipts below): bf16 = 2 B/weight, q8_0 mirror = 34 B per 32-weight block = 1.0625 B/weight (0.53125x). Against the nsys census this lane is scoped from (2x B200, GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4 mint, resident PP2, plain decode t=1): the BF16-resident KDA/attention trunk reads ~13.5 GB/token (211 launches/token); mirrored, that class projects to ~13.5 * 0.53125 ~= 7.17 GB/token, a ~6.33 GB/token reduction (~0.79 ms/token at 8 TB/s, against the trunk's ~1.7 ms/token share of the ~24 ms/token decode step). VRAM COST: the mirror is EXTRA storage, not a replacement — the bf16 original stays resident (every prefill/verify-arm class that is NOT rerouted keeps the arithmetic it was qualified against), so a mirrored weight's footprint grows from 2 B/w to 2+1.0625=3.0625 B/w. On the 34x33.5M-element kda_q/k/v/out set alone that is ~1.21 GB added (34 * 33.5M * 1.0625 B); the 12x6.3M indexer.attn_q_b set adds ~0.08 GB; any other resident tensor this door reaches (e.g. embed/head, per the census) adds the same 0.53125x its own bf16 bytes on top — the box run's `[glm5-w8] engaged` / `[w8-mirror] built` (`MEMRA_W8_TRACE=1`) lines are the receipt for the actual engaged set and total, not this arithmetic alone. COMPOSITION with `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8`: unrelated doors (different model families) but safe together — the shared cache is idempotent (keyed on pointer+shape), so setting both just serves both from one mirror. ROLLBACK SEAM: unset or `=0` (default) leaves every call site byte-identical to pre-lane behavior — a strict per-process `OnceLock` read, no persistent state. GATE: `glm5_w8_gate` (`crates/memra-engine/src/bin/glm5_w8_gate.rs`) — SYNTHETIC mode (no `GLM5_ARTIFACT`; safe on any card, incl. the 5090 rig): random bf16 rows at the census's representative KDA/MLA shapes -> q8_0 encode -> both matvec classes -> max abs error + argmax agreement over N random activations per shape (report-only; fails only on NaN/Inf or a wiring-sized error). REAL-ARTIFACT mode (`GLM5_ARTIFACT=<safetensors dir or .gguf>`; box-only, the checkpoint does not fit a one-card rig): fresh-boot-per-arm (self re-exec, so each arm reads the door's `OnceLock` from a clean process — the pin-against-truth law) 32-token greedy tape, door-off vs door-on, same prompt, argmax agreement reported. RECEIPTS (2x B200 SXM pair, GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4, PP-2 resident, 2026-09-02): real-artifact argmax gate PASS (32-token greedy tape, fresh boot per arm); 128-token greedy digits tape byte-identical across six boots; three interleaved fresh-boot pairs, vendor-default sampling, 512 tokens: plain code 45.0/44.9/44.9 -> 49.2/49.5/49.3 tok/s, prose 44.5/44.5/44.5 -> 48.6/49.0/49.0 (+10%); DFlash2 spec route code 77.6/76.2/77.3 -> 80.5/80.2/80.0, prose 53.8/53.5/54.2 -> 55.0/54.2/55.0 (+2-4%), spec engaged both arms. Source: darklanes research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md (14:27Z and 14:48Z entries) and floor/raw/ab-{plain,spec}-{allon,w8}-{1,2,3}. The door stays opt-in and model-signed: the B200 receipt does not authorize a global or cross-artifact default. See `research/b200-glm5-w8-20260902/LANE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS=1` | glm5_next PERSISTENT hc-glue DECODE WORKSPACE (lane/glm5-decode-diet lever 2, 2026-08-31): the T=1 hc decode walk (`hyper_range_decode`) lands its glue transients — mixes, Sinkhorn gates, comb, collapse y, both norm scratches, the per-site `hc_post` output — in one per-engine `HyperDecodeWs` (pooled on the Engine like `fa_part_pool`/`router_stage`; each ppN stage owns its own, allocated on its own device) instead of ~12-14 fresh `cuMemAllocAsync`+free pairs per layer per token of the census's 2,358-calls/token class (~2.5 ms/token of host time feeding the 43 sync-serialized drain cycles). The stream state ping-pongs against the workspace slot by OWNERSHIP SWAP (no copy), so the walk still returns an owned buffer and the ppN stage boundary is untouched. Same kernels, same call order, same operand bytes — every workspace buffer is fully overwritten before any read on every step, which is what makes reuse byte-identical. The mixes GEMM rides `linear_t1_into` (= `linear` at m=1: same cuBLASLt config, weight pointer, input bytes). Mixer/FFN/MoE internals are untouched by this lever. Announced once per boot (`[hc-decode-ws] engaged`), counted (`hybrid_forward::HC_DECODE_WS_DISPATCHES`); instrument `SCRATCH_ALLOC_CALLS` counts every `alloc_uninit`/`zeros` call for the per-step receipt. | **DEFAULT OFF, deliberately (2026-08-31, lane/glm5-decode-diet).** ON arm (gate RUN GREEN 2/2, rig 5090 TF32-off 2026-08-31, `crates/memra-engine/tests/hc_decode_ws_gpu.rs`): 24-step decode byte identity WS-ON == WS-OFF on the mini glm5_next hc fixture; scratch-alloc calls 76.0/step -> 48.3/step (-36.4%; -13.8 allocs/layer/step is the per-layer ratio that transfers to the 45-layer trunk, ~620 allocs/token class); compose arm WS+`MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE` byte-identical to both-OFF with both counters advancing (the doors share `pre_finish_into`). glm5 GPU suites green with the flag ON (tparallel/spec_session/dflash_session/mtp_head/hyper). OFF arm: the shipped allocating walk, untouched, and the rollback seam — read PER CALL. NOT default-ON: the alloc-call reduction is proven by counter receipt, but the ms/token value is arithmetic against the box's measured ~1.06 us/driver-call constant — nothing measured on serving hardware. Sync audit banked in the lane doc: the 42 remaining per-layer syncs ARE the router sel/w pinned-stage readback (`MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER`, one sync/layer already); killing them is the device-router-consumer arc, not this lever. Flip condition: interleaved x3 fresh-boot A/B on the serving card class (x5 on anomaly), greedy + vendor-default sampled twin, engagement announce in BOTH arms. Lane doc: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/decode-diet-20260831/LANE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_MLA_SEG_WS` | off | lane/glm5-mla-capture-20260904: the T=1 MLA core runs its PRE segment (q/kv projections, norms, latent splits, rope) into the SESSION's stable handoff buffers instead of fresh allocations, and MID/POST read them there. WHY: the decode graph leaves the 11 MLA/DSA layers eager (~600 of the ~1,890 launches per token, door-ON census 2026-09-04) because the append and the k-pool selection take the host-side slot as launch geometry; PRE and POST do not, so they can be captured, but a captured PRE graph and a captured POST graph cannot hand each other a fresh allocation (allocations INSIDE one capture are fine, which is how the KDA runs already capture). One set serves all eleven layers: they share one `MlaGeom`. A layer whose geometry differs from the set's refuses by name. EXACTNESS: the same kernels write the same values in the same order to a different address, and every consumer reads the value, never the address (door W's contract). Gate: `mla_gpu_forward` / `mla_decode_split_gpu` / `mla_tc_prefill_gpu` on the rig plus the box greedy tape. Read per call; announce once `[mla-seg-ws] engaged`. DEFAULT OFF until the capture lands on top of it. |
| `MEMRA_MLA_ABSORB_BF16` | **DEFAULT OFF** (2026-09-05), pending its model-scale row (new-flags law). `=1` makes the MLA decode `_wp` kernels (the served partition under `MEMRA_MLA_COALESCE=1` / `MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT=1`) read the absorb planes `wk_b` / `wv_b` as BF16 (`memra_mla_absorb_q_wp_bf16`, `memra_mla_decompress_v_wp_bf16`) instead of the f32 copy the loader materializes (memra#135: no quantized 3D layout, so `kv_b_proj` lands f32-resident whatever the checkpoint ships). WHY: 738 MB per token of plane reads on GLM-5.3-Flash (11 layers x 2 planes x 33.6 MB), the decompress kernel alone 34 us x 11 per token; BF16 halves the bytes. EXACT where it applies: the B200 hybrid mint ships `kv_b_proj` in BF16, the f32 copy is a widening, the kernel widens the same bits again, same products in the same order (gate `tests/mla_absorb_bf16_gpu.rs`: absorb + decompress bitwise vs the f32 `_wp` kernels at split 1 and 16, red arm). The BF16 copy is built at load ONLY when every f32 element round-trips (else `[mla-absorb-bf16] <plane>: ... f32 path stays`), so a checkpoint whose planes were dequantized from NVFP4 keeps the f32 path unchanged. Costs +369 MB of VRAM per device while the f32 copy stays resident for the prefill/TC paths. Announce once `[mla-absorb-bf16] engaged`; counter `MLA_ABSORB_BF16_DISPATCHES`. Rollback: unset |
| `MEMRA_MOE_SHEXP_OVERLAP` | **DEFAULT OFF** (2026-09-05), pending its model-scale row (new-flags law). `=1`: in the t=1 verify-rows MoE walk (`moe_ffn_sequential_zq8`'s vrows branch, the served path under `MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV=1`) the SHARED expert (gate/up fused, SwiGLU, down, copy into a main-allocated output) runs on `Engine::side_stream` under the ambient stream override (`memra_runtime::push_stream_override(side_stream, side_blas)`) while the routed experts' rows run on the main stream; forked with an event before `moe_vrows_pairs_q8`, joined with an event before the same `add_scaled_rows_ones`. `=2`: serial-join probe (fork on the side stream, main waits before the rows: stream placement without concurrency). `=3`: the fork program on the main stream itself (no override: the program without the placement). WHY: the critical-path pass (2026-09-05) shows one stream per device and zero overlap; the shared expert is ~17 us per MoE layer of independent work (42 per token) that today serializes after ~60 us of routed rows. EXACTNESS: same kernels, same operands, same add, so bit-identical by construction; gate `tests/glm5_decode_graph_capture_gpu.rs::moe_shexp_overlap_matches_serial_bitwise` (rig 2026-09-05: 16 steps bit-identical for `=3`, `=2`, `=1` against the serial walk, and graph door + `=1` against eager-serial over 32 replays with the self-check PASS; counter non-vacuity). TWO TRAPS the gate caught, both fixed in-lane: (1) the override must carry a cuBLASLt handle bound to the SAME stream (`Engine::side_blas`); with the main-bound handle a dense GEMM inside the fork issued on main, unordered against side producers, and every arm diverged 16/16 while every launch site read the ambient stream correctly (`MEMRA_EVT=1` hid it; a host `main.synchronize()` before the fork hid it; PDL off, pool cross-stream reuse off and keeping temporaries alive did not move it); (2) capture: nothing may be enqueued on the side stream after the join event (side frees or a replacement alloc after `record_event` leave unjoined nodes and `cuStreamEndCapture` fails `CUDA_ERROR_STREAM_CAPTURE_UNJOINED`; the door then latches eager, 0 replays), so every side temporary drops before `ev1`. Only the plain-add shape (no `gate_inp_shexp`, `MEMRA_HTOD_DIET=1`); other shapes keep the serial program. Read per call. Counter `MOE_SHEXP_OVERLAP_DISPATCHES`; announce once `[moe-shexp-overlap] engaged`. Rollback: unset |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_MLA` | **DEFAULT OFF** (2026-09-05), pending its model-scale row (new-flags law). `=1` lets the decode graph door (`MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH`) capture the MLA/DSA layers in HALVES instead of leaving them eager: the attention-site hc pre + norm and the PRE segment (q_a, q_a norm, q_b, split, rope, kv_a, split, kv norm, rope: nothing position-derived in the launch geometry) close the run's current graph piece; an eager middle (append, k-pool selection, attention core, decompress, `wo`) runs on the stage's own workspace; the layer's second half (hc post of the handed-over attention output + the FFN half) opens the next graph piece of the SAME run. WHY: the 2026-09-05 decode trace on the B200 pair puts ~0.65 ms per token of 3-30 us GPU-idle gaps in the eleven eager MLA layers (the GPU waiting on the CPU: ~600 launches per token from Rust and cuBLAS at 5-9 us of host cost each) plus ~500 in-graph-sized gaps; the halves capture ~40 of ~55 launches per MLA layer, expected ~+4% at c1 (bit-identical: the same kernels replayed). PREREQUISITE `MEMRA_MLA_SEG_WS=1` (the PRE outputs live in the session's segment workspace the eager middle reads back); without it the plan is the KDA-only plan and the announce says `MLA/DSA layers stay eager`. MECHANICS: runs become pieces (`RunPiece::Graph` / `Middle`), a run's span covers the MLA layer whole so the signature, phase and self-check bookkeeping stay per run; the stage owns a live `pos_d` refreshed per token before the first replay (the rope in a PRE half reads it; KDA runs ignore it) and a `mixed` handoff buffer; the self-check and the pre-capture warm walk snapshot and restore the MLA latent `len`/`len_d` beside the recurrent state (the eager reference appends too). Announce: the `[glm5-decode-graph] engaged` line carries `mla_halves=N`. Counter `GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH_MLA_HALVES`. Gate: `tests/glm5_decode_graph_capture_gpu.rs::graph_door_mla_halves_match_eager_bitwise` (fixture MLA layers 3 and 7; non-vacuity on the halves counter). Rollback: unset. Original OFF note follows. ON arm (gate RUN GREEN 3/3, rig 5090 TF32-off 2026-08-31, `crates/memra-engine/tests/mla_decode_split_gpu.rs`): bit identity split-vs-unsplit at splits {2,3,5,16,out_dim} x t in {1,2,8} on the serving geometry (64 heads, d_nope 128, kv_rank 512, d_v 128) and a mini geometry, including splits that do not divide the output width (tail guard); mutation reds (transposed per-head weight slab, input flip) move the output and stay finite; whole-model 24-step decode byte identity ON==OFF on the DSA-indexed MLA mini fixture with the engagement counter 0->100 ON / flat OFF. OFF arm: the shipped one-block-per-(token,head) launchers, untouched, and the rollback seam — read PER CALL. NOT default-ON: bit identity is proven, but the occupancy win is arithmetic (blocks 64 -> ~1024) with no throughput receipt — the rig is exactness-only. Flip condition: interleaved x3 fresh-boot A/B on the serving card class (x5 on anomaly), greedy + vendor-default sampled twin, engagement announce in BOTH arms. | `MEMRA_MLA_COALESCE=1` | **OFF (default)**; the rollback seam is its absence, read per call. Dispatches warp-per-row twins of `memra_mla_absorb_q` / `memra_mla_decompress_v` (`memra_mla_absorb_q_wp_f32`, `memra_mla_decompress_v_wp_f32`, kernels in `cu/mla_attn.cu`). THE DEFECT, which is ORTHOGONAL to `MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT` and `MEMRA_B200_MLA_DECODE_ARM`: those doors choose the output-range partition (the GRID); this one changes how a row is READ. The shipped kernel gives output row `l` to THREAD `l`, which walks its row serially, so at step `p` the 32 lanes of a warp read `w[l*d_nope + p]` for 32 consecutive `l` — addresses `d_nope` floats = **1 KB apart** on this artifact (`qk_nope_head_dim` 256), one sector per lane, 32 transactions where one would do. `memra_mla_decompress_v_kernel` is worse: the lane stride is `kv_rank` floats = **2 KB**, and with `d_v` 128 against `MLA_THREADS` 256 half the block never enters the loop. The fix is a WARP per output row with lane `k` reading `row[k], row[k+32], ...` (one 128-byte transaction per step) and a `__shfl_down_sync` reduction. MEASURED CONTEXT (nsys, 2x B200 SXM, GLM-5.3-Flash, current best posture, 2026-09-03): the pair is 70.8 + 70.6 us per launch x 11 MLA layers = **1.56 ms of an 18.44 ms token**; On the served geometry (`num_attention_heads` 64, `kv_lora_rank` 512, `qk_nope_head_dim` 256, `v_head_dim` 256, 11 MLA layers) the pair reads **738 MB per token** of f32 `wk_b`/`wv_b` (33.6 MB each per layer). At 8 TB/s that is **0.092 ms**; it takes **1.56 ms**, so **5.9% of roofline** — a 17x gap, and this access pattern is a concrete mechanism for it rather than another observation of the 11-34% band the in-tree matvec table reports. COMPOSES rather than competes: the dispatch asks `MEMRA_B200_MLA_DECODE_ARM` then `MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT` for a split and passes it through (`split == 1` = unsplit), so consulting the generic door also ticks its dispatch counter, which is correct — it WAS consulted and its partition IS the one running. **NAMED NUMERIC CLASS `mla_warp_row_reduce`, and this is the difference from the split doors**: they kept the one-thread serial ascending-index dot precisely so they could claim bit identity for every split value; this replaces it with 32 lane-partial sums (each ascending, stride 32) combined by a shuffle tree. Same terms, same values, different association. So it is NOT bit-identical and must not be compared to those doors' identity gates. MEASURED, 2x B200 pair, TWO interleaved rounds (`box/coalab.sh`, 20 reps per arm, vendor sampling, base posture including `MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK=512`): **off 57.34/57.19 -> 57.27** | **coalesce ALONE 51.01/51.13 -> 51.07 = -10.82%, a REGRESSION** | split alone 59.80/59.86 -> 59.83 (+4.48%) | **coalesce + split 60.96/60.90 -> 60.93 = +6.40%, i.e. +1.84% ON TOP of the split**; every arm's range clear of every other's. **AND EVERY ARM PRODUCED GREEDY TAPE sha16 `9437b599f6b9d2a9`, including coalesce**: the named class `mla_warp_row_reduce` (32 lane-partials + shuffle tree instead of one serial dot) did NOT change the served argmax over 128 tokens on this prompt. One prompt over two rounds is evidence the class is absorbed in practice, not a proof, so the class stays named and the argmax gate stays owed before any flip. THE MECHANISM, which is why the door now refuses to run at split 1: warp-per-row puts ONE row in flight per warp with a serial shuffle reduction after each, so at 64 blocks it replaces 16,384 threads x 1 row with 512 warps x 1 row — a 32x loss of memory parallelism that outweighs the coalescing outright. At split 16 (1,024 blocks, 8,192 rows in flight) the coalescing finally has parallelism to spend on and wins +1.9%. So the dispatch computes the split FIRST and falls through to the unchanged dispatch below whenever it is 1: the door is a no-op unless a split door gave it a grid. The remaining lever inside this kernel is multi-row ILP per warp (several independent accumulators per lane before any reduction), not more blocks. **DEFAULT OFF BY DESIGN** (new-flags law): non-bit-identical and one round, so it needs an interleaved fresh-boot A/B on the serving card class, a greedy tape, and an argmax gate before any flip. Announced once per boot naming the kernel and the composed split (`[mla-coalesce] engaged`), counted (`mla_ffi::MLA_COALESCE_DISPATCHES`). Rollback: unset, or `=0`. |
| `MEMRA_B200_MLA_DECODE_ARM=1` | B200 (sm_100a) t<=8 MLA/DSA decode arm, T-KEYED (lane/b200-mla-decode-20260902, owner order 2026-09-02 "hardly improve the decode on these cards, before the full 1M"). What it does: `memra_mla_absorb_q` / `memra_mla_decompress_v` / `memra_mla_attn_gathered` already grid `t_q * n_head` blocks (64 CTAs at t=1 on the glm5 geometry, under one wave on a B200 die); the arm swaps in the bit-identical-by-construction output-range split twins (`memra_mla_absorb_q_split_f32` / `memra_mla_decompress_v_split_f32`, MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT's kernels, plus this lane's `memra_mla_attn_gathered_split_kernel` in cu/mla_attn.cu, which reruns the shared score/softmax walk in full per split block and restricts only the per-l accumulate-and-write loop to an output range) at a split factor read from an explicit per-kernel, per-t_q table (`mla_ffi::MLA_B200_{ABSORB_Q,DECOMPRESS_V,ATTN_GATHERED}_SPLIT`, index = t_q in 1..=8; a cell of 1 = the shipped launcher, never the twin at split=1). Why a table: the first cut aimed at ~2048 blocks at every t_q <= 8 and the real box refuted it. B200 receipt (2026-09-02, 2x B200 SXM pair, `mla-decode-arm-gate` device 0, geometry nh=64 kv_rank=512 d_nope=256 d_v=256 d_rope=0 n_slots=2048 pool_rows=32768, N=5, every arm BIT-IDENTICAL): t_q=1 absorb_q 81.8 us shipped -> 49.1 split=4, decompress_v 82.2 -> 48.0 split=4, attn_gathered 564.6 -> 516.4 split=2; t_q=4 absorb_q 150.3 -> 133.2 split=4 (win), decompress_v 150.4 -> 246.6 split=4 (REGRESSION), attn_gathered 665.3 -> 822.7 split=2 (REGRESSION). t_q=4..8 is the DFlash2 spec-verify shape the box serves, so the shipped table is: t_q=1 -> absorb_q 4, decompress_v 4, attn_gathered 2; t_q=4 -> absorb_q 4 only; every other cell (t_q=2,3,5..8, and decompress_v/attn_gathered at t_q=4) = shipped, because those widths are unmeasured or measured losses. Checked FIRST in the three wrappers, ahead of the generic `MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT` door; a shipped cell falls through to that door (if set) or the unsplit kernel, so the two doors compose rather than race. Announced once per boot (`[mla-b200-decode-arm] engaged`), counted (`mla_ffi::MLA_B200_DECODE_ARM_DISPATCHES`). Investigation finding kept on record: the nsys motivation cited 321 cuBLAS gemv-class launches at t=1 attributed to `memra_bf16_gemm_sb` (batch=64, n=512, k=256); its only caller (`mla_tc_prefill_chain`, hybrid_forward.rs) is gated `t >= 16`, so no decode-time GEMM replacement was built (LANE.md section 1, open item for a fresh nsys capture). | **DEFAULT OFF (2026-09-02, lane/b200-mla-decode-20260902).** Gate: `mla-decode-arm-gate [device] [rounds=5] [max_kv=262144] [scrub=1]` (crates/memra-engine/src/bin/mla_decode_arm_gate.rs) asserts BIT-IDENTICAL output, shipped vs every split in {2,4,8}, at t_q in {1,2,4,8} x kv in {2k,32k,128k,256k} for all three kernels (lane/b200-mla-depth-20260902 added the kv axis); times every split in {1,2,4,8} at every (t_q, kv) in interleaved rounds (split=1 is the shipped launcher) with a 512 MB L2 scrub before every timed launch so each arm starts from the cold-L2 state serving leaves it in (`scrub=0` = the warm-L2 first-cut method); prints the per-(t, kv) winner table; and FAILS (`REGRESSION`, exit 1) when the serving table's arm is slower than shipped by more than 5% (`MLA_B200_ARM_REGRESSION_MARGIN`) at any measured (t_q, kv), so a box run either confirms the table at every depth or names the cell to change. | `MEMRA_B200_DSA_DECODE=1\|2` | B200 (sm_100a) t<=8 DEPTH-ORIENTED MLA/DSA decode door (lane/b200-dsa-decode-20260902; owner target 2026-09-02: 230 tok/s plain with the 1M window as the product). ROOFLINE FIRST, because it changed what this door does: the latent cache is f32 in this checkout and the DSA index list is shared across heads, so the gathered set is `2048 x 512 x 4 B` = 4.00 MiB per layer per token and is L2-resident. `memra_mla_attn_gathered_kernel` therefore is NOT bandwidth-bound: 726.2 us/layer against a 3.81 us f32-FFMA floor (190x) and a 0.52 us HBM floor (1390x), depth-FLAT because `n_slots` is pinned at the DSA top-k budget. Its cycles go to (1) 24 `expf` per thread per 8-slot tile where 8 distinct values exist -- 1.57M per CTA per layer, 98.4% redundant; (2) two barriers per tile with 8 warps and one CTA per SM to hide them; (3) a PV accumulate that re-reads every gathered row from GLOBAL, scalar, after the score pass already read it. Head-batching ("read each row once, serve all 64 heads") is the WRONG fix at t_q=1: it divides the only 64 CTAs the kernel has to save L2 traffic that is not the bottleneck. `memra_mla_kpool_score_*` is the depth-LINEAR half (`n_pools = t_kv / pool`; no score survives a decode step because `q_t` is new every token, so the full scan is required by the DSA program, not by the implementation): 167.2 us/layer at 128k against a 3.81 us FFMA floor (44x), ~14 ms/token extrapolated at 1M, which is the 31.1 -> 22.7 tok/s slide from 256k to 1M. The shipped decode config `tiled<64,1,1,1,16>` is BT=1/BP=64 with ONE accumulator per thread -- 2 shared loads per FFMA -- and ignores the only reuse axis the decode shape has, HEADS. WHAT THE DOOR DOES. LEVEL 1 (bit-identical arms only): `memra_mla_attn_gathered_dsa_kernel` keeps the grid, the MLA_WARPS-wide tiles, the warp-per-slot lane stride, the 5-step shuffle tree and the whole online-softmax fold, and changes only that each tile's KV rows are staged ONCE into shared memory with float4 loads (serving both the score dot and the PV accumulate, so the row crosses the L2/SM boundary once per head instead of twice) and that the 8 tile exponentials are hoisted into registers (24 -> 8 per thread per tile); barrier count per tile is unchanged at 2 (`__syncwarp` covers the staging, whose producer and consumer are the same warp). `memra_mla_kpool_score_dsa_kernel<H,RP,KC>` re-blocks decode scoring on (head, pool): one thread owns RP pools and ALL H heads with `dot[H][RP]` in registers, pool keys stream through smem in KC slabs stored transposed (row stride BP+1, conflict-free), and the q slab is read `float4` over FOUR HEADS at a time as a block-wide broadcast -- shared loads per FFMA fall from 2.0 to 0.156 at H=32,RP=2. Both are BIT-IDENTICAL BY CONSTRUCTION: the gathered arm reproduces the shipped operation order exactly, and the scorer keeps the `c`-ascending dot from +0.0f and the `h`-ascending head mix inside ONE thread with all six rounding steps spelled as explicit `__fmaf_rn`/`__fmul_rn`/`__fadd_rn` (the selection downstream sorts these scores with a score-DESC/index-ASC tie-break and ReLU makes exact 0.0 ties ordinary, so a last-ulp move is a different selection program). MEASURED FINDING THAT RESHAPED THIS DOOR, stated before the rest: the single-pass bit-identical arm is a LOSS (RTX 5090, `dsa-decode-gate 0 3 65536`, N=3 interleaved: 442-501 us shipped -> 480-545 us at t_q=1, 854-882 -> 1119-1154 at t_q=4). Both savings it was designed around were already gone -- nvcc had hoisted the loop-invariant `expf` out of the `l` loop, and the second `cache[]` pass hits L1/L2 so shared-memory staging adds a write and a read and buys back only L1 hits. BIT IDENTITY IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT on that kernel: the shipped fold is already at a local optimum inside it, which is why the depth win needed a named numeric class. It stays in the tree as arm code 1 because the gate measures it and that receipt is the reason the door does not select it. LEVEL 2 admits the WARP-ONLINE gathered arm (`memra_mla_dsa_attn_warp_kernel<J,JP>` + `memra_mla_dsa_attn_combine_kernel`): one WARP owns one (token, head, slot-chunk) and holds the whole kv_rank-wide accumulator in REGISTERS (`J = kv_rank/32` = 16 floats per lane), so every KV element is read from memory EXACTLY ONCE and consumed twice from registers (QK dot and PV accumulate share `kv[j]`), there is not a single `__syncthreads` (warp-local fold, 5-step `__shfl_xor_sync` butterfly so every lane ends with the sum and no broadcast is needed), and two `expf` per slot per warp replace ~196k per warp per layer; `chunks` is the occupancy knob the head axis cannot be, since t_q=1 has 64 independent (token, head) outputs for 148 SMs. It folds PER SLOT where the shipped kernel folds in 8-slot tiles -- same sum in real arithmetic, different rounding -- so it ships under the NAMED numeric class **`dsa-warp-online-f32`** with an ARGMAX gate, never as a bit-identity claim. **THE NAMED CLASS IS ADMISSIBLE AT PLAIN DECODE ONLY** (`mla_ffi::MLA_DSA_NAMED_CLASS_T_MAX` = 1), and that is a correctness rule the B200 run forced, not a tuning choice: at kv=131072 / t_q=4 EVERY swept chunk count (4, 8, 16, 32) moved 1 of 256 latent-row argmaxes (maxdiff ~1.7e-6). It was argmax-clean at t_q=1 in every measured cell (0 of 64, three B200 contexts plus five on a 5090) and clean at t_q=4 at 256k and 1M, but t_q=4..8 is the DFlash2 SPEC-VERIFY shape where a moved argmax is a moved draft acceptance. So the spec-verify batch never sees it: `mla_dsa_attn_arm_effective` demotes any arm >= 2 above that width to the SHIPPED kernel -- in code, not by table convention, and to the path that cannot be wrong rather than the one that happens to be bit-identical. B200 receipt (2x B200 SXM pair, sm_100a, device 0, N=5 interleaved, engine f3a0091cd, banked `research/b200-dsa-decode-20260902/gate-b200-20260903.txt`): at t_q=1 shipped 552.1-556.3 us, single-pass 571.5-573.1, chunks 4/8/16/**32** = 273 / 137-139 / 79.7-80.5 / **54.3-57.1** us, argmax MATCH at 128k, 256k and 1M; at t_q=4 shipped 641.3-667.6, **single-pass 616.6-618.8 (a 3.5-7.4% WIN)**, chunks 4/8/16/32 = 277 / 155-157 / 159-160 / 131-132. The arm code per width comes from the t_q-keyed table `mla_ffi::MLA_DSA_ATTN_ARM` (0 = shipped, 1 = single-pass, n >= 2 = warp-online with n chunks), shipping **32 at t_q=1** (10.2x on the shipped 552.1 us at 1M) and **1 at t_q=4** (the bit-identical single-pass kernel, so the spec-verify width carries no numeric risk at all), and 0 at t_q=2,3,5..8 because those are unmeasured and unmeasured behavior does not go on. THE TWO MACHINES DISAGREED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, which is the per-hardware-arm-selection law producing a real conflict rather than a hypothetical one: the single-pass arm is a 30% LOSS on an 82-SM 5090 and a 3.5-7.4% WIN on 148 SMs, and 16 chunks beat 32 on the 5090 while losing to it by 1.45x on the B200. That is also why `dsa-decode-gate`'s TIMING bar is hard only on an sm_100a build (the only hardware a 100a binary runs on) and prints as a DIAGNOSTIC elsewhere, while its correctness bars stay hard on every device. The scorer engages only from `MLA_DSA_SCORE_MIN_POOLS` (4096) up, where the block count can fill the die; below that the shipped dispatch's own measured crossover already sends decode to the reference kernel. Compile-time gated to sm_100a builds (`cfg!(memra_sm100_tcgen05)`); on 120a/90a/89 `mla_dsa_decode_level()` is 0 unconditionally, so the var cannot engage off-target. Checked FIRST in `mla_attn_gathered` (ahead of `MEMRA_B200_MLA_DECODE_ARM`, whose output-range split fights the same 64-CTA geometry more expensively) and in `mla_kpool_score`; a geometry the kernels refuse (rc 40020/40021/40023) falls through to the shipped path rather than failing the request. Announced once per boot (`[mla-b200-dsa-decode] engaged`), counted (`mla_ffi::MLA_DSA_DECODE_DISPATCHES`). | **DEFAULT OFF (2026-09-02, lane/b200-dsa-decode-20260902).** Both arms are an explicit decision, not an accident of implementation order: OFF because no B200 receipt exists yet, and level 2's numeric class would need one even if level 1 had it. Gate: `dsa-decode-gate` (crates/memra-engine/src/bin/dsa_decode_gate.rs) sweeps CONTEXT {2k, 32k, 128k, 256k, 1M} x t_q {1, 4} and (a) asserts BIT-IDENTICAL output for the single-pass gathered arm and the head-blocked scorer at every cell, (b) holds every `dsa-warp-online-f32` chunk count in {4, 8, 16, 32} (which is `MLA_DSA_ATTN_CHUNK_SWEEP`, so the sweep covers the shipped t_q=1 cell) to an ARGMAX gate over every (token, head) latent row plus a reported maxdiff and max relative error, (c) times every arm in interleaved rounds and FAILS (`REGRESSION`, exit 1) when the POLICY-SELECTED arm is slower than the kernel it replaces by more than 5% (`MLA_DSA_REGRESSION_MARGIN`) at any cell, and (d) prints a `note` naming any chunk count that beat the table, which is the edit a box run justifies. | `MEMRA_B200_DSA_SELECT=0` | **DEFAULT ON since 2026-09-04 on sm_100a builds (the `memra_sm100_tcgen05` cfg already gates the kernel's existence); `=0` is the rollback seam, read per call.** RECEIPT (darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`, onemsel.sh, 2x B200 pair, composed defaults, 1M context, four boots per arm, 256-token windows, against the banked band 46.20-50.24 from four identical boots): the armed arm reads outside the band. INERT BELOW ITS FLOORS by construction (65,536 pools = 262,144 tokens at `t_q == 1`; 262,144 pools = 1,048,576 tokens for the spec widths), so short-context serving is untouched and this default moves only the long-context shape the door was written for. B200 (sm_100a) EXACT MULTI-CTA DSA k-pool SELECTION (lane/b200-dsa-select-20260903). What it replaces: `memra_mla_kpool_select_kernel` grids `t_q` blocks, so plain decode runs the whole selection on ONE CTA -- 0.68% of a 148-SM die -- sweeping `n_pools` up to ten times (8 MSB-first radix passes, an optional unique-resolution scan, the membership count, the emit). It is depth-LINEAR in `n_pools = t_kv / pool` and it is exactly what the `MEMRA_B200_DSA_DECODE` lane's 4-7.6x scorer fix stopped hiding: measured 557.0 us per layer at 1M against a `n_pools * 4 B` = 1 MB byte floor, ~0.13 us at 8 TB/s. THE CLASS IS EXACT, NOT BANDED, and that is a construction rather than a hope: the emitted plane is a pure function of ONE 64-bit number, the `select_k`-th smallest order key `(desc32(score) << 32) \| pool_index`, which is a strictly decreasing injection composed with a UNIQUE index -- so keys are distinct, "the k-th smallest" is unambiguous, and reproducing it bit-for-bit reproduces the selection bit-for-bit. The parallel pipeline computes the same key and runs the same `key(p) <= thr` test. HOW, in six launches and with no grid barrier: (1) a 65536-bin histogram of the key's high word top half plus the finite count; (2) the same on its low half within the resolved prefix, which fixes `thr_s`; (3) a per-CTA count of the tie group and a rank locate, which fixes `thr_p`; (4) per-CTA membership counts; (5) an exclusive scan and the ascending emit, tail and -1 pad. Only 32 bits need a radix descent because the key's LOW word is the pool index, which is unique -- the threshold's index is just the rank-r smallest index among the pools TYING at the threshold score, and that is a count plus a scan. Ties are the common case, not a corner: ReLU zeroes every head whose query-pool dot is non-positive, so exact 0.0 ties are ORDINARY. Each pass ends with the LAST CTA to arrive running the epilogue (`atomicAdd` on a done counter plus `__threadfence()`), the standard two-phase-reduction idiom -- deliberately NOT a cooperative launch or a persistent-grid spin, so no occupancy change can hang a serving box. The two degenerate answers are copied rather than re-derived: `n_fin == 0` selects nothing and emits only the tail, `n_fin < select_k` clamps the rank so every visible pool is selected. Engages at `t_q <= MLA_DSA_SELECT_T_MAX` (8) and above a pool floor that is **KEYED ON `t_q`**: 65536 pools for plain decode (`t_q == 1`), **262144 pools for the spec-verify widths** (`t_q >= 2`, `MLA_DSA_SELECT_MIN_POOLS_SPEC`). For plain decode that is **a context floor of 262_144 TOKENS EXACTLY** -- and note that a serving rung CALLED "256k" is usually ~256_756 tokens = 64_189 pools, which is 2.06% UNDER the floor and engages NOTHING. A B200 cell was sized against that rung on 2026-09-03 and measured noise for exactly this reason, so every context figure in this row is an exact token count and never a "k"; the workspace is sized from the same `memra_mla_kpool_select_ctas` / `_ws_ints` entry points the kernels index with, so host and device cannot disagree about the layout. Compile-time gated to sm_100a (`cfg!(memra_sm100_tcgen05)`); on 120a/90a/89 `mla_dsa_select_on()` is false unconditionally. Announced once per boot (`[mla-b200-dsa-select] engaged`), counted (`mla_ffi::MLA_DSA_SELECT_DISPATCHES`). | **STILL DEFAULT OFF (2026-09-03, lane/b200-dsa-select-20260903), AND A DEFAULT-ON PR IS NOW JUSTIFIED ON EVIDENCE BUT IS A SEPARATE CHANGE.** The class is exact by construction, the gate is green with a red arm that fires, and the three-pair serving receipt below is the largest measured win of this lane -- so the case for flipping is made. It is deliberately not flipped here for two reasons. A default flip is its own PR with its own review, not a rider on a change that is already carrying a policy fix plus the evidence for it. Gate: `dsa-select-gate` (crates/memra-engine/src/bin/dsa_select_gate.rs) holds three bars. (a) EXACTNESS -- the `idx` plane must be BYTE-IDENTICAL to the shipped kernel at every score shape x context x width, and the shapes are chosen to hit the paths that decide the answer: heavy exact-0.0 ties, a plane where EVERY finite score ties, a sparse plane with fewer finite pools than the budget, and an all-`-INFINITY` plane. (b) RED ARM -- before any of that counts, the same pipeline runs with the resolved threshold deliberately lowered by one and MUST produce a different plane; if it matches, the gate exits 1 however green everything else is. The first run taught why the direction matters: a +1 bump silently MATCHED, because raising the threshold admits the next pool only if it ties, while -1 always drops the threshold pool itself. (c) ANCHOR -- the shipped radix kernel is checked against the in-tree reference selector, so the chain is pinned to the order definition and not to itself. Timing there, direction only under the rig law, by EXACT token count (t_q=1 / t_q=4): 16_384 0.20x/0.19x; 32_768 0.25x/0.25x; 131_072 0.90x/0.51x; 196_608 **1.13x**/0.64x; 256_756 **1.44x**/1.02x; 262_144 **1.53x**/**1.07x**; 1_048_576 **3.13x**/**1.95x**. That crossover is why `MLA_DSA_SELECT_MIN_POOLS` is 65536 and not the 4096 first copied from the scorer's constant -- which would have shipped a measured 4.6x regression at 32_768 tokens, caught by the gate's own regression bar. **The spec-verify width is what holds the floor up** -- and the B200 gate above showed it holds it far higher than the 5090 suggested: on the rig t_q=4 reached 1.07x at 65536 pools, on the pair it is 0.94x there and only wins at 262144. The door no longer engages uniformly across t_q for exactly that reason. KNOWN GAP, and be precise about which half of it this PR closed, because "keyed on t_q" names TWO different edits. The half that IS done: the SPEC-verify floor was keyed UPWARD here, from 65536 to 262144 pools, removing a shape measured to regress on the target. The half that is NOT done: the PLAIN-DECODE floor is still 65536 pools, so a real 256k rung (64_189 pools) still sits just under it and still engages nothing, while the rig measures that shape at 1.44x on the plain route. Lowering the decode floor would need its own sweep of the 32768..65536 band ON THE PAIR -- the rig's crossover did not transfer at the one point where both were measured (1.10x rig vs 0.94x pair at 65536/t_q=4), so rig evidence is not a basis for widening decode engagement on sm_100a. The timing bar is HARD only on an sm_100a build (the only hardware a 100a binary runs on) and prints as a DIAGNOSTIC elsewhere; the correctness bars stay hard on every device. **sm_100a SERVING RECEIPT, MEASURED, 2026-09-03 -- three pairs.** Main `3908a431` built fresh on the B200 pair, best posture, vendor sampling, 1_027_024-token prompt (256_756 pools), measured rep only, fresh boot per arm. 1M plain-route decode, tok/s: pair 1 35.17 -> 41.00 (+16.6%), pair 2 34.95 -> 41.07 (+17.5%), pair 3 34.72 -> 41.09 (+18.3%). **Median 34.95 -> 41.07, +17.5%**, and the OFF and ON spreads DO NOT OVERLAP (off 34.72-35.17, on 41.00-41.09), which is this lane's bar for a real win rather than a boot difference. In per-token terms that is **28.61 -> 24.35 ms, 4.26 ms saved**, against the rig-derived prediction of 4.19 ms over 11 MLA/DSA layers -- **the ratio transferred to within 1.7%**, which is a stronger statement about deriving a serving prediction from a synthetic kernel gate than about the number itself. Controls all behaved: `select=1 pools=256756 ctas=1003` on all three ON arms and `select=0` on all three OFF arms, TTFT flat at 488-496 s on both arms as a decode-only door requires, and the 66-token control unchanged across all six boots because it sits far below the pool floor. For where that lands on the lane's ladder: 1M decode went 22.7 base -> 34.9 with `MEMRA_B200_DSA_DECODE` -> **41.07 with this door on top, 1.81x over where the lane started**. NOT A DOOR DEFECT, recorded so it is not misread later: that run also produced a client `RemoteDisconnected` at 1M, and the cause was harness fratricide, not this door. The engine behaved correctly throughout (`SIGTERM: draining (1 in flight, deadline 30s)`); there was no panic and no defect in this path. HISTORICAL NOTE, kept because it explains why this row was written prediction-first: for part of 2026-09-03 the vast.ai account was out of credit and every instance was stopped, the B200 pair included, so the serving A/B could not be scheduled and the row carried rig-derived PREDICTIONS instead. That is resolved -- the three-pair receipt above is the measurement those predictions were waiting for. The KERNEL-LEVEL numbers in the ladder above remain RTX 5090 figures, which under the rig law are correctness receipts and a direction, and which -- because the door is sm_100a-gated -- were taken with the door unable to engage at all: that gate reaches the kernels through raw FFI. Read the per-`n_pools` ratios as 5090 ratios and the tok/s table as the sm_100a measurement. Both per-token figures are now MEASURED. 1M: 4.26 ms saved (above). Just above the plain-decode floor, measured end to end by the floor cell (`box/selfloor.sh`, two pairs): at **264_290 tokens (66_072 pools, just OVER the floor) the door is worth about +2%** -- deltas +2.7% and +1.2%, arms not overlapping across the two pairs -- and at **249_251 tokens (62_312 pools, just UNDER) the arms overlap and the two pairs disagree in sign**, which is what no effect looks like and is the negative control behaving correctly with the door armed. That +2% REPLACES the earlier "~0.55 ms/token, >= 1.5x" prediction, which was wrong in kind rather than degree: 1.5x was a KERNEL ratio, and at that depth the selector is a small share of the token, so it converts to a couple of percent end to end, not to anything like 1.5x. **KERNEL GATE ON THE TARGET, 2026-09-03** (`dsa-select-gate`, 2x B200 SXM sm_100a, dev 0, N=5 interleaved, binary built from main `3908a431`; receipts `darklanes:research/glm5-b200-20260902/box/selgate/gate-b200.{txt,full}`, driver `box/selgate.sh`). EXACTNESS IS PERFECT ON sm_100a: **40/40 cells EXACT** across mixed, all-ties, sparse and empty by t_q 1 and 4, the anchor against the in-tree reference selector is IDENTICAL, and **the RED ARM DIFFERS** -- so the gate is non-vacuous on the target and not only on the rig. Ladder, as `pools -> tokens: t_q=1, t_q=4` -- 4_096 -> 16_384: 0.20x, 0.20x; 8_192 -> 32_768: 0.25x, 0.28x; 32_768 -> 131_072: 0.67x, 0.35x; **65_536 -> 262_144: 1.31x, 0.94x**; 262_144 -> 1_048_576: **2.81x, 2.06x**. **THAT RUN FAILED ITS OWN REGRESSION BAR, and the failure is why the floor is keyed:** `REGRESSION kpool_select n_pools=65536 t_q=4: 311.6 us vs shipped 292.7 us (1.064x, margin 1.05x)`. A single floor of 65536 was chosen from RTX 5090 data where `t_q=4` at that point was 1.07x, a small win; on the silicon this door is gated to it is **0.94x, a 6.5% loss**. So the uniform floor ADMITTED A SHAPE THAT REGRESSES ON THE TARGET -- the door is sm_100a-only and its floor had been set by evidence from a card it never runs on. That is a policy-CORRECTNESS defect, not a missed optimisation, and it is fixed here by keying the floor: `t_q == 1` keeps 65536 (1.31x measured on the pair) and `t_q >= 2` takes 262144 (2.06x, the ONLY pool count where the spec-verify width was measured to win). Both are measured cells with NO interpolation; everything between them at `t_q >= 2` is unswept and unswept shapes do not engage. Note this NARROWS engagement -- it removes a measured regression and adds nothing -- which is why it lands with the evidence rather than waiting for a separate PR. Nothing here touches the 1M serving result: that cell ran `t_q=1`, and at 262144 pools the kernel is 2.81x at t_q=1 and 2.06x at t_q=4, so the door is good for both routes there. COMPOSITION AUDIT (2026-09-03, static; written out because a sibling lane's default flip was withdrawn for a compose-time defect no tape gate catches). Buffers this path touches: `score` (read-only, allocated and filled immediately before the call at hybrid_forward.rs:8658), `idx` (written here, allocated at :8673, consumed later by `mla_attn_gathered`), and `ws` (allocated per call inside the wrapper, touched only by these six kernels, never escaping). Checked conjuncts: `MEMRA_B200_DSA_DECODE` is the only door that meets these buffers -- `=1` may replace the producer of `score` (bit-identical scorer) and `=2` the consumer of `idx` (warp-online attention), both on the same stream in strict producer -> select -> consumer order with no shared scratch. `MEMRA_B200_MATVEC_ARM`, `MEMRA_GLM5_W8` and `MEMRA_B200_GEMV_V2` change the `mm()` that produces `q_index`/`head_weights`, i.e. the VALUES in `score`; that is input data, not a shared buffer, and the exactness claim here is conditional on the score plane handed in, not on how it was computed. `MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE`, `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ` and `MEMRA_GLM5_Q8_FUSE` do not appear anywhere in the kpool call chain (hybrid_forward.rs:8439-8690). `MEMRA_B200_PRIME_V2` arms 1 and 2 are the mHC PRIME schedule (hybrid_forward.rs:678-1405), a prefill mechanism, and this door is `t_q <= 8` only, so they are disjoint by width as well as by code path. Allocation: `ws` goes through `uninit_i32` -> `alloc_uninit` -> stream-ordered `alloc` plus `keep_if_capturing`, the identical contract `score`, `idx` and the sibling lane's `part_*` buffers already use on this same path, so `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` composes for the same reason they do. Streams: the engine has two (`stream()` and `copy_stream`, the latter only for MoeSlotCache weight prefetch); all six kernels launch on `stream()` and none of these buffers is touched by `copy_stream`. Slice arithmetic (the #114 failure mode): the kernels index only through `memra_mla_kpool_select_ctas`/`_ws_ints`, the same entry points the host sizes from, verified numerically across n_pools 0/1/255/4096..1M including the 1024-CTA clamp, with the emit's worst-case writes (pool slot 2047, tail slot 2050) under `width` 2051 -- and there is no Rust-side slicing of a device buffer anywhere in the path, so there is no `slice_mut` to panic. WHAT THAT AUDIT STILL DOES NOT COVER, stated rather than assumed: it is a STATIC audit, and no conjunct has been run in isolation against this door. The door itself HAS now been exercised end to end on sm_100a -- the three-pair serving receipt above is that exercise, and its `mla_dsa_select_announce` line only fires when `mla_dsa_select_on() && mla_dsa_select_engages(..)` -- but it ran in ONE posture, the full best-posture stack, so what is measured is 'the door composes with that whole stack', not 'the door composes with each of these doors independently'. Rollback seam: unset (or off-target build), read per call. Lane doc: `research/b200-dsa-select-20260903/LANE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_RECAPTURE=1` | When a captured `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` stage is INVALIDATED (its `cache.pos` moved, or a recurrent-state buffer was re-seated rather than overwritten), REBUILD it instead of latching that stage to the eager walk for the rest of the session. Default OFF. That default is a decision, not an omission: an invalidated stage falls through to the byte-identical eager walk, so the only cost of not rebuilding is that one stage of one session stops being graphed. Nothing is wrong, only slower. Box run 3 (2026-09-02) died in this teardown with `CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE` because it destroyed a stage's execs, freeing every buffer they baked, with a replay of those same execs still outstanding. The armed path fixes the ordering: drain the stream, THEN drop the stage, and refuse to drop at all if the drain fails (that case latches and says so). | ROLLBACK is the absence of the variable and it is the default. RECEIPT (rig 5090, `tests/glm5_decode_graph_capture_gpu.rs`, 2026-09-03): a captured layer's `ssm_state` is replaced mid-run with a fresh allocation holding the same bytes; the engine names what moved (`sig_diff=layer 0 ssm pair {0x304ae5000, 0x304af5000} -> {0x304ae5000, 0x304bafe00}`), rebuilds the stage, and all 16 decode steps stay bit-identical to the eager tape across the rebuild, with no driver error. The rebuild now genuinely reuses the stage's buffers (revuto MEDIUM on #116 caught that `reuse` had been hard-bound `None`, so the code allocated fresh while this row and the in-code comment both claimed reuse; the receipt above would have been read against a mechanism that was not running). The box has NOT retested the teardown on sm_100a, which is why the default stays OFF; `glm5-decode-graph-gate` arms this knob for its forced-re-seat arm, so a box run takes that receipt without an env change. Counter: `GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH_RECAPTURES`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_SELFCHECK_N=<n>` | DIAGNOSTIC depth of the `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` self-check (memra#131). Default **1**: the first replay of every captured run is compared bitwise against an eager walk of the same layers on the same state, OUTPUT and the recurrent STATE it wrote (conv_state, ssm_state, ssm_state_alt, and the ssm role parity) both, and the stage is latched eager on any mismatch; 1 is the fence and costs one eager step plus a state snapshot per run per session. `N>1` verifies the first N replays of each run the same way (so both ping-pong phases are covered from N=2) and every PASS/FAILED line names `replay=k/N phase=p`, which is how a divergence that starts past the first replay is located instead of guessed at. Never a serving value above 1: each verified replay is an extra eager step. Rollback seam: unset (=1). Receipts: box `selfcheck.sh` 2026-09-03 (first-replay output identical on 12 of 13 runs, tape still wrong: the output-only check could not see a wrong state write) and the `selfcheck2` cell in darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`. |
| `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH_SOLO=1` | **OFF (default)**; the rollback seam is its absence. At B=1 the batched hc decode walk (`hyper_batch_range_decode`) delegates to the solo walk (`hyper_range_decode`). | WHY: glm5 PP-N serving decodes through the batched walk, which is the ONLY hc decode walk it reaches and the only one with neither `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS` nor `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` — both guard `hyper_range_decode_eager`, reachable only through `hyper_range_decode`. RECEIPT that this is real, 2x B200 pair 2026-09-03: `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS=1` on serving moved 55.85 -> 55.96 tok/s (21 reps each, noise) and its once-per-boot `[hc-decode-ws] engaged` line printed ZERO times, so the walk was never entered. At B=1 the batch walk also pays a per-layer `h_row` alloc plus a `dtod_copy_view` of its single row. BYTE IDENTITY is the batch walk's own gated contract ("row b of a B-row step must be BIT-IDENTICAL to session b decoding alone through `decode_step_hyper`", `glm5-hyper-batch-gate`, red-armed with swapped-row and wrong-cache-slot mutations), so at B=1 the delegation is that contract's right-hand side rather than a new claim. `cache.pos` is untouched: the solo walk does not advance it either. NOT a default flip: it changes which walk serves, so it ships OFF until an A/B and a soak that includes boot churn. MEASURED, 2x B200 pair, four interleaved fresh boots, 21 reps each, vendor sampling, 66-token prompt, primitive 1 (`MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV=1`) armed in every arm: **off 55.92** (55.22-56.08) | **on 55.78** (55.06-55.92) | **on + `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS=1` 56.42** (56.10-56.57) | on + WS + `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH=1` see the graph row. The greedy 128-token tape is sha16 `9437b599f6b9d2a9` in the off and on arms alike, which is the byte-identity claim. READ THE ARMS THIS WAY: the delegation ALONE is neutral (-0.25%, ranges overlap), so its value is not its own speed; it is that the two doors it exposes now engage. Before it, `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS=1` printed its once-per-boot `[hc-decode-ws] engaged` line ZERO times in serving and moved nothing; with it, the line prints and serving reads 56.42 against 55.92 with NON-OVERLAPPING ranges (+0.9%). The decode-graph door likewise engaged in serving for the first time on the fourth arm (`engaged dev=0 stage=[0, 24) runs=6 captured_layers=18`, `engaged dev=1 stage=[24, 45) runs=6 captured_layers=16`). Expect the graph's serving delta to be SMALL and do not read the 31.3% gate figure onto it: that door's own row prices box take 13 at -31.3% with the full door but -29.7% with the device-table MoE arm alone, and that arm is primitive 1, already armed here in every one of these four boots. **DO NOT READ THE FOURTH ARM AS A RESULT: the graph door is CORRECTNESS-BROKEN on this path.** The fourth arm read 62.91 tok/s (+12.5%), and a follow-up decision cell (five greedy 128-token tapes per arm, two fresh boots per arm interleaved, identical solo+WS posture so the graph door was the only variable) found graph-OFF produced ONE tape sha16 across 10 runs (`9437b599f6b9d2a9`) while graph-ON produced THREE distinct sha16 across 10 runs (`346e967336339953` x5, `583b1627eb9f0f08` x4, `5ec4e68fd795f5a8` x1) and the text is garbage: OFF answers `'Print 1..400 space-separated.1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 ...'`, ON answers `'PrintiasArgsConstructorSuppressSupportSupportnings...'`. The door announced itself healthy throughout (`engaged ... recapture=false`, no `capture-error`, no `sig_diff`, no latch). So the +12.5% is VOID and was likely fast because it was wrong. This is the same 'plausible wrong answer, never a crash, never an error' signature the graph row records for its own takes 4 through 12. `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` stays default OFF and must not be armed in serving, in a posture, or in a bench until that is fixed; the engagement lines above are a REACH receipt for THIS door, not an endorsement of that one. |
| `MEMRA_MOE_EXPERT_RP=1` | **OFF (default)**; the rollback seam is its absence. lane/moe-expert-rp-20260904, memra#147. At resident-slab upload (`DevExps`, `hybrid.rs`) the three NVFP4 expert slabs of a layer are repacked ON THE DEVICE into the slot-major per-row layout the engine already names `QT_NVFP4_V2` (per row: slot g's 16 quant bytes at g*16, its two UE4M3 scale bytes at nsb*16 + g*2; `nvfp4_expert_split_repack`, the same bytes as tp.rs `nvfp4_matrix_v2_permute`; interleaved copies freed) and `DevExps::rp` is set. Readers are told `QT_NVFP4_V2` (`crate::rp_qt`): every expert dot (`expert_dot_g`, now carrying the row's slot count `nsb`) takes the V2 case on ONE shared pinned arithmetic core (`expert_dot_nvfp4_core`: inner multiply-add `__fmaf_rn`, outer multiply `__fmul_rn`, the original shipped bits; ptxas otherwise fuses per call site and the arms differed by 2 to 4 ulps), so the T=1 decode pair, the `_w4` twins, the verify `_rows` family and every other `expert_dot_g` kernel read one 16B window per lane-group at a 16B lane stride instead of five scattered 4B loads at a 36B stride; the grouped prefill (`moe_ffn_grouped_prefill_sigmoid` -> `moe_f16_grouped`) takes its existing `dequant_nvfp4v2` / `kq_fetch<QT_NVFP4_V2>` arms. WHY: root ncu at the served GLM-5.3-Flash geometry measured 24.97 sectors per warp global-load request on `moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8_w4` and 18.20 on `moe_down8_fma_q8_w4` (4 = coalesced), the largest row of the B200 decode budget; the same slot-major layout bought +21.5% wall on step37 TP-2 (verdicts-ledger `step37-140era-gap-attributed`). Resident-slab readers not yet handed the V2 qtype (pairs, sigrouter-dev, gemma4, frozen cache, grouped_resident_q8) refuse with a named error (`crate::moe_rp_refuse`) rather than read repacked bytes interleaved; `QT_NVFP4_RP` (the trunk's per-tensor planes) reaching an expert kernel is poisoned with NaN. Refused (announced once, layer stays interleaved) unless gate/up/down are plain NVFP4, no block-FP8 scales, and in_f % 256 == 0 (16B-aligned quant windows). Bit gate: `b200_matvec_bench` `w4_v2` arms plus `MEMRA_BENCH_DUMP` compare against the untouched-main outputs (0 diffs, both families). **VERDICT (box A/B 2026-09-04, 2x B200 pair, PP-2, c1 plain, vendor sampling, interleaved x3): bit-exact on the real model (tape `9437b599f6b9d2a9` on all six boots, prefill on the V2 arms with no refusal) and 1.3% SLOWER: OFF 66.50/66.22/66.40 -> 66.40 vs ON 65.32/65.53/65.52 -> 65.52. The rig structure predicted it: sectors per request fell, the DRAM fraction stayed flat, long-scoreboard stalls rose; the decode MoE pair here is latency/occupancy-bound with four groups per lane, so coalescing the weight side does not move it. KILLED as a c1 speed lever on this pair; KEPT default OFF as infrastructure: a slot-major re-mint (the owner's per-target mint) is read bit-exactly by `expert_dot_g`'s V2 case on the pinned core, and the bench dump compare is its gate.** Receipts: darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/ncu-rig/` (sectors tables), `LANE.md` (moerp3), verdicts ledger `glm5-b200-slot-major-expert-slabs-killed-at-c1`. |
| `MEMRA_BENCH_DUMP=<dir>` | BENCH INSTRUMENT (`b200_matvec_bench`), never a serving flag. Writes each family's raw f32 outputs (`gate_up_shipped`, `gate_up_w4`, `gate_up_rp`, `down_shipped`, `down_w4`, `down_rp` as little-endian `.f32` files) into `<dir>` so two BINARIES can be compared bit for bit, which the in-process shipped-vs-arm compare cannot do: it is how the split-plane core's pinned arithmetic was proven to reproduce the untouched-main bits (0 diffs, memra#147, 2026-09-04) after a shared source body alone still differed by 2-4 ulps at one call site (ptxas fusion is per site). Unset = no files written. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV=1\|0` | **ARCH-KEYED DEFAULT since 2026-09-04: forced ON on sm_100a builds (a SERVING flag there, the label below is history), OFF elsewhere; `=1`/`=0` override.** RECEIPT (darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`, t1devab.sh, 2x B200 pair, composed defaults, interleaved x3, greedy tape per boot): without the forcing the 11 eager MLA-layer MoE calls take the host readback (`[glm5-vrows-t1-deny]` x8 per boot) and read 79.60 / 79.81 / 79.31 -> 79.60; forced 84.11 / 84.19 / 83.29 -> **84.11 (+5.67%)**, tape `9437b599f6b9d2a9` on all six boots. Default OFF, gate harness only, never a serving flag. From 2026-09-03 the arm is keyed on an OPEN CAPTURE REGION rather than on the decode-graph door, because inside a capture is the only place it is required (a host sel/w readback cannot live in one). This knob is the cell that asks the first question alone. |

| `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_TRACE=1` | GATE-HARNESS TRACE for `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH`, never a serving flag (armed by `glm5-decode-graph-gate --trace`). The eager arm reaches those boundaries by SPLITTING its loop at the same run cuts (`hyper_range_decode_eager_traced`), which is a loop split and not a program change: the same kernels in the same order. | WHY IT EXISTS: box run 4 (2026-09-02) had the door running the whole walk cleanly — 564 replays, 2 captures, no `sig_diff`, no `capture-error` — and still produced token 0 at every step. A token stream cannot distinguish an all-zero hidden from a wrong-but-live one, and `nz=` does it on sight. Costs one `width`-float DtoH per segment per token; gate only, never a measured configuration. |
| `MEMRA_DTOH_TRACE=1` | GATE-HARNESS INSTRUMENT, never a serving flag (lane/dtoh-trace-20260904). Every Engine device-to-host wrapper (`dtoh`, `dtoh_i32`, `dtoh_*_one`, the views, pairs and pinned variants) prints `[dtoh-trace] <bytes> bytes from <file>:<line>` naming its CALLER (`#[track_caller]`), one line per copy. WHY: the nsys trace of the B200 GLM-5.3-Flash decode (2026-09-03, darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`, gaptrace3) attributed 3.4 ms of GPU idle per token to host re-fill after queue drains and named two blocking DtoH calls per token by size (4 B after `argmax_final_f32`, 2112 B after `moe_router_sigmoid_topk_f32`, each ~1.3 ms of API time) but carries no host stacks; this names the owning Rust line so the drain can be removed at its source. Default OFF costs nothing (one OnceLock read per copy). |
| `MEMRA_ALLOC_TRACE=1` | GATE-HARNESS INSTRUMENT, never a serving flag (lane/alloc-trace-20260904). Every Engine device allocation funnel (`alloc_uninit<T>`, `zeros`, `alloc_u8`, `alloc_u8_uninit`, `alloc_u32_zeroed`, the typed `uninit_*` wrappers, and the host uploads `htod`, `htod_i32`, `htod_i8`, `htod_u64`, `htod_bytes`) prints `[alloc-trace] <bytes> bytes from <file>:<line>` naming its CALLER (`#[track_caller]`), one line per allocation. WHY: the nsys trace of the B200 GLM-5.3-Flash decode (2026-09-03, darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md`, gaptrace3) counted 148,358 cuMemAllocAsync and 148,208 cuMemFreeAsync calls in 96 tokens (~1,545 pairs and 1.8 ms of host API time per token) and carries no host stacks; this names the Rust lines that churn the pool so a per-token arena can be sized from a histogram instead of a guess. Default OFF costs one OnceLock read per allocation. |
| `MEMRA_KDA_STEP_TRACE=1` | GATE-HARNESS INSTRUMENT, never a serving flag (lane/kda-step-trace-20260904, memra#131). After each sub-step of the KDA core (`proj`: input and the six fused projection planes; `conv+l2`: q/k/v after the decode conv and the l2 norms; `gates`: forget and beta; `scan`: state_in, core, state_out; `gated_norm`: gate and gated) one line `[kda-step-trace] t=<t> <stage>: name=<non-finite>/<len> ...` is printed, one dtoh per buffer, so a KDA step that returns NaN from a finite input names its kernel. WHY: memra#131 cells 8/9 placed the decode graph door's poison inside `kda_decode_cached` at layer 4 (finite normalized input, finite conv/ssm state, all-NaN mixer output after a capture), and cell 10 showed it rides the `MEMRA_GLM5_W8` path. Default OFF costs one OnceLock read per sub-step. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_SEL_LEDGER=1` | GATE-HARNESS INSTRUMENT for `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH`, never a serving flag (lane/b200-glm5-graph-20260902).
