Changes no kernel, no operand and no launch order on either arm. Module: `crates/memra-engine/src/glm5_sel_ledger.rs`. |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` | **DEFAULT ON since 2026-09-04 (lane/glm5-graph-default-on-20260904); `=0` is the rollback seam, read PER CALL.** Receipts, all on the 2x B200 SXM pair (vast 49626183), PP-2, the assembled serving posture (`MEMRA_B200_MATVEC_ARM=1 MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE=2 MEMRA_GLM5_Q8_FUSE=1 MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ=1 MEMRA_GLM5_W8=1 MEMRA_B200_GEMV_V2=1 MEMRA_B200_PRIME_V2=1 MEMRA_B200_DSA_DECODE=2 MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_GB=124 MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV=1 MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH_SOLO=1 MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS=1 MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK=512 MEMRA_HC_PRE_SINK_REG=1 MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT=1 MEMRA_MLA_COALESCE=1`), binaries built from main after #168, lane doc darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/LANE.md` (cells graphab, graphgates, census2): (1) c1 plain greedy, 512 tokens on `digits.txt`, interleaved x3 fresh boots per arm: OFF 66.40 vs ON **72.97 tok/s (+9.89%)**, tape `9437b599f6b9d2a9` on all six boots (= the eager walk), every ON boot `nonfinite-fails=0 output-fails=0`. (2) 1M gate with the door ON: prompt_tokens=1027024, TTFT 489.07 s, decode 45.08 tok/s, `engaged=5 warmed=12 pass=12 fails=0` (the 09-03 posture read 488.71 s / 38.83 with the door OFF; a different posture, so not an A/B, but the door does not regress the 1M shape and passes its own fences there). (3) DFlash2 spec route (`MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC=1` + the drafter): the door is INERT there by construction (the verify walk keeps `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_GRAPH`): two rounds `engaged=0 route-spec=13`, wall 89.85 / 89.76 vs 90.11 with the door OFF on 09-03, decode 93.51 / 93.86 vs 94.09: neutral, tapes correct. (4) 8-turn vendor-sampled multi-turn twin (`multiturn.py --corpus --chars 2200 --turns 8`, short sampled turns of 31..233 tokens, `cached_tokens=0` in this tool shape): OFF 65.69 / 66.82 vs ON 66.81 / 66.59 decode median, TTFT first 0.94 both arms: NEUTRAL, `engaged=17/16 warmed=12 pass=96 fails=0` per ON boot. The c1 gain does not show on short turns because the door pays a fixed per-SESSION cost (a capture per session, 12 eager warm runs, the N=1 self-check step per captured run: on the order of 0.1-0.2 s per request) before replay pays it back; that cost is the next lever (warm once per process, reuse captures across sessions), not a reason to stay eager. (5) Door-ON kernel census (nsys, 96 tokens): 1,893 launches per token of which 1,243 replay from graphs; each device is busy ~38% under the tracer and the remainder is the PP-2 bubble, so the next wins are fewer/fatter kernels in the eager remainder (MLA/DSA layers, the decode tail) and the batched trunk, which the door still does not cover. WHERE IT ENGAGES: the per-session SERIAL hc walk (`hyper_range_decode`), which serving reaches at B=1 through `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH_SOLO=1` (still default OFF, its own receipts) or without `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH`; the batched trunk at B>1 is not on this path and a boot on it prints one `NOT ON THIS PATH` line. Every refusal shape falls through byte-identically to the eager walk, and `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_SELFCHECK_N` (default 1) compares the first replay of every captured run against an eager step, output and written state, latching the stage eager on any mismatch. The OFF arm of the gate binary and the capture test now SET `=0` (unsetting would arm both arms). Priced on the pair only: on the SM120 fleet cards the door is exactness-gated (`glm5_decode_graph_gate`, `glm5_decode_graph_capture_gpu`) but its tok/s has NOT been measured; a deploy that relies on it there carries its own interleaved A/B, and `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH=0` in that box's serve env is the seam until it does. **Earlier status, kept for the record. STATUS 2026-09-04 (memra#131 root-caused, #168): the garbage output was a lazily built per-weight cache (the `MEMRA_GLM5_W8` q8_0 mirror, built on first decode use) being built INSIDE the capture, its quantize/repack kernels recorded and never executed; the door now warms every captured KDA range with one eager pass (state snapshotted and restored) before capturing, and the mirror builders refuse by name under an open capture. Box cell 12 on the assembled posture: 5/5 greedy tapes equal the graph-OFF tape (`9437b599f6b9d2a9`), 24 self-check passes (output AND written state bit-identical for the first two replays of every run), 0 non-finite, 0 output mismatches, 12 warmed runs. (The +12.5% below was measured on garbage; the corrected door's A/B is receipt (1) above.)** glm5_next WHOLE-RUN T=1 DECODE CUDA GRAPH, per pipeline stage (lane/b200-glm5-graph-20260902, owner order 2026-09-02 "hardly improve the decode on these cards"; target 230 tok/s plain = 4.35 ms/token on a 2x B200 pair). **Was DEFAULT OFF with the serving receipt OWED (2026-09-03); paid 2026-09-04, see the head of this row.** Box take 13 (2026-09-03) is a green GATE receipt (zero token mismatches, 64 steps x 5 reps, both runs). Its serving A/B ran and **did not engage the door at all**: six boots, `graph-lines: 0 latch: 0`, no `[glm5-decode-graph]` line of any kind on the graph-on boots. The numbers below are GATE-WALK numbers and are not serving numbers; do not quote them as one. Read PER CALL, so `=0`/unset is a live rollback seam, not a process-lifetime latch. WHY: the nsys census of a plain t=1 token on 2x B200 SXM (GLM-5.3-Flash NVFP4, resident PP2) reads ~2,900 kernel launches and ~6 ms of launch/gap at ~2.2 us each inside a ~24 ms token. That is ISSUE cost, and a replayable graph is the only mechanism that removes issue cost without touching a kernel. This family had no graph arm at all because the walk was not capturable: a capture region admits no `cuStreamSynchronize` and no pageable HtoD, and the T=1 walk carried 43 syncs and ~156 HtoD per token (`research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/host-audit-20260901/LANE.md`). WHAT IS CAPTURED: per stage, the maximal CONTIGUOUS runs of KDA-mixer layers inside that stage's `[lo, hi)` — hc pre/post glue, the KDA mixer, the FFN-input norm, the routed MoE, the shared expert. Those layers carry NO position-derived launch parameter (the conv ring rolls in-kernel, the recurrent state is addressed by pointer), so a run graph is position-INDEPENDENT: captured once per session per stage, never re-captured per token, keyed on `(device ordinal, lo, hi)` with the `cache.pos` it expects next (a rollback/reuse/prefix-restore seam moves `pos` and the pool is dropped, never replayed stale). The KDA recurrent-state ping-pong (`kda::kda_cached`, whose own doc says the host swap is "NOT capture-safe") is handled by capturing each run TWICE, once per ping-pong phase, and alternating the replays — no extra kernel, no copy-back, and the host fields are mirrored after every replay so a fall-back to eager, a prime, or a snapshot sees the pointers it expects. Capture uses `capture_graph_retained_nowarm` (RELAXED mode): stream capture RECORDS rather than executes, and the warmup-free contract exists for bodies with device side effects. Private hc workspace + private f16 scratch are owned by the pool and swapped resident around capture AND every replay; `vws_recycle*` retains into the graph's keeper while a capture is open, so nothing baked is ever re-issued to eager work. A captured run always takes the WORKSPACE form of the walk (`hyper_range_decode_ws_body` against the pool's private `HyperDecodeWs`) whatever `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS` says, because a capture needs stable operand addresses: the two hc walks are kept call-for-call in step and their byte identity is gated by `tests/hc_decode_ws_gpu.rs`, but the door makes that dispatch for the caller and says so here rather than leaving it to a counter. WHAT STAYS EAGER, and why: **every MLA/DSA layer.** `HybridModel::mla_attn_cached_pre_wo` derives `slot` from the host `layer.len` and the kpool derives `n_pools`/`select_k`/`width` from it, so its launch geometry changes per token — a captured node would replay last token's geometry. Also eager: prefill, the decode tail's logits DtoH (host sampling), the batched/exact-16 tier, and the spec verify walk, which keeps `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_GRAPH`. Composition with `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` is refused by name (a sharded KDA layer is not on this path). THE ONE PROGRAM CHANGE THE DOOR MAKES, and its identity argument: the routed MoE stops reading its selection back. The T=1 arm of door D (`vrows_t1_dev`) keeps `sel`/`w` on device, builds the pointer/scale tables with `moe_vrows_tables_from_sel`, and runs the verify-rows kernel pair at `n_pairs = n_used`. That pair is the per-row form of the fused epilogue, which is the per-token form of the sequential `qmatvec_expert_q8` + `ffn_act_lim` + `axpy_into` chain — same g-strided dots, same pre-clamped SwiGLU expression, same slot-ordered `__fmaf_rn` accumulation, same macro folds in the same places; the table values are term-for-term the host loop's (`base + ex*stride` exact integer arithmetic, the same macro planes, one IEEE-754 product for `w * macro_down`). THE ARM IS KEYED ON AN OPEN CAPTURE REGION, not on this flag (corrected 2026-09-03). Inside a capture is the only place it is required, because a host sel/w readback issues a `cuStreamSynchronize`. Keying it on the flag made the door change the program on every path where it captures nothing (the eager fall-through, a latched stage, a refused stage, the trace split walk), which broke this row's own promise that a refusal falls through byte-identically, and it confounded the door's two enablers so completely that box takes 4 through 12 could attribute a mismatch to neither. `MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV=1` forces the arm on with no graph, which is the bisect cell those takes never had. At t=1 this is the single-row case of the claim the vrest lane gated at t>=2. FAIL-CLOSED, and this is load-bearing: door D could ignore `promote_worker_h2d` because it required `t >= 2`; the T=1 arm carries that conjunct itself, plus every host-visible selection consumer (`moesd`, `hidden_trace`, `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE`/`STATS`/`WEIGHT_TRACE`/`INPUT_TRACE_DIR`, `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER=0`). PREREQUISITES, refused BY NAME with a once-note on stderr (`[glm5-decode-graph] eager: <reason>`): `MEMRA_HTOD_DIET=1` (without it the shared expert uploads a pageable `vec![1.0; t]` per MoE layer, which a capture region rejects), device sigmoid routing, no armed route/hidden observer, no NVMe worker H2D promotion, no cudarc event tracking (`MEMRA_EVT`), no TP shard, and at least one KDA layer in the range. FIRST BOX RUN (2x B200, int2 head `8c31be2f4`, real artifact, PP2 resident, 2026-09-02) exited rc=1 and found two defects, both fixed before this row settled: (1) the pool's recurrent-state pointer signature was ORDERED, but the replay mirrors `kda_cached`'s host ping-pong swap by design, so it read as \"moved\" every token and forced a re-capture per step on a session where nothing had moved — the pair is recorded UNORDERED now, phase-invariant by construction; (2) the re-capture path dropped the old `StageGraphs` (destroying the exec AND freeing every buffer it baked) while that exec's replay was still outstanding, which the driver reports as `CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE` — the stream is drained before the remove and again after. A capture failure now falls through to the byte-identical eager walk for the whole range, prints once, and latches the stage off for the session instead of failing the request. The gate FORCES the invalidation path (re-seats a captured layer's recurrent state mid-run) and fails as vacuous if no re-capture happened. SECOND BOX RUN (2026-09-02) confirmed the phase-invariance fix (the re-capture is now the gate's own forced one, 13 s further in) and isolated the remaining defect to the RE-CAPTURE itself, still `CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE`. Since cudarc's graph `Drop` discards destroy errors and this error is not sticky, the failing call is one this code makes and propagates — so every failable step in the capture and replay path now names itself before returning: `[glm5-decode-graph] capture-error: call=<which> dev=N stage=[lo,hi) run=i/n layers=[a,b) phase=p recapture=bool stream_capture=none/ACTIVE/INVALIDATED free=X/YMB ledger=bool err=...`, covering pre-begin-status, the synchronize, `cuStreamBeginCapture`, the capture body, `cuStreamEndCapture`+instantiate, `cuGraphUpload`, `htod_i32`, the `memcpy_dtod` into `x_io`, and `cuGraphLaunch`. Two variables were removed at the same time: a re-capture REUSES the stage's `x_io`, `x_out`, private hc workspace and private f16 scratch, moving them out of the torn-down stage BEFORE the value whose drop destroys the execs (the move does not free them and does not change their addresses, so the execs die while every address they baked is still live), and it destroys only the graphs, so a rebuild allocates and frees nothing of its own against the stream-ordered mempool the graphs' alloc nodes draw from and the rebuilt graphs bake the same addresses; and a capture body that fails now still ends the capture, so it cannot leave the stream ACTIVE for the next begin. THIRD BOX RUN (2026-09-02) showed the re-capture is triggered by the ENGINE on step 2 of the real artifact (the gate's forced re-seat at step 32 never announced itself), and that the failing call is in the TEARDOWN between the short re-capture note and the long decision line (synchronize, `stages.remove`, the exec drop) rather than in the capture, which stayed silent under full instrumentation. **RE-CAPTURE IS THEREFORE DISABLED**: when the pos or the state signature moves, the door prints one line naming the changed element and both pointers (`sig_diff=layer N ssm pair {0x..,0x..} -> {0x..,0x..}`, or `conv_state`, or a layer-count change), latches that `(device, lo, hi)` key, and runs the byte-identical eager walk for it for the rest of the session; the stale stage is left in the pool because destroying it is the call sequence that failed. That removes the failing teardown from the token path and lets the pair price the FIRST-capture case, which is the product question. Every fallible per-token call now routes through the same named-error wrapper as the capture (`alloc(x_io)`, `alloc(HyperDecodeWs)`, `alloc(F16Scratch)`, `htod_i32`, both `memcpy_dtod` legs, `cuGraphLaunch`, `alloc(out)`), and the gate takes `--trace` for one counter line per token. FOURTH BOX RUN (2026-09-02) is the first clean run of the mechanism — both stages engaged, no `sig_diff`, no `capture-error`, no latch, `replays=564 captures=2 captured_layers=34` over 47 steps — and every token was 0. The trace showed step 1 both capturing AND replaying its 12 graphs, so the capture step was not skipping its launch; the defect was the replay's OUTPUT CONTRACT, which was an ARGUMENT (the hc walk ping-pongs the stream state between `x_io` and `ws.xb`, two swaps per layer, so an even count was supposed to leave the answer in `x_io`). Whatever the parity is on this trunk, the eager remainder read a buffer the graph had not written, and an untouched `zeros(width)` reads exactly as the zero logits observed. The contract is now RECORDED rather than argued: the stage owns a third buffer `x_out` and the captured body ENDS with a copy of its live state into it — one memcpy node inside the graph — so `x_out` holds the run's output on every replay whatever the parity did. GRAPH-LAUNCH HEADROOM GUARD: below `GRAPH_LAUNCH_MIN_FREE` the range early-outs to the eager walk (`[glm5-decode-graph] graph replay suspended:`) — this route HAS a byte-identical eager twin, so it yields rather than failing the request.| RECEIPTS AND WHAT IS STILL MISSING: `crates/memra-engine/src/bin/glm5_decode_graph_gate.rs` (`glm5-decode-graph-gate`) runs 64 greedy tokens on each arm from one prompt and asserts byte-identical token ids AND identical per-(token, layer) expert selections and routing weights (weights compared on their BITS), refusing to pass if the door never replayed. BOX TAKE 13 (2x B200, 2026-09-03, 64 steps x 5 reps interleaved) is GREEN: **zero token mismatches on both runs**. Run B, full door: `replays=564 captures=2 captured_layers=34`, eager median 30.655 ms/token against graph 21.068, **-31.28% ms/token (+45.5% tok/s in the gate walk)**. Run N (`MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_NO_CAPTURE=1`, device arm forced on, `replays=0 captures=0`): eager 30.567 against 21.477, -29.74%. **THE SPLIT IS THE POINT: the device-table MoE arm (no host router readback, no per-layer device-wide drain) is ~93% of the prize, and the capture and replay on top of it buys 21.48 to 21.07 ms, about 2%.** The door's value is the drains it removes; the replay is the small tail. That also means the device arm is worth considering as its own serving flag on ranges where capture refuses, which is a separate decision and is NOT made by this row. Counters: `GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH_REPLAYS` / `_CAPTURES` / `_LAYERS`. WHY THE SERVING A/B MEASURED NOTHING, and it is a defect in this door's reach rather than in the run: `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` is wired into `hybrid_forward::hyper_range_decode`, the per-session SERIAL hc walk that `decode_step_hyper` / `decode_step_hyper_ppn` run. Serving with `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH=1` routes every session, including B=1, through `decode_batch::decode_step_batch_hyper` instead, whose trunk is `decode_batch_layers`. The gate binary calls `decode_step`, so it armed the door fine; the server never reached it. Take 13's serving medians (43.7/43.2/42.6 code and 43.2/43.3/43.2 prose graph-off against 43.4/43.1/43.8 and 43.2/42.5/43.1 graph-on) are therefore a measurement of the door's ABSENCE on both arms, and say nothing about its serving value in either direction. `decode_step_batch_hyper` now prints `[glm5-decode-graph] NOT ON THIS PATH: ...` once when the door is set on the batched walk, because a door that silently does nothing in serving is the same failure class as a vacuous gate. STILL OWED before any default flip: a serving A/B that engages the door (unset `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH` to price the serial walk, or extend capture to the batched walk in its own lane), on hardware that currently does not exist (the vast.ai account is out of credit and every instance including the B200 pair was stopped 2026-09-03). ROOT CAUSE OF TAKES 4 THROUGH 12, and the reason take 13 is green: the ping-pong `phase` was held on the STAGE and flipped after every run's launch, but the parity it selects is a property of the TOKEN. `kda::kda_cached` swaps `ssm_state`/`ssm_state_alt` once per KDA layer per token, so every run of a stage sits at the same parity and each advances by one per token. One stage-level counter flipped once per run made run 0 replay phase 0 (correct), run 1 replay phase 1 while its layers were still at phase 0, run 2 phase 0 again. A wrong-phase graph reads the buffer the eager walk wrote this token and writes the one it read: a plausible wrong answer, never a crash, never an error, and invisible at one run per stage. `phase` now lives on `RunGraph`. Two guards landed with the fix: `moe_router_sigmoid_topk_host` REFUSES inside a capture region (under `cudaStreamCaptureModeRelaxed` its DtoH is recorded and not executed, so the call returns success, the pinned stage keeps stale bytes, and the caller routes on uninitialised memory whose expert ids index the slab out of range, with the garbage pointers baked into the graph), and `[glm5-vrows-t1-deny]` prints every conjunct of the T=1 predicate when the arm stands down, because `arm=host` is the SEQUENTIAL loop's label while the verify-rows host arm prints `arm=vrows-host` and reading the two as one cost several takes. SM120 HISTORY, and why B200 is a different question: `MEMRA_STEP_TP_GRAPH` re-measured **-20%** on the SM120 stack 2026-08-25 (row below), and the reason is stated there — its remainder was dependency LATENCY, 405 small serialized kernels that cannot fill the device, which graph replay cannot recover. The B200 census reads the opposite shape: ~2,900 launches at ~2.2 us of GAP inside a 24 ms token is ~25% of the token spent NOT executing, on a card whose per-launch floor is a larger share of a smaller kernel. The two are only comparable through the launch-floor share, so this door must be priced on the pair with its own interleaved A/B and NOT inherited from that verdict in either direction. FLIP CONDITION: interleaved x3 fresh-boot A/B on 2x B200 (x5 on anomaly), greedy identity AND the vendor-default sampled twin, engagement announce in both arms. Lane doc: `research/b200-glm5-graph-20260902/LANE.md`. |

The FFN-input twin is `MEMRA_GLM5_Q8_FUSE`. WHY: the door-ON census on the 2x B200 pair (2026-09-04) reads 47 `rms_norm_f32_v2` -> `quantize_q8_1` pairs per token feeding the W8 q8 walks, 34 of them the KDA attention norm; the pair costs a launch, a gap and a re-read of `h` per layer, and the fused form reads `y` once for both views. EXACTNESS by construction: `rms_norm_zq8_f32` is `rms_norm` then `quantize_q8_1` bitwise (its header), and the launcher's `quantize_q8_1_into` is `quantize_q8_1` verbatim, so the six projections see the same bytes; MLA layers and sharded KDA layers keep the plain norm. Read per call; announce once `[glm5-q8-fuse-attn] engaged`. DEFAULT OFF until the pair prices it. |

FORM CHOICE stated for the record: the dual-form MLA law (materialized prefill / absorbed decode) is about DENSE prefill; glm5_next's DSA caps every query at topk+tail rows and selects ONE list per query SHARED ACROSS ALL 64 HEADS, which makes the ABSORBED form the GEMM-shaped one (head axis = MMA m, shared latent rows = one B operand per tile) — FlashMLA's own sparse-prefill geometry — while materializing K/V would give every head its own K plane and destroy exactly that sharing. CAP 15 IS DERIVED, not inherited (owner challenge 2026-08-28 "why 8?"): the term-by-term width audit (`hyper_batch_cap()` doc) found exactly one numeric-class knee — the MoE shared-expert trio's `verify_t = t < PRIME_MIN_T` branch crosses onto the prefill matmul class at t=16, breaking per-row bit-identity (gate knee probe: B=15 green, B=16 forced = mismatch from the first tick, `31-KNEE-b16-forced.log`); per-session mixers/state, hc glue, router, experts and head are width-safe to 64+, so widening past 15 is a named follow-up (decode-exact shexp arm for t>=16, NOT flippable inside the shared `!prefill` branch that also carries step35 MoESD bytes). `MEMRA_DECODE_BATCH_CAP` narrows only. Unset/`0` = eager per-session decode (the current serving behavior and the rollback seam); any OTHER value REFUSES LOUD at first use | **DEFAULT ON (2026-08-31, flip condition MET — the hbatch-battery box receipts, research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/hbatch-battery-20260831/): interleaved x3 ladder (amended law; spreads 0.031-0.188%, no escalation) on the deployed 3-card recipe with the real artifact — ON wins every rung c>=2 (aggregate 30.24->34.73 at c=4, 31.94->38.78 at c=12 = 1.214x, plateau ~1.20x from c=8), B=1 cost -0.30%, TTFT-under-load ON <= OFF everywhere, p95==p50 both arms, vendor-default sampled twin tracks greedy per rung, 8-turn twins identical, 36/36 concurrent tapes byte-identical to solo, admission clean to c=20 (16 admit + 4 FIFO), loop-law 0/448. `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH=0` = the rollback seam (eager per-session decode). Serving caveat: pin MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS<=15 or a multiple of the cap (16 = a 15+1 chunk split, unmeasured steady state).** Was DEFAULT OFF at ship (2026-08-28). ON arm exactness: `glm5-hyper-batch-gate` bit-identity green through B=15 — every row of every B-row tick identical to that session's isolated serial `decode_step_hyper` tape (full-logit bar, staggered depths, B=1 class pin, device-sample greedy arm), red-armed with swapped-row and wrong-cache-slot mutations (re-proven at the cap width) plus the forced B=16 knee. NOT default-ON because it has NO serving-box receipt: the rig is exactness-only by law, so no aggregate-tok/s claim exists for the ON arm yet. Flip condition: an interleaved x5 A/B sweeping c=1..32+ (the scheduler chunks c>15 into <=15 waves; high-concurrency aggregate is what the 4-card box buys) on the box with the real GLM-5.3-Flash artifact under the deployed PP placement, plus the vendor-default sampled twin and the 8-turn cache-on twin. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/batched-decode-gate/` |
| `MEMRA_QKV_APPEND=0` | fused-norm-rope + standalone-append pair (the exact-oracle bisect arm) instead of the m=1 norm+rope+append fold | fold default-on 2026-07-23 (`lib.rs qkv_append_on()`) |
| `MEMRA_PDL_NVFP4=0` | revert the PDL wave that covers the gemma NVFP4mix decode chain's hot kernels (nvfp4 fused2/mr2 + the q8_0 `_rp` singles) alone — scheduling-only, bit-identical by construction | gap-diagnosis arc verdict 8 (`lib.rs pdl_nvfp4q8_on()`) |
| `MEMRA_G4_PNFOLD=0` | restore the unfused two-launch rms chain (single-phase `rms_pre_add_scale_rms_norm_q8_1` fold off) everywhere. BITS-CHANGING between seam values (single-phase reduction's expansion rounding, E4B receipts); decode == verify == graph parity holds by construction within either value | `lib.rs g4_pnfold_on()` |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_FUSED2=0` / `MEMRA_NVFP4_FUSED4=0` | separate per-tensor NVFP4 matvec launches instead of the fused pair/quad twins. Widths: m=1 rides the original fused kernel, m=2..8 the batched fused twin (lane/samplat), and m=9..=16 delegates to the group3/group4 doors (lane/orndecode2 — `MEMRA_TK_FA_GROUP`/`MEMRA_TK_GDN_GROUP` are that class's own kill switches; `FUSED4=0` returns the quad to singles at every m) | `lib.rs matmul_nvfp4_fused2/4`; the e4m3 twin of this seam is `MEMRA_E4M3_DUAL` (§2) |
| `MEMRA_Q80_G2=0` | one-row-per-block q8_0 `_rp` matvec instead of the `_rp_g2` 2-rows/block twin (engages at m=1, out_f>=64, when the halved grid still fills the SMs) — bit-identical | `lib.rs` matmul dispatch |
| `MEMRA_F16OUT=0` | separate epilogue launches instead of the fused fp16-operand `_f16out` twins (silu_mul / gated_rmsnorm / sig_mul) on the f16 prefill lane. Engages only at t>=16 with `MEMRA_PP_F16`-class mirrors live; auto-off under verify-exact. Bit-identical class (the twins emit the cvt kernel's exact halves) | task #17 (`hybrid_forward.rs f16out_on()`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_EMIT=0` | f32 Q/K/V operands into prefill FA instead of the bf16 emit-straight-from-norm staging (t>=16; island/vision primes keep f32 regardless — the emit path has no island consumer) | `hybrid_forward.rs` emit gate |
| `MEMRA_FA_BF16KV=0` | in-kernel scalar K/V staging in the hd128/hd256 prefill FA instead of pre-converted bf16 mirrors — BIT-IDENTICAL (same `__float2bfloat16` values into the same mma); the pre-convert turned the 67%-of-stalls scalar staging into int4 vector copies | default-on 2026-07-26 (`lib.rs fa_prefill`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_VL=0` | per-seq prefill FA loop instead of the varlen batched arm on the batched-prefill path (engages at b=2..8, hd 128/256, FusedQ attention-gate class, fresh prompts) | `hybrid_forward.rs` (`use_favl`) |
| `MEMRA_FAW_P1=0` | SWA windowed prefill FA back to the g4/o2 arms (P1 = per-head Br=64 stamp with the FA2 schedule + boundary/interior mask split; FP order preserved → bit-identical, gated) | P1 default-on 2026-07-22 engine study (`lib.rs fa_prefill_w`) |
| `MEMRA_FAW_G4=0` | per-head SWA stamp instead of the MQA 4-heads/CTA staged-K/V-sharing twin (n_head_kv==1, n_head%4==0 class; per-(head,row) FP chain identical → bit-identical, gated) | `lib.rs` |

The `2` arm (w4a8v2 2-CTA/SM occupancy mode, net flat) was removed 2026-09-05 | 2026-07-09 |

## 4. Diagnostics & test config

| flag | what it does |
|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_CKPT_GATE=1` | dspark route only: run BOTH partial-accept restore arms (column-stash + snapshot-replay oracle) per partial round and byte-compare the resulting cache state — the same-binary exactness gate for `MEMRA_DSPARK_CKPT` |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_ANATOMY=1` | eager MTP draft-step phase timers (glue/attn/ffn/head, cumulative stderr summary every 128 steps). Phase boundaries sync the stream so absolute time inflates — the BREAKDOWN is the signal. Diagnostic only (lane/draftcost-moe, 2026-08-20; row backfilled by lane/dspark-sampled-admission after the flags census flagged the drift) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_STATS=1` | per-layer expert-cache hit/miss/staged-bytes prints (forces the stats-visible dispatch path) |
| `MEMRA_HC_GATE_ITERS` | `hc-fused-gate` ONLY, never read by the engine or the serving path: overrides the GLM-5.3-Flash `hc_sinkhorn_iters` (20) used by that gate's arms. Timing the same kernel at several iteration counts separates the Sinkhorn's serial per-iteration cost (the slope) from stages 1 and 3 plus launch (the intercept). That split is what decides whether `dsv4_hc_pre_fused` is worth restructuring: on the B200 hybrid mint it is 10.5% of decode (90 launches/token at 12.7 us) and ncu puts it on ONE block at 8.33% achieved occupancy, about 0.05% of the machine, because its grid is the SEQUENCE LENGTH and decode has s=1. The gate's correctness arms still run at whatever value is set, so a non-default value must still be bit-identical across the unfused, fused(=1) and fused(=2) arms. Invalid or <1 falls back to 20. WHAT IT ALREADY SETTLED: the served arm (v3, `sink_reg=1`, block 512) costs **26 ns per iteration** -- 90 us at 1 iteration, 91 at 40 -- so the Sinkhorn is ~0.5 us of a 12.7 us kernel and is NOT the cost. The ~700 ns/iteration that first looked like the target belongs to the v2 kernel's shared-memory Sinkhorn at block 128, which the served path does not take. The remaining ~12.2 us is stages 1 and 3 moving 130 KB on ONE block (~11 GB/s) because the grid is the sequence length |
| `MEMRA_HC_BW_PROBE` | `hc-fused-gate` ONLY, never read by the engine: set to a size in MiB to measure this part's ACHIEVABLE streaming bandwidth and exit. Uses `dsv4_hc_collapse_kernel` at hc=4 (reads 4*d floats, writes d, one pass, fully coalesced, grid d/256) as the cleanest streamer available without adding a kernel. Exists because every decode matvec on the pair tops out near 2.2 TB/s while the roofline argument for the 230 tok/s target was written against a NOMINAL 8 TB/s — a spec sheet, not a measurement. Calibrate before quoting a percentage of peak |
| `MEMRA_HC_PHASE_STAMPS=1` | gate-binary (`hc-fused-gate`) arm, never read by the engine: launches the BENCH-ONLY phase-stamped twin of the served hc pre-chain kernel (`dsv4_hc_pre_fused_v3_stamped_kernel`, `%globaltimer` ns + `clock64` cycles written by thread 0 at six boundaries) N=60 times at the served config (sink_reg=1, block 512) and prints the median per phase (`[v3-phases]` lines: loads+dfma, block_sum+rsq, warp-0 gates+softmax+Sinkhorn, block barrier, combine+store). WHY: the phase split is what designs the next kernel, and it does not transfer across cards (FP64 is 1/64 rate on the 5090 and near full rate on the B200: P0 was 61% on the rig and 20% on the B200; the warp-0 chain 16% vs 48%). Receipts: darklanes `research/glm5-b200-mint-20260904/LANE.md` |
| `MEMRA_Q8_CENSUS=1` | diagnostics: attribute every `quantize_q8_1` launch to its Rust CALL SITE (`#[track_caller]`) and print a cumulative histogram to stderr every 256 calls (`=<n>`: every n; `[q8-census]` lines: count, share, file:line, m, in_f); all three wrappers are hooked (`quantize_q8_1`, `_view`, the slot-fed `_into`). The threshold is low on purpose: under the decode graph door the walk's Rust call sites run once per CAPTURE and replay from the graph afterwards, so per-token counts never accumulate -- the census reads call SITES and their multiplicity per capture (a 4096 threshold printed nothing on the 2026-09-05 box boot). WHY: the mint decode census counts 327 `quantize_q8_1` launches per token -- 2.0 us each, 0.65 ms, 5.9% of decode -- across 109 call sites, and nsys names the kernel, not the caller. The only way to choose which PRODUCER to fuse the quantize into (the `rms_norm_zq8` pattern) is to know which sites fire and how often. Zero cost when unset beyond one OnceLock read per call; never set in serving |
| `MEMRA_HC_PRE_ZQ8` | **DEFAULT OFF** (2026-09-05), pending its model-scale row (new-flags law). `=1` runs each hc site's pre-chain AND the `rms_norm_zq8` that consumes its output as ONE launch (`dsv4_hc_pre_zq8_kernel`, launcher `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_zq8`). WHY: at decode both kernels are one block per position and both are the starved shape the ncu census names (~4 active warps per scheduler, 88% of cycles with nothing to issue); the norm reads exactly what the pre-chain wrote and nothing else reads `y` (served ws walk, both sites), so the second launch -- 79 per token at 6.7 us in the mint census -- is pure structure. EXACTNESS: stages 1-3 are the v3 body VERBATIM (generated from it) and the norm is `rms_norm_zq8_f32_v2` with its own block width (`rms_block()`) substituted for blockDim everywhere, so the per-thread partition, the shuffle tree, the cross-warp reduce and the q8 epilogue are the same statements in the same order; the norm's epsilon is passed separately (rms_eps, not the hc chain's). Engages only where the walk already fuses the norm's quantize (`MEMRA_GLM5_Q8_FUSE` / `_ATTN`), because that is the pair it replaces; refuses (falls back to the two launches) when `rms_block()` exceeds `MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK` or the fused pre arm is not V2. Announce once `[hc-pre-zq8] engaged`. Rollback: unset. MODEL-SCALE ROW (2026-09-05, full B200 hybrid mint, PP-2 pair, six boots interleaved, 21 samples each, engaged receipt in every ON boot log): off 80.35 / 80.55 / 80.35, on 80.32 / 80.42 / 80.42 tok/s = **+0.09%, NEUTRAL**; removing 79 launches per token bought nothing, so the launch was never the cost at this site (the rig capture shows the fused kernel itself is shorter, 23.9 vs 27.5 + 4.1 us). STAYS OFF. AND THE GREEDY TAPE FORKS per arm (off `c2721ece65dc1a3e` x3, on `ac74d7f5a951faa0` x3; coherent text, identical for 120 chars, then a rounding fork) although the identity gate is green on the B200 itself (`tests/hc_pre_zq8_gpu.rs`, sm_100a, bitwise), the served wrapper launches `v3(block 512, sink_reg 1, split 0)` with the same arguments the fused launcher takes, and both allocate through `alloc_uninit`. The discriminating twins (decode graph OFF pair, `MEMRA_DEBUG_ZERO_ALLOCS=1` pair) are the chain2 cell; until they name the cause, treat a kernel-level identity gate as NOT a serving-identity receipt for a fused door |
| `MEMRA_HC_PRE_V4` | **DEFAULT OFF** (2026-09-05), pending its model-scale row (new-flags law). `=1` runs each hc site's pre-chain on the v4 REGISTER SCHEDULE (`dsv4_hc_pre_v4_e16_kernel`, 1024 threads x 16 elements whatever `MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK` serves v3 at; launcher `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_v4`) instead of `_v3`. KERNEL ROW (B200, back-to-back x300, served shape): v3 10.3 us at 512 and at 1024; v4 8.34 us (-19%). A 32-elements@512 instantiation measured 12.6 us (+21%) with zero stack and was removed: the 32-load round is the pathology. WHY: B200 phase stamps of v3 at the served shape (hc=4, d=4096, block 512): warp 0's gates+softmax+Sinkhorn chain 3.9 us (48%), the x loads 1.6 us, the combine re-reading x 1.6 us, the ten-barrier block_sum 0.8 us; total 8.0 us warm, 12.7 us served. v4 keeps the same arithmetic in the same order and changes only the schedule: every thread issues its 32 x-loads once and keeps them (the combine reads registers, not L2), the block sum is one barrier plus warp 0 replaying the identical pairwise tree from shared memory, and warp 0 publishes `spre` BEFORE its Sinkhorn so warps 1..15 run the combine underneath it. Two barriers per launch instead of twelve, one read of x instead of two. EXACTNESS: bit-identical to v3 by construction (`tests/hc_pre_v4_gpu.rs`: pre/post/comb/y at blocks 512 and 1024, red arm on `base`, refusal test for an unschedulable d). Engages only under the V2 arm at a v3 width (block != 128 or sink_reg) (the split-collapse door it once excluded was removed 2026-09-05); the launcher returns 40025 for shapes the instantiation does not fit (hc != 4 or w != 16384) and the walk then runs v3 unchanged; any other rc is v4's own error. Announce: the `[hc-fused-pre] engaged` line reads `kernel=hc_pre_v4`. Rollback: unset. MODEL-SCALE ROW (2026-09-05, full B200 hybrid mint, PP-2 pair, six boots interleaved, 22 vendor-default samples each, `kernel=hc_pre_v4` in every ON boot log): off 78.04 / 77.85 / 77.26, on 78.92 / 78.59 / 79.70 tok/s = **+1.37%**, greedy tape `c2721ece65dc1a3e` on all six boots (bit-identical in serving, as the gate said). Still OFF: the flip waits for the composed posture's row (the halves and v4z sit on the same sites). Receipts: darklanes `research/glm5-b200-mint-20260904/LANE.md` |
| `MEMRA_HC_PRE_V4Z` | **DEFAULT OFF** (2026-09-05), pending its model-scale row (new-flags law). Under `MEMRA_HC_PRE_ZQ8=1`, `=1` makes the fused hc-pre + norm launch the v4 register schedule with `rms_norm_zq8_f32_v2` REPLAYED inside the block (`dsv4_hc_pre_v4z_e16_kernel`, launcher `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_v4z`): the y row is staged in shared memory after the combine, the served norm's pass 1 runs on `rms_block()` threads in the served per-thread order, its tree verbatim, pass 2 over all 32 warps (per-block arithmetic does not depend on the emitting warp). EVERY norm operation is PINNED to what kernels.cu compiles it to (`__fmaf_rn` for the sum of squares, `__fmul_rn`/`__fdiv_rn`/`__fadd_rn` elsewhere): this TU is built `-fmad=false` and the served norm's TU is not, so a verbatim copy of the text is NOT the same arithmetic (SASS 2026-09-05: `rms_norm_zq8_f32_v2` accumulates with FFMA, the zq8 copy with FMUL+FADD). WHY: the zq8 fusion returned only the launch gap because the norm's phases serialized inside a one-block kernel; with y in shared memory the replay costs ~1 us in-block against 6.7 us as its own launch (79 per token). Gate `tests/hc_pre_v4z_gpu.rs` (wide-range inputs, all seven outputs bitwise, red arm, width refusal). Refuses (falls back to the zq8 kernel) off hc=4/d=4096 or a replay width outside 32..1024. Counter `HC_PRE_V4Z_DISPATCHES`. Rollback: unset |
| `MEMRA_HC_MIXES_KERNEL` | **DEFAULT OFF** (2026-09-05), pending its model-scale row (new-flags law). `=1` runs the hyper-connection MIXES projection at t=1 (`y[r] = sum_k W[r,k] x[k]`, 24 x 16384 on GLM-5.3-Flash, at every hc pre site: 90 per token) through the native `hc_mixes_gemv_f32` kernel (one block per output row, 1024 threads x 16 contiguous elements as float4, pinned `__fmaf_rn` order, fixed reduction tree) instead of cuBLASLt. WHY: the nsys trace of a decode token (2026-09-05) shows the cuBLASLt path as `dot_kernel` + `reduce_1Block_kernel`, 3.3 + 1.8 us per site (0.46 ms per token of kernel time for 1.5 MB of weights, ~0.4 us at the measured 4 TB/s), and in the eager MLA layers each pair also carries ~9 us of host latency (the largest class of the 3-30 us GPU-idle gaps). NUMERIC CLASS: cuBLAS's summation order is its own, so the tape moves; the kernel is deterministic run to run and the gate is tolerance + determinism + red arm + shape refusal (`tests/hc_mixes_gemv_gpu.rs`); acceptance = the model-scale A/B with the vendor-default sampled probe, the tape recorded as changed, not claimed identical. Falls back to cuBLASLt (launching nothing native) when in_f != 16384. Announce once `[hc-mixes-kernel] engaged`. Rollback: unset. B200 KERNEL ROW (pair, back-to-back x300, three runs): cuBLASLt 10.6-11.5 us per call vs native 6.17 us (-42%; both arms launch-rate-bound in that harness, the in-graph GPU saving is what the model-scale row measures). MODEL-SCALE ROW (2026-09-05, full B200 hybrid mint, PP-2 pair, six boots interleaved, 22 vendor-default samples each, `[hc-mixes-kernel] engaged` in every ON boot): off 77.25 / 78.22 / 76.86, on 79.20 / 78.93 / 78.91 tok/s = **+2.17%**; greedy tape `c2721ece65dc1a3e` -> `ac74d7f5a951faa0` (the same tape the zq8 fused door produced: one borderline token flipped, a tape is not a fingerprint of a mechanism). Still OFF: the flip waits for the sampled quality probe on the composed posture. Receipts: darklanes `research/glm5-b200-mint-20260904/LANE.md` |
| `MEMRA_MOESD_DEPTH=<n>` | `target_efficiency` harness only (moesd dual-device sweep, `research/moesd-harness-20260811/`): prime_cache token depth for the target-forward matrix; default 128, floor 16 |
| `MEMRA_MOESD_CACHE_CAP=<n>` | `target_efficiency` harness only: expert-cache entry cap for the moesd target-forward sweep; refuses caps below the matrix's required residency |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_STATS=1` | print cumulative positioned-read snapshots when server requests finish: reads, logical bytes, errors, short reads, config-parse fallbacks (`config_fallbacks`), runtime mmap fallbacks (`fallbacks`), buffer waits, and worker ring-full events. Snapshots are totals, not per-request deltas; do not sum them |
| `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE=<path>` | append (layer, step, expert ids) per decode step — routing-locality analysis (`research/scripts/moe_trace_analyze.py`, 2026-07-07 M3 measurement) and the CO-OCCURRENCE input of the shared expert-placement mint (`tools/build_expert_placement_map.py` --trace; LAW:coactivation-expert-placement). Same coverage laws as its weight twin below: the glm5 EP walk is wired in; the device-routed step TP walk refuses an armed tap by name |
| `MEMRA_MOE_WEIGHT_TRACE=<path>` | append every host-routed walk's per-(layer, forward) selected experts + weights, token-major (`<layer> <t> <expert:weight,...>`, t*k pairs/row). Two consumers: (1) the routing-divergence oracle behind the m-invariance razor (`concat_prime_probe`, `research/concat-prime-exact-20260802/`); (2) the HOTNESS signal of the shared expert-placement mint (`tools/build_expert_placement_map.py` --weight-trace: summed routing weight replaces pick count for seed/frequency/entry-rank choices; co-occurrence comes from the `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE` id twin; maps land via `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_MAP` — LAW:coactivation-expert-placement, lane/glm5-ep-place 2026-08-31). Rides the EXISTING host route readbacks (glm5's 42 router readbacks/token — zero new device syncs; the glm5 EP walk is wired into the tap too). Forces the stats-visible dispatch path, and the device-routed step TP walk (whose selection never returns to host) REFUSES an armed tap by name rather than silently dropping its rows — a trace missing whole layers poisons any placement mint built on it. ON receipts: `glm5-tp-gate` arm T — trace ON byte-identical to the banked plain walk in BOTH regimes, token events per MoE layer counted exactly (2*(P+N)). Diagnosis only, never a serving mode |
| `MEMRA_MOE_SEL_DUMP=<path>` | BINARY per-token routed-expert selection dump for the expert co-activation question (lane/moe-coactivation-20260902; owner question 2026-09-02: can GLM-5.3-Flash's 288 routed experts per MoE layer be split across two cards by co-activation, always-active experts replicated on both, so a token's 8 selected experts rarely cross cards?). DEFAULT OFF (unset); rollback is unsetting the var, no code path to disable. Unset costs one `OnceLock` read per entry point, no env scan per call; ON, the cost is a per-token host write (buffered 1 MiB, flushed at most once per second, one append-mode file per process — wait a second after the last request before stopping the server). WHAT IT RECORDS: one record per (routed token, MoE layer) during BOTH prime (the grouped prefill `moe_ffn_grouped_prefill_sigmoid`, whose CSR is built from this same host selection) and decode (the t=1 walk: the pinned `[sel, w]` readback `Engine::moe_router_sigmoid_topk_host`, AND the device-table arm `vrows_dev`, which reads its own device selection back with one extra DtoH pair per layer-call, diagnostic only). FORMAT `memra-moe-sel-v1`, little-endian, no header, records back to back: `u8 layer, u8 n_sel, n_sel x (u16 expert_id, f32 routing_weight)` in slot order as the router emitted them (glm5_next: 50 bytes per record). DIFFERENT FROM `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE`/`WEIGHT_TRACE`: those are text, one line per (layer, forward), and they DIVERT dispatch to the host-routed path (the `observe_routes` conjunct); this tap is in no dispatch conjunct and records the served arm's rows. The device-routed walks whose selection never returns to the host (the step TP walk, the automatic W4A16 device-routed EP walk, and the softmax device-routed decode arm `moe_ffn_dev`, `MEMRA_MOE_DEV` default ON for any fully-resident non-sigmoid MoE) REFUSE an armed dump by name, exactly as the text taps do, rather than silently dropping layers. Engagement receipt: the `[moe-sel-dump] armed path=...` boot line plus `memra_engine::moe_sel_dump::RECORDS`; a dump file with zero records is a declined run, not a measurement. Reader: `tools/moe_coact.py` (numpy only; per-expert activation frequency, the 288x288 co-activation matrix, greedy Kernighan-Lin 2-way partition under a replication budget R in {0,16,32,64}, P(all n_sel experts on one card), mean cards touched per token, per-card load, against the random-halves baseline). Usage and interpretation thresholds: `research/moe-coactivation-20260902/LANE.md`. Receipts: darklanes `research/glm5-b200-20260902/box/coact/report.md` and `research/glm5-b200-20260902/EP2-DESIGN.md`. Module: `crates/memra-engine/src/moe_sel_dump.rs` |
| `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER_LOGIT_TRACE=<path>` | write one bit-preserving real decode router-logit row per layer, with correction bias, original-id active mask, scaling, and normalization metadata. The explicit diagnostic performs a DtoH readback and is not a performance mode |
| `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER_REPLAY=<path>` | `kernel-check` input for the served-logit host-vs-device replay cell; malformed, duplicate-layer, selected-id, or routing-weight-bit disagreement fails the required manifest gate |
| `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER_DISPATCH_TRACE=1` | print every engagement of Step-3.7's resident zero-DtoH sigmoid dispatch arm, including layer, token count, clamp form, and gate/up interleave state |
| `MEMRA_DUMP_HN=<path>` | append pre-head hidden states per decode step (head-MIPS feasibility probe, offline bound analysis; both decode arms share the door so they stay observably identical). Diagnostic only |
| `MEMRA_F16G_DEBUG=1` | grouped f16 expert lane stage bisect: full NaN/Inf scans of w/act/y plus post-permute/post-scatter bad-value counts — localizes a corrupt stage. Adds D2H syncs; diagnosis only |
| `MEMRA_FA_V4_MAX=1` | force the v4 FA lane at every t_kv (bypass the crossover) — correctness-forcing knob for the fp8 lane matrix (2026-07-12 closure battery) |
| `MEMRA_DRAFT_GRAPH_CHECK=1` | re-run the gemma draft chain eagerly after each graph replay and diff the drafted slots (non-destructive replay-vs-eager bisect) |
| `MEMRA_E4B_GRAPH_GATE=N` (gemma-gate) | E4B graph-door stream gate: `generate()` door OFF then ON on fresh caches, streams must be identical (the warmup-side-effect + exec-update oracle) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_GATE=N` (gemma-gate) | 12B/31B graph-door stream gate: eager vs graph token streams over N tokens must be identical + both throughputs printed |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_DRAIN=N` | raise the graph door's drain window above the pinned 1 (EXPERIMENTS ONLY: relaunching the same graph exec before its prior launch completes is ILLEGAL — chunk=4 reproduced ILLEGAL_ADDRESS) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_CENSUS=1` | print the captured graph's node-type census (kernel/memset/memcpy/alloc/free counts) at instantiate |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_WARMUPS=N` | eager warmup runs before `capture_graph` captures (DEFAULT 1 since lane/graph-warmups 2026-08-05; min 1; `=2` = the rollback seam). The win: recapture −38% q27 / −41% q9 (pod) re-minted −42% q27 / −41% q9 on the 5090, decode +~1% both, capture+prime −13ms. Warmup 2's address-stability job (warmup 1 may grow/map the async pool; a stale capture reproduces the #68 baked-address class) is guarded structurally — balanced in-graph alloc/free pairs, retire-on-grow pools, capture-retain on the draft path — and was arbitrated adversarially: `tools/graph-warmup-stress-gate.sh` (pool-growth cycles both directions x10 + overlap arm + forced recaptures over freed blocks, bit-identity vs eager, canary teeth) GREEN at `=1` on the deployment rig. Receipts: `research/graph-warmups-5090-20260805/`, pod `research/graph-allocfree-20260805/logs/warmup-lever-N5.txt` |
| `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE2=K` (gemma-gate) | CHAINED batched-verify oracle: prefix tokenwise, then two back-to-back `decode_step_t` calls — per-position argmax must match the tokenwise chain. `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE2_DEV=1` runs the device-token verify arm (the spec round's exact path) |
| `MEMRA_ROUND_GRAPH_CHECK=1` | round-graph bisect: run the captured round body EAGERLY (no capture/replay) — splits body-semantics bugs from replay-mechanics bugs |
| `MEMRA_E4B_DCG_EAGER` | E4B door bisect arm: run the dcg step eagerly per token instead of capture/replay — `1` = exact live bucket, `2` = the capture's win bucket (separates bucket-path numerics from the replay mechanism) |
| `MEMRA_E4B_GRAPH_TIMING=1` | per-phase host timing of the E4B graph loop (dtoh-wait / fa_apply / launch sums) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_NODES_DUMP=1` | dump the captured E4B graph's kernel-node inventory (symbol + grid + count) + fa update-unit count at capture |
| `MEMRA_BURST_VCHECK=1` | run the gemma verify-STREAM on each eager round's batch before the eager verify and diff argmaxes + per-layer KV byte sums (the burst verify's in-place gate harness) |
| `MEMRA_GATE_SEED=<n>` | decode-batch-gate synthetic-prompt token-pattern offset (default 0 = the historic prompt). Cross-config drift is near-tie roulette on any single synthetic prompt, so calibration re-sweeps draw several seeds — gate1-config sweeps 6 and FAILs iff >= 4 of the 6 draws diverge before step 3 (the fraction rule; 2026-08-02 5090 recal, `research/gate1-recal-20260802/`). The per-draw step floor of the round-45 H100 rule ("any seed < step 3"; tie flips assumed 6+) did not transfer: 5090 dice land at steps 0/1/3/4, proven dice by strict-equalized bit-identity on the same draws. Plumbing (wrong token/KV) diverges at step 0-2 on EVERY draw — the fraction separates at margin 2 on both sides |
| `MEMRA_GATE_CANARY=1` | decode-batch-gate TEST-ONLY plumbing canary: feeds gate1's batched lane one wrong token at step 1 — every draw diverges early and the fraction rule MUST FAIL (the teeth check for gate recalibrations; also trips strict bit-identity, as a real plumbing bug would) |
| `MEMRA_EXACT16_WHY=1` | print WHY a checkpoint is refused the exact-16 serve tier: `decode_batch_exact16_ok` is an ALL over every matmul in the model, so one class without a bit-exact b16 kernel refuses the whole checkpoint back to chunk 8, silently. Prints `[exact16] REFUSED by L<n>.<tensor> qtype=<q> rp4=<bool>` per refusing tensor (plus architecture / MLA-mixer / MoE-ffn refusals, which are structural and can never be admitted). Default off, no cost when off. Added lane/rp-on-st 2026-08-06, where it found FOUR blocking classes one at a time on real MIXED checkpoints — NVFP4, Q8_0-base, Q4_K, Q5_K — that had kept chunk 16 unreachable for every shipped artifact; without it each "still refused" round was a guess (`research/rp-on-st-20260806/`) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE=0` | skip run-gen's batched-prime gate (#46): the last-position logits of `prime_cache` (the config that seeds real generation and serving) vs the tokenwise reference. A near-tie argmax flip is REPORTED non-fatally (the accepted cross-config drift class — measured 10/144 prompts across the six-model 2026-08-02 sweep, all at tokenwise margins <= 0.70; `research/prime-gate-coverage-20260802/`); a wide-margin flip or out-of-bounds drift FAILs. Dedicated multi-prompt battery: `prime-gate <model> --prompts-file f [--chat] [--strict]` |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE_MAXDIFF` / `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE_MARGIN` | #46 verdict bounds (defaults 8.0 / 1.0, calibrated on the 2026-08-02 supported-set sweep: legal maxdiff up to 5.5 on gemma Q4_0, legal flip margins <= 0.70). Recalibrate when the kernels under them move — the H100 stale-verdict law |
| `MEMRA_TICK_TRACE=1` | memra-server: per-tick phase timing of the batched scheduler to stderr (diagnosis only) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PROF=1` | memra-engine + memra-server: ONE `[spec-prof]` line per served glm5 spec request, printed by `step_glm5_spec` after the first burst's emission, with every phase between admission and the first streamed token in ms (lane/b200-spec-ttft-20260902): `since_admit_ms` (Session creation to the first spec tick), `session_ms` (the `glm5_spec_session_new` / `_from_restored` wall) broken into `cache_alloc` (trunk cache), `prime` (target prime over the prompt, including the DFlash2 host-staged tap DtoHs and the full-vocab logits readback), `capture` (prefix boundary capture, 0 with `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_PREFIX` off), `anchor` (boundary token draw), `draft_alloc` (DFlash2 `DflashKv::new` at the session ctx, or the native MTP plane fill); `round1_ms` for the first round only: `draft_prime` (the drafter's ctx ingest of the whole prompt, the one prompt-length-linear cost after the target prime; 0 on the native arm), `draft` (block forward + head + selector), `verify`, `accept`, `roll`, `maint`, and the round's committed `tokens`; `burst1` wall/hook/rounds/tokens, where `hook_ms` is the time spent inside the round-cadence commit hook (detext + `Event::Token` sends; 0 with `MEMRA_SPEC_FIRST_TOKEN_EAGER=0`) — under the eager door (default ON) the hook runs inside the burst window, so `wall_ms - hook_ms` is the engine-only burst wall on either arm; `first_emit_ms` (the first `Event::Token` send, from step entry) and `step_ms`. DEPTH ATTRIBUTION (lane/spec-route-depth-20260902) appended to the same line: `draft_prime: arm=eager\|chunked rows= chunks= h2d= feat= kv=` (the drafter prime split: host->device movement, the fc feature GEMM, the 5-layer k/v ingest; `arm=eager` = at session creation, `eager-lazy` = round 1 under `MEMRA_GLM5_DRAFT_PRIME_LAZY=1`, `chunked` = the removed `MEMRA_GLM5_DRAFT_PRIME_V2` arm (2026-09-05), `device` = `MEMRA_GLM5_DRAFT_TAPS_DEVICE`), `sink_alloc` (the eager arm's host tap sink allocation), `tap_dtoh` (the eager arm's synchronous tap DtoHs inside the target prime), `draft_kv_mb` (the drafter ctx KV at the session ctx), `free_mb_before`/`free_mb_after` (free device memory per device around the session's allocations — the graph-launch headroom guard's input, so a per-boot bimodality shows here first). Plus two more line shapes per session: `[spec-prof-rounds]` after every burst with the rounds logged since the last print (first 64 rounds of the session: k = drafts that entered verify, j = accepted, wall/draft/verify/accept/rest ms under drains, `seq` = verify rows that took the per-row mixer arm, `ctx0`), and ONE `[spec-prof-summary]` when the log fills or the session ends (k/j means, accept rate, tokens per round, wall mean/min/median/max, `slow_rounds(>1.5x med)` = the within-boot bimodality count, verify mean, `seq_rows_total`). Distinct from `MEMRA_SPEC_TRACE` (per-burst round SHARES) and `MEMRA_TTFT_TRACE` (HTTP-side marks, no engine phases): this is the once-per-request first-token attribution. Phase boundaries synchronize the stream (the `SpecPhaseNs::clock` contract), so the traced first burst is slightly slower than the untraced one: attribute with this line, claim with the untraced TTFT. Reader `spec_phase::spec_prof_on` (once per process). Diagnostic only, default OFF. |
| `MEMRA_TTFT_TRACE=1` | memra-server: one `[ttft]` line per completion request with request-parse, HTTP admission, worker queue wait, tokenization, pre-prime setup, prime, first-decode wait, first serialized SSE body byte, cumulative boundary, and total timings. Debug-only; disabled requests allocate no trace state |
| `MEMRA_CONFIDENCE_TRACE=<path>` | memra-server: per-token confidence JSONL (`memra-token-confidence-v1`: reference logprob, top1 margin, entropy). Forces single-session serving and disables KV reuse + spec — a measurement harness mode, never a serving config |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_STATS=1` | per-slot accept histogram + draft-length histogram |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_DEMOTE_AT=N` | **test door, NEVER set in a deployment**: force the spec→batched demotion handoff when a session reaches N generated tokens, regardless of active-session count, and demote ONLY that session. Exists because the shipped trigger (`active >= MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_HIGH`) cannot be used for an exactness test — load-triggered demotion is nondeterministic in BOTH arrival timing and batch composition, and batched decode is not bit-identical across batch shapes (`fa_decode_batch_seqs_v4` carries one `split_keys` for sessions at different depths, and the batched-linear tier changes with B — pre-existing, found by lane/spec-gate: a spec-OFF discriminator arm diverges from its own solo reference at bytes 2379/1347 on two runs with no gate code involved). The door pins B=1 with no load, which makes the handoff itself — pending flush, `(cache, next_pred)` transfer, position, stream continuation — the only variable. Used by `research/spec-gate-20260806/exactness.py` (arm DEMOTE_SOLO vs REF); logs `FORCED at DEMOTE_AT=N (test door)` so a forced demotion can never be misread as a policy one |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PHASE=1` | per-round wall decomposition of the spec loop (draft / verify / accept+commit) — no tracing, no extra syncs (each phase is naturally sync-bounded); printed once at loop end via spec-stats. Built by the verify-economics lane (2026-08-02, `research/verify-economics-20260802/`) |
| **step37 serving defaults** (not a flag) | **OWNER FLIP 2026-08-27: the gated step37 serving doors default ON, family-armed.** `arm_step37_serving_defaults()` fires at model load when the plan compiles to the SlidingGatedMoe program (hybrid.rs, before any tensor upload or cache sizing reads a door) and flips the unset-default of: `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME`, `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_EAGER`, `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_TCOL`, `MEMRA_TCOL_FFN`, `MEMRA_SPEC_FA2`, `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8`, `MEMRA_W8_HYBRID`, `MEMRA_SWA_RING`, `MEMRA_MTP_HEAD_NVFP4`, `MEMRA_SPEC_HEAD_ROWS`, `MEMRA_Q8T_WONCE`. **`MEMRA_TN_PREJOIN` WAS REMOVED FROM THIS SET 2026-08-29** with the t-row verify program and the `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_V2` banks it required (see those rows; `research/step37-bankv2-removal-20260829`); it had been unreachable in serving since the bank-v2 boot refusal, so the armed set's behavior is unchanged. `MEMRA_TCOL_FFN` stays armed: its surviving effect is implying the `MEMRA_TCOL_OPROJ` defer. **`MEMRA_PRIME_TROWS` WAS REMOVED FROM THIS SET 2026-08-27** after the flip broke server priming: the walk's prime omits its sub-32 tail chunk from the distributed kv, so the server refuses before decode ("cache lengths diverged local=445 distributed=416") on any prompt whose length is not a multiple of 32. Isolated on the server route (defaults ERR / `MEMRA_PRIME_TROWS=0` OK); invisible to run-gen, which calls `decode_step_t` on the whole prompt and never enters `prime_cache`. Back to opt-in until the tail append is fixed and gated on the SERVER route. Other families are untouched (default stays OFF; the W8 mirror routing sits inside generic matmul paths, which is why the arm is per-family, not global). Every door is tri-state: `=1` forces ON for any family, `=0` is the kill switch — the rollback seam. RECEIPTS the flip stands on (all interleaved, all on the agentic-8 turn-8 shape, tip b2c722e5a..7a784b67c): plain 81.95 -> full stack K=3 **93.18 tok/s greedy (1.137x, byte tape == plain in every arm)** and **81.79 vs 78.50 sampled (1.042x**, vendor-default temp .6 / top_k 20 / top_p .95, engagement receipts drafted=127 accepted=97); run-spec K=1..8 SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS with all doors on; prime/TTFT -10%. POLICY knobs are NOT armed here and stay serve-config: `MEMRA_SPEC_K=3 MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=3 MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN=0.5 MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN0=1 MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM=<ranks> MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM_NVFP4=1` (trim needs an artifact path; the fence is a serving policy, not a kernel door) |
| `MEMRA_Q8T_WONCE` | **DEFAULT ON (family-armed) since the 2026-08-27 owner flip — see the step37-serving-defaults row; `=0` kills.** Routes the q8 t-column verify kernels to their weight-once `_tw` twins. THE DEFECT: the `_t` forms put the verify column on blockIdx.y (a full row-grid copy per column) and load weights with `__ldcs` — streaming, evict-first — so the columns' fully-shared weights are re-read from DRAM once per column. Right at t=1, exactly wrong at t>1. NAMED BY THE NSYS KERNEL DIFF (2026-08-27, K=1 doors, per-round vs per-token): `qkv_rp_t` 36.5 vs 21.8 us = **1.67x** for 2 columns, `b4_rp_t` 29.0 vs 20.3 = **1.43x**, where weight-bound scaling says ~1.1x; the MoE pair (1.81x/1.84x) is at its structural union law and attention (1.36x) is fine, so these twins are the remaining walk-vs-plain-tick gap (~1.1 ms wall/round of the unattributed ~1.4). THE FIX: drop the column grid axis, load each weight int4 once, run t independent accumulator chains against it — per-column float program unchanged (same lane-strided blk order, own single-dependence chain, same warp_reduce; the LAW:vl-bit-identity-order-pinning pattern). Covers `qkv_rp_tw`/`b4_rp_tw`/`rows_tw`, t<=8. SIBLING NOT YET COVERED: `matvec_bf16_dual_silu_rows` shows the same 1.44x class on the bf16 shexp and gets its own arm after this one gates. Rollback: unset = the `_t` forms, byte-for-byte prior path. GATED 2026-08-27 (doors+head-rows, tip b2c722e5a): K=1 interleaved x5 off 78.85 mean [76.85, 80.10] -> on **82.54** [81.78, 82.95], +4.7%, no overlap — fixed-K=1 spec now MATCHES the plain tick (82.14) with no gating at all; K=3 69.49 -> 75.67 (**+8.9%**, the excess grows with t exactly as the __ldcs theory predicts); PRIME 25.15 -> 22.56 s (**-10.3% TTFT**) — unpredicted bonus, the chunked prime walks the same `_t` kernels at t=8 where the re-read excess was worst. Canonical tape == plain (83af249419adbd16587b7b53) in EVERY arm incl. both K=3; acceptance identical (K=1 88/111, K=3 121/234); run-spec K=1..8 SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS with wonce+head-rows ON. Still owed for a default flip: the vendor-default sampled cell |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HEAD_ROWS` | **DEFAULT ON (family-armed) since the 2026-08-27 owner flip — see the step37-serving-defaults row; `=0` kills.** Batches the eager verify tail's LM head over its t columns instead of running it at m=1 once per column. THE DEFECT IT REMOVES: the eager tail loops `matmul(output, row, 1)` per column, so step37's LM head — ~0.49 GiB per card after the rank split — is re-streamed **t times per verify pass**, ~1.07 ms of pure re-read at t=2 and worse at every wider t. That is a large part of why the fixed ladder loses on wall (K=1 81.2 > K=2 73.1 > K=3 62.7 tok/s) even as tok/round rises (1.793 / 2.073 / 2.117), and it is ~43% of the measured 2.49 ms gap between the verify walk at t=1 (14.76 ms) and the plain decode tick (12.27 ms). WHY IT IS NOT THE ULP TRAP THE LOOP GUARDS AGAINST: that comment is about the batched **cuBLASLt** head, and a `FloatBf16` head at 1..=32 rows never reaches cuBLASLt — `matmul` routes it to `matvec_bf16_rows_into` (lib.rs:12248), whose doc states `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_rows` "runs the t=1 decode head program PER ROW (identical dot + reduce), so decode/verify tiers keep the t=1 numeric class"; under the W8 doors both widths route to the q8 mirror and the t-column mirror is documented "bit-identical to t single-row calls". `rms_norm` is row-wise, so norm(t) is per-row identical to t x norm(1). Rollback seam: unset = the per-column loop, byte-for-byte the prior path. GATED 2026-08-27 (doors config, tip 0aac57e87): K=1 interleaved x5 off 77.22 mean [77.01,77.45] -> on 79.99 [79.81,80.14], **+3.6%, no overlap**; K=2 72.81 -> 75.54 (+3.7%); K=3 66.93 -> 69.47 (+3.8%). BYTE TAPE: plain == spec-off == spec-on = 83af249419adbd16587b7b53 (generated text only — hashing the log region falsely FAILED this gate three ways: the tok/s line, the [ttft] line, and the per-run "logit maxdiff" receipt, which legitimately moves with any numeric-path change while output is unchanged). Acceptance 88/111 identical both arms; verify-vs-decode logit maxdiff IMPROVED 9.145e-2 -> 8.716e-2; run-spec K=1..8 SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS with the door ON. Model correction from the ladder: the saving is ~1.1-1.2 ms/round at every K — flatter than the ~1.07 ms/extra-column prediction (the m=1 read achieves better bandwidth than assumed) — so the fix does NOT re-rank the ladder; K=1 stays optimal. Still owed before the default moves: the vendor-default sampled cell |
| `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME` | **FAMILY-DEFAULT ON since 2026-08-28 — on the server route it is the only prime that works.** Measured on that route (not run-gen, which never enters `prime_cache`): walk = ERR ("cache lengths diverged"), fallback chunked prime = 29 s at ~450 tokens and a **90 s TIMEOUT at 4k**, grouped GEMM = **3.5-4.9 s** with coherent on-topic output. `=0` falls back. Both arms stated. Prime chunks (t>=16, prefill) route the routed MoE through ONE grouped f16 GEMM per projection over the RESIDENT NVFP4 banks (`run_tensor_parallel_routes_nvfp4_prime_grouped`) instead of the per-token device routes. WHY (TTFT lane, owner bar sub-second cold): the t-row walk primes 4,092 tokens in 19.8 s at its widest (GEMV-bound, road closed by the width cell); the generic batch prime's decode-class MoE measures 238-243 s at m=4092; the TP_PREFILL/EP_GROUPED doors land in that same slow path or refuse this layout (cell receipts 2026-08-27); and the CUTLASS sizing rows put GEMM-class expert math at 170-270 TFLOP/s here — a sub-second cold prime by arithmetic. MECHANICS: host CSR from the sigmoid router oracle (the EP arm's router), per-rank pointer tables into the bank shards, `moe_f16g_act` -> `moe_f16_grouped` (direct-from-NVFP4 lane, n_active<=512) -> silu pairs -> grouped down per rank; gate/up column-halves with per-expert macros applied BEFORE silu (nonlinear), down row-shard partials joined in the pinned canonical order, weighted scatter with a fixed slot-order per-token sum (no atomics); shexp via the canonical grouped add. NUMERIC CLASS: the f16-mirror grouped-prefill class other families serve; admission = prefill-KV acceptance gate + ship-shape tape + interleaved wall, never byte identity. Requires `MEMRA_MOE_F16G=2` for the ordered single-kernel lane. Rollback: unset = the walk. **`=0` REFUSED AT SERVER BOOT since 2026-08-28 (owner order): the fallback walk primes at ~57.5 ms/token, so every fresh prompt past ~1500 tokens dies on the 90 s first-token deadline (walk-only attribution arm: every row of a 1500+-token battery timed out). A rollback that cannot serve is an outage with a flag name; the server fails loudly at load (`dead_prime_kill_switch_refusal`, worker.rs) instead of booting the half-dead shape that answers /health while timing out real work. `=0` stays usable offline (run-gen, engine bins)** |
| `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME_SUFFIX` | **ON by default since 2026-08-29, by decision, under the flip bar the previous (OFF-era) version of this row wrote down.** That row closed the first lane OFF because its pre-registered byte-identity gate failed - and then attributed the failure correctly: the m-dependence is shared by EVERY measured prime path including the walk (0/2) and predates the door, so byte identity is not a usable gate for any prime path. It demanded three things of a future flip lane, and all three are now banked: (1) VENDOR-DEFAULT SAMPLED ROWS - the blind, rubric-pre-registered 8-turn quality A/B (`research/step37-sampled-quality-20260828`, rubric committed before any generation, 72/72 valid rows, per-row engagement receipts: gemm `ENGAGED base>0`, walk `WALK base>0`, cold fresh `base=0`): WARM-GEMM sits inside the COLD arm's own self-spread at t4 and t8, with the t8 claim carried at n=16 per arm (the round-1 walk-over-gemm signal collapsed under extension, Mann-Whitney p~0.91); loop/derail DQs are statistically equal across ALL arms including COLD, so they are a model-robustness property, not a door property. (2) THE 8-TURN CACHE-ON TWIN: warm-turn TTFT 0.58 s (door) vs 7.15 s (walk) on the real warm serving shape, zero faults. (3) THE BATCHED PRIME OWN STANDARD, not byte identity: acceptance 0.80-0.86 across all arms with the door arm highest at t8, interleaved arms, zero ILLEGAL/#87/panics in 19 boots, binary md5 in every results header. PRECONDITION SHIPPED FIRST: the SWA-ring checkpoint restore fix (`c9a617ca99`) - real session reuse crosses the grow path before the door matters, and that path panicked the worker until then. WHY IT IS WORTH IT, measured (five-point sweep, `research/gemm-suffix-20260828/PREDICTION.md`): walk continuation 0.2529 s + 5.5978 ms/suffix-token (R^2 0.9976) vs this path at 0.99 ms/token - a 7.97x suffix-slope collapse; warm multi-turn TTFT 0.6-0.7 s vs ~2.3 s door-off at short context, 2.3 s at 36-40k context (30k cell). MECHANISM: does a CONTINUATION prime (`cache.pos > 0` - a rewound session suffix or a prompt remainder split across scheduler ticks) ride the batched GEMM prime like a fresh prompt does? Default/`=1`: yes (GEMM trunk at m = chunk plus grouped NVFP4 MoE). ROLLBACK: `=0` puts continuations back on the walk (fresh primes keep the fast path); `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME=0` is still the whole-path seam. ENGAGEMENT RECEIPT, both directions: `[gemm-prime] ENGAGED t=.. base=..` with `base>0` is the suffix arm, `[gemm-prime] WALK t=.. base=..` counts the other; a log showing neither line is an instrument fault rather than a pass |
| `MEMRA_STEP35_PRIME_BATCH_TSEND` | off — CANARY SEAM restoring the pre-fix chunk-local `seq_end` in the step35 batched prime (`ts[s]`, this chunk's own length, where the request's absolute end belongs). Not a performance knob: `seq_end` is the only thing step35's SWA arm keys on (`swa && seq_end > win`, win = 512 here), so a chunk SHORTER than the window at a NONZERO base selected the UNWINDOWED FA arm over a view the `off` trim leaves at ~`win-1+t` rows — it attended OUTSIDE the sliding window. Worked example at the geometry the affinity lane measured (`base_len` 1440, `t` 254): `off = (1440-511) & !31 = 928`, `t_kv = 766`, and the unwindowed arm hands query 1693 all 766 causal keys from 928 when its window starts at 1182. **This fired on committed code with no continuation involved**: the chunk loop folds only a trailing chunk below `PRIME_MIN_T`, so a FRESH prompt of 4096+k for k in [16, 512) already ended in a chunk of exactly that shape at base 4096. Fixed by threading the request-absolute `seq_end` (`cache.pos + t + queued_after`, computed once above the batched entry, the same chunk-invariant value the walk has used since lane/step35-chunkfix) through `step35_prime_cache_batch` and `step35_prime_batch_layers`; the fix is unconditional, not gated on `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME_SUFFIX`. The seam is chunk- and suffix-VARIANT by construction, which is what gives the suffix byte-identity gate its teeth — the same canary role `MEMRA_STEP35_SWA_TKV` plays for the walk. Receipts: `research/gemm-suffix-20260828`. Never on in a measured default run |
| `MEMRA_STEP35_DRAFT_DCW` | Default **on** (default flipped ON 2026-08-30 by lane/step37-draft-graph-serving-20260830 — it is the kernel prerequisite of the captured chain on the qualified serving shape; `=0` reverts). Arms the step35 (Step-3.7-Flash) DRAFT-CHAIN dcw door: the MTP block's draft attention runs `mtp_step35_attn_dcw` (`append_kv_quantized_dcw` + `fa_decode_dcw`, the step TP graph arc's windowed device-counter kernels), whose SWA view derives entirely from device state (`len_d`, `base_d` host-written at ring rebase only, window 512), so the per-session draft-chain CUDA graph CAPTURES instead of printing the boot WARN and falling back to eager. OFF arm (`=0` rollback, the pre-2026-08-30 serving byte-for-byte): host-len eager `mtp_step35_attn` (`fa_decode_kvmod` over a host-offset ring view) + the named capture refusal. ON arm: BOTH draft modes (eager fallback and captured) run the ONE dcw launcher at bucket `min(cap, window)`, so graph-vs-eager draft parity holds by construction; vs the OFF arm the draft-side numeric class moves only in the sub-vec-floor region (t_kv < 96: kvmod ran scalar) and any live-len split-ladder rung below the bucket's, and the verify arbitrates every emitted byte either way (spec-vs-plain identity is the gate, acceptance the battery metric). Requires the v3-vec class live (`fa_v3_active`, default KV formats); ineligibility falls back to the OFF arm loudly at capture and silently at dev dispatch. Ring headroom for captured appends is pre-armed host-side at capture and round start (`ensure_dcw_headroom`); rebases refresh the `base_d` mirror inside `prepare_kv_append`. Rollback seam: unset the env (or `0`) and restart; no persisted state. Gate: run-spec K=1..8 spec-vs-plain byte identity on real prompts + boot receipt pair (`[mtp-geom] arm=dcw` + WARN absence vs `arm=eager` + WARN presence) + vendor-default sampled interleave x5 with `usage.spec` engagement per arm. **MEASURED 2026-08-29** (2x RTX PRO 6000, curve-0400, binary e42d389d01ba): gates GREEN at MEMRA_MTP_HEADS=1: capture LIVE (WARN 8-vs-0 both directions), K=1..8 identity PASS in all arms, per-K acceptance IDENTICAL across off/dcw-eager/dcw-graph (pure latency door). Reach findings: the qualified serving config runs the 3-HEAD chain, for which `graph_draft` is structurally off (`mtp_extra.is_empty()`), and vendor defaults temp 0.5 / top_p 0.9 keep the SAMPLED graph out by the pure-temp exactness law, so capture is live today only for greedy 1-head shapes. Serving matrix interleaved x5 (vendor-default sampled): h3-off med 142.66 / h3-on 136.67 (pairwise median -1.63 inside a 25 tok/s spread = NEUTRAL) / h1-off 126.78 / h1-on 128.37; the 3-head chain beats the captured 1-head chain, so serving kept heads=3 and the door shipped OFF as a kernel seam. SUPERSEDED 2026-08-30: the two follow-ups landed (multi-head chain capture via MEMRA_MTP_CHAIN_GRAPH; in-graph filtered sampling via MEMRA_SPEC_GRAPH_FILTERED), making this door load-bearing for capture on the qualified heads=3 vendor-default shape — default flipped ON in the same lane, receipts `research/step37-draft-graph-serving-20260830/RESULTS.md`. Rollback: `MEMRA_STEP35_DRAFT_DCW=0`. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PHASE_SYNC=1` | syncs the stream right after the verify walk is ISSUED, so `MEMRA_SPEC_PHASE`'s split can be read correctly instead of guessed. `ph_mark` is a bare `Instant`: `verify-issue` is the host QUEUEING the walk while the GPU already runs under it, and `verify-wait` is only the residual drain at the accept readback — one overlapped interval cut at the first blocking call, NOT "GPU time" beside "host time". With this on, `verify-issue` absorbs the GPU wall, `verify-wait` collapses to the readback, and the round wall must NOT move; if the round wall does move, the queueing was exposed and launch-count work is back on. Built 2026-08-26 after I misread the step37 split as 14.1 ms GPU + 7.9 ms exposed host — it is ~22.0 ms of GPU with the host hidden inside it, i.e. two verify columns cost ~1.8x a 12.16 ms plain token, which IS the 0.90x spec gap. Corroborated twice: `launch-econ` prices graph replay on this box at 1.02x eager (0.12 ms/round, not 7.9), and the one family where verify-WAIT really is 0.0% is the GDN+MoE route below, where capture won +19.7%. Diagnostic only |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_SETUP_TRACE=1` | per-call wall decomposition of the serve spec burst's SETUP + TAIL segments (the round loop's internals are `MEMRA_SPEC_PHASE`'s job) — pins the per-burst fixed cost (`research/spec-serving-20260801/`) |
| `MEMRA_ECON_N` / `MEMRA_ECON_TMAX` / `MEMRA_ECON_ONLY` | `spec-econ` probe-bin controls (verify-economics receipts): iterations per arm (default 50), max verify T (default 6), single-arm nsys attribution mode (`decode_h`/`verify_tN`) |
| `MEMRA_DEBUG_SPEC=1` | per-round spec decode trace |
| `MEMRA_FILTER_COOP` | Default **on**. `0` = single-block `filter_stats` form (one block per row) instead of the cooperative multi-block kernel (`filter_stats_coop_f32`: 16 blocks/row, grid-synced bisection totals — lane/samplat 2026-08-21; the one-block form left ~94% of the device idle ~620us per B=8 serve tick). Same algorithm; slice-partial f32 sums are the accepted device-sampling class (sample-check arbitrates). Deterministic keying (hermes sweep 2026-08-23): the old per-call occupancy fallback (16*nrow <= SM count, else single-block) was a second numeric program selected by batch width — measured non-bit-identical (~1e-7..1e-6 rel renorm mass). Coop now engages on every >=16-SM device and CHUNKS rows to the co-residency cap (SM/16 rows per cooperative launch); per-row bits are batch-width-independent (kernel-check FILTER-COOP-CHUNK, bit-bad=0). `0` (or a <16-SM device class) is the only path to the single-block form — deployment-keyed, never per call; its distance is pinned by FILTER-COOP-VS-PLAIN (<1e-5) |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_FUSED3B` | Default **on**. `0` = the attention q/k/v trio leaves the batched fused NVFP4 tier at m=2..8 (fused3-b2..b8, lane/samplat 2026-08-21) and runs as singles; m=9..=16 rides the group3 delegate regardless (its kill switch is `MEMRA_TK_FA_GROUP`) — rollback seam for the batched-tick decode increment |
| `MEMRA_DEBUG_PRIMESEG=1` | memra-server: prints the plain-tier prime CALL SEQUENCE per request — each prime segment's boundary/stop and whether the W1 tokenwise door engaged (lane/spec-longctx-20260821; the diagnostic that made the sub-floor-remainder path loud). Diagnostic only, no numeric effect |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_FULLG_DEBUG=1` | dspark verify graphs (slice 4c): per-round print of why the full-verify graph did or did not capture/replay (rung, coverage, batchable checks) — the door that diagnosed the MTP-slot walk-coverage bug (`f4da30f3d6`). Diagnostic only |
| `MEMRA_SKEY_PROBE=1` | sampled-draft-graph key probe (lane/graph-s-key-exactness-20260819): prints the burst-entry sampler facts (`[skey] burst sampled/pure_temp/top_k/top_p … graph_s_parked`) and flags `[skey] EXACTNESS q=0` when a drafted token gathers a zero filtered-q — the impossible-if-sound signature that confirmed the pre-fix key collision. Diagnostic only; no numeric effect |
| `MEMRA_MOE_CSR` | `0` = rollback the CSR expert-dedup gate_up on spec verify (default ON 2026-07-10: owner-scan dedup of the 38-40% duplicated expert weight-stream+decode, +1-2% spec e2e all K); `2` = run BOTH paths + byte-compare (debug) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_OVERLAP` | `1` = log cross-token expert-activation overlap at spec verify (unique/pairs ratio, diagnostic) |
| `MEMRA_FA_V4` | `0` = rollback to the v3 FA lane. DEFAULT ON 2026-07-10: key-per-lane score phase (zero shuffles/key), kernel -7.5..-15% at depth, e2e p2 +5.7% (35B), p3 +0.7-3.9% (all models), p1 -0.8% (noise); full battery green on all three models incl sampled seeded identity + kernel-check; acceptance deltas trajectory-class (±). `noB3`/`stage` = phase probes (WRONG OUTPUT, bench only) |
| `MEMRA_FA_DEEP` | `0` = rollback to the v4 twins from the deep twins (`fa_decode_vec_q_v4_deep`/`_deep_dc`). DEFAULT ON 2026-08-02 (lane fa-decode-deep): BIT-IDENTICAL rewrite of the v4 program — padded smem rows kill the 32-way score-phase bank conflict (1.40M → 3.5K conflict cycles/launch at d6144), packed 16B staging stores, L2 tile prefetch. NOT a numeric config: kernel-check pins deep-vs-v4 BYTE identity (2 geometries × 6 depths × eager/dc/bucketed-replay). Vec kernel d6144 29.7 → 20.8 µs (1.43x, quiet nsys); flat-or-better at every depth 96..6144 (`research/fa-decode-deep-20260802/`) |
| `MEMRA_FA_DEEP_MIN` | engagement floor for the deep twins (default 0 = always where v4 ran — the swept verdict, no losing cell on the fine grid). Sweep/diagnostic seam only; not a tuning knob |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_STREAM` | `1` = pre-issued M-round zero-readback bursts (round-stream stage c, `MEMRA_SPEC_STREAM_M` rounds/burst default 4). MEASURED NEGATIVE 2026-07-10 (35B serve p2 -16%, p1 -4%; 27B p2 wash): always-K draft + fixed-width verify waste > the ~1.5-2ms/round trip savings at real acceptance rates. Kept as the experimental seam.
