Token-identity is NOT universal: step35 has no ROUND-STREAM verify arm (hard refuse, spec.rs), and the qwen35 rowwise verify (t>16 or `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_ROWWISE=1`) refuses the stream too — only the paths that accept the burst carry identity |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_GRAPH` | **DEFAULT ON for the GatedDeltaNet + routed-MoE family** since 2026-08-23 (`=0` = kill switch, eager walk, byte-identical; `=1` opts other families in). Arms the slice-4c verify capture for the MTP spec round — the door already existed inside `qwen35_verify_tparallel` and had no caller on this route. WHY a default: this route reads verify-ISSUE 44-58% with verify-WAIT **0.0%** (`MEMRA_SPEC_PHASE=1`), i.e. host-launch-bound, and replay collapses that phase (55-62 -> 8-10 ms/burst). MEASURED on a non-serving current-generation host (9950X), balanced 4+4 boots both orders: OFF 266.0-266.5, ON 318.8-319.5 tok/s = **+19.7%**, no overlap, sub-1% per-arm spread, per-round 6.9 -> 5.7 ms; Zen 3 host +3-9%. The ON arm sits at ~320 on BOTH host generations while OFF tracks host speed — the arm moves the round off the host onto the device. EXACT: fixed-seed sampled hash identical ON/OFF and across hosts (`08941d5bb9762b21`), greedy seed-pinned likewise, run-spec K=1..8 PASS both arms with identical acceptance, kernel-check ALL GREEN. Default verified as a default (naked env -> pool ENGAGED + 319; `=0` -> pool absent + 266, twice each). SCOPE is the measured family only: Qwen3.8-27B is GDN + DENSE mlp and opts in with `=1` once it has its own interleave. Never co-armed with ROUND-STREAM; a round wider than the pool declines it. GRAPH-LAUNCH HEADROOM GUARD (lane/graph-launch-guard-sweep-20260831): below `GRAPH_LAUNCH_MIN_FREE` the round declines the pool and rides the byte-identical eager walk (the round's `[spec] graph replay suspended:` line names it). Receipts: `research/orndecode-20260822/VGRAPH.md` |
| `MEMRA_ROUTER_KERNEL` | `0` = rollback to the cuBLAS router (per-column gemv at verify t, gemvx at t=1). Default ON since 2026-07-10, extended to t=1 the same day: decode + verify route through the SAME kernel (parity by construction — the cuBLASLt n-dependence class is structurally gone). Verify +2-4% spec e2e; t=1 e2e wash (kept for parity) |
| `MEMRA_ROUTER_PREFILL_EXACT` | `0` = rollback to the batched cuBLASLt GEMMs for the MoE **prefill** router (`ffn_gate_inp`) and the shared-expert gate dot (`ffn_gate_inp_shexp`). Default ON 2026-08-02 (`lane/concat-prime-exact`): both cuBLASLt forms are **m-DEPENDENT** — a row's output changes when other rows join the call (router rows [0,19) move 3.9e-3 between m=19 and m=65; shexp gate 1.07e-4 between m=74 and m=75), and under cross-request concat prime batching that made a served request's own expert selection a function of its CO-ARRIVALS (16% of (layer,token) pairs got different experts). The in-house `router_gemv` / `sigmoid_dot_rows` twins — the ones decode and spec verify already use — are m-INVARIANT, so prefill rides them at every t. Fixes serve greedy c=1-vs-c=16 byte identity: Ornith-35B 6/16 -> **16/16**, KAT 7/16 -> **16/16** (Ornith-9B + Qwen3.6-35B ctrl stay 16/16). Receipts: `research/concat-prime-exact-20260802/`. The `=0` arm is a numeric A/B seam, NOT a serving configuration — it forfeits the isolation guarantee |
| `MEMRA_PROFILE_GEN` | `1` = cudaProfiler{Start,Stop} brackets run-gen's timed generate_with (prime included); `2` = capture starts at the DECODE LOOP (prime excluded) — pair with `nsys -c cudaProfilerApi`. Window-cutting whole-run captures misattributes the argmax-gate loop into decode shares (2026-07-10) |
| `MEMRA_PROFILE_SPEC` | `1` = cudaProfiler{Start,Stop} brackets generate_spec (prime included); `2` = capture starts at the ROUND LOOP (prime excluded) — pair with `nsys -c cudaProfilerApi`. Built 2026-07-10 after phase-isolation-by-subtraction proved unworkable on MoE (primes are not fungible: the first cold-stages the expert cache) |
| `MEMRA_LAYER_PROBE=1` | sync+print after every forward stage — bisects an in-graph ILLEGAL_ADDRESS (M3 bring-up tool) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_DIFF=1` | dual-run oracle: chunked GDN prefill checked against the sequential scan per call |
| `MEMRA_MOE_GATE=1` | byte-identity oracle: grouped-vs-sequential MoE FFN compare (pair contract; known benign q8-quantize diff class documented at the gate site) |
| `MEMRA_FA_FLOOR=1` | prefill-FA floor kernel variant (fa_hd128_check gate bin) |
| `MEMRA_MSCALE_PROFILE` / `MEMRA_MSCALE_NOEAGER` / `MEMRA_MSCALE_INTERLEAVE` | verify-mscale probe bin controls (`INTERLEAVE=1` alternates the arms) |
| `MEMRA_TEST_MODEL`, `MEMRA_LLAMA_TOKENIZE`, `MEMRA_ST_TEST_DIR` | test-suite input paths (tokenizer parity, safetensors header tests) |
| `MEMRA_GATE_DUMP_TOKENS=1` | test-only `gemma-gate` token-stream dump used by exact ON/OFF benchmark harnesses; does not alter inference |
| `MEMRA_KC_MODELS_DIR` | model dir for kernel-check's REAL-WEIGHT sections (the router weight-oracle bit-identity + m-invariance sweep added by `lane/fast-router`; battery scripts set it, unset skips with a note) |
| `MEMRA_KC_FAST=1` / `MEMRA_KC_ONLY=a,b,...` | kernel-check dev-loop scoping (fast-gate tier 0, 2026-08-02): `FAST` skips ALL weight-oracle sections (synthetic arms only, ~2s vs ~4.5min); `ONLY` runs sections whose name contains a csv term. Every skip prints LOUD; the full battery (no env) still gates merges/tags — see docs/TESTING.md |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_SK_FORM=sk\|tile` | pin the stream-k or xy-tile numerical form for measurement/rollback, bypassing the device-keyed default |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_TOK_PER_MS` | dark-prefill chunk estimator: prime rate (tok/ms, default 8.0) used to size within-SLO-headroom prefill chunks in the x-lane QoS tick |

### Diagnostics backfill (2026-08-19 audit — all internal, none are operator knobs)

| flag | what it does |
|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DEBUG=1` | one-shot ENGAGEMENT receipts for fused/batched dispatch arms (`[memra] ... ENGAGED` to stderr) — the first dead-arm A/B lesson: a silent gate no-op'd a whole experiment, so the arm proves it is live in the log (`lib.rs`, `spec.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_DEBUG_AFFINITY=1` | memra-server: log WHY a plain-affinity checkpoint capture was skipped (the session then re-primes in full next turn) — `worker.rs` |
| `MEMRA_DEBUG_FAPOOL=1` | fa partials-pool tracing (`[fa-pool] REALLOC/retire` lines) — the instrument for the #68 dangling-pool class (`lib.rs`, 7 sites) |
| `MEMRA_DEBUG_ZERO_ALLOCS=1` | memset EVERY engine allocation — the global uninit-read discriminator (task #14 defect hunt; the prime-fn-scoped zeroing experiment could not cover engine-internal buffers). Massive launch overhead; debug only (`lib.rs alloc_uninit`) |
| `MEMRA_ALLOC_SITES=1` | diagnostic (2026-09-05): every engine allocation whose block was last handed out on a DIFFERENT stream prints both call sites (`[alloc-sites] XSTREAM reuse ptr=.. now file:line .. <- last file:line ..`, first 24 lines), naming the main-stream owner a side-stream allocation lands on (the async pool's cross-stream reuse under internal dependencies). One hash-map lock per allocation; debug only (`lib.rs alloc_uninit`, `#[track_caller]` through the `uninit*` wrappers) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_TRACE=<path>` | chunk-invariance bisect seam: one JSONL row per (chunk, layer) with a hash of that layer's last-row post-residual hidden. Forces a dtoh + host hash per layer — never on in a measured or gated run (`hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_HYPER_TRACE=<path>` | **OFF by design.** Hyper-connection (mHC) trunk bisect seam, written by BOTH `memra-reference`'s executor and the CUDA `forward_hyper` walk in the same `memra-hidden-trace-v1` format, so the two sides are diffed stage-by-stage on the same token ids instead of only at the logits. One line per stage per layer — `expand`, `mixer`, `attn`, then on MoE layers `router` (all expert logits), `route` (the selected (expert, weight) pairs) and `routed` (routed sum, before the shared expert), then `ffn`, `layer`, and finally `collapse` — each carrying the LAST token row (`memra_reference::hidden_trace`, emitted from `hybrid_forward.rs::forward_hyper` + `moe_ffn_sequential_zq8` on the engine side and `execute_hyper_layer` + `moe_mlp` on the reference side). Covers the STATELESS prefill walk only: `prime_cache_hyper` and `decode_step_hyper` carry no taps. Default OFF because it forces a `dtoh` per stage and writes tens of MB of f32 rows per run: a diagnostic, never on in a measured or gated run. Unlike the `MEMRA_MOE_*` traces it selects no arm and changes no dispatch — the emit sites are pure readbacks after the value is already computed, so a traced run computes exactly what an untraced one does. Receipts: `research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/` (GLM-5.3-Flash forward bisect, 2026-08-28) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_ANATOMY=1` | per-stage prime wall breakdown (attn_full / gdn_linear / moe / dense / norms): syncs the stream around each `prime_layers`/`forward_last` stage and prints cumulative ms — attribution shares only, the syncs serialize overlap (lane/moebatch-q35moe; the receipt that found MoE at 88.6% of the ornith15 14.7k prime) (`hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_DBB_SAMP=greedy\|temp\|filt` | decode-batch-bench serve-tick meta isolator: runs the batched step through `decode_step_batch_sampled_lean_masked` with that device-sample meta on every row — the instrument that split the filtered-sampling serve wall from the engine tick (`decode_batch_bench.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_G4_PRIME_TRACE=1` | gemma4 prime bring-up trace: layer-0 stage stats (NaN count + amax of q/attn/moe stages) to stderr (`hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_INPUT_TRACE_DIR=<dir>` | append per-layer MoE FFN input rows (t x n_embd f32) — forces the stats-visible dispatch path plus a dtoh per layer; diagnosis only (`hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_PROBE=1` | gemma bring-up bisect: per-layer trunk stats (host rms + max of the LAST token row) vs llama-eval-callback node stats (`hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_VISION_DEBUG=<dir>` | vision-tower stage dumps (`rust_<tag>.bin`, f32 LE) for the HF parity bisect — qwen (`vision.rs`) and gemma (`vision_gemma.rs`) towers; `gemma-vision-oracle` sets it to its own out dir |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_SK_DEBUG=1` | per-call `[mmq-sk]` dispatch print (in_f/out_f/m/nsm, env-vs-device source, SK\|TILE pick) from the q4_0 MMQ launcher — CUDA-side `getenv` (`cu/mmq_q4_0.cu`) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PP_ANATOMY=1` | one-shot PP-2 spec-verify boundary pricing to the server log (reverse hop / stage 0 / TX / stage 1 + head) without nsys. N=2 stages ONLY — the report names exactly two stages and must never imply it measured middle ones (`spec.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_NLL=1` | replay-acceptance's trunk arm also accumulates mean teacher-forced NLL over the forced tape (`spec.rs replay_acceptance`) |
| `MEMRA_GV_FORCE_CAUSAL=1` | **WRONG-ARM probe seam**: splice the vision overlay rows but keep the plain causal mask — exists only so the decisive probe can show the island mask itself changes the answer. Never on in serving (`hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_GEMV_JSONL=<path>` | gemv-e4m3-bench: append one JSONL row per measured cell |
| `MEMRA_GS_PROF=1` | graph-session-gate: decompose the replay step (fa_apply vs launch+sync vs token D2H) on a fresh session |

### Merged-lane diagnostics backfill (2026-08-22)

| flag | default | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_CHAIN_PHASE` | off | `1` alternates token-chain work across phase streams without the cross-token event edges. This is a timing-ceiling probe with deliberately racing/wrong output; never use its tape for correctness or serving. |
| `MEMRA_DOWN_X4` / `MEMRA_MMV_BLOCK` | off / 128 | `DOWN_X4=1` assigns four short BF16 output rows per block when `in_f <= 2048`; `MMV_BLOCK` selects the BF16 matvec block size (64..256, multiple of 32). Both change kernel scheduling; require a fresh tape and battery before promotion. |
| `MEMRA_EXACT16_MOE` | off | Admit the exact-width-16 MoE decode tier. It is byte-safe but currently slower than two width-8 waves, so it remains an explicit measurement door. |
| `MEMRA_FA_DCW_MEMSET` | on | `0` skips three dead partial-pool memsets in DCW attention. Token-graph capture forces them back on because graph retargeting identifies the attention children by that signature. |
| `MEMRA_HEAD_SPLIT` / `MEMRA_OPROJ_TAIL` / `MEMRA_SHEXP_SPLIT` | off | Step TP probes for splitting the LM head, deferring the output-projection join into the residual/norm consumer, and splitting the shared-expert down rows. Each falls back when its exact geometry is ineligible. |
| `MEMRA_LOAD_MTP` | off | `run-gen` directory-checkpoint input: `1` loads embedded NextN/MTP heads instead of the trunk-only loader so `MEMRA_SPEC_K` can exercise the attached chain. |
| `MEMRA_NO_LOCAL_SHADOW` | off | Skip Step TP eager local-KV shadow gathers/appends while advancing their lengths. The local contents feed spec/MTP scratch, so this is a decode-only diagnostic door. |
| `MEMRA_SIG_EXPF_DEV` / `MEMRA_TOPK_FAST` | off | Router numeric-class and scheduling probes: device-libm sigmoid `expf`, and the barrier-lean warp-local top-k merge. Both require a new tape plus the full battery. |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_EAGER` / `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_TCOL` / `MEMRA_TCOL_REFKERN` | off | Step serving-class verification: row-outer replay of the live t=1 program; optional T-column QKV amortization at T=2..8; and the per-column t=1 reference-kernel bisect for T-column slabs. `TCOL` is subordinate to `EAGER`; `REFKERN` is diagnosis only. |
| `MEMRA_TCOL_OPROJ` / `MEMRA_TCOL_OPROJ_REF` / `MEMRA_TCOL_PROF` | off | Tcol verify-walk o_proj levers: `OPROJ` defers each column's o_proj into one batched b4_tcol + slab join per layer (measured -6.5% at K=1 — door stays OFF until the FFN side is also column-batched); `OPROJ_REF` fills the partial slab via the t=1 b4 kernel per column, the permanent choreography-vs-kernel bisect twin; `PROF` prints a synchronized per-segment wall split of the walk — the syncs serialize the stream, so it targets amortization work and is never a perf claim. |
| `MEMRA_TCOL_FFN` | on (family-armed) | Today this flag ONLY implies the `MEMRA_TCOL_OPROJ` defer (and stays family-armed in the step37 serving defaults for exactly that effect; `=0` kills). Its NAMED feature, the two-column device-routed MoE FFN sweep for the verify walk, was REMOVED 2026-08-29 with the `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_V2` banks it required (see that row; `research/step37-bankv2-removal-20260829`); since the 2026-08-29 boot refusal that arm had been unreachable in serving anyway, so removing it changed no serving bytes (byte-gated in the removal lane). The flag is not folded into `MEMRA_TCOL_OPROJ` here because that would silently drop the o_proj defer from recipes that set only this flag; a future lane may unify them with its own receipts. Historical: exactness banked 2026-08-23 (200-token greedy tape byte-match), never benchmarked interleaved. |
| `MEMRA_TAIL_ADD3` | on | `0` disables the MoE tail fusion that folds the shared-expert apply into the routed join add. Default-on identity-preserving launch reduction; zero is its rollback seam. |
| `MEMRA_TG_PROBE_LAYER` | none | Token-graph bisection layer index. Dumps the eager attention output and post-FFN residual at that layer, adding synchronization and host I/O. |

### Gate & bench-bin inputs backfill (2026-08-19 audit — inputs to specific test binaries, not serving config)

| flag | binary | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DC_GATE=N` | gemma-gate | device-counter decode gate: the dc chain's N-token greedy stream must be IDENTICAL to the eager `decode_step` chain; prints both throughputs (default N=64) |
| `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE=K` | gemma-gate | batched-verify self-consistency (the base of the documented `MEMRA_VERIFY_GATE2`): tokenwise reference, then ONE `decode_step_t` over the last K tokens — per-position argmax must match (default K=4) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC=K` (+`MEMRA_DRAFT`) | gemma-gate, gemma-spec-session-gate | MTP spec loop cell: prime, draft K, verify, accept; compares the spec stream against plain greedy + times both (default K=4; the FR-rank corpus mint reads it too, default 7 there). Distinct from the serving flag `MEMRA_SPEC_K` |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_ONLY=1` | gemma-gate (dflash cell) | skip the plain-greedy reference arm (timing-only run) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_CORPUS=<prompt dir>` + `MEMRA_GEN_OUT=<ids.txt>` | gemma-gate | FR-rank corpus mint: one model load, run the spec loop over every `*.txt` prompt, append each generation's token ids one line per prompt. Needs `MEMRA_DRAFT`; setting these together with `MEMRA_SPEC_DFLASH` REFUSES (the two select different gemma-gate runs and the mint branch would win silently — the 2026-08-16 "summary never emitted" incident) |
| `MEMRA_FA_SHAPE` | fa-v2-bench | head geometry: default 35B (nh16/nkv2/hd256), `9b` = nh16/nkv4/hd256, or explicit `nh,nkv,hd` |
| `MEMRA_PP_TOKENS=<n>` | fp8-mmq-stream | prefill token count for the `MEMRA_PP_ONLY` timing cell (default 512) |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ_NLL=<txt>` | fp8-mmq-stream | mean token NLL over a real text window (one prefill) — the quality-sanity cell |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ_TF=<log>` | fp8-mmq-stream | teacher-force from a prior run's `stream ids: [...]` line (cross-arm identical trajectory) |
| `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ_LOGITS=<file>` | fp8-mmq-stream | dump the logit rows raw for cross-arm drift comparison |
| `MEMRA_PPN_GATE_WINDOW` | ppn-gate | pipelined-arm tokens-in-flight cap (default 3; `1` = drain every step) — bisect seam, not a config |
| `MEMRA_PROBE_DEVICE` | spec-st-probe, vision-gate, spec-serve-gate | CUDA device ordinal for the probe engine (default 0) |
| `MEMRA_PROBE_CTX` | spec-st-probe | session cache capacity — reproduce the worker's `MEMRA_CTX`-floor allocation (scratch.cap feeds `fa_decode_dc`'s n_splits geometry), default prompt+gen+64 |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_K` / `MEMRA_REPLAY_STRIDE` / `MEMRA_REPLAY_CHUNK` / `MEMRA_REPLAY_T` | replay-acceptance | draft depth (4) / positions between draft chains (16) / prefill chunk (512) / token cap (65536 in directory mode, 0 = whole file in single-file mode). Needs `MEMRA_PROMPT_FILE` (file OR directory) |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_HDUMP=<dir>` | replay-acceptance | hidden-state dumps (`<stem>.hiddens` + `.meta.json`) — REQUIRED in directory mode (bulk distillation extraction; skip draft chains there via a huge STRIDE) |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_DUMP=<file>` | replay-acceptance | per-position draft/target/hit JSONL dump |
| `MEMRA_REPLAY_GATE` (set) | replay-acceptance | chunk-offset exactness gate: re-run at chunk 64 — the greedy track must be EXACT; draft slots allow <=1% disagreement (chunk-FP-order near-ties, measured 2/127) |
| `MEMRA_ST_NGEN=N` | run-safetensors | greedy continuation gate (native-vs-GGUF byte compare), default 32 |
| `MEMRA_ORACLE_OUT=<file>` | run-safetensors | Write the native checkpoint oracle TSV used by `memra model verify checkpoint`; the onboarding CLI sets this for its pinned native runner. Gate output path, not serving config. |
| `MEMRA_ARTIFACT_LOCK=<file>` / `MEMRA_REWRITE_RECEIPT=<file>` | rewrite gate binaries | Bind a generated rewrite receipt to the exact artifact lock and select the output path. Qualification refuses to emit a receipt without the lock; these are gate-harness inputs, not serving knobs. |
| `MEMRA_NATIVE_CHECKPOINT_RUNNER=<binary>` / `MEMRA_NATIVE_SERVE_RUNNER=<binary>` | memra model verify | Environment alternatives to `--native-runner` for checkpoint and serve qualification. There is no compatibility fallback when the runner is absent or fails. |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_VERIFY_TIMEOUT_S=N` | memra model verify serve | Readiness deadline for the temporary native server, default 180 seconds. Timeout is a failed serve gate and never promotes support. |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_TOKEN_FILE=<file>` | step-prefill-rollback-gate | Whitespace-delimited token-id fixture replacing tokenizer/chat rendering, used to replay an exact served prompt. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_BENCH_ITERS` / `MEMRA_STEP_EP_BENCH_WARMUP` | ep-step-fp8-gate | Enable the EP microbenchmark with 1..1000 measured iterations and 0..1000 warmups (warmup default 5). An absent/zero iteration count keeps benchmark mode off. |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_DYNAMIC_CAPACITY_TOKENS` / `MEMRA_STEP_EP_NUMERIC_ITERS` | ep-step-fp8-gate | Dynamic owner-grouped workspace capacity (default initial token count + 2) and numeric probe repetitions (default 50, must be positive). |
| `MEMRA_TP_DEVICES` / `MEMRA_TP_TOKENS` / `MEMRA_TP_REPS` | Step TP/EP gates | Comma-separated gate device ordinals (normally `0,1`; allowed rank count depends on the gate), test token count (gate-specific default 1, 2, or 3), and interleave count for `tp-step-resident-device-gate` (default 5 — host-arm/device-arm alternation per rep, the box-drift protocol). These select fixture topology and gate repetition only. |
| `MEMRA_TP_GATE_GROW` / `MEMRA_TP_GATE_SWA` | tp-step-attention-gate | `1` exercises transaction growth or the official sliding-window layer; invalid values and an SWA request on a non-window layer fail closed. |
| `MEMRA_CPU_FILE_IDENTITY_KEEP=<path>` | cpu-native-check | persistent fixture path (written only if absent, never removed) so shm-cache warm restarts can be exercised across processes; default is an unlinked per-process temp file |
| `MEMRA_CPU_NATIVE_BENCH_QTYPE` / `MEMRA_CPU_NATIVE_BENCH_THREADS` | cpu-native-check (under `MEMRA_CPU_NATIVE_BENCH`) | bench quant type (`Q8_0/Q4_0/Q2_K/Q3_K/Q4_K/Q5_K/Q6_K/IQ4_XS...`, default Q2_K) / OMP threads (default 8) |
| `MEMRA_GV_CAUSAL=1` | gemma-vision-e2e | run the WRONG arm end-to-end (sets `MEMRA_GV_FORCE_CAUSAL=1`): overlay rows spliced but plain causal mask — the ship-by-analogy bug, kept as the probe's negative control |
| `MEMRA_LOCKSTEP_M` | run-lockstep | lockstep stream count (`1..=16`, default 2) |
| `MEMRA_INVALID_PINNED_FRAC_TEST_CHILD` | cargo test (spill.rs) | test-internal self-exec marker for the invalid-pinned-frac child-process test; never set by hand |
| `MEMRA_PROMPTS_FILE=<file>` | run-lockstep | one prompt per line, one per stream — the mixed-workload regime (same-prompt streams route identically and overstate expert overlap). Default: `MEMRA_PROMPT` for every stream (the identity-gate regime) |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_DIR=<dir>` | run-spec | BENCH-SWEEP mode: iterate every `*.txt` prompt in the dir with ONE model load |
| `MEMRA_BENCH_QT` | gu-wall-bench | expert-quant candidate: `iq3s` (default) \| `iq4xs` \| `nvfp4` |
| `MEMRA_SG_SEEDS` / `MEMRA_SG_TOKENS` / `MEMRA_SG_TEMP` / `MEMRA_SG_TOPK` / `MEMRA_SG_TOPP` / `MEMRA_SG_REP` / `MEMRA_SG_FREQ` / `MEMRA_SG_PRESENT` / `MEMRA_SG_LASTN` / `MEMRA_SG_PROMPT` | dspark_sample_gate | hist-mode distribution gate for the dspark route's SAMPLED admission (lane/dspark-sampled-admission-20260820): seeds per arm (2000) / positions compared (4) / temperature (0.8) / top-k (0) / top-p (1.0) / repetition penalty (1.0 = off) / frequency penalty (0.0) / presence penalty (0.0) / penalty window (8,192 tokens, the serve API's cross-path bound; lane/dspark-penalized-sampled-20260821 — with a penalty set BOTH arms carry it, the plain arm through the same boundary-sampler penalty pass, so hist gates penalized spec against penalized trunk-only) / comma-separated prompt token ids (default a fixed 24-id prompt). Spec-on vs trunk-only per-position two-sample chi-square; the tiny mode takes no knobs; t0 additionally gates the penalties-at-T=0 refusal |
| `MEMRA_NMEASURE_REPS` | run-gen | steady-state benchmark repetitions (default 1, min 1) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_STATS=1` | (engine, gemma graph door) | print `[gemma-graph] captures=/buckets=` at loop end (`hybrid_forward.rs`) |
| `MEMRA_RESIDENCY_CENSUS=1` | run-gen | print the model residency census report — the primary evidence separating "arm ran, flat" from "arm never engaged on this checkpoint" |

## 5. Experimental doors (opt-in, documented block)

> **DFlash2 qualification boundary:** The 2026-08-24 RTX PRO 6000 LOW/HIGH
> throughput and demotion ladder below is **greedy only**. Historical gates prove route
> correctness, but ordinary omitted-temperature serving is sampled and may carry vendor
> penalties; that c1-to-saturation DFlash2 path remains unqualified, and sampled sessions
> intentionally do not demote. While one is live, it also blocks a later greedy request from
> widening through LOW: otherwise the pair would serialize two DFlash bursts below HIGH. The
> route remains default-OFF and must not be flipped in production until a current-head
> served-default sampled-plus-penalty ladder is green.

| flag | door | block |
|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC=1` + `MEMRA_DSPARK_DRAFT=<export_dir>` | dspark drafter spec route for the qwen-hybrid trunk (lane/dspark-q38-recover): one `DflashDraft` per non-gemma model, `DsparkSpecSession` bursts on the gemma-route policy shape — greedy (argmax verify, unpenalized — penalized greedy stays on the plain path and the engine refuses temp==0+penalties by name) OR sampled (temperature>0 rides the route's rejection-sampling verify, lane/dspark-sampled-admission-20260820; penalties INCLUDED since lane/dspark-penalized-sampled-20260821 — the accept walk penalizes the verify columns over the true per-state window, within-round accepts included), unconstrained, text-only, COLD sessions only; GREEDY admission takes the spec-gate LOW band wave-projected (`projected_admission_wave <= LOW` — this request + arriving wave + HTTP in-flight; a wave never admits a drafting session it is about to demote) and a live greedy session DEMOTES to batched plain decode at `n_live >= HIGH` via `DsparkSpecSession::into_demoted` (byte-identity gate: plain == dspark == dspark+MEMRA_SPEC_DEMOTE_AT, 16/16, PRO 6000 2026-08-24, darklanes research/dflash2-pro6000-20260824; ladder receipts: c=1 +1.7%, warm c=4/c=8 at plain parity 273.8/429 vs the pre-lane -37% c=8 solo-burst drag); SAMPLED sessions share the greedy LOW band since lane/dspark-sampled-wave-20260825, and since lane/dspark-low-band-stack-20260825 dspark rows STACK inside that band (wave-projection-bounded at LOW, the same bound MTP's sampled admission has; sampled rows still never demote — session-owned philox; `MEMRA_DSPARK_SAMPLED_WAVE=0` restores the pre-stack posture: solo sampled AND the one-row block; see the `MEMRA_DSPARK_SAMPLED_WAVE` row), and `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` or an operator `MEMRA_SPEC_K>0` pin restores solo-only admission alongside the no-automatic-demotion policy; `MEMRA_SPEC_K=0` pins plain and refuses DFlash2 admission entirely. Arming it DISABLES the MTP spec arm for that model and the server refuses un-co-gated combinations at boot (`MEMRA_SPEC_DFLASH`, `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC`, `MEMRA_PP_STAGES>1`, armed nonzero `MEMRA_STEP_TP`, or armed nonzero `MEMRA_STEP_EP`) — two spec programs on one model must never silently coexist. Unset = route absent, zero served-path change | Runtime default OFF: the server never auto-attaches a DFlash2 artifact — arming is the operator's explicit opt-in. It is, however, the qualified SERVING route for Qwen3.8-27B on both measured card classes: on RTX PRO 6000 it beats the MTP arm on every rung of the vendor-default sampled shape (c1 127/117, c2 128/120, c4 87/85 agg tok/s, x3 interleaved, darklanes research/dflash2-pro6000-20260824/stack-cell/) and has carried both production q38 origins since 2026-08-25 (v0.113.0, deploy gates with sampled speculation receipts); on RTX 5090 the q4 + target-head-rank recipe is the measured opt-in (+3.87% E2E). Per-card recipes in `docs/COOKBOOK.md`. That model/hardware evidence does not transfer to other GPUs. The older box-2 cum1000 1.00x own-traffic / 1.11x math result remains historical evidence for the untrimmed recover-era artifact, not a prohibition on the newer 5090 recipe. |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_SAMPLED_WAVE` | on | Sampled DFlash2 admission takes the spec-gate LOW band wave-projected, exactly like greedy (lane/dspark-sampled-wave-20260825; owner serving law: the vendor-default serve shape IS sampled — under the old solo law the route lost speculation for the fleet's dominant traffic whenever anything else was live, measured on the 2026-08-25 DE flip). Sampled sessions still never demote; since lane/dspark-low-band-stack-20260825 dspark rows STACK inside the LOW band (the one-non-demotable-row block was analogy, refuted by the MTP arm on the same box: two sampled MTP sessions at c=2 = 121.6 vs the blocked 1-spec+1-plain mix's 105.9) — the wave projection bounds the count at LOW, exactly as it bounds MTP's own sampled admission, and greedy rows among them still demote at HIGH. `0` restores the solo law without touching the greedy band. Serving default requires the sampled ladder receipts in darklanes research/dflash2-pro6000-20260824/sampled-wave/ — this row ships in the same PR as the flag, per the flags law. |
| `MEMRA_NONSTREAM_DEADLINE_GATE` | on | Refuse a NON-STREAMING request whose pessimistic estimate cannot finish inside its `timeout_ms` deadline, at admission — 400 `nonstream_deadline_infeasible`, naming the `max_tokens` that WOULD fit and the streaming alternative. Costs no slot, opens no receipt, burns no GPU. `0` lets the request run; on `/v1/completions` and `/v1/chat/completions` it is then covered by partial delivery, but on `/v1/messages` and `/v1/responses` it 408s and discards the work as before (those dialects have no honest partial shape — see SERVING.md), so turning the gate off is strictly worse there. Default ON with receipts (darklanes research/nonstream-deadline-20260826): the owner-reported bug was a 30,278-token non-streaming request answering **408 after 90.7 s with every generated token discarded**, while the SAME work streamed 200 in 93.8 s. Measured ladder at that prompt — 4096 out 52.0 s, 5120 61.9 s, 6144 71.5 s (200); 8192 90.7 s, 16384 91.5 s (408) — and the gate's boundary is pinned to it by test (`the_feasibility_gate_boundary_matches_the_measured_ladder`): allows through 6144, refuses 8192+. Streaming is NEVER gated (its deadline bounds only time-to-first-token). A ctx-bounded request (no `max_tokens`) is never gated either — the owner ruling is that context is then its only limit. Rollback seam: this flag; the partial-delivery half has no flag because discarding a customer's tokens is not a posture worth preserving. |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_FLOOR_TOK_S` | 2000 | Pessimistic prefill rate for the feasibility estimate ONLY (never billing, never admission accounting). Deliberately below measurement — ~2.9k tok/s observed at 30k tokens on the serving cards — because a tight estimate would refuse requests that succeed, and a false refusal is worse than a slow success. Matches the floor the api-router already uses for its own header budget. |
| `MEMRA_DECODE_FLOOR_TOK_S` | 60 | Pessimistic decode rate for the same estimate. The slowest arm measured through the customer path on the current fleet is ~100 tok/s (verbose prose at 30k context); 60 leaves headroom for a busier box. Together with `DEADLINE_INFEASIBLE_MARGIN_PCT` (150, compile-time) this is what places the refusal boundary between the measured 6144-pass and 8192-fail. |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_COLD_DECODE_RATIO_DIV` | 8 | The DFlash cold-prime-vs-prefix-hit trade, as a ratio: a request whose usable decode budget is below `prompt_len / DIV` DECLINES the cold prime, keeps the prefix probe, and serves the hit on the plain path. Bypassing a hit to cold-prime spends the prefill the hit would have skipped in order to buy speculation on the decode — so it only pays when the decode is long enough. Before this the decision read route readiness, sampling shape and LOAD only, and never either side's size: a 30,312-token prompt answered in 86 tokens paid a **9.6 s** cold prime with `lcp=30312` — a FULL-prompt hit — sitting unused (darklanes research/nonstream-deadline-20260826/CONTINUATION.md). With the measured fleet rates (~2.9k tok/s prefill, ~100-300 tok/s decode, dspark accept 0.68-0.74) break-even sits near decode ≈ prompt/10, so the default of 8 is that boundary with margin toward KEEPING the old behaviour; at a 30k prompt the request must be able to generate ~3.8k tokens before it cold-primes. Prompts below `PREFIX_CACHE_MIN_TOKENS` are exempt (no hit can exist for them). **Larger DIV = more willing to cold-prime; a very large value restores the pre-lane posture** — that is the rollback seam. Boot-visible when it fires: `[dspark] cache-preferred: prompt=… decode_budget=…`. Boundary, the unchanged long-decode case, the probe re-enable, and the cache-off arm are pinned by test. TWO COVERAGE LIMITS, stated because the win is easy to overclaim: (a) the veto only fires when a hit ACTUALLY EXISTS for this prompt (`prefix_on` AND `px.lookup` finds a whole-entry full prefix at or above `PREFIX_CACHE_MIN_TOKENS` — the same lookup the restore consumes; a bare `best_lcp > 0` is NOT sufficient, it matches any overlap with no floor) — with the cache off, or on the first request of a prompt class whose probe will miss, a declined request would lose the cold prime and get nothing back, so it cold-primes as before; (b) it binds on the request's CLAMPED decode budget, so a client that OMITS `max_tokens` gets the remaining context window as its budget (8192 on the default `MEMRA_CTX`) and the flagship 30k shape does NOT trip the ratio. The fix therefore covers clients that DECLARE a small `max_tokens`; unbounded long-context traffic still cold-primes and needs the restorable-draft-plane lane instead. |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_PREFIX_RESTORE` | **off** | Let a cross-request prefix hit RE-ARM a dspark session instead of forcing a cold prime, by restoring the drafter's KV tail published on the entry (lane/dspark-draft-plane-20260827). Without it a speculating request must discard even a full-prompt hit and re-prefill — ~10 s at 30k tokens — because the draft KV derives from trunk hidden FEATURES that a KV restore cannot return; the tail travels on the entry so both halves become restorable. Only the READABLE tail is stored (`sliding_window + block` rows/layer ≈ 85 MB at 30k, against ~1,229 MB for the full history, which would exceed the entry's own ~1,057 MB of trunk planes): every DFlash2 draft layer is `sliding_attention`, asserted at load, so a round at `pos` reads `[pos-window+1, pos+block)` and nothing older. WHOLE-ENTRY hits only — the GDN hybrid trunk cannot rebuild recurrent state mid-sequence. Two guards: the probe is only re-enabled for a hit the conversion will actually consume — tail present AND whole-entry cover (`dspark_hit_is_restorable`; probing a tail-less entry would hand a long-decode request a plain hit and trade its speculation away, and a strict-prefix hit — the multi-turn shape — would pay the full carrier restore only to be dropped, review round 3), and a hit that cannot re-arm is DROPPED so the cold prime still happens rather than downgrading to plain. KERNEL-ARM COMPOSABILITY: `from_tail` ZEROES the rows below the tail base, so the restore is byte-exact under BOTH SDPA arms — the default clipped kernel never reads them, and the legacy full-scan rollback (`MEMRA_DFLASH2_SDPA_CLIP=0`) reads every row relying on masked rows contributing exactly zero, which holds only for finite data (an uninit NaN K row poisons the softmax sum; review round 3). A clip rollback on a restore-armed box therefore stays correct instead of silently decoding garbage. **Default OFF by decision:** operator opt-in, consistent with the rest of the dspark stack (`MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC` itself is opt-in) — prod arms it in the serving launcher, which is also the rollback seam. The flag gates BOTH sides: consumption (probe override + conversion) and PUBLICATION (the tail export at the boundary capture), so unset restores the pre-lane behaviour exactly, memory profile included — no ~85 MB/entry tail is stored with no possible consumer (review round 5; ~8% of a 30k entry, ~34% of a 7k one, all charged to the SLRU byte budget). After arming, real long-decode traffic republishes tailed entries within minutes, so a flag flip needs no pre-armed entries. **GATED 2026-08-27** on a cloudbox bench (2x RTX PRO 6000 Server, 500 W to match prod): restored-vs-cold BYTE-IDENTICAL over 24,890 output chars, accept trajectory identical 125/125 bursts, 56.3 s → 46.8 s (the ~9.5 s prefill back); the short shape keeps the plain hit both arms, byte-identical (darklanes research/dspark-trunk-hit-20260827/evidence/restore-gate-run4-PASS.log + LONG-ANSWER-DESIGN.md, which also records the three defects the gate caught en route — a wrong-shaped gate arm, a tail cut at the wrong boundary, and the conversion overriding the shape guard for tiny budgets). CONVERSION SCOPE: only a request that would have COLD-PRIMED converts (`dspark_prefers_cold`) — the #55 behavior split: short answers keep the plain hit, long decodes restore. The formerly-named "tiny-budget hazard" was ROOT-CAUSED on the bench (2026-08-27) and was never a budget property: the dispatch flag `dspark_on` was computed before the restore fold, so a restored session could be stepped by PLAIN `step_session` over its absent cache, decoding coherent garbage from an empty context. Fixed twice over — the flag now derives from the actually-installed session at the Session literal, and `step_session` loudly REFUSES any session holding a dspark session, turning a future disagreement into a request error instead of wrong customer output. A briefly-added absolute decode floor (4096) justified by the then-unexplained hazard is REMOVED with the root cause: 414-prompt/64-budget restores byte-exact under correct dispatch, measured. Restored requests bill `cached = prompt` (the whole prompt came from cache) — the fold order that briefly billed cached=0 is also fixed and the accounting rides the resume seam. |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH2_SDPA_CLIP` | on | `0` restores the LEGACY full-scan windowed SDPA for the DFlash2 round attention (`sdpa_naive_w`: dynamic shared memory sized `T_kv*4` bytes and an O(ctx) key scan) — the rollback seam for the lo-clipped kernel (lane/dflash2-longctx-20260821) and the long-ctx gate's crash-reproduction arm. Default ON uses `sdpa_naive_w_lo`: keys below every query's window floor are never read, shared memory is `(window-1+T)*4` bytes (~8KB at window 2048) and the launch bound that killed the route above ~12k ctx (GATES-SMOKE-20260821 B2: DriverError(CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE) at ctx 16,571/30,157, last success 9,510) is gone. BYTE-IDENTICAL output (masked keys contribute exact zeros to same-order reductions; kernel_check `sdpa_naive_w_lo` cell pins bit-identity at Tkv 4104 and, as teeth, the legacy launch failure at Tkv 16392), and a measured wall WIN at agent ctx (the O(window) scan: dspark_q38_gate mean spec 123.8 vs 104.1 tok/s on 0.6-8.9k-token real agentic prompts, box NJ 2026-08-21). DFlash2 route only; the cum1000/markov full-attention arm keeps the T_kv*4 bound (a full-attention drafter cannot clip — named residual). Never off in a measured default run |
| `MEMRA_MMQ=1` (+`MEMRA_RP=0`) | native W4A4 FP4 MMQ prefill. Uncorrected (`MEMRA_MMQ_RESIDUAL_K=0`) 1.53–1.64x our default; with the k=32 residual correction 1.34–1.43x (q9, three prompt lengths, interleaved x5, 2026-08-03) | STILL EXACTNESS-BLOCKED as a general sm_120a default, block NARROWED not lifted (2026-08-03, lane/w4a4-rescue, receipts `research/w4a4-rescue-20260803/`). The k=32 residual correction fixes the original three-prompt reject corpus — 5/5 cells token-IDENTICAL 48/48, reproduced across four kernel revisions — and the battery is green under it (kernel-check ALL GREEN, run-gen argmax MATCH q9+q27, run-spec K=1..8 PASS, `logs/battery-k32.log`). But WIDENING the corpus to five previously-untested prompts broke it: 4/10 cells identical (`residual-k32-wide.jsonl`). Five of those six divergences are inside the reference arm's own entry-point noise (near-tie drift). The sixth, q27/board-2048, forks at token 0 with 0/48 agreeing, deterministic 3/3 (`residual-k32-board-rep{1,2}.jsonl`); a k sweep on that cell (`board-k*.jsonl`) shows it diverging at 8 of 9 depths including k=0, so it is a prompt W4A4 forks on largely independent of k, not a k=32 defect. Either way the corpus is not clean, so 32 is not a global default. **sm_100a hardware result (2026-09-01):** true W4A4 requires BOTH flags because an RP weight always forces W4A8. The tcgen05/TMEM twin passed the exact synthetic oracle, 64-token real-model equality, and K=1..8 on a pinned Qwen3.5-9B artifact, but pp1483 measured 4,758.6 versus 9,134.3 tok/s for raw-layout W4A8 = **0.521x**. It remains explicit and is rejected as the B200 default. Receipts: `research/b200-kernel-twins-dry-20260901/receipts/b200-e2e-nvfp4-20260901T1804Z/`. |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_RESIDUAL_K=<k>` (default 0) | residual outlier channels kept in f32 alongside the W4A4 path: the k largest-amax activation channels are zeroed before quantization and their exact contribution is added back as a rank-k update. Clamped to 64, rounded up to a compile-time bucket (8/16/32/64) whose padding lanes are numeric no-ops | **32 is the exactness point, and it is a plateau — not a monotone trend.** Token-identical cells on the ORIGINAL reject corpus (`residual-k*.jsonl`, 5 measurable cells): k=4 → 3/5, k=8 → 2/5, k=16 → 3/5, **k=32 → 5/5** (reproduced across 4 kernel revisions), k=64 → 0/5. On five ADDITIONAL prompts k=32 drops to 4/10 (`residual-k32-wide.jsonl`), so 32 is tuned to that corpus and is NOT a general exactness setting. Do not assume "larger k is safer". Costs 1.62x → 1.34x of the uncorrected arm. Read live per call, since the gate sweeps arms inside one process |
