Block = `(32, n_sel)`: one warp per routed slot instead of one warp per (row, slot), and the `n_sel x out_f` partial-buffer round trip disappears. BIT-IDENTICAL to `qmatvec_nvfp4_sel_into` + `axpy_rows_seq_md_into`: same dot program, same 32-lane reduce tree, and warp 0 lane 0 replays the slot-ordered accumulation of `axpy_rows_seq_md_f32` in its own expression shape so the same compiler contraction applies. **DEFAULT ON SINCE 2026-09-01**, with `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM` as the coupled half of the same decision (see that row: this door alone is inert, and that is receipted, not assumed). It is the only one of the three restored programs with a measured win, it is bit-identical, and per the better-wins-by-default law that is a default-ON with a kill switch. Successor to `MEMRA_SEL_DOWN8`, which is REFUSED at boot: the old name required the old bank door, so one env var moved three programs. **THE DEFAULT IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKS, AND THE RECEIPT SAYS SO:** `default ON` and `engaged` are two different facts here, because the call-site eligibility below (device-routed, slot-major, `nsb <= 32`) can disqualify the arm on a route or geometry the operator did not expect. Only the `[nvfp4-sweep] down8=… door=… door_source=…` line settles the second, and `door_source` distinguishes 'armed by the 2026-09-01 default' from 'armed by a recipe' from 'rolled back by the seam' — a default-flip receipt that cannot tell those apart has not measured the default. **ELIGIBILITY, all checked at the call site**: device-routed arm only (the host-routed arm folds the macro into `combine_w` instead of reading `md` on device), the down shard must report `slot_major`, and `nsb = local_in >> 5` must be `<= 32` — the fit-block class the reduce identity is argued at, launcher-enforced together with `n_sel <= 8` so `s_dot[8]` cannot overflow. **BOTH ARMS.** OFF = `_sel_v2` down sweep + `axpy_rows_seq_md_into`, byte-for-byte the prior path. ON = the fused sweep, which produces the accumulator itself (the combine is skipped, not duplicated). **ROLLBACK SEAM**: `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8=0` rolls back this program alone (keeping the bit-identical slot-major layout); `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM=0` rolls back both programs at once. Both seams are exercised as live arms, not just documented: the joint seam is the 6-boot `flip-off` arm of the qualification battery, and the SURGICAL seam (down8 `=0` alone on the flipped default, layout still slot-major) is the `flip-d8off` arm (raw/boot-D8OFF1.receipt: `layout=slot-major source=default-on` + `down8=false door=false door_source=env=0 (rollback seam)` + `slot_major=true`, engagement verified from the server log after a live request; the composition's output program was byte-gated in milestone 4's `v3-sm` arm). **GATES: ALL GREEN** — oracle DIFF=0 against `_sel` + `axpy_rows_seq_md` on the down geometry (the gate/up geometry is out of class at nsb=128 and the instrument says so rather than skipping silently), and the 18-cell greedy tape identical on the cumulative arm. **PRICED, x5 interleaved fresh boots, vendor-default sampled, engagement proven: THIS IS THE ONLY ONE OF THE THREE THAT SEPARATES.** decode **113.83 vs 107.91 (+5.48%)**, wall **107.75 vs 102.53 (+5.09%)**, within-arm spread 1.97%/2.33%, and the per-boot range `[112.59, 114.82]` has **NO OVERLAP** with the OFF arm's `[105.11, 107.09]` — nor with `layout+gu`'s own `[105.48, 111.44]`, so it separates from the arm directly beneath it. NOT a loop or acceptance artifact: **zero** loop exclusions in 20 boots and acceptance FALLS as the doors arm (0.7926 → 0.7747), the opposite of the degenerate-output signature; TTFT flat at 0.243–0.246 s, as a decode-sweep fusion should be. **CONTEXT FOR THE DEFAULT DECISION:** the removed bundle was priced at −21.5% wall / −23.7% decode, and all three programs together measure **+6.60% decode / +6.89% wall** here — ~3.6× smaller. The era measurement's door-ON arm ran the corrupted program and its own receipt shows acceptance 0.970 vs 0.935, the inflation signature; treat the ~24% figure as an artifact of the defect it was measured on, not as headroom still owed. **DEPLOY-GRADE RE-QUALIFICATION FOR THE DEFAULT FLIP (2026-09-01, the 12-boot battery this row previously said it owed):** measured as the DEFAULT on a flipped binary with NO door env set, against the rollback seam (`=0`) as the other arm, 6 interleaved fresh boots each, both request shapes out of the same boots, 12 distinct boot nonces, one binary. **Vendor-default sampled (the serving shape): decode 106.76 -> 112.57 (+5.44%), wall 101.20 -> 107.19 (+5.92%).** Greedy instrument: decode 113.68 -> 121.12 (+6.54%). RULE A fires on every arm and RULE B on both deltas, so the medians decide nothing and the NON-OVERLAP test decides: per-boot ranges `[105.58, 108.64]` vs `[111.16, 113.80]` decode and `[100.80, 103.75]` vs `[106.13, 107.72]` wall -- **SEPARATED on both metrics in both request shapes, 4 of 4**, with the per-boot sign **UNANIMOUS 6/6**. Not an artifact: **zero** loop exclusions in 192 reps, acceptance FALLS (0.785 -> 0.780), TTFT flat, spec engaged in every rep. 8-TURN CACHE TWIN (cache on, spec on, real conversations to **41,474** prompt tokens): the win holds in **16/16 turns (+3.99% to +6.08%)** and does not decay with depth, all 16 turns' output **byte-identical**, acceptance identical to 4 decimals, `cached_tokens` identical between arms, cache engagement proven by a bust probe (170,080 -> 0 cached, TTFT 3.09x slower). **ELIGIBILITY IS NARROWER THAN THE DEFAULT** -- unset the door and the program still needs `device_routed` and `nsb <= 32`; on the prod launcher that means `MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES=1` must be set or the default is INERT, which the `[nvfp4-sweep]` line settles in one grep. RESIDENCY CONSTRAINT: the mirror is ~0.9 GB per card and does NOT fit beside a 262144-token cache reservation on 96 GB — the first long-context cell died in CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY, and the interleave above therefore ran MEMRA_MAX_CTX=16384 on BOTH arms. A serving flip needs either a smaller ctx reservation or dropping the bf16 slab for decode-only ranks. The door also covers the bf16 GEMVs OUTSIDE the TP banks — the LM head, the shared-expert rows and the dense-FFN layers — through a pointer-keyed mirror cache built on first decode use (`matvec_bf16_via_q8_mirror`), which adds roughly 1.7 GB per card on top of the 0.9 GB the attention mirrors cost. **BOTH ARMS (QKV + o_proj), ctx 4081, interleaved x3, tip 98d601256:** off 75.74/75.78/75.93 -> on 80.27/80.78/80.29 tok/s = **+6.1%**, argmax gate MATCH in every cell at logit maxdiff 2.055e0. That is 12.46 ms/token against the 13.19 ms baseline; 90 tok/s needs 11.1 ms, so the measured remainder is a q8 head (-0.16 ms), q8 shexp down (-0.33), q8 dense FFN on layers 0-2 (~-0.22) and then a W4-class attention projection running at the ~1.9 TB/s the q8 mmvq path already demonstrates (~-0.48). |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_FA2` | off | T-ROW verify attention over the per-session fa table kernel (supersedes the dcw2 pair-join 2026-08-24): the walk stashes q+gate per column (append lands per column), then ONE `fa_decode_vec_q_v3_dcw_rows` per rank attends every row — each (row, head, split) block IS the t=1 dcw program with that row's own ring/len/base and split geometry (len_back = t-1-r for same-session verify rows), so per-row bit-identity holds at ANY t in [2,8] INCLUDING capped SWA (the dcw2 partition guard + SWA refusal dissolved; same-session rows dedup K/V through L2). Combine = t=1 kernel over t*n_head stacked heads (empty splits are the NEG_INF no-op). Launcher embeds the big-rig split ladder and refuses env split overrides. GATES: run-spec SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS K=1..8 (tiny) + K=1,2 at agentic ctx. Spec ladder at agentic turn-8 ctx: 50.6 -> 54.4 (rows fa) -> 62.4 (head convert killed: the FloatBf16 chunked matmul dequanted the whole 1.24GB LM head per verify round; bf16 rows fast path = t=1 head program per row) -> 66.87 (verify rope pass: same-session rope-rows, 3 launches/rank/layer) = 0.97x eager at accept 89.6%. Requires MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_EAGER=1 + MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_TCOL=1. |

| `MEMRA_SPEC_FA2_LAYER` | unset | Debug bisection: engage the fa2 join on ONE layer index only (all other layers run the ordinary per-column program). Divergence isolation; no use in serving. |

Prime rate 17.2->126 tok/s. IDENTITY: the served token ids match the tokenwise-class run-gen reference exactly `[764,624,15059,2859,223,19,63,342]`; the server's own host-canonical batched prime is the outlier (`20` at slot 6), so this arm moves serving TOWARD the oracle. Still off by default pending the owner's flip. PREFILL-BY-T-ROWS (the #2 serving arc, 2026-08-25): prime the prompt through the same-session t-row walk in 32-row chunks — one rope/append + one fa + one o_proj join + one t-row MoE sweep per rank per layer per chunk, every row the t=1 decode program bit-for-bit. This is the TOKENWISE-prime ORACLE class (strictly more conservative than the batched host-canonical prime it replaces), so the gate is token identity vs MEMRA_PRIME_TOKENWISE=1. Motivation receipt: step37 TP2 host-canonical prime = ~17 tok/s (4.1k prompt = ~240 s TTFT; the memra-server bench 408d on it) vs vLLM prefill 0.6 s. | `MEMRA_TEMP` / `MEMRA_TOP_K` / `MEMRA_TOP_P` / `MEMRA_MIN_P` / `MEMRA_SEED` (safetensors path) | greedy | **FIXED 2026-08-25:** the safetensors TEXT path IGNORED these and still printed `(ST greedy decode)`, so every sampled step37 measurement taken with run-gen was really greedy — the sampler it parsed belonged to the GGUF `generate_with` path. `MEMRA_TEMP>0` now arms the serving device sampler INSIDE the async chain (`filter_stats` -> `gumbel_perturb_filtered_col` -> argmax into the resident `token_d`), so a sampled stream keeps the chain's no-host-sync property and the per-step counter gives each token its own draw. Sampling forces the PLAIN head, because the split-head fast path never materializes a logits row. The bench line now names the shape it measured. Owner rule this serves: "we dont serve greedy, for real benchmarking we use sampling". **MEASURED 2026-08-25 (W8 armed, ctx 4081, chain sampling on device):** greedy 82.21 tok/s, temp-only sampling 82.12, filtered (top_k 20 + top_p 0.95) 78.72. So UNFILTERED sampling is free once the per-token sampler allocations are hoisted, and the entire remaining sampling cost is the top_k/top_p threshold search — `filter_stats_coop_f32` measured ~500 us per token, one instance per token, which is ~4% of a 12.7 ms token and the next thing to attack in that path. Two bugs were fixed on the way here: the chunk-boundary token was drawn by a host argmax (one token in eight was greedy in a run that called itself sampled), and sampling had been giving up HEAD_SPLIT although the split path does materialize the full row. |

At the model's NATURAL 262144-token context this OOMed — **until `MEMRA_SWA_RING=1` freed 16.4 GB on card0 (2026-08-26); with the ring the whole door fits with ~15-17 GB spare and PAYS: W8 80.16 and W8+hybrid 81.46 against plain 75.97, i.e. +5.5% and +7.2% at the served context.** The hybrid half, which measured +0.1% at MAX_CTX=16384, earns +1.6% at natural ctx where cache pressure is real — so its default-off is now a memory choice, not a measurement one. **THE DOOR NOW COVERS THE SPEC VERIFY WALK TOO (2026-08-26):** nsys over a K=1 spec window put `matvec_bf16_b4_tcol` at 24.8% of GPU time and `matvec_bf16_qkvg_tcol` at 12.3% — 37% of the verify still streaming bf16 because only the decode twins had been replaced. `qmatvec_q8_0_qkv_rp_t` / `_b4_rp_t` (one warp per (row, column), per-row program identical to the single-row kernels) fix that: spec K=1 at agentic ctx goes **58.44/58.14 -> 66.01/66.08 tok/s (+13%)** with verify-wait 4310 -> 3484 ms (-19%), and because the PRIME rides the same walk its wall drops **33.74 -> 28.51 s (-15.5%)** on a 4092-token prompt. run-spec SELF-CONSISTENCY PASS both arms (output identical to the plain target), acceptance unchanged (230 vs 231 drafted). Verify logits decide what is accepted, so this is the same numeric class and the same argmax acceptance as the rest of W8. |
| `MEMRA_W8_VIEW` | off | Strict `1`: extends the W8 hybrid half (`MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8` + `MEMRA_W8_HYBRID`) to the bf16 GEMVs whose weight is a ROW-RANGE VIEW of a resident slab, i.e. the lo halves that `MEMRA_HEAD_SPLIT` and `MEMRA_SHEXP_OVERLAP` keep on rank 0 (LM head lo 4096x64448, shexp down lo). Those halves run CONCURRENTLY with their q8-mirrored hi twins on the other card, so the slow bf16 half was the critical path: the byte ledger prices them at 0.16 + 0.17 ms/token (`research/step37-decode-headroom-20260828/census-ledger-at-78.6.txt`). Mechanism: a view-typed twin of the mirror path (`matvec_bf16_view_via_q8_mirror` + `encode_q8_0_from_bf16_view`, identical kernel and per-row program), engaged from `matvec_bf16_into`'s view branch only when W8 + hybrid are already on. Landing it forced a mirror-cache correctness fix that applies to ALL W8 mirrors: the cache key is now `(ptr, in_f, out_f)`, not the pointer alone, because a view starting at row 0 carries the PARENT slab's base pointer and a pointer-only key would hand the lo half the FULL head's mirror and read 2x past its rows. Engagement is announced unconditionally (`[w8-view] mirror built ...`), one line per distinct shape, so a sweep can prove the door in BOTH directions. NOT armed by `arm_step37_serving_defaults`; unset or `=0` is the rollback seam. NUMERIC CLASS: same class and argmax acceptance as the rest of `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8` (weight-precision q8_0 mirror of bf16 rows); run-gen decode argmax MATCH on real prompts at logit maxdiff 9.546e-2 (curve-0400, equal to the w8view=0 baseline's own 9.546e-2) and 1.034e-1 (curve-1000), the `MEMRA_BF16_MMV` ~9e-2 pass class, with the door ENGAGED (view_mirrors=1). **MEASURED INERT ON THE SERVED ROUTE (2026-08-28, interleaved x5 vendor-default sampled, tip 553a072471+patch):** plainv 78.46 vs plain 78.41 (+0.1%), and the mirror announce fired in ZERO of 20 server boots including every `=1` arm, because the served step37 decode (`MEMRA_STEP_TP_DECODE_V2` t-walk / spec verify walk) never calls `matvec_bf16_view_into`; only host `decode_step_h` and `decode_step_chain` (run-gen's paths) do. The specv arm's 94.61 vs spec 92.13 is spec noise (spec's own spread 89.66..97.69), not this door. **Default stays OFF**: it buys nothing in serving until the walk's head/shexp lo GEMVs are routed through the view mirror, and the byte ledger caps that prize at ~0.33 ms/token. The `(ptr, in_f, out_f)` mirror-key fix that landed with it applies to ALL W8 mirrors and stands regardless. Receipts: `research/step37-decode-headroom-20260828/` (`RESULTS.md`, `s37h-spec-results.txt`). |

The t-row sweep runs one slot per (column, expert) and re-reads an expert both columns chose, the same 'weight re-read per (token,slot)' class the NVFP4 CSR twin fixed elsewhere (+6.8% at B=8). Measured marginals on step37 (2026-08-26, sync-bounded `MEMRA_TCOL_PROF`): per extra verify column norm+qkv ~0.02 ms (fully amortized), attn 2.6-2.9 ms, ffn 4.0-4.5 ms — so the MoE is the biggest marginal term and worth deduping only if the real overlap is large. One dtoh per layer while on; diagnostic only. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS` | unset | Gate/battery instrumentation for the qwen4_exp eager arm (`qwen4exp_gpu::apply_env_seams`, called by the qwen4exp gate binaries only): comma list of `--ab-seam` names (`projstack`, `hcmicro`, `selv3`, `vgraph`, ..., optionally `name=0`) applied to the seam setters at startup, so the tiny + real gates can prove a NEW seam green while its shipped library default is still OFF (flags law: correctness receipts precede the default flip). Never read by serving; the library defaults are the `*_DEFAULT` consts in qwen4exp_gpu.rs with their receipts in research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/. **SCOPING CORRECTION 2026-08-31 (round 2):** `qwen4exp_real_gate` pins the cache seams to the f32 exactness instruments (`set_kv_quant(false)` / `set_idxq("f32")`) unless this variable names them — and that pin used to be UNCONDITIONAL, which its own comment did not say. It therefore made the binary an f32-only instrument: the long-context ladder, the spec-at-depth cells and the TP2 gates are not reference-parity runs, yet every one of them silently measured the f32 cache while being written up as running the ship defaults. Cost: a full 100k ladder rung (~9 min of prefill) before a state-alloc probe against `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=kvq` read 91,475 MiB against the default arm's 96,243 MiB — the 4,768 MiB the default was supposed to be delivering. The pin is now scoped to runs that actually make a golden comparison (`--goldens` or `--prompts`), and **every receipt header carries a `# cache kv_quant=... idxq=... golden_pin=... seams_env=...` line**, because the root cause was not the pin but a receipt that could not state which arm it ran. Golden-comparison receipts are BYTE-IDENTICAL across the change (hidden, verify-bit, tp2-gate, tp2-prefill-gate). |

Rollback: `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=idxdev=0` (pure host scorer). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/yarn/. |
| `set_router_dev` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=routerdev=0` disables) | **ON** (`ROUTER_DEV_DEFAULT` true, flipped 2026-08-31 on receipts) | qwen4_exp DEVICE MoE router (devtwin lane): `qwen4exp_route_topk_f32` runs the full `host_route_softmax_topk` program on device — softmax over 512 logits (order-sensitive reductions sequential on one thread, host op order verbatim; exp through double, the one op not bit-pinned to host libm), top-10 under the pinned tie rule (weight desc, index asc), renorm with the 6.1035156e-5 floor — and writes sel/w(/tok-map) straight into the slots/arrays the grouped NVFP4 dispatch and the captured MoE-tail graphs consume. Replaces the census's **48 blocking router dtoh + host top-k + selection h2d per forward** (devtwin/CENSUS.md; PROFILE-7 §2 sized the per-layer host-twin bubbles at ~12 ms/round ≈ 1/3 of verify wall) and removes the host boundary that made whole-step graphs structurally impossible (PROFILE-2 §graphs doctrine). Engages on grouped NVFP4 paths only (t==1 decode, graph-driver route slots, merged verify columns); the per-expert prefill executor, the DeviceBf16 draft bank, and the TP2 route keep the host twin (they consume host expert ids by construction). **Default ON by design, with the reasons and receipts stated:** exactness contract = selection ids+order EXACT vs the host twin + weights ULP-bounded, gated by tiny arm 0f `gate_route_kernel` (real 512/10 geometry + boundary-straddling tie batteries; rig AND box receipts: worst weight ULP 0) and the live cross-surface audit `MEMRA_Q4E_ROUTER_AUDIT=1` (every device route in a real run recomputed on host and hard-compared, sigrouter precedent; **250k+ rows, ZERO selection mismatches, worst weight ULP 4 on box / 0 on rig** — the bound is per-host because the kernel's exp is double-rounded rather than glibc's, which is why the audit rides every gate battery). Perf (interleaved x5, decision medians, box): **spec at ship admission thinkon 1.168x (75.0 -> 87.6 tok/s), thinkoff 1.174x (-> 120.4), efflow 1.160x (-> 90.2), raw 1.194x (-> 136.2), long-724 1.116x (-> 68.3), all with BYTE-IDENTICAL 256-token chains**; K ladder 1.14-1.18x over K=1..8; plain decode 1.099x with decode graphs ON, 1.112x with them OFF. Rule gates with the seam armed: verify-bit 24/24, spec-gate byte identity raw/thinkon/long, seam-gate 24/24 argmax (worst KL 0.00000), **tp2-gate 24/24** (worst rel 3.0e-5), tiny 26/26. **PAIR IT WITH `idxcache`** (both defaults flip together — the stack is the unit): this seam ALONE with decode graphs ON measured **0.906x**, a real hazard for anyone enabling half the stack (PROFILE-9 §3a; mechanism named unexplained with an nsys residual). A/B twins `--ab-seam routerdev` and `--ab-seam devtwin` (the stack). Rollback: `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=routerdev=0` / `set_router_dev(false)`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/devtwin/, perf/PROFILE-9.md. |

Exists to split "the route kernel costs" from "the missing per-layer sync costs" in a measured regression: on the box the `routerdev` seam WINS 1.083x with decode graphs OFF (13.97 vs 15.13 ms/token) and LOSES with graphs ON (16.46 vs 14.91) — same kernel, so the interaction is in the graph-replay sync structure, not the arithmetic. Read once per process; changes no arithmetic (a sync cannot move bits). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/devtwin/, perf/PROFILE-9.md §3. |
| `set_idx_cache` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=idxcache=0` disables) | **ON** (`IDX_CACHE_DEFAULT` true, flipped 2026-08-31 on receipts) | qwen4_exp DEVICE-resident indexer raw-key cache (devtwin stage 3): below the QSA selection horizon ((base_pos+t)/block <= budget — every row structurally full, real geometry position < 2051) the per-layer idx_proj dtoh exists ONLY to feed the host raw-key cache for a possible future scored row; this seam appends the k-part rows d2d (`copy_rows_col_f32`, exact byte moves — no arithmetic) and materializes the host cache LAZILY at the first scored chunk (same bytes dtoh'd later, so the scored path is bit-identical by construction; mid-run flips pay their debt in both directions — h2d backfill on OFF->ON, delta dtoh on ON->OFF). Kills the census's **12 idx_proj blocking dtoh per forward (+1 per draft chain step)** on every sub-horizon shape and the [t x 640] f32 dtoh per chunked-prefill chunk. Rewind clamps the device row count alongside the host truncation (verify_rewind + mtp_rewind). Selection sets are STRUCTURALLY identical below the horizon (the same fast path, decided from positions alone) and bit-identical above it (same bytes); gates: the tiny chunked arms (mtp-spec-ring chunk 8 / prefill-extend chunk 5) cross the tiny horizon (position 11) with the seam armed, exercising device-append, the lazy catch-up boundary, and the scored path inside existing byte-identity/tolerance arms. The TP2 indexer keeps its host twin and PAYS THE HOST-CACHE DEBT on entry (a single-card prefill under this seam can leave the host cache lagging, and the TP2 migration is one-way — a silent lag would build pooled keys from a short cache and select wrong blocks, with the length assert compiled out in release). **Default ON by design, decided WITH `routerdev` as one stack** (see that row): isolated plain-decode A/B 1.024x, and it is the half that makes the router's sign positive with decode graphs ON; stack rows and gates are in the `routerdev` row (incl. tp2-gate 24/24 with both armed). A/B twins: `--ab-seam idxcache` (decode) and `--router-ab` with `--ab-seam idxcache`/`devtwin` (spec loop). Rollback: `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=idxcache=0` / `set_idx_cache(false)`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/devtwin/, perf/PROFILE-9.md. |
| `set_longatt` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=longatt` forces ON, `longatt=0` forbids) | **AUTO** (`LONGATT_MODE` init 0) | qwen4_exp long-context QSA attention form (yarn affordability lane): attention runs `sdpa_blocklist_f32` over each row's own bounded position list (<= 2052 entries on real geometry) instead of a dense [t, t_kv] mask. **AUTO engages when the block-list form reads strictly fewer KV rows** — as soon as the indexer drops blocks (any non-full row; real geometry: position >= 2051) — **and always past t_kv 12288**, the masked kernel's smem bound (where the dense path REFUSED before this seam existed). **Default AUTO by design, with the reason stated:** this is where QSA's bounded-attention claim becomes real — the dense mask still READS every t_kv row (it only zeroes scores), so masked decode is O(context) bytes, while the block-list form reads <= 2052 rows at any depth (motivating measurement: smoke ladder at a 4k fill spent 97% of the decode token in `qsa.sdpa`, 673 ms/token). Below the drop point every row IS the full causal prefix, so both forms read the same rows and AUTO keeps the untouched masked path (every historical receipt byte-stable). The ON arm is BIT-IDENTICAL to the masked kernel on the same selection (a masked entry contributes exact-0.0 softmax/V terms in the same ascending order). Gates: tiny arm `fixture-longatt` (whole tiny program forced through the block-list path, bit-identical to the masked run) + kernel oracle arm 0f (bit identity vs masked at t_kv 4096; host-twin tolerance past the bound) + `prefill-extend` (chunked driver, modelplan tolerance class — chunk-shaped GEMMs) + the real-checkpoint yarn/ladder receipts. Rollback: `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=longatt=0`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/yarn/. |
| `set_kv_quant` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=kvq` arms, `kvq=0` disables) | **ON** (`KV_QUANT_DEFAULT` true since this lane's receipts landed — owner decision K=q8, V=q5 executed per the flags law: within-config exactness green (spec byte-identity 6/6, verify-bit 24/24 ×3, envelope 24/24 @ 3.0e-5), cross-config drift = the near-tie quant class stated in KVQ-CELL.md (worst rows flip between the eos pair), greedy forks on the valid raw instrument in the f32 class, and the quantized cache measures FASTER: 13.36-13.39 vs 13.53-13.57 ms/token interleaved **- that perf half is DEPTH-SCOPED to SHORT CONTEXT and no longer supports the flip on its own: measured 2026-08-31 at a 100,000-token fill the sign REVERSES to -7.4% decode (33.62 vs 36.30 tok/s) and -7.3% prefill wall (561.4 vs 523.3 s), because the block-list path reads up to ~2,052 rows/token at depth while the shallow row reads almost none. **MECHANISM CORRECTED 2026-08-31 (memory lane, PROFILE-C0.md §2): it is NOT dequant ALU cost, it is the LOAD INSTRUCTION COUNT.** `q4e_deq_q8` recomputes the block pointer from the element index, so the score loop re-loads the SAME fp16 block scale once per element — sm_120 SASS, score-loop inner iteration per 8 K elements: f32 twin 37 instrs / 8 KV loads, `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5` **120 instrs / 8 KV loads / 8 redundant scale loads**. Phase 1 is thread-per-position, so lanes sit on 32 tokens 544 B apart and EVERY load replays 32 ways into 32 distinct sectors: the transaction count is set by the load instruction count, not the byte count, so q8_0 reads 3.76x fewer bytes and issued 2x the transactions and the byte saving could never land. That also explains the flip receipt's OPPOSITE sign (+1.3% shallow): below selection saturation phase 1 barely runs. Same kernel, two sides of saturation — so this is a fixable READ-PATTERN artifact, not the price of quantization; see `set_kv_hoist`. THE DEFAULT STANDS ON MEMORY GROUNDS, which got stronger, not weaker: 11.08 KiB/token vs f32's 49.0 lifts the single-card context ceiling ~165k -> ~731k (4.4x) and buys 4,256 MiB of card-0 headroom at 100k. Do not quote 'kvq is faster' at depth; for a short-prompt product the seam is a ~7% cost, not a free win. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/round2-box-receipts/LADDER.md §4, perf/PROFILE-10.md**) | qwen4_exp QUANTIZED QSA KV cache (kvq lane, 2026-08-31): K rows stored q8_0 (34 B/32 elems, symmetric — K feeds the score dots + rope), V rows q5_1 (24 B/32 elems, affine — V errors average under attention weighting); 48.9 -> ~11.1 KiB/token vs the f32 cache (the yarn cell's 1M blocker was 47.8 GiB of f32 KV). STORAGE-ONLY: attention math runs f32 on dequanted values — the block-list kernel's program with in-place dequant (`q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5`), gated BIT-IDENTICAL to the "dequant rows then `sdpa_blocklist_f32`" composition; a quantized state has no masked-kernel form and always takes the block-list path. The format LATCHES PER STATE at `alloc_state`/`mtp_state` (a byte cache cannot flip mid-run; A/B harnesses allocate per arm); rewind/replay stay slot-addressed. ON arm = Q8Q5 byte caches; OFF arm = the historical f32 exactness instrument (every banked receipt). Gates: tiny arm 0h kernel oracles (append-quantize vs host twins BYTE-identical incl. adversarial blocks — the battery's first run caught device `lrintf(inf)` on subnormal-amax blocks, fixed with an isfinite guard on BOTH sides; fused attention vs composition BIT-identical), kvq determinism + spec byte-identity same-config arms, and the real-checkpoint envelope/ladder receipts this lane banks before the flip. QUANTIZE THE CACHE, DEQUANT AT READ: the fp32 mean-pooling into pooled keys is op-order identical on dequanted values, so the output can only move THROUGH the selection set (near-tie blocks can flip — the 1-ULP FMA lesson). | `set_idx_sel` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=idxsel=0` disables) | **ON** (`IDX_SEL_DEFAULT` true, **flipped 2026-09-01 on receipts**; introduced OFF 2026-08-31) | qwen4_exp DEVICE QSA indexer top-k SELECTION (`qsa_index_topk_u32`, 262k perf lane). `set_idx_dev` moved the block SCORING to the GPU and then dtoh'd the whole score slab so the HOST could run `top_blocks_ascending` per row; at the product window that host half WAS the wall. Measured before: at a 131,072 fill `qsa.idx_host` was **51,235 ms — 83.0% of a prefill chunk** with every GPU section flat within 4%, growing 16x for a 9% depth increase between 120,000 and 131,072, which priced the whole 262,144-token window down from ~32 to ~15-18 tok/s. (The sub-batch/transient-allocation hypothesis for the same cliff was TESTED AND KILLED first — `MEMRA_Q4E_IDX_SCORE_CAP_MF=128` removed the sub-batch transition and bought 4.4%.) This seam runs the selection on device and reads back `rows x budget` u32 (4 MB at the target geometry) instead of the `rows x blocks` f32 slab (up to 128 MB per sub-batch). After: `qsa.idx_host` is **1,066 ms / 9.6%** of the same chunk (48.0x less). **Default ON by design, and every arm is measured** (new-flags law: default, both arms, rollback seam, receipts, in the change that flips it). **Perf, both arms:** interleaved SAME-FILL A/B at 131,072 (`--ladder-ab-seam idxsel`, one prefill, both arms, exclusive measurement lock, sole tenant) off 56.64 ms / 17.66 tok/s vs on 32.25 ms / **31.01 tok/s = 1.7562x**, 7 reps per arm (escalated from 5), 224 warm samples per arm, spreads 2.40% / 2.12%; reproduces an independent two-process pair (1.76x) on both arms. Target window **262,144: 15.21 -> 23.44 tok/s (1.54x)** with prefill **4,779.1 -> 1,439.2 s (3.32x)**. The DISCONTINUITY is gone: 100,000 -> 131,072 was 1.9x slower for 1.31x depth and is now -7.6%, with prefill flat across a continuous 262k fill (82.8 -> 96.4 s per 16k against the OFF arm's 105 -> 475 step). Variance improves as well — both deep rungs auto-escalated to x5 OFF (2.74% / 1.62%) and sit at 0.36% / 0.02% ON, because the 48-thread host top-k pool was also the jitter source. **Exactness is by CONSTRUCTION, not tolerance:** the key is `(~f32_total_asc_u32(score) << 32) \| block_index`, whose ascending order IS the host `sel_cmp` (score desc under `total_cmp`, block index asc) over the WHOLE f32 domain, and keys are distinct so the k-th smallest is a single element. Gates: tiny arm 0i (`gate_qsa_index_topk`) compares ids AND emitted order against `top_blocks_ascending` at REAL budget 512 up to **65,536 blocks = the 262,144-token window's fill/4**, on ragged sub-batch slabs, plus the tie classes — all-zero (STRUCTURAL here: a relu-sum score means a deep row carries long runs of exact +0.0 and a tie-blind kernel is green everywhere else and silently wrong there), boundary-straddling duplicates, and the `total_cmp` domain (signed zeros / subnormals / negatives / NaN) — green on BOTH card classes. Live at-depth audit **rows=1,549,452 mismatched=0 deepest_blocks=32,793**; decode-row-volume audit **120,000 decode-row selections, 0 mismatches**; greedy chain BYTE-IDENTICAL across the seam at a 100,000-token fill; all four rule gates green and identical to the prior battery to the digit. **No pairing requirement** (unlike the devtwin pair, which measured 0.906x when half-enabled): this seam wins alone on every measured surface, on top of the shipped `routerdev` + `idxcache` + `kvq` stack. Rollback: `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=idxsel=0`. Live audit: `MEMRA_Q4E_IDXSEL_AUDIT=1`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-11.md. |
| `set_kv_hoist` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=kvhoist` arms, `kvhoist=0` disables) | **OFF** (`KV_HOIST_DEFAULT` false at introduction 2026-08-31) | qwen4_exp HOISTED K BLOCK SCALE in the quantized block-list attention (memory lever lane, 262k). Selects `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5_hoist` over `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5`. It is the fix for the kvq depth penalty recorded in the `set_kv_quant` row above, and that penalty turns out to be a READ-PATTERN ARTIFACT rather than a tax: `q4e_deq_q8` recomputes the block pointer from the element index, so the score loop re-loads the SAME fp16 block scale ONCE PER ELEMENT, 32 times per 32-element block. sm_120 SASS, score-phase inner loop per 8 K elements — `sdpa_blocklist_f32` **37** instrs / 8 KV loads / no scale loads; `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5` **120** / 8 / **8 redundant scale loads**; `..._hoist` **52** / 8 / **0** (one per 32-element block). Phase 1 is thread-per-position, so a warp's lanes sit on 32 different selected tokens `k_tok_bytes = 544 B` apart and every load instruction replays 32 ways into 32 distinct sectors — the transaction count follows the load INSTRUCTION count, not the byte count, which is why the quantized cache issued 2x the f32 twin's KV transactions while reading 3.76x fewer bytes. The hoist brings KV-cache loads to PARITY with f32 while keeping the 3.76x byte saving. **BIT-IDENTICAL, and the bar is bit-identity rather than a band** — same product, same `acc +=` order, phases 2 and 3 verbatim. **Default OFF by design at introduction** (new-flags law: unmeasured behaviour does not default ON); the flip rides its own interleaved A/B, which is valid SHALLOW because `qsa.sdpa` is depth-INVARIANT (the selection budget saturates at 512 blocks x 4 = ~2,052 rows from ~8k of fill, measured flat at 10.3 ms across 100k/131k/150k) — one confirmation rung at 262,144 is owed before any number is quoted at the target window. Gate: the `gate_kvq_kernels` arm compares the two kernels BIT-IDENTICAL over 18,432 values at REAL geometry (hd=256 = EIGHT 32-element blocks per head slice, nh=24, nkv=2 so the second KV head starts at element 256, max_count=2052) — a tiny head_dim would run the block loop ONCE and never take the per-block scale advance, i.e. the only thing the seam changes, which is the tiny-green/real-broken shape this lane has been bitten by twice. RECEIPTED DEAD END inside the same seam: the identical hoist in phase 3 (weighted V) was tried and REJECTED by the SASS — the base already lets the compiler merge `d` and `m` into one 32-bit load, and phase 3 is already coalesced (adjacent lanes take adjacent `e`, so a warp covers one 24-byte q5_1 block in ONE sector, where f32 needs 128 B for the same dims), so there was no transaction win to buy; `kvhoist` is therefore a ONE-VARIABLE seam and its phase-3 SASS loops are identical to the base kernel's. Rollback: the default, or `kvhoist=0`. Mid-run flippable (no layout latch). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-C0.md. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_PEER_KV_MAX_CAP` | **8192 rows** (`peer_kv_max_cap`, introduced 2026-09-02) | Row ceiling for a PEER-RESIDENT QSA KV state — `alloc_state_reserve` with a `kv_engine` on a different card, i.e. `qwen4exp_real_gate --ladder-kv-dev1`. Past the ceiling BOTH the engine (at state allocation) and the ladder CLI (at arg parse, so the operator does not pay a ~100 s checkpoint load to learn it) refuse by name with the mechanism and the route that works. **Why a ceiling and not a knob to tune (memra#53):** the block-list form is the ONLY read path for a quantized cache and it is a SCATTER reader — `q4e_sdpa_blocklist_q8q5` phase 1 is thread-per-position, so a warp's 32 lanes sit on 32 different selected rows 544 B apart and every load instruction replays 32 ways into 32 distinct sectors (the `set_kv_hoist` row above measures that replay in SASS). On the local card the reading SM's L2 absorbs it: the selected rows are shared by all 24 query heads and by neighbouring query rows in the chunk, and `qsa.sdpa` is depth-INVARIANT. Peer memory is NOT cached in the reading card's L2, so each selected position becomes a PCIe round trip and the redundancy L2 used to hide becomes wire traffic. **Measured, same rung, same binary, one card apart** (`research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/yarn/box-ladder-kvdev1-partial.tsv` vs `box-ladder-smoke2-local.tsv`, rung 4,096, chunk 8,192): first-chunk `qsa.sdpa` **501,436.6 ms = 97.2%** of the prefill vs **1,255.4 ms = 8.2%** local (**399x**); prefill **519.1 s vs 18.7 s** (27.8x); decode **462.6 vs 25.7 ms/token** (18x), decode `qsa.sdpa` 430.5 ms = 95.1% vs 7.8 ms = 26.3%; effective P2P throughput ~220 MB/s. The 32,768 rung of that same run never completed, which is why the file is named `partial`. Scaling the 4,096 point by selected-row count puts a 262,144 prefill at **~9 hours** on this route. **This is what memra#53 was:** the 262k spec cell was scheduled on `--ladder-kv-dev1` anyway and ran twice without ever banking a rung row (45 min, then 113 min, killed by an operator both times), trunk card at **sm 100% / mem 0%** with the KV card's memory flat — warps resident and stalled on peer loads while the local framebuffer sat idle. It never deadlocked and no timeout wrapper would have found it; `YARN-CELL.md` had already ruled the route out on these receipts, but the ruling lived in a lane doc instead of the code, so this row and the two refusals are that verdict made enforceable. **The placement was also backwards on its own terms:** the QSA KV is the SMALL allocation at 10,368 B/row across the 12 QSA layers (q8_0 K 544 B + q5_1 V 320 B at kv_width 512), i.e. **2.7 GiB at a 262,144 capacity**; what does not fit beside ~90 GiB of trunk weights is the **~17.6 GiB MTP draft state**, and `--mtp-dev1` / `load_from_dir_dev1` already place that on card 1. **Default 8,192 by design:** it keeps the shallow smoke arm that motivated the flag legal (the 4,096 rung above completes, slowly) and refuses the depths where the route cannot arrive; raise it only to deliberately re-measure the cliff. Changes no arithmetic — a refusal cannot move bits, and the rig tiny fixture gate is byte-identical across the change (sha line only). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/kvdev1-hang/HANG.md. |
| `set_pool_t` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=poolT` arms, `poolT=0` disables) | **OFF** (`POOL_T_DEFAULT` false at introduction 2026-08-31) | qwen4_exp DIM-MAJOR POOLED-KEY device plane (memory lever lane, 262k). Selects `qsa_index_score_f32_t` over `qsa_index_score_f32` and reads a transposed mirror of the pooled cache. It targets the SECOND depth-scaling term of the deep decode profile: with `idxsel` armed, `qsa.idx_host` measures 2.5 ms at 100,000 / 3.0 at 131,072 / 3.2 at 150,000 — linear in context, extrapolating to ~5.7 ms at the 262,144 target, behind only `ple.host_ngram_gather` among the terms that grow. The score kernel is thread-per-block, so lane L reads `pooled[(block0+L)*head_dim + d]`: lanes are `head_dim*4 = 512 B` apart, one warp's `k[d]` load touches 32 DISTINCT sectors and moves 1,024 B to use 128 B — **8x sector amplification on the one array whose size IS the context** (33.5 MB per scored row per layer at the target geometry). Dim-major makes the same 32 lanes read 32 consecutive floats: 128 B, 4 sectors, zero waste, for about one extra address op per element (46 vs 37 instrs per 8 elements). **BIT-IDENTICAL** — identical loop order and the same explicit `__fmul_rn`/`__fadd_rn`/`__fdiv_rn` the kernel's existing 1-ULP comment requires (default `-fmad=true` contraction here once flipped near-tie top-k picks), only the address changes. BOTH LAYOUTS ARE MAINTAINED UNCONDITIONALLY on append and the seam selects only the kernel, for two reasons and the second is the load-bearing one: (a) the append cost is then identical in both A/B arms, so the measurement isolates exactly the read pattern; (b) a seam that is flippable between timed rounds plus a layout maintained only while armed means an arm that sat OFF leaves the plane missing every row appended meanwhile, and a stale pooled plane scores stale keys — which reads as plausible output rather than as a failure. `pooled_dev_rows` is therefore the single truth for BOTH layouts, so a flip needs no rebuild and can leave nothing behind. Instrument cost, stated: one extra pooled plane (33.5 MB at the target geometry, 1.6% of the ~2 GB free there) plus a transpose over the delta (512 rows per 2,048-token prefill chunk, 0-1 rows per decode step); the losing layout goes away in the commit that records the verdict — carrying both is an A/B instrument, not a shipping design. Mirror growth no longer copies the old prefix forward: a capacity change changes the plane's PITCH, so every dim would land elsewhere and the plane would be half-addressed; it re-mirrors from the host cache instead, one H2D per capacity DOUBLING. **Default OFF by design at introduction** (new-flags law). Gate: the `gate_qsa_index_score` arm runs transpose + transposed kernel against THE SAME HOST TWIN (not device-to-device, which would let a shared mistake pass twice) and includes the pitch trap on purpose — the plane's pitch is the mirror's block CAPACITY (a power of two) while `n_blocks` is whatever the fill is, so `cap_rows == n_blocks` is the ABNORMAL state and a kernel handed `n_blocks` as its pitch reads dim d of block b as dim d of a DIFFERENT block for every d > 0 (silent wrong values, green in any gate where the two numbers coincide); the arm uses cap_rows=4096 with n_blocks=1031, plus an aligned case. Rollback: the default, or `poolT=0`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-C0.md. |

| `set_sel_group` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=selgroup` arms both families AUTO, `selgroup=dn:4:4+gu:16:4` pins the A/B ladder's arms, `selgroup=0` disables) | **ON** (`SEL_GROUP_AUTO` on both families since 2026-09-02; introduced OFF 2026-09-01 and flipped on the box receipts below) | qwen4_exp SUB-WARP PAIR GROUPS for the NVFP4 MoE sel matvecs (`downsel` lane, mtp14). Selects `qmatvec_nvfp4_modelopt_sel_g_f32` / `..._gu_silu_g_f32` over `..._sel_f32_v3` / `..._sel_gu_silu_f32`. **The defect:** the shipped kernels partition the pair loop over all 32 lanes (`for p = lane; p < pairs; p += 32`, `pairs = in_f/32`), and this artifact's geometry does not fill a warp — the DOWN launch has `in_f = expert ff = 640` so `pairs = 20` and **lanes 20-31 hold no pair for the entire kernel** (62.5% lane occupancy, ONE loop iteration per active lane, then a full 5-step shfl tree over 4 accumulators), while gate+up (`in_f = hidden = 2560`, `pairs = 80`) pays 3 warp iterations for 2.5 iterations of work (83.3%). KNEE:q4e-sel-slots-not-bytes established that this section is per-SLOT-WORK bound and not weight-traffic bound (10->60 slots costs 4.13x at fixed bytes; a 6x distinct-byte cut buys 1.101x), so an idle lane is wasted per-slot work. **The shape:** `g` lanes cooperate on one output row, the warp carries `32/g` groups of `rows` rows each, and the reduce is log2(g) shfl steps inside the group; rows per warp is `(32/g)*rows` and the launcher tiles `out_f` by it, falling back to the shipped kernel for any geometry it cannot tile exactly (groups inside one warp have different `o0`, so a ragged tile would put live and dead lanes in the same `__shfl_down_sync` — hence the fallback rather than a clamp, and hence dead groups run the reduce with zeroed accumulators instead of returning early). AUTO is `rows = 4` with `g` = the largest power of two dividing `pairs`, which resolves to down `g=4,rows=4` and gate+up `g=16,rows=4`. **That rule is measured, not chosen:** rows-per-LANE is what pays, not lane occupancy — v3's body exists so ONE pair's 8 activation float4 loads are shared across 4 rows with 4 independent uint4 code loads in flight, and arms that reach 100% occupancy by SPENDING rows-per-lane measure WORSE than the 62.5% kernel they replace (gate+up `g=16,rows=2` hits 100% lanes and loses 12-14%; down `g=8,rows=1` goes 62.5%->83.3% and loses 27%). **ACCUMULATION CLASS, not bit-identical** — a lane chains several pairs and the tree is shallower, so the pair summation order changes, exactly as v3 was against v2. **Except at `g=32,rows=4`, where the kernel degenerates to the shipped program and IS bit-identical**, which is the rollback (same binary) and the A/B control. **Default OFF by design at introduction (new-flags law), and the reason is hardware, not doubt:** this lane had NO approved timing hardware (cloud fleet closed account-wide, no provider approved) and the rig is exactness-only (LAW:rig-gpu-exactness-only), so there is no interleaved serving A/B to flip a default on. What exists: a priced ceiling of **6.42% of the K=5 round (136.2 -> 145.5 tok/s)** from box nsys absolutes already in the repo (`spec/mtp10/nsys/win-spec_cuda_gpu_kern_sum.csv`: gu 111.008 us x 48 layers = 5.328 ms = 16.68% of the round, down 64.576 us x 48 = 3.100 ms = 9.70%) times the geometry's idle-lane fractions; and interleaved rig RATIOS with the bit-identical control arm showing section 1.17-1.21x at t=6 AND t=1 (down 1.33-1.38x, gate+up 1.07-1.11x), reproducing to under 0.5% across three independent passes. Composing the two gives a ~4.2% realization estimate (~142 tok/s), i.e. BELOW the ceiling — which is why the box cells decide and not this row. **ON arm:** the default (or `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=selgroup` explicitly). **OFF arm / rollback:** `selgroup=0` — since the flip an omitted name means AUTO, so the OFF arm of any A/B must be pinned, never implied. **Gate:** `gate_nvfp4_sel_group` (arm 0a2 of `qwen4exp_gpu_gate`) at REAL MoE geometry — which is the point, since the defect only exists at `pairs` 20 and 80 and the shipped tiny arm's shapes have `pairs` 1 or 2: 64 (geometry, shape) cells, `g=32,rows=4` BIT-IDENTICAL to both shipped kernels and every shape's fused arm BIT-IDENTICAL to its same-shape chain (39,168 f32 byte-compared), plus the count-gated pack twin and the tok_map verify merge, planted modelopt NaN scale bytes, non-pow2 macros and duplicate slots; rig receipt `spec/downsel/rig-gate-selgroup.tsv`, `failures=0`. The arm also fixes a trap it found: the shipped sel oracle's 1e-5 rel bound is a TINY-geometry bound and the SHIPPED v3 kernel is 3.672e-5 off the exact host chain at `in_f=640` (6.735e-5 at 2560), so the arm measures the shipped kernel's own deviation per geometry and bounds the twin at 4x it, checking against BOTH the host chain and the shipped output. **Flip receipts (box, 2026-09-01/02, `spec/downsel/box/`):** cell B K=5 spec A/B on the SERVING caches (`kv_quant=q8_0/q5_1 idxq=q8`, thinkon shape, 5x64 interleaved, arm order flipped per hold, spec-vs-plain byte identity on every arm): auto 90.07 / 90.60 / 90.08 vs off 87.38 / 87.14 / 87.47 tok/s (+3.1 / +4.0 / +3.0%); the f32-pinned first run agreed (+2.6 / +3.1 / +2.9%). Cell C t=1 decode at 32k: 0.9999x / 1.0001x; cell D 262k rung: 1.0003x (5 reps each, escalated) — no depth regression. Honest note: the pre-registered bar in DOWNSEL.md section 6 (gain > BOTH arms' spread) was missed by a hair on every hold (gain 2.9-3.8% vs per-arm spreads 2.3-4.0%) while the sign never flipped in six holds; the owner took the flip on that record (2026-09-02, PR #56). The sampled twin is still owed for a serving claim (greedy is the instrument). **Rollback:** `selgroup=0`. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_IDXSEL_AUDIT` | unset | **INSTRUMENT, never a perf arm** (262k perf lane): with `idxsel` armed, dtoh the score slab anyway and recompute `top_blocks_ascending` on the host from the SAME bytes, hard-comparing block ids AND their emitted order; any difference is an Err, not a warning. It deliberately restores the very dtoh the seam deletes, which is why it can never ride a timing row. The receipt line is `# idxsel-audit rows=<n> mismatched=<n> deepest_blocks=<n>`: `rows=0` is the silent-no-op failure the counter exists to catch (seam off, or no query row past the 2,048-token selection horizon), and `deepest_blocks` is what makes an audit an AT-DEPTH audit — a shallow run can report thousands of clean rows without ever exercising a 2^16-block selection. |
| `set_ple_cache` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=plecache` arms, `plecache=0` disables) | **ON** (`PLE_CACHE_DEFAULT` true as of 2026-09-01; introduced OFF 2026-08-31 and flipped on the receipts below) | qwen4_exp INCREMENTAL PLE n-gram id cache (262k perf lane). `ple_block` calls `host_ngram_ids`, a `ngram_ids` twin over the FULL token history, then slices the last `t` rows — so a decode step at a 150,000-token fill rebuilds 150,000 rows of hashes to consume ONE. Measured on the deep decode profile with `idxsel` armed: `ple.host_ngram_gather` is **7.3 ms, 19.5% of the token**, second only to `qsa.sdpa` and the largest remaining HOST section. This seam appends to a per-state id cache instead. **It is also a receipted correction of the assumed mechanism, which is why it is written down rather than quietly fixed:** the owner's lever named the 102 GB host table gather as the synchronous cost to overlap with compute (the vendor's own cookbook design point). The gather is `t x 16` random rows — 16 reads of 160 f32 at decode, microseconds. The 7.3 ms is the O(context) ID RECOMPUTE in front of it, so async-prefetching the table would have bought ~nothing. Profile before each change, and the profile moved the target. **Default ON as of 2026-09-01, by design** (new-flags law: the decision, its reasons and its receipts are written, and the receipts landed before the flip). Introduced OFF because it was unmeasured; flipped on: **131,072 33.52 -> 25.91 ms (1.2938x), 262,144 41.38 -> 28.30 ms (1.4620x)**, x3 interleaved with both arms sharing one prefill and one exclusive lock hold, lead flipped on odd reps, no escalation owed. The gain GROWS with depth because the deleted work is O(fill) per token, and at 262,144 this was **the largest section of the whole token** (13.2 ms, 28.9%, ahead of `qsa.sdpa`); armed, it leaves the top twelve while every other section holds to a tenth of a millisecond. It also removes decode JITTER (cv 2.48% -> 0.11% at 131k; p99 41.70 -> 28.38 ms at 262k), which is a p99-latency result and not only a throughput one. EXACTNESS rests on the two arms that can falsify it: the **real-geometry truth pin** `MEMRA_Q4E_PLECACHE_AUDIT=1` -> `rows=32828 mismatched=0 deepest_fill=32828` (cached ids vs the full twin at the CHECKPOINT's own multipliers/sizes/offsets, over both growth shapes), and the **behavioural control** — the greedy chain is IDENTICAL across the seam on the same artifact (`-1/0/-1/26` both arms, argmax 10/10 both arms). `verify-bit` 24 and spec byte-identity 256 also pass, but they are INTRA-ARM and an intra-arm identity gate cannot detect a CONSISTENT error, so they are not what the flip rests on. STILL OWED: `--verify-bit-deep 131072` with the seam armed, which failed three times on the box with ~96 GB free (an instrument failure, not a seam one) and is itself intra-arm. COST: one i64 id vector per state, `fill*16*8` = 33.5 MB of HOST memory at 262,144; no device memory, no new kernel. Exact by CONSTRUCTION: `shift_right_ignore_eos` at position p reads `history[p - shift]` under an eos scan that only moves left-to-right, so `ids[token]` is a pure function of `token_ids[..=token]` and never changes when a token is appended — the cache appends and never recomputes a row. Rewind/divergence is a longest-common-PREFIX compare, never a length compare (a length-only check keeps another sequence's hashes, and the failure is fluent output from the wrong embedding rows). Gate: tiny arm 0j (`gate_ple_ngram_cache`, host-only) compares ids EXACTLY against the full twin over decode-shaped and ragged-prefill-shaped growth, eos segment resets (adjacent, leading, trailing), all-eos, repeated rewinds to DIVERGING prefixes, and shorter-unrelated-sequence state reuse. Rollback: one token, `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=plecache=0`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-12.md (A/B, profiles, variance, the audit) and PROFILE-11.md §5 (the diagnosis that retargeted the lever). |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_PLECACHE_AUDIT` | unset | **INSTRUMENT, never a perf arm** (262k perf lane): with `plecache` armed, recompute the FULL `host_ngram_ids` twin and hard-compare the chunk's ids; any difference is an Err. It restores exactly the O(context) work the seam deletes. Receipt line `# plecache-audit rows=<n> mismatched=<n> deepest_fill=<n>`, where `rows=0` is the silent-no-op failure the counter exists to catch and `deepest_fill` is the history length actually reached — the field that separates an at-depth audit from a shallow one. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_MEASURE_LOCK_TIMEOUT_S` | **7200** (2 h) | **INSTRUMENT deadline, never a perf arm** (262k host-lever lane): how long `MeasureLock` keeps retrying `flock(..., LOCK_NB)` before it hard-fails. It exists because the obvious implementation — a plain BLOCKING `flock` — turns one specific mistake into a silent hang with no output: **nesting this lock inside an outer `flock(1)` wrapper on the same path self-deadlocks.** flock locks are per open-file-description, so taking `LOCK_EX` on a fresh fd while the PARENT process holds `LOCK_SH` on the same file waits on one's own parent, forever, and during a 25-80 minute prefill that is indistinguishable from a slow box. So the wait is polled, announced every 60 s (`# measure-lock-waiting mode=.. waited_s=..`) so a stall is visible WHILE it is happening rather than after, and the deadline error names the self-deadlock as the FIRST SUSPECT with the fix ("use EITHER the outer wrapper OR `MEMRA_Q4E_MEASURE_LOCK`, never both"). The default is generous on purpose: under the whole-invocation protocol a legitimate wait is a sibling's ENTIRE cell, load and prefill included, so a tight deadline would fail honest waits. Lower it only in a harness that knows its siblings' cell lengths. This is the "loud failures fail quietly" law applied to the lock itself — the diagnostic written to end a silent failure must not itself be silent. Owned by the host-lever lane; documented here because the flags census reads the WORKTREE, so an uncommitted row reds CI for every lane behind it. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_MEASURE_LOCK_PREFILL` | **`sh`** (prefill chunks take the shared lock — today's behaviour) | **INSTRUMENT, never a perf arm** (262k host-lever lane, 2026-09-01): `off` skips the per-prefill-chunk `LOCK_SH` while leaving every TIMED block exclusive. **Default `sh` by design** (new-flags law): per-chunk yielding is the better protocol and stays the default. `off` exists because per-chunk yielding only bounds a measurement's wait when EVERY participant is fine-grained — a sibling holding `LOCK_EX` across a whole cell starves a per-chunk `LOCK_SH` acquirer completely, **measured at 960 s on this lane with BOTH cards at 0% utilisation and both lanes blocked**, which is a worse outcome than an unlocked prefill. With `off`, the timed rows stay exclusive and therefore stay valid — the part that actually matters — instead of being traded for an idle box. Use it only while a coarser sibling protocol is in force, say so in the cell's receipt, and prefer `sh`. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-12.md §6. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_MEASURE_LOCK` (equivalently `qwen4exp_real_gate --measure-lock <path>`) | unset = **NO serialisation** | **INSTRUMENT, never a perf arm** (262k host-lever lane, 2026-08-31): path to an advisory lock file that `qwen4exp_real_gate` takes in TWO MODES. **`LOCK_EX` around timed blocks** — the ladder's rung timing rounds, and each interleaved `--ladder-ab-seam` rep (a PAIR; both arms must be taken under one lock, which is what makes them comparable), released BETWEEN reps so an escalating A/B cannot monopolise the box. **`LOCK_SH` around each prefill CHUNK**, taken and released at every chunk boundary. **Why not the existing `flock <lock> -c '<cell>'` convention:** at 262k that puts 11-80 minutes of PREFILL inside the exclusive window to protect a prefill wall the cell is not claiming, while the siblings sharing the box need seconds-to-minutes of serialised decode; inside the instrument the exclusive window per cell drops from ~30 minutes to ~2. **Why the shared lock is per CHUNK rather than per prefill, which is the subtle part:** `LOCK_EX` blocks until no holder remains, shared ones included, so a 25-minute prefill holding `LOCK_SH` would block every exclusive waiter for 25 minutes and rebuild the very window this removes — and `LOCK_SH`→`LOCK_EX` upgrade on one fd is not atomic on Linux (the shared lock drops first, so two upgraders can interleave or deadlock). Releasing at each chunk boundary makes prefills YIELD: a waiting exclusive gets in at the boundary and the prefill's next acquisition blocks until the timed block finishes, so nothing runs unlocked and the worst-case wait for a measurement is ONE CHUNK (~11 s at chunk 2048 with `idxsel`). Untimed cells (oracles, audits, traces) should use `flock -s` on the same path and then yield the same way. **It also makes the discipline checkable rather than promised:** the receipt line `# measure-lock what=<block> mode=<ex\|sh> path=<lock> waited_s=<n>` states that a timing was serialised and in which mode, where otherwise the only evidence is the launcher script — this lane has already discarded TWO banked arms for exactly that gap (`r2ab131k-off-1`, timed with no lock present; then an interleaved pair timed while three gate processes computed at once), each caught by a sibling agent reading `ps`/`ls /tmp` rather than by the receipt. `waited_s` is the second signal: a long wait proves a sibling was measuring; a zero wait during a known battery is itself worth a look. **Default unset by design** (new-flags law: unmeasured behaviour does not default ON) — it changes no arithmetic and no timing when unset, and a lock that cannot be opened or taken is a HARD `Err` rather than a warning, because "proceed unserialised" is exactly the invalid timing the flag exists to prevent. Advisory per-file, so an in-process holder and an outer `flock(1)` wrapper serialise against each other correctly and agents can adopt it incrementally. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-12.md. |
| `qwen4exp_real_gate --ladder-ab-seam <seam> [--ladder-ab-rounds n] [--ladder-ab-steps n]` (CLI, no env) | unset = **OFF** | **INSTRUMENT, never a perf arm** (262k host-lever lane, 2026-08-31): WITHIN-PREFILL interleaved decode A/B over one `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS` name. After each rung's normal timing row the arms alternate OFF/ON on the SAME state with the lead flipped on odd reps, so the arms share one prefill and box clock drift cannot sit between them. Exists because a DECODE-only lever (`plecache`: `host_ngram_ids` is O(context) per CALL, amortised 2,048 ways by a prefill chunk and paid in full by a decode step) otherwise costs 6 prefills at 25-80 minutes each to time ~100 decode steps. Escalation is x3 then x5 when either arm's own spread exceeds 0.5% **or the verdict sits inside 2x the pooled spread** — a verdict smaller than the instrument's noise is not a verdict. **SOUNDNESS BOUND, the caller's to respect:** eligible only for seams whose state is rebuildable from the token history, so arming mid-run cannot leave stale state behind (`plecache` qualifies; its oracle covers cold cache, stale cache and diverging prefix). A seam that latches a layout or mutates a device mirror arm-dependently must use per-arm processes. Seam names are validated against `seam_exists` BEFORE the checkpoint loads, and the ladder's state reservation covers the A/B's own positions and its own escalation. Restore is exact (`seam_state`) and named in `# ladder-ab-restore`. Emits `# ladder-ab`, `# ladder-ab-verdict`, `# ab-profile` (a section profile per arm at depth). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-12.md. |
| `qwen4exp_real_gate --host-probe` (CLI, no env) | unset = **OFF** | **INSTRUMENT, never a perf arm** (262k host-lever lane, 2026-08-31): the host-side census `prof_section` is blind to, since bracketing a section with device syncs prices host wall time but says nothing about why the host thread was slow. Three fields, all from /proc and `sched_getcpu`: **voluntary** context switches are the blocking-sync counter (a CUDA wait that SPINS parks nothing and shows zero; one that BLOCKS shows one switch per wait), so `vol_cs` over a decode segment is the spin-vs-block audit and the event-wait-bubble census as a number rather than a claim read off a flag default; **nonvoluntary** switches are preemption, which on a shared box is a sibling agent's run, making this the field that says whether a timing was taken against contention; **`sched_getcpu` sampled per step** counts launch-thread migrations, i.e. the sticky-thread question as a measurement. Deliberately NOT sampled per step: the per-thread /proc parse (four threads x a file read x a 65 ms step is ~0.1% and would land inside the number it measures) — the census is taken at segment boundaries and `sched_getcpu` (vDSO) rides every step. Emits `# host-thread` rows and the `vol_cs`/`nonvol_cs`/`launch_cpu_migrations` fields of `# ladder-ab-verdict`. Independently, `# ladder-jitter` (sd / cv / p99 / max / count-above-1.5x-median over the same samples the rung row medianed) rides EVERY ladder run, because a median is not a measurement. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/perf/PROFILE-12.md. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_IDX_SCORE_CAP_MF` | **32** (= `32 << 20` floats = 128 MB, today's behaviour exactly) | qwen4_exp QSA indexer block-score slab cap, in MEGA-FLOATS (long-context/262k perf lane, 2026-08-31): bounds the row sub-batch in `indexer_select_rows`, which scores `n x max_blocks` on device, `uninit`s that slab, and ends each sub-batch in a BLOCKING `dtoh` before the host runs top-512 per row. **Exists to test a measured cliff, and it may set the 262k performance ceiling.** Measured: `qsa.idx_host` grows LINEARLY with fill to 120,000 (2,709.9 -> 2,914.7 -> 3,198.7 ms at fills 100k/110k/120k) and then jumps **16x to 51,235.1 ms — 83% of a prefill chunk — by fill 131,072**, with every GPU section flat across the step; decode steps with it (31.2 ms at 120,000 -> 57.4 ms at 131,072, i.e. ~32 -> ~17 tok/s). The arithmetic lands exactly on the boundary: rows per sub-batch is `cap / complete_blocks`, and at fill 131,072 `complete = 32,768` gives **exactly 1,024**, so 2,048 scored rows fit in EXACTLY 2 sub-batches and one block deeper needs 3 — at depths where card 0 has only ~2-4 GB free. **Default 32 by design:** it reproduces today's behaviour byte-for-byte, and a cap change alters no arithmetic at all (identical scores, identical selected sets — only the batching of a device launch moves), so this cannot be a correctness seam in either direction. Decisive test: if the cliff MOVES with the cap the mechanism is confirmed and the fix is a persistent pooled slab (no per-sub-batch `uninit`/`htod`) or a cap that keeps the transition out of the product window; if it does not move, the hypothesis is dead and the next suspect is the blocking `dtoh` count. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/round2-box-receipts/LADDER.md §4c. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_IDXQ_AUDIT` | unset | **INSTRUMENT, never a perf arm** (idxq lane): with a quantized raw-key cache, maintain a parallel f32 twin cache (re-adding the idx_proj dtoh the idxcache seam removed) and recompute every scored row's block selection from it, counting rows whose selected set differs (`idxq_audit_stats`, `# idxq-audit` receipt line in the real gate: scored_rows/flipped_rows/symdiff_blocks). This is the selection-identity gate for picking the idxq precision on real prompts at depth; rows=0 in a receipt means the audit never engaged (loud-failures law). Read once per process. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/kvq/. |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_EP_MAP` | unset = **EVEN split** | qwen4_exp TP2 per-layer MoE EXPERT PLACEMENT map (tp2-prefill lane, owner directive 2026-08-31 `LAW:coactivation-expert-placement`): path to a `memra-ep-map-v1` JSON minted by `tools/build_expert_placement_map.py` from `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE` route traces, assigning every expert id to card 0 or 1 per layer. Reads the FROZEN SHARED format rather than a lane-local one on purpose — the glm5 arm consumes the same maps through `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_MAP`, so a map minted from qwen4_exp traces is comparable with theirs, and the placement lane becomes a measurement + config exercise instead of an engine rewrite. One resolved `LayerPlacement` per layer is carried ON THE SHARD and drives BOTH the card-1 bank upload and the decode/prefill route splits, so the upload and the routing cannot disagree (a half-applied placement moves expert weights under the router and reads as a model bug, not a config bug). **Default OFF by design, with the reason stated:** no placement has been measured yet on this artifact, and under the new-flags law an unmeasured behaviour does not default ON. The unset arm is the even split `rank = expert / (experts/2)` and is BIT-IDENTICAL to the pre-seam engine BY CONSTRUCTION, not by tolerance — an even assignment makes the card-1 bank gather a contiguous copy of exactly the suffix the old code sliced, in the same order — which is what makes it a usable CONTROL ARM for the placement A/B. Fail-closed, loudly, on: `format` != `memra-ep-map-v1`, `ranks` != 2 (TP2 is two cards), `expert_count` != the plan's, a MoE layer the map does not cover, a rank id outside {0,1}, and an UNBALANCED layer — card 1 must own exactly `experts/2` because the card-1 bank halves are equal-size device allocations, so an unbalanced map is out-of-bounds rather than merely slower. Rollback: unset (or `=0`). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/kvq/ROUND2-STATUS.md (seam banked; the placement A/B is the NEXT lane). |
| `MEMRA_Q4E_TP2_GATE_RED` | unset (`none`) | **GATE HARNESS ONLY, never a serving door** (tp2-prefill class gate): injects one deliberate defect into the TP2 MoE split so the class gate's near-tie band can be shown to DISTINGUISH rather than absorb — a band is only a bar if a wrong program lands orders outside it (the `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_GATE_RED` pattern). Values: `skip-peer-moe` (drop the peer card's routed-expert contribution from the join), `peer-local-ids` (send peer-owned experts to card 0's bank at their peer LOCAL slot — the plausible off-by-remap bug: right magnitudes, wrong experts), `reverse-peer-weights` (reverse the peer half's slot weights within each token), `none`/`0`/unset. An UNKNOWN value refuses at the first MoE layer rather than silently running clean. A red arm PASSES by being LOUD — `max_rel` past `TP2_RED_FLOOR` (1e-3) or a broken greedy tape — AND by having engaged the peer card (`tp2_expert_split_stats().peer_slots > 0`); a red that never routed a peer expert is a no-op and proves nothing, which is the vacuity the glm5 lane's first seed search actually hit. Paired instrument: `--tp2-class-calibrate` measures the bands without barring, for the calibration run that SETS the constants (calibrate downward, never up). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/kvq/ROUND2-STATUS.md. |
| `set_prefill_grouped_all` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env of its own; set by `--tp2-prefill-gate` around its PRIME forward only) | **OFF** (`PREFILL_GROUPED_ALL` init false) | **GATE INSTRUMENT, never a serving seam** (tp2-prefill class gate, 2026-08-31): run a `HeadMode::All` SINGLE-CARD forward on the GROUPED MoE executor instead of the per-expert one. Exists because the class gate's PRIME regime compares an all-rows single-card forward against an all-rows TP2 forward, and TP2's `tp2_moe_rows` is grouped on BOTH cards — so with this OFF the comparison straddles two independent variables (the TP2 expert-half split AND the grouped-vs-per-expert executor difference) and **the executor term dominates by ~100x**: measured on this artifact, grouped-vs-grouped lands at **1.4e-5** while per-expert-vs-grouped lands at **2e-3..4e-3** (and the tiny gate's own `prefill-extend` arm prices the executor difference alone at 1.865e-4 on a fixture). A band calibrated against the straddled number would be two orders too loose for the question the gate asks — the same 'calibrated against nothing' failure the two-regime gate was written to end. **Default OFF by design, with the reason stated:** OFF is byte-for-byte today's behavior, because `HeadMode::All` selects the per-expert executor and that is the reference-shaped program the goldens capture and every hidden/greedy exactness receipt in this lane rest on; flipping the default would silently re-base all of them, and there is no perf argument on the other side (per-expert is the SLOW path). Proven, not asserted: with the seam present and default OFF, `hidden-gate` and `verify-bit-gate` and `tp2-gate` receipts are **BYTE-IDENTICAL** to the pre-change round-2 baseline (round2-box-receipts/, `g2-*` vs `r2base-*`). Scoped ON around one block in the gate and restored immediately, so it cannot leak into the chunked/decode regimes, which already run the serving program on both sides. Rollback: it is off; nothing in serving reads it. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/round2-box-receipts/TP2-CLASS-GATE.md. |
| `set_verify_graphs` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=vgraph`, `--vgraph-ab`) | **OFF** (`VERIFY_GRAPHS` init false) | mtp-spec VERIFY chunks capture the per-GDN-layer scan run — `dwconv` + t x (`gdn_scan_step_at` + per-column state snapshot) + the conv-history roll — as one segment graph per chunk width, replayed on later chunks of that width. 14 launches x 36 GDN layers = 576 launches/round at t=6, the densest all-device launch run in the chunk, and the only SERIALLY DEPENDENT one (every column reads and writes the recurrent state), so its issue latency cannot overlap. **Default OFF by design, with the reason stated:** unmeasured behavior does not default ON, and this box's own graph receipt is small — the trunk decode graphs bought +1.3% (22.24 -> 21.96 ms) for an 84-graph, 2,400-launch reduction (PROFILE-2.md), i.e. issue is otherwise well overlapped here. ON arm = replay; OFF arm = the identical eager launch sequence (one shared closure, so bit-identity is by construction — same kernels, same parameters, same baked addresses, same order; only the CPU issue path moves). Rollback seam: set it false, or drop `vgraph` from `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS`. Requires the step workspace (address stability) and no section profiler (sync boundaries cannot cross a replay). Gates: `--verify-bit-gate` (24/24 bit-identical rows) + `--spec-gate` (byte identity) — never a tolerance. **MEASURED 2026-08-30: FLAT, stays OFF** — eager 9.96 vs vgraph 9.97 ms/token interleaved x5, speedup 0.9992 inside a 0.11 ms per-arm spread, chains byte-identical (first_divergence -1). 576 launches/round collapsed to 36 replays moves nothing, so launch issue is not this model's decode bottleneck at t=1 OR t=K+1; the seam is kept as the receipt and the rollback path, not as a candidate default. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/spec/mtp9/ab-vgraph-k5-trim0-nvfp4.tsv, perf/PROFILE-6.md. |
| `set_verify_fused` (qwen4exp_gpu library seam, no env; `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS=vfuse`, `qwen4exp_real_gate --verify-cost-probe <reps>x<chunks>`) | **OFF** (`VERIFY_FUSED_DEFAULT = false`) | COST INSTRUMENT for the mtp12 fused-verify lever, not a serving arm. Routes a `1 < t <= k_cap` mtp-spec verify chunk through the FUSED (prefill-style) program instead of the exact per-row programs, so the lever could be PRICED before any kernel was written. **Default OFF by design, and it is not a candidate default:** the arm has NO REWIND. The fused GDN chunk scan materializes only the final recurrent state, so no per-column GDN/PLE stash exists and `verify_rewind` refuses by name (`fused_chunk`) rather than silently rewinding to a wrong state — which is why the seam is wired to a throwaway-state timing probe and NOT into `spec_generate`. It also FORCES `grouped`, because a fused verify chunk that fell into the per-expert prefill executor would measure that executor (minutes/chunk) instead of the fusion. **Both arms** = the same chunk shape at the same absolute positions with the same fed tokens and the SAME 4t-byte argmax-sink readback (so the delta is the program, not a ~6 MB logits dtoh on one side); `base_pos == 0` stays fused on both (the gen-157 rule is untouched). **Rollback seam:** drop `vfuse` from `MEMRA_Q4E_SEAMS`, or `vfuse=0`. Gates: rig tiny-gate arm `mtp-vfuse`, gated like the reference class rather than by byte identity — vfuse is a different program allowed to differ in BITS but not in MEANING (argmax 4/4 per row, worst abs 2.506e-4 rel 2.506e-4 vs the reference executor, 2.636e-5 vs the exact arm; argmax sink fed; rewind refusal executed). The exact per-row arm REMAINS the byte-identity gate arm (spec-gate, verify-bit) and is unaffected. **Why the lever is dead (code fact, not estimate):** every trunk dense mat and the MoE column merge dispatch on `t`, not on `exact` (`(2..=12).contains(&t) && verify_mt_on()`), so at t=6 BOTH arms already take the weight-shared multi-token kernels for everything expensive; flipping `exact` off changes only the hyper read gate (which LOSES the hc-diet path), the GDN scan, and the m=1 indexer/PLE projections. Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/spec/vfuse/ (VFUSE.md, verify-cost-k6-*.tsv, verify-cost-sections-k6-*.tsv, tiny-fixture-gate-vfuse.tsv). |
| FR-Spec draft-head TRIM (qwen4exp_gpu `build_draft_trim`; `--draft-trim <ranks.txt> [--draft-trim-n N]`, `--trim-sweep`, `--trim-ab`) | **OFF** (no trim = full-vocab draft head) | The DRAFT scores only the top-N own-gen rank subset instead of the full 248,320-row vocab (`mtp.lm_head` is 4.757 ms/round at K=5, 54% of the draft's cost — mtp5 spec-profile); rows are gathered D2D from the SHARED lm head (bf16 twin when present, 168 MB at N=32768), so a trimmed row's logit is bit-identical to its full-vocab twin and only `out_f` changes. The TARGET verify stays full-vocab and the accept walk compares against it, so the trim cannot move the committed output BY CONSTRUCTION — only ACCEPTANCE (an out-of-list token is unproposable, i.e. a guaranteed one-round miss). **Default OFF by design:** a trim is a distribution artifact of the EXACT artifact and requant (DRAFT-REGIME.md law 1), so it is armed by naming a ranks file and is never inferred; absent one, the draft head is byte-for-byte the mtp8 program. Ranks are built by `--owngen` from this artifact's OWN generations over the real-shaped, class-labelled prompt pack, with the coverage table (global + per class) and law 1's >=4x-topN corpus floor in the receipt. ON/OFF arms: `--trim-ab` (interleaved, hard-fails on any chain divergence); width table: `--trim-sweep`. **MEASURED 2026-08-30: -16.6%, stays OFF** — full head 8.26 ms = 121.03 tok/s (accept 0.840, len 5.12) vs trim N=5,538 9.91 ms = 100.93 (accept 0.561, len 3.82), speedup 0.8339; every width 1,024..5,538 loses and the table is monotone in coverage. The 44.8x head cut was real; acceptance paid more than it back. Binding constraint is corpus SCALE (93,152 counted tokens discovered only 5,538 distinct ids = 2.2% of the vocab) — law 1's >=4x-topN floor was met 4.2x over and did not catch it. On an in-class held-out cell the loss shrinks to 2.6% but does not cross zero. Exactness held at every width (all five d2t maps reproduced the control chain). Receipts: research/qwen4exp-bringup-20260829/spec/mtp9/{ab-trim,trim-sweep}-*.tsv, perf/PROFILE-6.md. |
| Card-1 draft placement (qwen4exp_gpu `load_from_dir_dev1` / `from_loaded_checkpoint_dual`; gate binary `--mtp-dev1`) | **OFF in the gate binary** (explicit flag; the single-card program stays the mtp9 regression twin) — **the RECOMMENDED spec-serving placement is ON**, and it is a PREREQUISITE, not a preference, past ~400 prompt tokens | mtp10: the MTP draft block (weights + the ~5 GB DeviceBf16 expert bank) plus a private same-bytes copy of the shared lm head (f32 + bf16 twin) build on card 1; draft state/workspace/trim live there; the spec loop P2P-crosses the wide seed rows per round (`memcpy_peer_async` on the draft stream) and the drafted ids keep their 4-byte dtoh. Draft-engine identity ENFORCED (`check_draft_engine` — UVA would make a wrong-engine call "work" at PCIe speed instead of failing).
